Absolute Regression
===================

Chapter 451: Be Careful with Documents You Sign.
------------------------------------------------
Juhyangwol walked toward the inn entrance.

A martial artist from the Hwangdo Trading Company recognized her and greeted her.

"Young Lady Ju, have you arrived?"

"What is the matter?"

"It is nothing. If you enter, the young master will arrive shortly."

Juhyangwol looked at the man who had been arguing. She turned her head without much thought, but inwardly she flinched.

The gaze of the young, handsome man was clear and deep. His unforgettable appearance captivated her gaze.

Behind the young man stood a middle-aged man. He was not looking this way. With his arms crossed, he was looking at the people passing by on the street.

The moment she saw them, some indescribable emotion surged up abruptly, then vanished. Since this was her first time experiencing such a thing, she could not tell what emotion it was.

At any rate, the moment she saw the two men, she could sense it.

'They are father and son.'

They did not resemble each other in face, yet somehow their auras were alike.

And behind the middle-aged man stood another man.

'A bodyguard?'

They were none other than Geom Mugeuk, Geom Woojin, and Hwi.

Hwi had said he would eat separately, but Geom Mugeuk had forcibly dragged him along. This trip was a journey for him and his father, yet at the same time, he wished for it to become a journey for Hwi and his father as well.

At least during this trip, he wanted Hwi to walk on his own two feet as much as possible rather than remaining hidden. To meet eyes with his father and eat together.

That was why this moment was precious. Once the trip ended and they returned, they would go back to their original lives.

If asked why he was going this far for him—

—it was because he knew. He knew Hwi's life, who had gladly given his life for his father. Having protected his father all his life, he ended his life by sacrificing it in the end.

Juhyangwol asked the martial artist of the Hwangdo Trading Company.

"Why are you preventing these people from entering?"

"Today, Young Master Hwang has rented out this entire inn."

Juhyangwol's expression hardened.

It was Young Master Hwang who had set the meeting place at this inn. It had been strange that he insisted on meeting here when he could have simply come to the Ju Clan Sword Manor; it seemed he had done so to flaunt the wealth capable of renting out the entire inn.

Geom Mugeuk spoke to her.

"I am a man traveling with his father. I have come a long way to treat my father to the cuisine here."

She looked at Geom Mugeuk and Geom Woojin. Normally, she would have sent them away, telling them to go to another inn. That would have been the right thing for them as well, because more bad than good would come of entering.

But today's choice was different.

"Let them in."

"Young Master Hwang ordered that no one be allowed inside."

"I will take responsibility. Come, let us enter together."

As Juhyangwol tried to enter with the three of them, the martial artists still blocked the way.

When Juhyangwol looked at them coldly, only then did the martial artists step aside.

"I am truly grateful. Thanks to the young lady, I can treat my father to a meal."

And so Juhyangwol led the three of them inside.

She was not originally this kind of person.

'Yes, it was because he said he was traveling with his father.'

Because on her way here, she had been thinking of her late father the entire time.

"Was there a reason it had to be this inn?"

"Among the dishes here, the cold platter made with twelve kinds of vegetables and the duck boiled with fine medicinal herbs are truly delicacies. They are good for one's health as well. I desperately wished to treat my father to them."

She envied the man before her eyes.

If her father were alive, she would truly dote on him. She would have bought him all the healthy, delicious food he wanted as they traveled together. But by the time she had come of age, it was already too late.

"Still, making a scene with the Hwangdo Trading Company's martial artists was not a wise choice."

Recently, the Hwangdo Trading Company's influence was formidable, so she had thought the men before her would know of them. Yet the young man regarded them as no great matter.

"Even if it were the Murim Alliance blocking the way, not the Hwangdo Trading Company, I would have to enter. It is my first trip with my father."

Still, to go so far as to bring up the Murim Alliance so grandiosely? As if he didn't even know what kind of place the Murim Alliance was.

Geom Mugeuk looked at Geom Woojin and added,

"It is a chance that was somehow obtained."

Geom Woojin was simply going along with what his son wanted.

The one most surprised by that sight was Hwi.

While Geom Mugeuk was arguing in front of the inn, the Cult Leader had silently waited. It was something unimaginable given the personality of the Cult Leader he knew.

"Mister, please choose something you'd like to eat as well."

"I am fine."

"No, you must. You absolutely must choose. If you don't choose, I won't order. Did you just hear that? The growling sound from Father's stomach? Ah, our poor father. Unable to maintain his dignity……"

"I will choose."

How could anyone win against Geom Mugeuk? In the end, Hwi chose a dish as well.

Juhyangwol, watching that scene, flinched in surprise. Without realizing it, she had been watching them with a smile on her lips.

"If anyone comes and asks why you are here, give them my name. I am Juhyangwol of the Ju Clan Sword Manor."

"Thanks to the young lady, I was able to become a dutiful son."

As she turned to leave, she offered a piece of advice.

"If this is fate, then let me speak out of goodwill. Your swords stand out too much."

Geom Mugeuk had wrapped his sword in top-grade heavenly silk, while Geom Woojin had wrapped his in bandages. She meant that the sight of father and son both hiding their swords stood out instead. In truth, it was not the swords that stood out, but the two men themselves; yet she was not aware of this herself.

"It is because they would stand out even more if the cloth were removed."

"What do you mean?"

Geom Mugeuk merely smiled. No matter how much they stood out, one could not roam the Central Plains carrying the Cheonma Sword.

"Please enjoy your meal. You likely won't need to pay for the food."

She said this because he had rented out the entire inn.

"I appreciate the words, but I don't think we can do that."

"What do you mean?"

"Those people likely did not pay money when renting this place. At least we should pay."

Juhyangwol was startled.

"We heard it together, didn't we? That he rented out the entire inn."

"Then go ask the innkeeper how much was agreed upon as compensation. They likely did not set an amount. Avoid the martial artists at the entrance and ask the innkeeper discreetly."

She went to speak with the innkeeper and returned.

"How did you know?"

The innkeeper had said this: "It's fine. Since esteemed guests are holding an important meeting, I should help out."

In other words, he would not be properly paid.

"It is something martial sects often do. They say they will settle everything later, but you'd be fortunate if they even threw down payment for the food they ate when leaving. There are few cases where they compensate for the other guests they turned away."

Juhyangwol's face flushed slightly.

If that was the case, then strictly speaking, it was shameful that she had tried to curry favor with them.

"I will verify it when Young Master Hwang arrives."

"If you confront him about it, think carefully about who will suffer the damage."

She understood what he meant. In the end, it was the innkeeper here who would suffer.

"Still, I will surely repay the favor I owe the young lady when the opportunity arises."

It was then that a man entered the inn.

"Lady Ju, have you arrived already?"

He was Hwang In, the successor of the Hwangdo Trading Company. She had thought he would come laden with bodyguards, but surprisingly, he had come alone.

As Hwang In walked toward her, his gaze naturally shifted to the people with her.

"Who might these people be?"

Then the martial artist at the entrance came in and whispered to him what had transpired earlier.

Hwang In thought it unexpected. Based on prior investigation, Juhyangwol was not someone who meddled in others' affairs so broadly.

Since they might have been chased away had she not intervened, Juhyangwol said to Hwang In,

"They are my guests."

"Ah, I see."

Hwang In responded gently but was inwardly puzzled.

'She is not someone who would go this far. Was the investigation wrong?'

Then one thought came to mind.

'Could they be on her side, pretending not to be?'

He thought it might be so. The family head must have been uneasy sending her daughter alone.

"It is an honor to meet you."

They were truly three extraordinary individuals.

Since there had been no such individuals in the prior investigation, they must surely have been called in from outside.

"I am Hwang In of the Hwangdo Trading Company."

Geom Mugeuk then greeted him on behalf of the others.

"We are merely passing travelers; you need not concern yourself with us."

Hwang In thought the other party was intentionally concealing their identities.

'Are they hired hands? Or are there still those who remain to help the Ju Clan Sword Manor?'

It didn't matter anyway. Today's matter was not something to be resolved by force.

Just then, the food Geom Mugeuk had ordered arrived.

"Ah, Father. Please try this first."

As if he were truly someone who had come here to eat, Geom Mugeuk focused on the food.

He placed the food into small bowls and served them to his father and Hwi.

Their disregard for him only strengthened Hwang In's conviction.

'They are striving not to be suspected because they are on the same side.'

The pride of martial artists, indeed!

If asked who was easiest to deal with as a merchant, Hwang In could say with confidence: martial artists. That foolish pride.

"Come, let us go as well."

Hwang In sat across from Juhyangwol at the center table of the inn.

"Have a drink."

Hwang In poured her a drink, but Juhyangwol set the cup down and did not drink.

"Why did you choose me?"

"I suppose I thought young people would have an easier time understanding each other."

Juhyangwol had never heard rumors about Hwang In. He was a man with neither particularly good nor bad reputation.

"What is your reason for wanting to invest in us?"

"There is only ever one reason a trading company seeks to join hands with a sword family: we need the power of the sword."

"Could you not invest that money directly and establish a sword troop within the trading company?"

"Of course, we are raising our own martial artists. But do you know? The Murim does not acknowledge a merchant's sword. They openly look down on you for buying your sword with money. That is why it is difficult to recruit experts. They hate hearing that they were sold for money. Honestly, do you not think so as well?"

Caught off guard by the sudden question, Juhyangwol could not answer no. She had indeed harbored such feelings, at least a little.

"That is why. To do great business in the Murim, the name value of the Ju Clan Sword Manor is necessary. Ah, of course, I do not mean to belittle you or your family, so please do not misunderstand. We desire a relationship of mutual aid and coexistence."

But Juhyangwol did not wish to join hands with them.

It was not because she knew the Hwangdo Trading Company well, nor because she knew the internal affairs of the sword manor. Nor could she predict what results this cooperation would bring.

It was because she could not trust her mother's judgment. Because her mother always made mistakes when hasty.

She should not judge matters with the Hwangdo Trading Company that way, but emotionally, she kept thinking so.

Was it because her expectations were low? Hwang In seemed decent, at least better than she had thought. She had assumed he would be a man who only flaunted his money and chased women, but he appeared to be someone with considerable purpose and thought.

"How do you propose we join hands?"

"We merchants do not believe in promises made with words. We only believe in contracts."

Hwang In handed her the documents he had brought.

"This is an agreement stating that our trading company and the Ju Clan Sword Manor will join forces. You need only read it and sign. Normally, the family head should do this, but since the family head sent you as a representative and you are also her daughter, Young Lady Ju is fully qualified. Take your time reading it, even if it takes a while. Documents that require a signature must be read carefully."

Juhyangwol was inwardly flustered. She had thought they would meet today and have various discussions, but she had not known he would prepare an agreement.

'Did Mother know?'

At least one thing was certain: if she returned having reached an agreement with them, her mother would be pleased. After all, it was her mother who had tried to send her out dressed in a splendid palatial gown.

She wanted nothing more than to refuse and storm out, but given the sword manor's current circumstances and her mother, she could not.

Juhyangwol read through the agreement.

Some parts were understandable at a glance, while other passages remained confusing even upon rereading.

Such unnecessary words—why were they needed? She wanted to say: erase it all and write only this.

She wanted to say this, but the problem was that she could not concretely say what should remain.

And certain clauses seemed necessary yet also seemed unnecessary at the same time. There were many ambiguous parts.

'To roughly understand is to know nothing at all.'

No, it was worse than knowing nothing. She was glossing over uncertain clauses with vague understanding.

'How dare they toy with us?'

She read to the last page but could not find any passage worthy of such an outburst.

Should she just sign it and take it to her mother? Shouting that it had been her mother's will, not her own, so don't regret it later?

She wished she could return to a few years ago, to those immature days.

'If only Father were still alive......'

At that moment, her gaze unconsciously turned toward where Geom Mugeuk sat.

As if aware that this was an important occasion, they were conversing quietly as they ate. Only when she pricked her ears did their conversation reach her.

"Is it delicious?"

"It is decent."

"What about you, mister?"

"It is delicious."

"You see, didn't I say it was delicious? Let us travel to every famous inn in the Central Plains, then kidnap the best chef from the tastiest place and take him back home! Or rather, one chef each to suit our tastes—three in total!"

At his joke, Juhyangwol smiled without realizing it. Though it was no time to laugh, she felt an inexplicable sense of relief merely from watching them.

She had been plagued by nightmares lately and her mind was unstable; why did she feel so at ease? They were people she had met today for the first time.

Then, her eyes met Geom Mugeuk's.

Could eyes that had been speaking such frivolous words truly be so deep?

The moment that thought crossed her mind, she surrendered herself to the pull of fate.

She sprang up from her seat, took the agreement, and began walking toward Geom Mugeuk.

Hwang In was inwardly flustered by her sudden action, but he watched carefully for the time being.

As she walked up to Geom Mugeuk, she thought,

'This is madness.'

Today was truly a day when her instincts ran wild.

"You said you would repay the favor you owe me, did you not?"

"I did."

"Can you repay that favor right now?"

"We are martial artists, not merchants; we are people who cannot stand being in debt."

She held out the agreement to Geom Mugeuk.

"Then find me a reason to tear this up."

Absolute Regression, Episode 452

Chapter 452: When a Merchant Gives Up Profit
--------------------------------------------
It was impossible.

She had gone to someone she had met for the first time today and asked him to help with something this important.

If her mother or the warriors of the sword house heard this story, they would ask if she had gone mad. They would consider it more than a mistake—an incident.

‘But why do I feel so relieved?’

When she asked him to find a reason for her to tear it up, her whole body had tingled, and she had felt good. It was as if her instincts were shouting at her. That she had done truly well. She had never experienced anything like this before.

On top of that, the other party’s answer was perfectly suited to such madness.

“How many should I find for you?”

Such confidence, when he had not even looked at the agreement?

Whether those words were bravado or confidence did not matter. Because the sense of relief she had felt earlier returned to her heart.

“The more, the better.”

Just as she was about to hand the agreement in her hand to Geom Mugeuk—

Hwang In, who had been watching them until now, stepped forward.

“Miss Ju, that will not do.”

At Hwang In’s calm restraint, Ju Hyangwol’s hand stopped in midair.

“Miss Ju may not know this well, as you are not yet accustomed to agreements of this sort, but such an agreement must not be shown to an outsider.”

Hwang In was certain that Geom Mugeuk was an outsider. When he had investigated the Ju Clan Sword House in advance, there had been no one who gave off such a distinctive impression. And judging from the way Ju Hyangwol had spoken and acted toward them earlier, he was clearly not one of their people.

Then Geom Mugeuk asked Ju Hyangwol,

“Did you make a promise beforehand that you would not show it to outsiders?”

“No. I saw that agreement for the first time today.”

Hwang In cut in again.

“It is a contractual convention.”

Geom Mugeuk also dealt with him calmly.

“I wonder why someone so well versed in convention would make such a mistake.”

“What do you mean?”

“Isn’t it convention, when drafting an agreement like this, to send it in advance so the other party has ample time to review it before meeting? It seems to me that the one who broke convention was you.”

At those words, Hwang In could not refute him.

Ju Hyangwol only learned that fact now. She had never experienced anything like this before.

“So let us leave the word convention out of this today. It was absent from the start.”

Hwang In realized that this young opponent before him was not as easy to deal with as he looked.

“Who are you?”

“A man who became a filial son thanks to Miss Ju.”

Hwang In could not understand what that meant. From the words alone, he could not tell whether they were on Ju Hyangwol’s side or truly people she had met at the entrance of the inn.

In any case, it was certain they were unusual people. Even while this tense exchange was going on, Geom Ujin and Hwi continued eating.

They even exchanged natural words such as, “Try this as well,” and, “Thank you.”

“If you read the agreement, you will know, but our merchant guild has made many concessions. That is why we were negligent in sending the agreement in advance. We thought you would be satisfied once you saw it in person. Read it. Then you will understand what I mean.”

There was clear confidence in Hwang In’s eyes.

‘Do you think you will find anything just by reading it?’

Geom Mugeuk accepted the agreement from Ju Hyangwol’s hand.

“Let’s have a look, then.”

Geom Mugeuk placed it on the table and began to read it slowly.

Even then, Geom Ujin and Hwi continued their meal. They paid no attention to this matter at all. At a glance, one might think he was doing so because he was angry at his son, but judging from his expression and the atmosphere around him, there was no sign of anger whatsoever.

Under the concentrated gazes of Ju Hyangwol and Hwang In,

Geom Mugeuk read the several-page agreement all the way to the final page.

“Why is there so much difficult wording?”

“Agreements use their own terminology. So? Did you find the parts that would justify tearing up that agreement?”

After a brief pause, Geom Mugeuk answered.

“I don’t think there are any such parts.”

Disappointment flickered across Ju Hyangwol’s face. She had thought that if it was this man, he might find some kind of answer.

By contrast, a satisfied smile appeared on Hwang In’s face, as if to say, I knew it.

“Now that you have confirmed it, please sign.”

Since she had even taken the unexpected action of showing the agreement to an outsider, it became even harder to delay signing now.

‘Was Mother’s choice right this time?’

She politely bowed to Geom Mugeuk.

“Thank you for reading it.”

Just as she was gathering up the agreement from the table—

An unexpected person broke the silence.

“First page, sixth line.”

Surprisingly, the one who spoke was Geom Ujin.

Ju Hyangwol quickly read the clause Geom Ujin had mentioned.

It was a clause stating that if anything occurred that damaged either party’s honor, they would compensate each other monetarily by mutual agreement.

“Is there a problem with this?”

Ju Hyangwol asked cautiously.

If the sword house and the merchant guild came to an agreement, who would have the advantage? For that reason, she had thought this clause was actually favorable to the Ju Clan Sword House. Had he perhaps named the wrong item?

Instead of answering, Geom Ujin pointed out other places.

“Second page, ninth line. Third page, second and seventh items.”

Ju Hyangwol read all the places Geom Ujin mentioned. They were not difficult contents; rather, they were parts she had thought were favorable to them. Parts she had believed the other side had conceded in order to join hands this time.

For example, there was a clause like this: if it was clearly revealed that the other party was at fault, then beyond monetary compensation, the use of force would also be permitted.

As Hwang In had said, it was a clause in which they had conceded as much as possible, allowing the sword house to attack the merchant guild.

‘Why is he only pointing out the parts they conceded?’

She looked at Geom Ujin in puzzlement.

Geom Ujin, who had been quietly gazing at her, abruptly asked,

“Are you certain that your side is stronger?”

“!”

In that instant, Ju Hyangwol’s heart dropped.

It was something she had not doubted even once throughout her reading of the agreement—no, not even once since entering this inn. She had naturally believed that the sword house was the stronger side.

What if that was not true? What if we are not the stronger side?

Then all of these clauses would become poisonous clauses, returning later as blades.

Preoccupied with the vague and difficult-to-understand parts of the agreement, she had overlooked the simplest and most important part.

The most frightening trap is not the trap that is hidden. When it is plainly exposed but no one regards it as a trap, its power is incomparably great.

Geom Ujin’s final words resolved all those doubts in one stroke.

“When a merchant gives up profit, there is always a reason.”

Ju Hyangwol felt that Geom Ujin was right.

‘That was what I had been missing.’

At the same time, the emotion that surged over her was the comfort and relief she had felt earlier.

She thought that perhaps the relief from before had not been because of the younger man, the son, but because of this father.

Geom Mugeuk smiled brightly and said,

“You were watching everything while pretending not to.”

“Didn’t you place it on the table and read it so that I could see it too?”

“I get my quick wits from you, Father. Ah! I should have inherited your brilliant mind as well.”

“You must have noticed too.”

Geom Ujin believed that Geom Mugeuk had thought the same thing. He must have noticed that he had been glancing at it from time to time. But Geom Mugeuk denied it to the end.

“In a parent’s eyes, their own child always looks the smartest.”

Ju Hyangwol realized that whenever she watched the two of them, she kept being drawn into them. It was the same now.

This was no time to stare at them blankly, and yet she had been looking enviously at the conversation between the two.

She looked at Hwang In.

As befitted the blood of a merchant, he controlled his emotions well.

“It seems there has been a misunderstanding. We are nothing more than a mere merchant guild. If we were that strong, why would we seek to join hands with the Ju Clan Sword House?”

Then Ju Hyangwol asked him,

“You tell me. Why?”

If they possessed military force stronger than the sword house’s? If they had hidden that fact and presented this agreement? Then this was not simple cooperation but a conspiracy.

Hwang In remained calm.

“It is a misunderstanding.”

“I’m sure it is.”

Ju Hyangwol lifted the agreement and tried to tear it up before his eyes.

At that moment, Geom Mugeuk stopped her.

“Would tearing it up truly feel satisfying?”

Ju Hyangwol turned her head toward Geom Mugeuk.

“It would be better to keep it without tearing it. What you are holding is evidence.”

“Evidence?”

“Is it not evidence that they tried to swallow the sword house whole? If you tear it up, it may feel satisfying to you, but the one who will feel truly satisfied is that man.”

“!”

Ju Hyangwol felt dizzy.

At first, she had brought it to Geom Mugeuk and asked him to find a reason to tear it up.

So she had thought that reason had finally been revealed. She had been about to tear it up with delight. But that was not it. Even now, she had narrowly avoided the danger of falling into a trap.

And the traps continued to be discovered.

“Yes, I will keep it well.”

As she tried to place the agreement into her bosom, Geom Mugeuk spoke up again.

“You must not put it there.”

“Why?”

“Weren’t you about to tear up that agreement because you acknowledged that the other side is stronger?”

“That’s right.”

If not, then there would be nothing wrong with this agreement.

“If you possess evidence that is unfavorable to them, how do you think they will act?”

When Ju Hyangwol looked at him with eyes that seemed to say, surely not, Hwang In calmly said,

“I will say it again, but we are merely merchants.”

Ju Hyangwol could now see him objectively. That this mere merchant was far too composed.

She asked Geom Mugeuk,

“Then what should I do?”

“You should store it somewhere safe. Entrust it to the safest place you can think of.”

Ju Hyangwol fell into thought for a moment.

“Since I was young, there was someone I considered the safest.”

“Then entrust it to that person and have them keep it.”

“I can’t. He has already passed away.”

The place where she had felt safest was in her father’s arms.

It was something she did not need to say, yet for some reason she wanted to say it. Was it because she had been under such great psychological pressure today? Or was it because she wanted to tell these two men who had strangely captivated her? She could not understand her own emotions.

She forced herself to be brave. In any case, this matter was her own, and it concerned her family.

“I will handle it myself. Thank you very much for your help.”

The moment her words ended,

another unexpected situation unfolded.

Geom Ujin extended his hand toward her and said,

“I will keep it for you.”

The moment she heard those words, Ju Hyangwol’s chest tightened.

Over the sight of Geom Ujin holding out his hand, the image of her father overlapped.

Geom Ujin was looking at her bluntly, but the father superimposed over him was smiling.

“I found the parts that should have been torn up, so I suppose I should take responsibility as well.”

Geom Mugeuk knew that was not the reason.

His father, influenced by him lately, could help her briefly. Yes, he could even gladly find the contents of the agreement for her.

But to take custody of this?

When there was no knowing when or how their affairs would be concluded? When they had matters to handle in Shaanxi? This was absolutely unlike his father.

Then there was only one conclusion.

The Hwangdo Merchant Guild was connected to the matter we had to handle in Shaanxi.

The person who had heard from Sima Myeong and knew about this mission was Father. I had deliberately not asked. Since I would find out once we arrived anyway, I wanted to focus entirely on traveling with Father at least during the journey there.

When all the Demon Venerables had gathered, Strategist Sima had said this:

—To move experts and an organization of this caliber, an enormous amount of funds would be necessary. After investigating in secret, we found a clue to track down the bastards behind them.

Since he had said they had tracked the source of funds, they would likely have to deal with people of immense wealth even after going to Shaanxi. Perhaps the Hwangdo Merchant Guild was one of their subordinate organizations, or a place connected to them in some way.

“It is all right. Give it to me.”

After hesitating, Ju Hyangwol, as if possessed by something, handed the agreement to Geom Ujin with trembling hands.

Hwang In, who was watching the scene, felt stifled in his chest.

When he had come here, he had truly never imagined he would experience something like this.

Hwang In’s gaze turned toward Geom Mugeuk.

‘Whoever you are, you have provoked the wrong opponent.’

They must have thought him a mere peddler. But if they had known what kind of place the Hwangdo Merchant Guild was, they would not have dared step forward so fearlessly.

“I am busy, so I will take my leave for today.”

Geom Mugeuk did not simply let him go as he turned away.

“Oh, and don’t just leave. Pay the price for renting out the entire inn.”

To Hwang In, who turned back with a slightly stiffened face, Geom Mugeuk added with a smile,

“If you are a merchant, shouldn’t you settle accounts properly?”

Soon, Hwang In smiled calmly and replied,

“Of course. I am someone who is precise when it comes to accounts given and received.”

He called the innkeeper, paid the price, and left the place.

Once Hwang In had left, Ju Hyangwol felt as though a storm had swept through.

What if I had not risen from my seat? What if I had signed right there?

How would her fate have changed?

“According to what you said, they may come to steal that agreement.”

At her concern, Geom Ujin called the innkeeper and ordered new dishes.

“Since you have sat down, eat before you go.”

Ju Hyangwol experienced an unexpected situation until the very end. She had never thought she would end up eating with these people.

The one who reassured her in Geom Ujin’s stead was Geom Mugeuk.

“You need not worry. The thing you entrusted is now…”

Geom Mugeuk looked at his father with gentle eyes.

“Stored in the safest place in this murim.”

Absolute Regression, Chapter 453

Chapter 453: What If I’m More Popular Than My Son?
--------------------------------------------------
The safest place in all the martial world?

Joo Hyang-wol did not take those words as mere bluster or bravado. She understood them like this.

‘This man trusts his father so much.’

She knew what that felt like. Because she had been the same. To her, her father’s embrace had been the safest place in the world.

Like a revolving lantern, memories of her father rose in her mind.

His appearance teaching her martial arts when she was young.

Leading the martial artists of the Sword Manor with magnificent dignity.

His back as he stood alone in the courtyard, gazing up at the moon.

Even his appearance, frail from illness before he passed away.

In those many scenes, there was no sign of her stepping forward proactively. She had never taken the initiative to ask her father to share a meal, nor, like these people, had she ever taken the lead to ask him to travel.

There had been so many things she wanted to do with her father when she was young, but as she grew older, they gradually faded. Even if there had been something she wanted to do, she had likely postponed it. Because she had always believed her father would be by her side.

‘Father, I miss you.’

Her gaze turned toward the person who had made her miss her father so keenly today. Toward the one who had silently stretched out his hand to receive the agreement.

When she first saw him in front of the inn, some indescribable emotion had surged up suddenly before vanishing. A feeling she was experiencing for the first time. Had it all been leading to this kind of connection?

Then she realized she hadn’t properly conveyed her gratitude to him. She sprang up from her seat and expressed her thanks with a polite bow.

“Thank you from the bottom of my heart for taking the agreement.”

Geom Woo-jin raised his head and looked at her.

“It’s fine.”

It was a brief word, but it sounded incomparably steadfast and gentle.

Geom Muguk sensed that the gaze with which she looked at his father was extraordinary.

If he let this pass at a time like this, he wouldn’t be Geom Muguk.

—What would you do if you’re more popular with women than your son?

At Geom Muguk’s telepathic message, a scoff formed at Geom Woo-jin’s lips. Surprisingly, that scoff did not deny his son’s words.

‘Father is a man too,’ is that what you mean?

While he was looking on with such eyes, Joo Hyang-wol, who was beside him, spoke to Geom Muguk.

“It seems the young hero likes his father very much.”

“Can you feel that?”

When she nodded, Geom Muguk raised his voice toward his father.

“Did you hear that? The whole world knows how much I love my father.”

Of course, this poignant echo did not reach his father. Geom Woo-jin spoke to Hwi as if uninterested.

“Pass the tea over here.”

Hwi lifted the teapot and politely poured into Geom Woo-jin’s cup.

They were at the same table, yet the two seemed to be in different worlds.

‘Pretending not to see, pretending not to care, even though you read every line of the agreement!’

That was why every moment of this journey with his father was joyful.

“It’s enough that the young lady understands.”

“Come to think of it, I don’t even know your name.”

“I am Geom Yeon.” (劍煙)

He used the alias he always used, and added the words he always spoke when saying this alias.

“The yeon of smoke, not the yeon of fate. We met like this and shall vanish like smoke.”

Geom Muguk introduced his father and Hwi.

“As you may already know, that heartless man is my father, and this man is my uncle.”

At the introduction of “uncle,” Hwi flinched.

Having expected to be introduced as a clan martial artist or a bodyguard, how flustered must he have been to be introduced as his father’s brother?

He was bewildered, but his father wore an expression that said he had done well.

Uncle Hwi, you may be called an uncle. You are more than qualified for it.

“If there were a competition for taciturnity, those two would take first and second place side by side.”

At Geom Muguk’s words, Joo Hyang-wol smiled in empathy. Truly, those two were taciturn.

Especially the man introduced as uncle did not say a single word. However, she felt one thing. He was watching everything, and listening to everything.

“Of course, I’m speaking enough for the two of them as well.”

Joo Hyang-wol laughed. He certainly talked a lot. But then her expression soon grew serious. This was not a time to be sitting around laughing; she had been smiling thoughtlessly again.

In the meantime, the newly ordered dishes came out.

The new dishes were placed before Joo Hyang-wol, and Geom Muguk ate the remaining dishes he hadn’t been able to eat while talking earlier. His father and Hwi had separately set aside food for him.

Even though time had passed while they talked, the food did not cool and remained warm.

Geom Muguk could tell. That his father had warmed the remaining food with his Yang internal energy.

‘No one would believe it, but I think I take after that father.’

During this journey, Geom Muguk was gradually seeing new sides of his father.

Joo Hyang-wol ate silently. There was a strange comfort this place gave her, but worry lingered in her heart.

How could Geom Muguk not read such a heart?

“Are you worried?”

“To be honest, I don’t know what I should do from here.”

This was also a statement that could tarnish the honor of the Joo Family Sword Manor. Such concerns were things she should have discussed with her mother or the clan’s experts upon returning.

But she could already picture what the situation would be like when she returned.

Her mother would loathe that the negotiations had fallen through, but if she admitted she had entrusted that important agreement to a complete stranger, it would cause an absolute uproar. She could picture her mother screaming and getting angry.

But her mother wouldn’t know. That the reason she had suspected the alliance with the Hwangdo Merchant Guild was a wrong choice from the start was because she couldn’t trust her mother.

That the distrust had been born from her habit of unfiltered emotional outbursts at her child.

This time too, that distrust had been accurate. Because her mother had misjudged the opponent. Moreover, though she couldn’t know their exact intentions, the situation was more serious than ever.

Because she felt that, she didn’t want to leave this place. The moment she rose from here, that serious situation would become her reality in full.

She wanted to rely on these people. Even though she knew she shouldn’t, she kept wanting to escape.

“Return and prepare.”

Joo Hyang-wol, who had been silently looking down at her food, raised her head to look at Geom Muguk.

“Your opponent has bared their fangs to devour you. Then you must prepare to fight.”

She should have drawn strength from Geom Muguk’s words, but worry took precedence instead.

“My mother won’t believe me.”

From that single sentence, he could guess what kind of relationship she had with her mother.

Geom Muguk, who had been gazing at her for a moment, spoke unexpected words.

“Parents don’t usually believe their children. Do you know why?”

“Why?”

“Because they’ve been through that time. Saying absurd things, insisting, lying, rebelling. They watched over our entire growth, didn’t they? How could they believe it when such a brat says they’ve finally grown up? Even if you said you mastered the world’s best lightness skill, wouldn’t your child be the one who makes you tell them to watch out for carriages when crossing the road? Because they remember us running forward without looking left or right.”

Joo Hyang-wol smiled faintly.

“You speak as if you’ve raised three children.”

“Parents and children are a two-way relationship. We keep forgetting that.”

Why was it? The moment she heard those words, Joo Hyang-wol thought of her mother.

Geom Muguk looked at his father.

“Our father doesn’t believe me either.”

It was something he had said before, referring to when he had stepped far back from a dangerous spot while training in the Nine Transformations Demonic Art.

Then Geom Woo-jin casually tossed out to Joo Hyang-wol.

“Young lady, don’t believe that fellow either.”

At those words, Joo Hyang-wol smiled without realizing it.

“That is too much!”

Contrary to his words, the gaze with which Geom Muguk looked at his father was soft.

You can slowly believe in me. After this journey ends, later, much later, it is fine even if you say you believe in me five years from now.

Geom Muguk spoke to her again.

“So go back and try talking with your mother.”

“I don’t have the confidence to persuade my mother.”

“The merchant who left earlier will also be persuading someone upon returning.”

“What do you mean?”

“Since today’s agreement failed, he will have to explain and persuade someone of this matter. To be entrusted with the task again. To say he will definitely succeed next time. Even your opponent, who is in a more advantageous position than you, is making such efforts, so why are you already trying to give up?”

“!”

It was a rebuke she had never expected.

“It’s because you’re thinking roughly, relying only on intuition. Don’t do that.”

Geom Muguk rose from his seat, went to the table where the Hwang merchant and she had been sitting earlier, and brought back the brush she had been about to sign with.

“Write it down. What words to say and how. How to begin your first sentence, and if your mother reacts this way, how to respond. Write it all down.”

Joo Hyang-wol looked at Geom Muguk in surprise. At first she thought it was a joke, but he was speaking in earnest.

“What if it still doesn’t work?”

“Write that down too. What you will do then.”

“!”

“It may look simple and easy, but nothing is as difficult as persuading people. Among them, persuading family is the hardest. It’s difficult precisely because you know each other too well. So don’t try to persuade her; do it with the mindset of having a long-overdue conversation. Have you ever asked your mother what is troubling her these days? Do you know if she is eating properly?”

Joo Hyang-wol could say nothing.

Because at some point, she had only been watching for her mother’s mistakes while longing only for her father.

Joo Hyang-wol rose from her seat. The words that the Hwang merchant would also be persuading someone, and that rather than persuading her mother, she should have a conversation. They made her spring to her feet.

Yes, it wasn’t the time to be fleeing. I will not become such a weak person.

“Thank you for the advice.”

She looked at Geom Woo-jin. Over his appearance, her father was superimposed once again. Father, I will be strong.

“Thank you so much for today.”

Just as she was about to turn and leave, she glanced back and spoke.

“But the safest vault in the martial world—you can find it when you want to, right?”

Geom Muguk accepted her first-ever joke with a smile.

“Judge for yourself by looking at that face. For now, I can’t open it.”

As if she were truly trying to judge that, she quietly looked at Geom Woo-jin, then politely bowed at the waist and left the inn.

Now only three people remained in the inn.

“Seeing that she has the composure to make jokes, I think she will do well when she goes back.”

Separate from that, Geom Woo-jin had a different concern.

“The Hwangdo Merchant Guild is not an opponent the Joo Family Sword Manor can face.”

This confirmed it. That the Hwangdo Merchant Guild was somehow connected to the mastermind behind this incident.

“That is likely.”

“Why did you provoke her when you know that?”

“We will take the lead in handling this matter anyway, but they need to change as well. If they don’t, they will be devoured by others someday.”

There, a frivolous joke was added.

“She is a woman who likes my father more than I do, so I must help her. I truly felt my father’s popularity this time. As expected, I am unmistakably my father’s son!”

As before, this joke did not land.

He seemed about to say, “Nonsense!” Instead, his father made an expression as if to say, “I was quite popular in my youth.” Anyway, no one could match that arrogant, self-satisfied expression.

“What will you do now?”

Geom Woo-jin asked his son’s thoughts first.

“They will probably come to seize the agreement first, won’t they?”

It was not a guess made because they were connected to the mastermind.

It was because of what the Hwang merchant had left behind earlier.

—“I am a man who is exact in matters of give and take.”

It was disguised with a merchant’s smile, but he was a man who could not stand being indebted.

“He seems cautious, so he will try to uncover our identities first.”

Predictions born from Geom Muguk’s vast experience flowed smoothly.

“Since we emphasized that we are merely merchants, he probably won’t act directly. Then he will entrust an information broker to ascertain our identities.”

It was absolutely impermissible for his father’s or his own identity to be revealed.

“I intend to lay groundwork with the information brokers first. So that our false identities flow to them.”

Geom Woo-jin silently nodded. Watching that, Hwi could feel it. That the Cult Leader was satisfied with his son’s judgment.

He had served the Cult Leader for a long time, but he had never seen him express his emotions as openly as he had recently.

Hwi understood this change in the Cult Leader.

Because he had always been by the Cult Leader’s side, he had heard all kinds of information about Geom Muguk. Where he had eliminated whom, and where else he had eliminated whom.

But the Geom Muguk he heard about and the Geom Muguk he experienced directly were completely different.

Through this journey, he was feeling it clearly. That the Young Cult Leader was a person whose process shone brighter than the results.

If he had heard only the results of this matter at the Heavenly Demon Hall, he would know only the fact that the Young Cult Leader had helped the Joo Family Sword Manor from the Hwangdo Merchant Guild’s hidden scheme.

Then these things would have been omitted.

The fishing bets made on the way here, the walks and martial arts training with the Cult Leader, the advice given to Joo Hyang-wol. Even the effort to find a place for himself, a mere bodyguard.

He would not have known of all those emotional exchanges; they would have been omitted. Had he not experienced them by his side, he would never have known how brilliantly they shone.

The Cult Leader was now experiencing such a son. The five years of time the Young Cult Leader had requested were flowing by even in this moment.

Until this journey, Hwi had thought it an excessive gamble for the Young Cult Leader. That the Cult Leader’s heart would never change.

But during this journey, he felt the thought that perhaps it might not be so growing little by little.

“We will disguise ourselves as the Gansu Seodo Sect.”

Whenever he used the name Geom Yeon, he had always disguised himself with that sect. Since it was a sect actually operated by the Heavenly Demon Cult, the more thoroughly they investigated, the more solid the disguise would become.

“An orthodox sect.”

As expected, his father wore a displeased expression, but.

“If not for this opportunity, when else would you try being the head of an orthodox household?”

Geom Woo-jin snorted and asked.

“What next?”

He looked curious as to what choice his son would make.

“Aren’t there people behind them that we need to find?”

As if those words were true indeed, Geom Woo-jin silently waited for the next words.

“Dealing with these fellows is easier than turning my hand over. But if we handle them rashly, the forces behind them will hide themselves thoroughly or prepare themselves.”

They had to approach them naturally and dig in. For that, the Hwangdo Merchant Guild could rather be an opportunity.

Geom Muguk revealed his plan.

“That is why I intend to make them write one more agreement. The kind of agreement that is absolutely favorable to the stronger side, I mean.”

In other words, it meant he would make them draw in the Seodo Sect, the sect they were disguised as.

“We will sign exactly according to the terms of that agreement.”

Absolute Regression – Episode 454

Chapter 454: If You Want to Persuade My Father.
-----------------------------------------------
Juhyangwol returned home.

As expected, her mother had been waiting only for her return.

“What happened?”

Before answering, Juhyangwol asked her mother.

“Did you know that Hwangin was bringing the agreement today?”

“I didn’t know.”

Had she truly not known? Suspicion arose immediately. That was the reality of the relationship between her mother and herself.

“So? Did you sign the agreement?”

And just from this reaction, Juhyangwol could tell how much her mother was rushing.

Normally, she should have said something like this:

“They presented the agreement to you? Without sending it to us beforehand?”

And she should have asked worriedly,

“Surely you didn’t sign that agreement?”

But now her mother was looking at her with worried eyes. Eyes that asked, *Surely you didn’t fail to sign it?*

She recalled the paper she had tucked into her bosom. She had written down everything she wanted to say to her mother, just as Geommugeuk had advised.

She hadn’t simply written it down. She had anticipated various situations and even written how to respond to each. Among what she had written was this very situation.

“No, I put it off.”

A look of disappointment crossed her mother’s face. She had anticipated disappointment as well, so she had written what to say in this moment.

The fortunate thing was that her mother had a predictable personality. That was why they fought so much, and that was why Juhyangwol could never truly hate her.

That was why, at the very top of all her writing, she had written this first:

*Don’t shout no matter what! Speak softly!*

Because the moment she raised her voice in conversation with her mother, she had never once properly finished talking. Though she well knew that a low voice carried strength and that her emotions were conveyed best when she spoke calmly, with her mother alone, this never worked well.

But today, she was doing well.

“It’s something that must be handled carefully.”

There was no point telling her about what had happened at the inn earlier; her mother would never accept it. Having entrusted the agreement to someone she had just met was enough to make her mother jump in shock.

“We need to learn more about the Hwangdo Merchant Guild and have an expert look over the agreement to see if there are any unfavorable conditions for us.”

If this were the past, she would definitely have added this:

*“Did you look into it properly? You didn’t, did you? It’s always like this with you.”*

It would have been the opening act that signaled the start of a fight and raised both their voices. But on the paper she had written today, those words were not there.

It seemed her mentioning that she would entrust it to an expert was a bit unexpected. Her mother asked with a surprised expression.

“Which expert?”

“I’ll tell you once the results come out.”

She said what she had to say more calmly than ever before.

“I know the main family’s situation is difficult right now. I think we need to be all the more careful at times like this.”

The emotion contained in her mother’s puzzled eyes was this: *Since when were you careful? Since when did you care about the family’s difficulties?*

If this were the past, she would have snapped back fiercely. But instead, she spoke even more softly.

“I’m all grown up now, aren’t I? From now on, I’ll do my best to help you, Mother.”

She bid her mother farewell and went outside. Somewhat taken aback by her daughter’s somehow different demeanor, her mother was unable to say anything.

Juhyangwol, now outside, let out a sigh.

She took out the paper from her bosom. She hadn’t even said half of half of what was written, but the conversation was clearly different from usual. Whether she wrote it down or not, there was definitely a difference.

*Ah! I didn’t say this.*

Before leaving, she had even underlined the words telling her mother not to worry too much about family affairs and to take good care of her meals, but she had simply walked out.

Anyway, today they hadn’t shouted at each other.

* * *

Hwangin had been trained since childhood not to show his emotions. He had learned that this was the most important thing for living as a merchant.

But at least for this moment, hiding his emotions was not easy.

It was when he stood before Quan Luo.

He was the devil with whom Hwangin had joined hands.

“He is too dangerous a man. You must not join hands with them.”

When his father tried to dissuade him, he had said this:

“I do not wish to spend the rest of my life as the chief of a backwater merchant guild.”

Because he had not wanted to live such a life. Because he had held the ambition to make the Hwangdo Merchant Guild the greatest merchant guild of the Central Plains.

And so the one with whom he had joined hands was this Quan Luo. Once they joined hands, he knew for certain—that he was the same kind of person as Quan Luo.

That while Quan Luo wielded a sword, he merely wielded an abacus. That when Quan Luo glared coldly, he merely smiled—the only difference between them was this. Ultimately, they were the same kind of person.

Perhaps that was why. After joining hands with Quan Luo, the Hwangdo Merchant Guild had grown rapidly.

And there was one other fact he had learned. He had learned that this Quan Luo was nothing more than a subordinate taking someone else’s orders. He had naturally realized this while talking with him.

He sometimes worried about just what whirlwind he had been swept up in, but it was already too late to turn back. The only choice left was whether to make the Hwangdo Merchant Guild the greatest under heaven, or to die by Quan Luo’s hand.

“The agreement fell through?”

Quan Luo spoke with the same polite, low tone as always.

But whenever Hwangin heard his voice, he always got chills.

Because he knew. He knew that Quan Luo was a butcher without blood or tears. Because he felt that even if they stood on the same side for the next thirty years, the moment it became necessary to kill him, Quan Luo would not hesitate for an instant.

“That is correct.”

“Did you not say you were confident about this matter?”

“The Sword Family brought in outside help.”

“Whom?”

“It is the Xidao Sect of Gansu. They say the sect leader of the Xidao Sect owes a debt to the late head of the Sword Family.”

Just as Geommugeuk had expected, he had not moved directly but had instead used an information broker to find out their identities. He had found out faster than expected. The broker had already possessed information on them.

When he realized that his opponent was the Xidao Sect, Hwangin felt relieved.

The people he had met at the inn had given him a strange, indescribable feeling. An inexplicable, strange leisure had weighed on his mind the whole time, but now he could guess the true nature of that leisure.

*Is it because they’re a clan that produced a winner of the Lesser Dragon Tournament?*

That incomprehensible leisure had come from precisely that. And because of that, he felt relieved instead.

*If that is the only reason?*

They were opponents he could handle with confidence.

Quan Luo spoke in a dry tone.

“Understood. I shall handle the rest.”

Hwangin knew. He knew what sort of method Quan Luo meant by “handling” it. Quan Luo would kill the three people from the Xidao Sect and retrieve the agreement. He had always liked his work handled cleanly.

But Hwangin wanted to handle this matter himself.

“Though the Xidao Sect is merely a regional sect, by producing a winner of the Lesser Dragon Tournament, they have also formed ties with the Martial Alliance. If we mishandle them, the Martial Alliance may intervene. This matter must be handled with care.”

This was precisely why Geommugeuk had chosen the Xidao Sect for his disguise—so that the opponent would not try to resolve things through force, but choose another method. Geommugeuk had intended to find out who was behind them through this.

“Please lend me the Qian Gui.”

Money ghosts. That was what Quan Luo called the experts he kept with him.

They were truly like ghosts when it came to money. Whenever they had moved until now, every matter had been resolved.

Those who had stubbornly refused changed their minds; those who had pointed swords met with accidents; those who had cursed vanished without a trace.

In short, when they intervened, the very next day a signed contract would land in one’s hands.

“I believe this matter calls for a merchant rather than a martial artist. If I cannot resolve things through persuasion, then it will not be too late to handle matters as originally planned.”

Soon, Quan Luo opened his mouth.

“How many do you need?”

“Enough to suppress the Xidao Sect’s leader.”

Hwangin spoke with eyes that asked to be trusted.

“Do not worry. Would a merchant ever make a losing deal?”

* * *

That evening, Hwangin came to find Geommugeuk.

Geommugeuk’s group was staying in a small manor slightly removed from the main street.

From Hwangin’s perspective, it was rather fortunate that they were not staying at a conspicuous inn.

As he resolved this matter, Hwangin wanted to see Geommugeuk pleading.

Pay for the inn costs and leave? A merchant should keep proper accounts? He had been humiliated when he called the innkeeper to settle the bill.

How dare he lecture a merchant about accounting before his very eyes!

He didn’t intend to kill the man, but he planned to humiliate him thoroughly before sending him off.

When leaving the inn, he had told him: he was a man who always repaid his debts.

When he entered the courtyard guided by Hwi, Geommugeuk was making something where the trees were planted.

“Welcome!”

Geommugeuk greeted him gladly. As if he had forgotten what happened at the inn earlier, anyone watching would have thought they were close friends.

“You’ve come at just the right time. Please hold this rope for me.”

In a daze, Hwangin took the rope Geommugeuk held out. Geommugeuk tied the other end to a tree opposite.

In the middle, fixed between both sides, was a net large enough for one adult to lie upon.

“It’s a net bed. Originally, it’s a bed you make temporarily in regions with many venomous insects to sleep in.”

Another one had already been made a short distance away.

“That one is for my father. Tonight, I plan to lie here side by side with my father and look up at the night sky.”

Just thinking about it seemed to put Geommugeuk in a good mood, and he smiled happily.

“You seem to truly like your father, young hero.”

“Of course. Though my father does not trust me, I like him more than anyone else in the world.”

Geommugeuk spoke loudly on purpose so that it would be heard inside the building. Though his father must have been able to hear even whispered conversations here, no answer came from inside.

“Now that it’s finished, let’s give it a try.”

Geommugeuk lay in the net. When he swayed his body, the net swayed gently between the trees.

“This is so comfortable and nice.”

Hwangin quietly looked down at Geommugeuk like that.

He had felt it at the inn as well, but this was truly not a common sort of person. Perhaps that was why he asked such a question in this situation.

“What is your father like?”

Caught off guard by the sudden question about his father, Hwangin was inwardly flustered.

“What do you mean?”

“I mean, does your father not startle his son? I was quite surprised then. I didn’t know your father would step forward for that woman.”

Hwangin did not know what to say about his father.

*My father…….*

His father had joined hands with Quan Luo according to his son’s wishes. Had it been a choice for his child? Or had his father harbored the same ambition as him? Hwangin could not know his father’s true thoughts exactly.

“I suppose a father is the easiest person in the world to know, and also the most difficult.”

At Geommugeuk’s words, Hwangin said nothing.

Then Geommugeuk suddenly raised himself from the net.

“You lie down too.”

“I am fine.”

“I said lie down.”

He forced Hwangin to lie down there.

“Look up at the sky from there. How is it?”

It was his first time lying in such a bed, and it was more comfortable than he had thought.

But then, suddenly thinking that now was not the time for this, he raised himself up.

As he did, he staggered and nearly toppled to the ground, but Geommugeuk caught him.

“Thank you.”

“It’s because you’re not used to it.”

What he wasn’t used to was Geommugeuk’s attitude. He was treating him far more familiarly than he had at the inn earlier.

“Don’t think this means I’ll let this matter slide.”

On the wall Hwangin looked at, three men were already standing with arms crossed, looking this way.

They were the Qian Gui he had brought with him—ones Quan Luo particularly cherished. They were experts who could easily handle a sect leader of a regional sect like the Xidao Sect.

“You said you were a mere merchant, but you are no mere merchant.”

“There are many who look down on merchants and try to resolve things through force, so I cannot help but seek assistance at times.”

“If that is the reason, you could have come alone. As you can see, I am a man who prefers conversation with his mouth to conversation with a sword.”

Hwangin stared intently at Geommugeuk.

*Just what are you counting on?*

Though he had brought experts capable of killing even his father, Geommugeuk showed no sign of being cowed or dispirited. Did he even understand what situation he was in?

“At my command, they will seize the agreement by force if necessary.”

Then, unexpected words spilled from Geommugeuk’s mouth.

“Will those three be enough? If you want to make my father draw his sword, you’d need about three hundred people like them.”

At that, the War Ghosts burst into laughter all at once.

They were experts, but not experts enough to see through Geom Mugeuk’s true depths. More than anything, the problem was that they already knew who their opponent was.

The heir to the Seodo Sect.

Since they already thought of him that way, he was nothing but laughable. On top of that, before coming here, Hwang In had told them that the man had quite the tongue on him.

“If you hand over the agreement quietly, offer a proper apology, and leave, nothing will happen.”

“My father will never hand it over. He’s already made a promise to that woman.”

“If you want to save your father, go persuade him yourself.”

Geom Mugeuk sighed and said,

“That is impossible. My father is the sort of man who would require at least five years to persuade.”

Five years? What on earth was he talking about? Hwang In gave him a puzzled look, but Geom Mugeuk remained perfectly at ease.

The three men who had been watching from atop the wall until then lightly leaped down.

“Once they begin to move, I won’t be able to stop them. This is your last chance. Go persuade your father and bring back the agreement.”

One of the three War Ghosts stepped forward.

“There’s no need for all three of us to go. I’ll go and bring it back. I’ll bring your father too and toss him into that hammock.”

Even as he strode toward the building, Geom Mugeuk lay calmly in the hammock.

“Aren’t you worried about your father?”

“Even if I worried about everyone else in the world, he is the one person I need not worry about.”

Hwang In guessed the source of that composure like this.

‘Does he perhaps believe the Murim Alliance will protect him?’

Perhaps that was indeed the case. The confidence that no one would dare act recklessly against the family that had produced the winner of the Little Dragon Tournament. Yes, that had to be the only thing the Seodo Sect could trust in.

‘That half-baked pride of yours is what ruined you!’

How much time had passed like that?

The War Ghost who had gone inside showed no sign of coming back out. Yet no sounds of quarrelling or fighting could be heard from within either. Only silence flowed from the building.

The second War Ghost, tired of waiting, stepped forward.

“I’ll go see.”

He walked into the building. In skill, he was no less than the first War Ghost, and on top of that, he possessed a calmer temperament.

But there was no word from him either.

The expression of the remaining War Ghost hardened. He realized that something had gone wrong. They should have gone in together from the start.

“It could be a trap!”

At Hwang In’s attempt to stop him, the third War Ghost said coldly,

“Then do you think this place is safe?”

The third War Ghost drew his sword and carefully entered the building.

Three men as ferocious as could be had gone in, yet the fact that not even the sound of a chair toppling over could be heard was all the more frightening and astonishing.

There was no word from the third War Ghost either.

Hwang In, who had been staring at the building in fear, turned his gaze toward Geom Mugeuk.

At last, Geom Mugeuk rose from the hammock. In the distance, the sun was beginning to set. A red glow spread through the world like ink bleeding into paper, and the sky seemed wider than ever.

With mysterious eyes that held the sunset within them, Geom Mugeuk asked calmly,

“If you are truly a merchant, shouldn’t you be making a deal instead of waving blades and issuing threats?”

Absolute Regression Chapter 455

Chapter 455: My Father Is Rich Too.
-----------------------------------
Geom Mugeuk’s words flew like a dagger and lodged in his heart.

“If you are a merchant, should you not make a deal?”

Hwang In had often displayed the pride of being a merchant.

But he knew. This pride was nothing more than a sandcastle that would collapse in a single crashing wave of truth. Because at the decisive moment, he had handled matters not by dealing as a merchant, but by mobilizing the Jeon-gwi.

“Originally, you should have come alone and made me an offer. ‘I want that agreement back. What do you want?’ Isn’t that how a true merchant speaks? Discerning what I want and offering something suitable. A person who accomplishes what a sword cannot—isn’t that a merchant?”

Without realizing it, Hwang In bit his lip tightly. The reason he had flared up earlier at the inn when Geom Mugeuk said that a merchant should keep his calculations straight was likely this very inferiority complex.

Silent for a moment, Hwang In turned his gaze toward the building the Jeon-gwi had entered.

“What happened to the people who went inside?”

“Well, I don’t know either.”

“Did you use poison or something?”

Geom Mugeuk smiled.

“My father is someone who despises poison. And because of that, there is someone who feels slighted as well.”

What on earth did that mean? Hwang In could not understand.

Geom Mugeuk rose from his seat.

“Now then, if you’re curious, let us go inside.”

Geom Mugeuk took the lead and walked in.

Hwang In wanted to run straight out through the main gate, but he could not.

Even if he could flee, and even if they let him go, what could he say to Jeon Nak upon returning like this?

That he had fled without confirming whether the three Jeon-gwi he cherished were alive or dead? Since he himself knew no martial arts, that might be plausible. But Jeon Nak would not believe it. He would think there was no way they had simply let him go.

With a fearful heart, Hwang In followed Geom Mugeuk inside.

Walking along the neatly decorated hallway, there was no trace of a fight, let alone the corpses of the Jeon-gwi. Not a drop of blood had splattered on the flowers in the vase upon the display cabinet.

They had definitely said those were experts capable of killing the Seodopa Sect Leader.

‘Where on earth did they disappear to?’

He thought that perhaps the three of them might be sitting in the inner room at the end of the corridor, drinking tea. Please, let it be so! Or perhaps, after killing the Seodopa Sect Leader, they were exchanging idle jokes while looking down at the corpse.

But what if it were the opposite?

While all manner of thoughts raced through his mind, the two arrived at the room at the end of the corridor.

When they entered the room, Geom Ujin was sitting at a table drinking tea.

The Jeon-gwi were not in the room.

“Father, the merchant from the inn that day has come again.”

Geom Ujin, who had been looking out the window, turned his gaze to Hwang In.

“I greet the Sect Leader.”

Hwang In greeted him in a trembling voice.

Geom Ujin looked at him with a blunt expression. It was the same gaze as when he had seen him at the inn, but the heart of the one receiving it was different.

He should have continued with further courtesies, but he could not contain his curiosity.

“The people who came with me entered this building first. By any chance… did you see them?”

Honestly, he wanted to ask outright. Whether Geom Ujin had killed them.

Geom Ujin shook his head.

Geom Mugeuk already knew. That the one who had dealt with them was not his father, but Hwi.

Unless an order had been given to let them in, Hwi would never have stood by while someone targeting his father entered the house. To Hwi, it would truly have been ‘How dare they!’

She had probably remained concealed and silently dispatched the Jeon-gwi as they entered one by one. Without a drop of blood, without a single scream—this was the skill of the guardian tasked with protecting the Cheonma of the current era.

“Then where did those people go?”

Speaking cautiously, Hwang In looked at Geom Mugeuk. Naturally, should he not be curious as well? Yet he showed such a composed demeanor?

At that moment, a thought suddenly flashed through Hwang In’s mind.

‘Could it be? These people?’

Like the protagonists of secret tales quietly passed down in the Murim.

‘A hidden secret sect that has lived concealing its strength?’

And another thought that followed.

‘Then one of the young successors, unable to endure the frustration, enters the Murim and participates in the Soryeongjeon and wins? Eventually, because of that, even the family head appears in the world.’

He did not think this naturally constructed story absurd. Without such a background, how could a mere martial artist from a regional sect win the Soryeongjeon, and make three Jeon-gwi disappear in an instant without a sound?

It was a plausible story. The Murim was a place where all manner of beings with secrets and mysteries existed.

Thinking so, he even understood the incomprehensible composure he had seen from them at the inn.

At the same time, fear welled up within him.

‘They might kill me too, to protect their secret.’

The secrets of a mysterious force had to be hidden.

‘If not that.’

Hwang In looked at Geom Mugeuk. Seeing those clear, deep eyes so distant from killing intent, he conceived hope.

‘This could be an opportunity.’

An opportunity granted by heaven to make the Hwangdo Merchant Guild the world’s greatest merchant guild.

Geom Ujin broke his silence and asked.

“What business brings you to me?”

Should he answer honestly? That he had come to retrieve the agreement? No, to steal it back?

While worrying, Hwang In suddenly recalled what Geom Mugeuk had said.

If you are truly a merchant, should you not make a deal?

Hwang In took a deep breath and spoke to Geom Ujin.

“I have come to make a deal with the Sect Leader.”

Geom Ujin listened in silence.

“Please make my merchant guild the world’s greatest merchant guild.”

Anyone hearing this would call it madness. One might ask if he could betray Jeon Nak so quickly just because three Jeon-gwi had disappeared.

But this was not betrayal.

It was a weighing of survival. At least for this moment, he had to become a true merchant. He had to calculate accurately.

“Not only the money earned in the process, but half of all money earned thereafter—I will offer it for life.”

It was Hwang In’s instinctive gamble thrown at an opponent who might kill him or spare him.

The Jeon Nak he knew was a butcher without blood or tears.

Would such a man let them be after losing the three cherished Jeon-gwi? No. If a fight broke out, he had to side with the victor.

‘Let us wager my fate on these people.’

Because for now, they were right before his eyes.

Soon, Geom Ujin answered briefly.

“Consult my son.”

Hwang In became certain from Geom Ujin’s reaction that his guess was correct.

Would an ordinary sect have reacted this way?

—Make you the world’s greatest merchant guild? What kind of nonsense is that?

But he was told to consult his son. As if discussing it might make such a thing possible.

As if he had nothing more to say, Geom Ujin drank his tea and turned his gaze back out the window.

“Now then, let us take our leave.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s words, Hwang In respectfully bowed his head to Geom Ujin.

Hwang In followed Geom Mugeuk out of the room. The moment he stepped out, a sigh escaped him unconsciously.

When they had exited the building, Hwang In asked Geom Mugeuk.

“Did I… make any mistake toward the Sect Leader?”

“You made no mistake. However, I cannot say the terms were excellent.”

“What do you mean?”

“What you offered as the price of the deal was money.”

Geom Mugeuk added with a faint smile.

“My father also has a great deal of money.”

Hwang In spoke as if protesting.

“The amount I guaranteed is not merely great, but an enormous sum.”

“My father is already enormously wealthy.”

What on earth was he saying? Where the world’s greatest merchant guild was offering half its earnings, how could one compare it to personal wealth?

Every now and then, this man said things he could not understand.

“Anyway, now you are acting a bit more like a merchant.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s gentle expression, Hwang In felt inwardly relieved. At least because it seemed he would not be killed.

Then naturally, his worries shifted to Jeon Nak.

“If that man learns that the three who came with me are dead, he will not sit still. He will bring all his subordinates to kill you.”

“Do his subordinates number about three hundred?”

It was a joke Geom Mugeuk had made earlier. The words that his father would only draw his sword if three hundred came—they no longer sounded like mere jokes.

“In total about thirty or so, but among them, I know there are experts greater than those I brought earlier.”

Geom Mugeuk gazed at Hwang In for a moment. When the smile vanished from Geom Mugeuk’s face, Hwang In anticipated that the important words determining his life would now follow.

“Go back and bring only that man here.”

Hwang In was startled. What could this mean? Did it not mean to lure Jeon Nak here to kill him?

“He might kill me out of suspicion.”

“You might betray us.”

Hwang In could tell. That this was a test of him. That if he passed this test, they would join hands. The scale in his heart wavered up and down. Now if he chose wrongly, he would die.

“If I bring him? Can you handle him? He is a far greater expert than those who came earlier.”

“Whether we can handle him is also for you to judge. However, you should know this. Your dream is so grand that you have drawn in a very dangerous person.”

He would think the dangerous person was the one he had joined hands with, but the person Geom Mugeuk spoke of was his father.

“So judge well. In this deal, there will be no backing down.”

* * *

To state the result first: Hwang In chose the secret sect.

The reason was simple. The fact that his opponent had left the judgment to him was a display of confidence that they could handle it even if he betrayed them and brought everyone along.

Of course, it was a temporary choice.

He intended to straddle both sides and watch how things unfolded. For do scales not tip up and down?

For now, he would bring Jeon Nak to them. If the Seodopa killed Jeon Nak, he would side with them as originally planned; if Jeon Nak defeated them, he would pretend nothing had happened and stick to him.

What bothered him was that Jeon Nak had backing, but that was a worry for later. What he had to worry about now was how to bring him without arousing suspicion.

On the way, he had thought of many things. Should he say the Jeon-gwi were calling him? Then why send you instead of one of the Jeon-gwi? Would not all three need to be there? No. Such reasons would not work.

After agonizing over and over, he thought of one method.

“I wish to absorb the Seodopa into our side.”

At Hwang In’s words, Jeon Nak cast a puzzled gaze. He had sent three Jeon-gwi along, yet Hwang In had returned alone and was saying this.

Without revealing such inner thoughts, Jeon Nak asked calmly.

“What is the reason you wish to draw them in so suddenly?”

“As we talked, their successor caught my fancy.”

“For what reason?”

“He reminded me of the Sangdo, the way of the merchant, that I had forgotten lately.”

Jeon Nak’s expression slightly furrowed. His very existence was the exact opposite of the way of the merchant. Now that he was bringing up Sangdo, it was grating.

“They are clearly a sect useful in many ways. Please trust my judgment.”

Only now did Jeon Nak ask about the Jeon-gwi.

“Where are the Jeon-gwi who went with you?”

“I ordered them to be detained. Please simply give your permission. I will go and get their signature on the agreement. I believe them holding the agreement with the Jussa Sword Family was fate guiding us to this connection.”

He absolutely did not suggest going together. If he had, the suspicious Jeon Nak would have sensed danger immediately.

“You have not listened to my words well until now. So please leave this matter to me.”

Hwang In was acting with all his might. He had to. Because he could truly die in this very spot.

Jeon Nak, who had been watching Hwang In silently, accepted his request.

“Very well, draw them in as well.”

“Thank you. I will go immediately and get the signature on the agreement.”

Turning around, Hwang In inwardly screamed.

‘Say you’ll go together! You should suspect me!’

Just as he was opening the door to leave.

“Let us go together.”

Turning around, Hwang In deliberately stiffened his expression.

“I can handle it alone. You need not come.”

“Let us go together. I have come to want to see for myself what kind of person changed your mind.”

It was a success born of suspicion, but that suspicion left no gaps. Because he secretly ordered the remaining Jeon-gwi to follow him before departing.

* * *

Late at night, Geom Mugeuk and Geom Ujin lay side by side in a hammock, looking up at the night sky.

“Is this your first time lying on a hammock?”

“Yes.”

“Another first experience with Father!”

Geom Mugeuk was genuinely happy. In the past, he had wished his father would recognize such feelings, but now he did not. Because he believed his father would understand.

It did not matter if he passed by without knowing. He intended to continue conveying his heart going forward.

“I will take this hammock and hang it between the fingers of the large stone statue in front of the Cheonma Hall. If you are bored, lie there and rest.”

If installed between the tilted fingers, the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult would be fully visible below. Of course, it did not seem likely that his father would lie there.

“You are the one who intends to lie there, are you not?”

“When will you teach me the martial art that reads people’s minds?”

While looking up at the night sky for some time like that, Jeon Nak and Hwang In arrived.

Seeing the two lying in the hammock in the courtyard, Jeon Nak was inwardly dumbfounded.

‘Did he get my permission just to draw in people like that?’

Martial artists lying side by side in a hammock at play? He had never seen such martial artists before.

Geom Mugeuk raised himself from the hammock.

“You’ve come again!”

Seeing Geom Mugeuk smiling brightly as he looked this way, Jeon Nak felt a strange sensation. Because he seemed far too pure and cheerful for a martial artist.

Jeon Nak’s gaze remained fixed on Geom Ujin, still lying in the hammock.

Geom Ujin was looking up at the stars in the night sky. Seeing that figure, Jeon Nak found himself unconsciously following that gaze and looking up at the night sky.

He did not know why he looked up. The thought simply arose on its own—he is looking, so I should look too.

Even while gazing upon the incomparably beautiful stars, Jeon Nak felt an inexplicable unease. And so he knew.

‘Something has gone wrong.’

Absolute Regression, Episode 456

Chapter 456: If You Kill Me, Someone Terrifying Will Come
---------------------------------------------------------
There are days like that.

Days when the stars look unusually lonely, and the moonlight feels cold.

Days when an ominous sense that something is about to happen wraps around your entire body, and you feel a powerful discomfort and incongruity, as if you are in a place where you should not be.

That day was right now.
