The source of all this unease was that man lying in the hammock. That man, who made him feel this way merely by looking at the stars in the night sky together.

A man with such a presence was nothing more than the master of some local sect? Impossible.

And there was one more thing.

The three Battle Ghosts he had sent earlier with Hwang In were nowhere to be seen.

‘I’ve walked into the tiger’s den.’

This was not because his instinct for danger was excellent. It was because that man’s presence was drawing out even premonitions that had not existed before.

‘Stay calm.’

Jeon Nak settled his heart and regulated his internal energy. Yes, there had always been crises. And he had survived to this day by somehow overcoming them. He had to trust this instinct of his.

At that moment, Hwang In, who had come with him, said to Geom Mugeuk,

“I have received permission for my merchant guild to invest in your sect. Let us sign the agreement at once.”

If he was sharp-witted, he would understand what he was saying right now.

Hwang In, for his part, was tense as well. After all, he was still within reach if Jeon Nak stretched out his hand.

With his eyes, he said to Geom Mugeuk,

‘I brought him here, so hurry up and kill this man!’

He was still standing in the middle of the scale. Once it tilted decisively, he would throw himself to that side. Until one of the two died, he was on no one’s side.

Geom Mugeuk stepped forward and asked,

“Who is the person who came with you?”

Jeon Nak introduced himself humbly.

“I am someone who serves Guildmaster Hwang.”

Jeon Nak carefully observed Geom Mugeuk. If this ominous feeling about the middle-aged man lying in the hammock was accurate, then this young man before his eyes must absolutely not be underestimated either.

The clear, bright first impression he had felt earlier now came across as the composure of an expert.

Naturally, his resentment turned toward Hwang In.

‘Where the hell has this bastard dragged me?’

Strictly speaking, he had chased after him of his own accord.

Geom Mugeuk said to Hwang In,

“You have such an impressive person under you. Do you really need us?”

When he spoke as if he had recognized Jeon Nak’s ability at a glance, Hwang In forced himself to respond calmly.

“Doesn’t everyone have a different use?”

“You are quite right. Now, let us go inside and draw up the agreement.”

He should have done that from the start. Just as Hwang In, relieved, was about to step toward Geom Mugeuk—

“Please wait a moment.”

Jeon Nak politely stopped Hwang In. It was because he thought he must not hand Hwang In over to the other side in this situation.

“Guildmaster Hwang.”

“What is it?”

“Did I not say that I left my subordinates here? Yet I do not see them.”

At that, Hwang In asked Geom Mugeuk,

“Where are those men?”

This was also a sincere question. What on earth happened to them?

“They’re inside the house, waiting for you to return. I suggested we look at the stars together, but they ignored me and went in.”

Hwang In was inwardly dumbfounded by how calmly he lied. Hwang In recalled the moment the Battle Ghosts had disappeared earlier. That house was a man-eating house. Once you went in, you did not know how to come out.

“I will go inside to write the agreement and tell them you have arrived.”

Hwang In tried to hurry inside, while Jeon Nak tried to leave.

“Please do. I will wait outside the manor, so sign the agreement and come out with me afterward.”

Jeon Nak’s only thought was that he had to get out of this place first. Truly, since becoming a martial artist, this was the first time he had been so frightened when the other party had not made any threat at all. That was why it was even more terrifying.

But Geom Mugeuk did not let him leave so easily.

“Since you’ve already come, why not go in together and have a drink?”

Putting aside all premonitions and instincts for danger, Jeon Nak could sense the crisis he was in from that fact alone.

He was not the sort of person someone would want to invite for a drink. He was the sort people wanted to avoid when they saw him, the sort they would rather not see at all if possible. And yet they were asking him to drink?

“Shall I?”

Jeon Nak turned and walked naturally to Hwang In’s side.

‘If I go in, I die.’

At the same time, Jeon Nak drew his sword.

As if his drawing the sword were a signal, the ten Battle Ghosts who had been waiting outside leaped over the wall all at once and landed within.

Hwang In felt worry before surprise that they had followed.

‘I must not let them misunderstand and think I betrayed them!’

So Hwang In asked, as if he had known nothing at all,

“Why did you bring your subordinates?”

The gaze Jeon Nak turned on Hwang In was cold.

“I arrived here, and yet my subordinates did not come out to greet me? Unless they are dead, such a thing would not happen.”

He was still speaking politely.

That was why Hwang In found this Jeon Nak terrifying. Because he had always handled matters with that polite attitude. Leave it to me. With that one sentence, things had always ended.

Now that frightening courtesy was directed at him.

“Tell me what happened.”

Even as killing intent faintly bloomed in Jeon Nak’s eyes, Hwang In flatly denied it.

“They were here when I left. Is that not so?”

Hwang In’s eyes as he looked at Geom Mugeuk were desperate.

Please, say that they were.

Geom Mugeuk said to Jeon Nak,

“I told you, the men who came with him are inside the house. If you’re that suspicious, go in and check.”

He said it so calmly that it truly seemed to be the case.

Jeon Nak’s gaze turned past Geom Mugeuk toward Geom Ujin. He was lying in the hammock with his eyes closed. Whether he had fallen asleep or was lost in thought, Jeon Nak could not tell, but that sight alone was enough to seize his nerves.

“Check.”

Jeon Nak sent one of the Battle Ghosts into the house. Through sound transmission, he ordered him to look carefully for anyone hiding inside.

But he could not receive a reply to that order. The Battle Ghost who had gone in did not come back out.

“It seems a drinking party has started.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s calm words, Jeon Nak’s eyes sank coldly. It was the moment he confirmed that the enemies were not only the two before him.

‘They all got caught.’

The first three subordinates he had sent had been among the most skilled of the Battle Ghosts.

Jeon Nak asked Geom Mugeuk,

“What is your true identity?”

“I am Geom Yeon of the Seodo Sect. And this is my father.”

“No. You are not the Seodo Sect.”

Jeon Nak’s sword aimed at the neck of Hwang In, who was standing beside him.

“Why are you doing this to me? I am on your side!”

The reason he believed Hwang In had lied was simple.

“There is no way such formidable people would have decided to join hands with you. You lured me to them.”

His respectful attitude changed. It was the first time since they had met.

“Even if I die, I will make certain you die.”

Because Hwang In could feel that it was no simple threat, fear seized him.

Now, the only person he could trust was Geom Mugeuk.

‘Are you going to abandon me?’

At that desperate gaze, Geom Mugeuk said something unexpected.

“A fine merchant buys this item and that item as well. He does not do business by leaving things to luck.”

At those words, Hwang In understood. He was reproaching him for trying to straddle both sides.

“I…”

The chill of the blade against his throat.

The moment he said he was on Geom Mugeuk’s side, Jeon Nak would kill him. This mad butcher had no mercy.

Whether he knew that danger or not, Geom Mugeuk’s gaze beyond the blade demanded an answer.

Are you really the heir of the Sinbi Family? If I choose you, can you save me?

He felt that this was precisely the moment when he had to stake his life and make his final weighing.

Since he was not a martial artist, he could not know which side was stronger. He had to choose by wit and intuition, by instinct alone.

At last, Hwang In made his decision.

“I have already chosen you.”

The scale in Hwang In’s heart tilted toward Geom Mugeuk.

“So save me.”

Terrified that the sword aimed at his neck might cut him down, Hwang In squeezed his eyes shut. But the butcher’s sword was unusually cautious today.

Geom Mugeuk demanded another answer.

“Who are these men trying to kill you?”

You really intend to push me all the way to the edge of the cliff.

He had already come this far, and there was nowhere left to retreat.

“These men deserve to die. Many innocent people have died by their hands.”

“Were those not things you ordered?”

“Because I joined hands with them, I cannot say I bear no responsibility. But I never ordered them to kill people, nor did I ever wish for it.”

“But thanks to that, the Hwangdo Merchant Guild grew, did it not?”

At those words, Hwang In could make no excuse. He was the one who had joined hands with them, after all. Though he had not known they were this cruel.

He felt the sword aimed at his neck grow even colder.

Hwang In could feel it. Jeon Nak had decided to kill him.

‘Why has he not killed me yet?’

He did not know. The reason this brutal Jeon Nak had not yet cut off his head was because he intended to use him as a hostage, even if only to gain the slightest advantage.

At that moment, hopeful words came from Geom Mugeuk’s mouth.

“There is one technique that can save you. It is a technique I have been practicing diligently lately. And as it happens, there are ten opponents.”

No, why are you explaining that to the enemy? Use it right now! Hurry!

But soon, hope turned into a bolt from the blue.

“However, its accuracy is poor, so you may die.”

A technique with poor accuracy? Is this really the situation to mention some shoddy technique like that?

How could Hwang In know?

That the shoddy technique was none other than the fifth form of the Nine Flames Demonic Art, the Soul-Severing Demonic Strike.

Listening to the conversation, Jeon Nak grew more and more uneasy. It was because he thought Geom Mugeuk would not have mentioned a technique he lacked confidence in.

“You were aiming for me from the start, weren’t you?”

There was no way experts like these had been after a mere merchant guild. To be precise, they were probably not after him, but the ones above him.

He thought that it was rather fortunate. Perhaps he could use that point to get out of this place.

“If I die, a terrifying person will come. When that time comes, even you will not survive.”

At that, Geom Mugeuk asked,

“Who is that terrifying person?”

“Someone who can kill the likes of you with ease. If you kill me, that person will come.”

At the look asking whether he would still kill him, Geom Mugeuk asked Hwang In,

“Are you the lucky sort?”

Hwang In let out a sigh.

“If I were lucky, would I have ended up in this situation?”

“Then I will have to trust my own luck.”

What on earth was he talking about?

“Close your eyes.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s words, Hwang In closed his eyes.

Blood trickled down from Jeon Nak’s sword where it touched Hwang In’s neck.

“If you move, this man will definitely die!”

No matter what enemy he faced, he was confident he could kill a man whose neck he held at sword point. If they attacked, he would cut Hwang In’s throat and fight.

The next moment—

Ten sword qi fell vertically.

The ten streams of sword qi struck down like lightning and pierced precisely into the crowns of the Battle Ghosts’ heads. The Battle Ghosts each differed in skill, but before the Soul-Severing Demonic Strike, all were equal.

They collapsed at the same time like sheaves of straw.

When the first sound burst out, Hwang In thought he was dying.

‘This is the end!’

His father had told him that if a merchant became entangled with martial artists, it would not end well. He should have stopped him to the very end back then.

At the thick scent of blood rising hotly, Hwang In slowly opened his eyes.

And before him unfolded an astonishing sight.

All of the Battle Ghosts lay dead on the ground. Blood flowed from the crowns of their heads. And Jeon Nak, who had aimed his sword at him, had also collapsed beside him, dead.

‘Jeon Nak is dead? That terrifying Jeon Nak? How on earth?’

It felt as though he were dreaming. Hwang In stood there blankly, his soul half gone.

At that moment, Geom Ujin, who had been lying in the hammock, sat up.

“With those shoddy skills of yours, what were you going to do if it fell on that man’s head?”

“Then Father would have saved him.”

When he realized his son truly meant it, Geom Ujin asked with an expression that said it was absurd.

“From where I was lying in that hammock?”

“Yes. Because you are my father.”

That sneer formed at the corners of Geom Ujin’s mouth. But today’s sneer held a pleasant emotion. His son’s trust surpassed even the Nine Flames Demonic Art.

“Only ten strands now. You still have a long way to go.”

Leaving those words behind, Geom Ujin went into the building.

“Sleep well, Father.”

After loudly bidding farewell to his father, Geom Mugeuk walked over to Hwang In, who was still standing in a daze.

“Are you all right?”

Only then did Hwang In come to his senses and bowed deeply on the spot.

“Thank you for saving my life.”

His weighing had been correct. Now, whether he lived or died, he had to trust and follow these people. There were many things he wanted to know and many things he wanted to ask, but what filled Hwang In’s heart right now was this worry.

“But will it be all right?”

“What do you mean?”

“Did he not say that if you killed him, a terrifying person would come?”

“That is why I killed him.”

Seeing Hwang In’s eyes widen because he did not understand, Geom Mugeuk spoke as if that person were someone he wanted to see.

“He said he would come only if I killed him.”

Absolute Regression, Chapter 457

Chapter 457: Isn't It Great to Be in the Central Plains?
--------------------------------------------------------
“Have you calmed down a little now?”

At Geom Mugeuk’s words, Hwang In nodded and answered.

“Yes, thanks to you.”

Contrary to his words, Hwang In’s heart was still pounding wildly.

In the urgent moment itself, he had not trembled this much. Everything had passed in a blur.

Rather, now that it was all over and he realized he truly had nearly died, his heart was beating like mad.

So, unable to return in such a state, he was drinking water in Geom Mugeuk’s room.

He gulped the water down, then choked on it. Only after coughing several times did he finally settle down. What had happened earlier truly felt like a dream. Even the fact that he had nearly died did not feel real.

“That martial art you displayed at the end was… No, never mind.”

Because he had closed his eyes, he had not seen it himself, but feeling that it was not something he should ask about carelessly, he shut his mouth.

When he recalled the sight of the Battle Ghosts lying dead earlier, his breathing quickened again.

He did not know much about martial arts, but there were still things he could understand. Those men were not people who would die so easily.

The martial art this man before him had displayed must have been the peerless divine art of the Shinbi Clan.

“What should I do now?”

With Jeon Nak dead, a new worry came upon Hwang In.

“If they learn that Jeon Nak is dead, the terrifying person he mentioned will come looking for me first.”

Jeon Nak had been terrifying enough. How frightening, then, would the person he had used to frighten him be? He might well end up wetting his pants.

Should he simply flee far away?

He considered it briefly, but he could not do that. If he abandoned the Hwangdo Merchant Guild and ran, what would he live on?

“Please stay with me. You must not leave my side even for a moment.”

“I cannot do that. Right now, every single moment is precious to me. I have no time to spend with you.”

“Then what am I supposed to do when that person comes? News of Jeon Nak’s death will have to be delivered first, so he may not barge in immediately, but… no. If my luck is bad, he could drop by out of nowhere while passing through, couldn’t he?”

At that moment, Geom Mugeuk suddenly asked.

“Is only the person coming frightening? Am I not frightening?”

Hwang In flinched.

Now that he thought about it, this was the man who had killed Jeon Nak and the Battle Ghosts in a single move.

If he was going to fear anyone, he should fear this man.

For some reason, Geom Mugeuk felt comfortable to him, and he had momentarily forgotten just how terrifying he was. This was not someone he should be whining to or clinging to like this.

“I apologize.”

Hwang In lowered his head. To the dispirited man, Geom Mugeuk said,

“If someone comes looking for you, bring him to me.”

Hwang In’s expression brightened. He had been terrified that Geom Mugeuk would say, Since you were involved too, handle it yourself.

Now, this man was the only person he could trust.

“Ah, what should be done about the remaining Battle Ghosts?”

“We will take care of them, so do not worry.”

Those words inspired confidence.

Through the window, the courtyard could be seen. The corpses that had been scattered there earlier had already all been cleared away. Not even a single drop of blood remained on the ground.

Just as the Battle Ghosts who had entered the building earlier had vanished, the corpses too had vanished without a trace. In that short time, Hwi had cleaned everything up perfectly. People who handled matters like this could be trusted.

Once his heart steadied, Hwang In spoke cautiously.

“I hope the deal I mentioned before can continue.”

Hwang In desperately did not want to miss this opportunity. Now that he had personally confirmed just how powerful Geom Mugeuk’s martial arts were, he wanted to let it go even less.

“You said your dream was to become the master of the greatest merchant guild under heaven?”

“That is correct.”

“Are you sincere?”

“If I had not been sincere, I would never have joined hands with someone like Jeon Nak.”

Geom Mugeuk stared directly into his eyes and asked,

“Was it not because you were not sincere that you joined hands with him?”

As Hwang In was struck right where it hurt, Geom Mugeuk continued.

“I do not know much about the world of merchants, but I do know this. To become the master of the greatest merchant guild under heaven, you must become someone worthy of that position.”

Hwang In silently listened to Geom Mugeuk.

“I do not think your dream should contain Jeon Nak, or me, or my father, or even that terrifying person who is said to be coming.”

Hwang In could not understand what he meant.

“Then what should that dream contain?”

“A merchant. A merchant with the ability to lead the greatest merchant guild under heaven. A merchant with trust and honor, negotiation skills and crisis management, intelligence networks and leadership, insight into the world, and even an eye for people. Is there room for anything else in such a place? Is there room for this stench of blood and this cold sword to intrude?”

Hwang In felt so ashamed that his face grew hot. When he asked himself which of the things Geom Mugeuk had mentioned he possessed, he could not answer confidently for even one of them.

After being lost in thought for a long while, Hwang In revealed his decision.

“I would like to join hands with the Ju Sword Family with the mindset of starting anew. May I write a new agreement and send it to them?”

Geom Mugeuk could at least tell that he was not a hopeless man.

That was why he nodded. In truth, this permission was not for Hwang In’s sake. It was for Ju Hyangwol of the Ju Sword Family.

She must still be worrying even now about what to do with this matter. The Ju Sword Family was still facing difficulties, and through this opportunity, it had to be reborn anew.

That was why this one sentence was for Ju Hyangwol.

“I will be watching.”

How could Hwang In not understand that it meant he should not try anything foolish? He lowered his head and answered politely.

“Thank you for allowing it. Then I will take my leave now.”

As Hwang In walked out across the courtyard where corpses had lain only moments ago, Geom Mugeuk gave him one final piece of advice.

“Do not be so captivated by your dream that you forget reality. Your world is not here, but there. I am someone who will vanish like smoke, but the Ju Sword Family will remain.”

* * *

The next morning, I headed to Father’s quarters.

“Father, may I come in?”

“Come in.”

When I entered, Father was checking several letters.

They were letters sent by Sama Myeong from headquarters. Matters that absolutely required Father’s approval had arrived as urgent correspondence.

“You work even after coming out here?”

“You try sitting in my seat someday.”

I waved my hands in exaggerated refusal.

“I do not want to. Hand it over to me later. Much, much later.”

“You want me to keep suffering like this?”

After a brief pause, I asked Father,

“If I inherit it, will you go traveling? If you promise me you will, then I will consider it too.”

Father gave no answer.

Since I had no intention of prolonging this awkward silence, I smiled and changed the subject.

“Very well. Let me practice in advance. Is there anything I can help you with?”

Then Father handed me one of the letters. It contained the recent movements of a certain family.

At last, Father revealed our objective.

“The reason we are going to Shaanxi is because of the Jinlong Clan.”

A far greater name than I had expected came out.

The Jinlong Clan.

It was a clan ranked among the top five in all of the Central Plains when it came to wealth. It was truly a symbol of immense riches.

They were not merely a sect with a lot of money. The head of the Jinlong Clan, Jin Qianfang, was not only well versed in the affairs of the merchant world, but also a supreme master of the clan’s secret martial art, the Jinlong Divine Art, and was renowned in the murim. As a result, the Jinlong Clan had countless experts who followed him.

“At some point, the Jinlong Clan secretly began absorbing and operating merchant guilds.”

It was possible for the enormously wealthy to secretly acquire merchant guilds. The problem came afterward.

“The Hwangdo Merchant Guild was one of those guilds.”

Father nodded.

“They used martial artists to expand those guilds by force and made a great deal of money. But that money did not flow into the Jinlong Clan.”

“That immense sum was being siphoned off somewhere.”

Having sensed the Jinlong Clan’s strange movements, Sama Myeong began investigating them in earnest and found clues that connected them to the force behind the scenes.

Well, considering what that hidden force had shown so far, it would have taken something on the scale of the Jinlong Clan to handle it.

“The one coming down this time must be the person secretly in charge of the Jinlong Clan’s external affairs.”

Because this had been carried out in secret, the dead Battle Ghosts were likely not martial artists directly under the Jinlong Clan, but men hired from outside.

Aside from Jeon Nak, they probably had not even known they belonged to the Jinlong Clan.

“Since it involves supporting them with a large amount of money, the clan head would naturally have to permit it. In that case, the force behind this either occupies a position from which they can persuade the clan head…”

After a brief pause, I added,

“Or they have already seized power within the Jinlong Clan.”

* * *

Im Sohwa, the head of the Ju Sword Family, entered her daughter’s quarters.

Her daughter, Ju Hyangwol, was sitting at her desk looking at something. Surprisingly, it was a book. When was the last time she had seen her daughter reading a book?

“What are you doing?”

“Ah, you’re here?”

Ever since the day she had met the heir of the Hwangdo Merchant Guild, her daughter had been acting differently than usual.

“I was reading a book that explains contracts in detail.”

“Why that?”

“The Hwangdo Merchant Guild sent over a new agreement in advance.”

“Let me see.”

Im Sohwa read the agreement.

After reading a few lines, she quickly lost focus. Martial arts manuals were interesting enough to read all night, but writing like this truly did not enter her eyes.

“So I was looking up the words I didn’t understand.”

She had never expected the Hwangdo Merchant Guild to send a new agreement.

She wondered what kind of trick this was.

“This time, they sent an agreement without any toxic clauses.”

“You know that just by looking at it?”

In the past, she would surely have flared up at those words. She would have taken them as an insult.

But now, in her bosom, she had a piece of paper with the words “Let’s not get angry” written at the very top.

Perhaps because she had thought, I must not get angry?

From her mother’s perspective, she felt it was something that could be said. Yes, that could happen.

That was the next line written beneath “Let’s not get angry.”

Yes, that could happen.

“Don’t worry. I’m not going to sign it as is. I plan to verify it again.”

“With whom?”

“With someone who knows this sort of thing well. I’ll tell you when I get back.”

Im Sohwa was angry. They should simply reach an agreement; what was she investigating when she did not even know much?

And yet, she had no chance to get angry. Somehow, her daughter’s attitude toward her was different from usual.

Her daughter, who used to snap and talk back sharply, was speaking so gently and calmly that she could hardly explode all of a sudden.

“I’ll be back! Oh, make sure you eat properly!”

Im Sohwa was most surprised by her daughter’s final words. When had she ever been told to make sure she ate? No, had she ever heard such words at all? Now she found herself worried about her daughter.

Ju Hyangwol’s expression was bright as she left the Sword Family.

This time, too, she had ended the conversation with her mother without raising their voices, and she felt proud.

Since she had spoken kindly with the mindset that she must endure, there had been no reason to fight with her mother.

Of course, she did continue to feel the sense of crisis that, at some point, everything might explode properly.

* * *

“I think you may sign the agreement.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s words, Ju Hyangwol was delighted. She had asked Geom Mugeuk to examine the agreement Hwang In had sent. And the result matched what she had found herself.

“The fact that they changed their minds is all thanks to you, Young Hero.”

“No. It is because Lady Ju asked me for help.”

If she had not asked Geom Mugeuk for help at the inn that day, she would have entered a different fate.

“My relationship with Mother has improved too. That’s why I’m actually uneasy. I’m worried that everything that’s been building up will suddenly explode in a big way.”

Geom Mugeuk asked back with an expression that said it was no great matter.

“So what if it explodes?”

Geom Mugeuk’s solution was simple.

“Something that used to explode ten times has been reduced to one.”

“What if it builds up and then explodes?”

At her worry that the explosion might become ten times greater, Geom Mugeuk cut the fuse clean off.

“If you endure, and endure again, and then explode, it might indeed explode greatly. Did you endure things with your mother today before coming here?”

She recalled her conversation with her mother, then soon shook her head.

She had not gritted her teeth and suppressed herself. It had been closer to trying to see her mother from a different perspective. With the thought, That could happen.

“Then it will be fine. What you are worried about is due to a misconception.”

“What do you mean?”

“It is a misconception we often have. The misconception that if a relationship is good and then explodes, it might explode in a big way.”

“Is that not true?”

“Rather, if you keep having small fights, one day it is bound to explode greatly. Those small fights may seem insignificant, but they wound people’s hearts and serve to heighten the atmosphere. Conversely, if you continue to maintain a good relationship, even something that might explode greatly will instead explode only a little. Do not doubt the power of those moments when you exchanged kind words, one sentence at a time.”

Those last words in particular touched her. She still felt good that she had ended the conversation with her mother without raising her voice.

Ju Hyangwol stared at him. It was as if her gaze were saying this.

How can you speak so well?

It would take a very long time to learn. I spent an entire lifetime becoming who I am now.

“Then I’ll be going. Thank you very much for today.”

At Ju Hyangwol’s farewell, Geom Mugeuk said,

“Since you have come all this way, you should meet the safest vault in the murim before you go.”

Geom Mugeuk sensed that she wanted to see his father. He also sensed that she could not bring herself to say it.

“Father is taking a walk in the back garden. Let us go.”

As she followed Geom Mugeuk toward the back garden, she stopped.

“I’m sorry, but I’ll just go today. Please only convey my regards later.”

“I will.”

Her steps quickened without her realizing it as she turned away. It was not because seeing him kept making her think of her own father. It was because she felt she must not meet him.

Watching her back as she departed into the distance, Geom Mugeuk spoke as if to himself. His gaze was directed toward her, but the words were for the person who would be listening clearly from far behind him.

“Isn’t it wonderful to come out into the Central Plains? You even get to confirm how popular you are. Father, when we get to Shaanxi, let us have a popularity contest.”

I could not see Father’s face at that moment, but somehow, I could imagine it.

The image of Father, with his aloof expression, not refusing this contest.

Absolute Regression, Chapter 458

Chapter 458: They Wouldn't Have Set a Trap
------------------------------------------
After sending off Ju Hyang-wol and heading to the backyard, Father was looking up at the sky with his hands clasped behind his back.

It had been that way ever since I could remember—Father’s back always looked lonely.

That loneliness was an innate loneliness that no amount of tender companionship from my side could erase.

Father, please do not be so lonely. Your son is right here. A son who respects and loves you more than anyone else in the world.

“Miss Ju, who likes you even more than I do, came by. She asked me to send you her regards.”

Father turned toward me. To think that even though I had deliberately teased him, he showed no sign of embarrassment—that shameless gall of his.

“Shall we really stick together?”

As if he had heard everything I’d said in the front yard, Father did not ask what I meant. Instead, he wore an expression that said, “Anytime.”

I imagined traveling the Central Plains at ease with Father. Not a situation where we had to inevitably shed blood as now, but literally a comfortable, leisurely trip.

What if we mingled with female warriors and drank wine? I could not imagine what sort of demeanor Father would show in front of them.

‘Father, I do not wish for anything grand. I simply want to enjoy myself like this with you.’

Then Father suddenly asked.

“Why do you take care of those around you to this extent?”

He was asking about the matter of bringing the Ju Family Sword Manor and the Hwangdo Merchant Group together. Because it was something that never would have been accomplished if not for me.

What should I answer?

That it is worth taking care of before I go? That I am trying to become meticulous because evil is meticulous? Or should I use Father as an excuse? Since Father took charge of the agreement, it was Father who handled it this time.

Today, I answered like this.

“Because it is interesting.”

A faint smile formed at the corners of Father’s lips. Yes, rather than anything else, saying it was interesting was the easiest to understand.

Father slowly began to walk. I walked with him through the rear garden, speaking of what needed to be done hereafter.

“When Jeon Nak’s death is reported, someone will come down to investigate the matter. I intend to use him to approach the Golden Dragon Clan.”

Eliminating everything indiscriminately is not the answer.

Our goal is to find out what relationship the Golden Dragon Clan has with the forces behind them, and to eliminate those who have taken root there.

This time, I intend to find a way to approach them using Jeon Nak’s death.

“We must handle them without killing, so it will be more difficult.”

As if agreeing with my words, Father silently nodded.

“Moreover, since our opponent is the Golden Dragon Clan, we must deal with them carefully. I understand that the head of the Golden Dragon Clan is close to the Martial Alliance Leader. That is why they also provide a large sum of money to the Martial Alliance every year.”

A displeased aura flashed across Father’s expression. Because to him, it must have sounded like this: since they are close to the Martial Alliance Leader, we must be careful. Rather, I would have had to say the opposite to suit Father’s taste.

Since they are close to the Martial Alliance Leader, I believe we should properly show them what’s what this time.

That would be the answer Father desires.

But Father, that is an answer you will never hear from me again.

“Do you have something in mind?”

“It will not be easy to approach the core of the Golden Dragon Clan without revealing our identities.”

Because this matter is one that can only be found out by approaching even the head of the Golden Dragon Clan. It is not easy to do so through a local sect like the Seo Do Sect. And if we pretended to be from a Mystic Faction, we would be even more heavily guarded.

“Therefore, I wish to seek help from one place.”

“Where is that?”

After a brief pause, I spoke of a place Father could never have expected.

“The Eunha Merchant Group (銀河商團).”

As expected, a look of surprise flickered in Father’s eyes.

The Eunha Merchant Group was, in name and reality, the wealthiest plutocrat known to have the most money in all of murim.

If the Eunha Merchant Group was the greatest, below it were the Golden Dragon Clan and the Continent Merchant Group (大陸商團). These three were known to have the most money in the world.

“We will make them know us as people of the Eunha Merchant Group.”

Only with that level of status will we be able to approach the core of the Golden Dragon Clan. Of course, we will have to use a few tricks in the process.

“Will the Eunha Merchant Group help us?”

The Eunha Merchant Group is a place that does not lean toward either the righteous or the demonic. However, while maintaining deep relations with all three, they succeeded in keeping that balance and became the number one merchant group in the Central Plains.

“That is beyond my ability, so please make it happen, Father.”

At my words, Father made a dumbfounded expression.

But this request is not an unreasonable one.

From my life before the regression, I knew. I knew of the special connection between the head of the Eunha Merchant Group and Father.

The head of the Eunha Merchant Group, Yong Jamyeong (龍滋明), had left words about Father before his death.

In his youth, while being chased by assassins, he was saved by Father at the brink of death. It was when Father was in his prime, traversing the Central Plains with Gwonma.

At the time, Yong Jamyeong did not know that the one who saved him was the Cheonma. Because after saving him, Father simply left.

He said that he only learned of this fact by chance long after Father had passed away.

It is said that he regretted learning of it too late. He had searched his whole life yet could not find him, and then learned why. How could he have found him when that man was the Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult?

Originally, this was not the type of matter to be revealed even after his death, but since Father had already passed away and both the righteous and demonic sides had sealed their doors, it became known.

‘Father, in this life, let us let him know a little earlier that the one who saved him back then was you. Only then can he repay the grace. It was his regret, they say; he searched for his benefactor his whole life.’

Gaining help for this matter, and also putting an end to his fated connection with Father.

Of course, I cannot know whether I will obtain the desired two-birds-with-one-stone result. Everything will be told by fate. Let us do it this way in this life, I thought.

“There is a saying in the world. That the Eunha Merchant Group’s intelligence is as fast and accurate as our main cult’s Tongcheon Pavilion.”

Of course, it may not actually be so, but it means their information-gathering capabilities are tremendous.

“They must have been watching the movements of not only the Martial Alliance and the Sahdo Alliance, but also our main cult. They will also know that the greatest successor in history has been born.”

“You certainly have no trouble saying such things yourself.”

“As if that’s all? They will know that our main cult recently led the meeting between the demonic and righteous factions, so they will not be able to refuse easily.”

If they know that the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult is the most prominent place these days, would they refuse if the Cheonma makes a personal request?

“Furthermore, the relationship between the Eunha Merchant Group and the Golden Dragon Clan is not bad, is it?”

Though they compete fiercely in the business world, surprisingly, the relationship between the two sides is not bad.

“You are the only one who can accomplish this. Even if the Martial Alliance Leader or the Sahdo Alliance Leader requests it, he will not accept.”

“Then why do you think he would accept my request?”

To Father, it may sound like flattery, but this is all I can say.

“Because it is you, Father.”

Father, who had been gazing at me for a moment, asked one last time.

“What should I ask them to do?”

I could tell that Father had decided to follow my plan.

“One person need only stop by for a brief moment.”

* * *

Hwang In and Ju Hyang-wol met again on the day set by Geom Muguk, with the agreement between them.

Amidst the awkwardness, the one who stepped forward first was Hwang In.

“I formally apologize for the previous matter.”

He rose from his seat and bowed his head politely. Ju Hyang-wol felt sincerity in his attitude.

“Why did you change your mind?”

Apologizing sincerely and sending a proper agreement. She was curious about the reason.

Hwang In answered honestly.

“It is because of Sir Geom.”

Of course, he could not speak of the matter of killing Jeon Nak and Jeon Gwi’s men, but aside from that, he spoke honestly.

“Meeting him, I was ashamed of the life I had lived so shoddily. Though I spoke of wanting to become the owner of the world’s greatest merchant group, I was a man who did not even befit a small merchant group in some backwater.”

He wanted to be honest with her, if no one else. Because he considered investing in the Ju Family Sword Manor a new beginning.

“I wanted to try to do things properly from now on. That is why I asked to meet at this inn. Because this place was the beginning of all that.”

The place where the two were was the very inn where she had first met Geom Muguk.

Had it been for any other reason, she would have doubted Hwang In. But this reason, she believed.

‘You too were influenced by that person.’

She was the same. Because through meeting them, her relationship with her mother had changed a little as well. Of course, neither her mother’s nor her own innate personalities would change. She did not expect the relationship to change overnight.

However, as Geom Muguk had said, she intended to believe in the power of that moment when they exchanged kind words and to strive. She would not burst out in anger. She would understand, thinking that such things happen.

“Now, shall we sign?”

The two signed the agreement together. They each took one of the two copies. For both Hwang In and Ju Hyang-wol, it was the first moment they had properly accomplished something.

“I look forward to working with you.”

“I look forward to working with you.”

The two politely clasped their fists.

And so Ju Hyang-wol left the inn first, and Hwang In remained. Because it was a day of new departure, he wanted to have a drink in his overwhelming emotion.

“Truly, destiny changed at this place.”

As he was drinking, immersed in such emotions, a woman sitting a short distance away spoke to him.

“I did not mean to eavesdrop, but it seems something great happened here.”

She was a woman in her thirties, neatly dressed in martial attire. She was a beauty in whom youth and maturity were revealed at once, and a somehow cold impression made her appear even more attractive.

“Ah, I am sorry. I heard it unintentionally. I am simply very curious, you see.”

The woman took a bottle of wine and sat at Hwang In’s table.

“As an apology, let me pour you a drink.”

She poured him a drink.

Hwang In froze, saying nothing.

“Why are you like this?”

When the woman asked with a gentle smile, Hwang In answered in a low voice.

“The person from the higher-ups was you, Lady Muin.”

As soon as he recognized her, the smile vanished from the woman’s face. Like an actor, the difference between her friendly expression and her current one was vast.

“How did you know?”

She raised her internal energy and surveyed the surroundings, but there was no situation to be wary of.

The reason Hwang In had noticed was extremely personal.

“Because a beauty such as yourself would never approach me first.”

He spoke from the heart as well. The woman was truly captivating. Thinking she was a dangerous woman, his heart trembled even more, so such a thought might have occurred to him.

A strange smile formed at the corners of the woman’s lips.

“You have a good grasp of yourself.”

She possessed a visage that did not look too displeased even by such humiliation.

“I have been waiting for you, Lady Muin, to arrive.”

She was none other than Geum A-rin (金芽鱗), the leader of the secret organization the Golden Dragon Clan secretly nurtured. She was a woman whose martial arts were as profound as her beauty.

Though unseen to the eye now, she had also brought all her trusted subordinates to resolve this matter. Because the deaths of Jeon Nak and Jeon Gwi’s men were not a matter to be taken lightly.

Hwang In had not known that the fearsome person Jeon Nak had spoken of was this woman. And he could not tell whether this woman before his eyes was such a fearsome person. Because martial artists were people one could never truly know.

In any case, he had thought dozens of times about what to say when this moment came.

First, he conveyed the facts as they were.

“The enemies are three in total.”

He had even practiced calling them “enemies.” Of course, it was not because he was still weighing his options. Right now, it was to survive this situation.

“A man called Geom Yeon killed both Jeon Nak and Jeon Gwi.”

He could be honest about everything. Because he had been told to do so.

Though he said he killed them all alone, the woman did not show great surprise. Did she think she could kill Jeon Nak and Jeon Gwi by herself? Or did she not believe his words?

“How did he kill them?”

Of course, that did not mean he was honest about everything. Such as the fact that he had been there together. If he said he was at the scene where they died? What would he do if she cut him down without even giving him a chance to explain?

“I do not know how he killed them. Because after I went to meet him, there was no news. Perhaps he may still be alive.”

Geum A-rin’s suspicious gaze grew stronger.

“They died, yet you did not.”

“Geom Yeon came to find me and told me to deliver this message if anyone came about this matter.”

“What message?”

“‘I have something to say, so come to me.’ Let us go. I will guide you to him.”

Hwang In sprang to his feet as if to leave at once, but Geum A-rin remained seated with an expression that said not a chance.

“He will have laid a trap and be waiting? Do I look so foolish?”

“No.”

At her cold momentum, Hwang In sat back down and spoke with a frightened face.

“He will not have laid a trap.”

“Why do you think that?”

A thought he could not bring himself to say to her came to mind.

He would not need a trap. Because you have not yet seen those people.

As Hwang In worried about what to answer, his face brightened.

“He is here.”

Geom Muguk was entering through the inn’s entrance.

Absolute Regression, Episode 459.

Chapter 459: You Are a Woman Blessed with Plenty to Eat.
--------------------------------------------------------
Geum Arin’s first impression of Geom Mugeuk was this.

‘He’s far younger than I expected.’

It was hard to believe. That Jeon Nak had been defeated by someone so young. And the Jeongwi had fallen along with him?

It wasn’t only his age; he was far too different from what she had imagined. He was handsome, and there was an indefinable air of dignity about him. She had expected someone extremely rough, overflowing with killing intent.

Combining Geom Mugeuk’s age, her first impression of him, and the results of what he had done, Geum Arin reached this conclusion.

‘He has learned a supreme martial art!’

The fact that he had appeared before her alone with such confidence was proof enough. The question was whether he was stronger or weaker than she was. It was impossible to tell from the outside.

“I was passing by the inn when I saw Branch Leader Hwang through the window, so I came in.”

As if he had truly just happened to stop by, Geom Mugeuk greeted them cheerfully and walked over.

“You had a guest with you.”

As though he did not know who she was, Geom Mugeuk cupped his fist toward Geum Arin in greeting.

“If a place with such a beautiful lady exists in the merchant world, then perhaps I should enter commerce myself.”

It was a pleasant greeting, but Geum Arin showed no particular reaction.

At that, Hwang In cautiously stepped in.

“Watch your tongue. The person here is not someone you may speak of so carelessly.”

It sounded as though he was speaking out of respect for Geum Arin, but in truth, it was a warning to Geom Mugeuk.

‘This woman. She’s the terrifying person they said was coming down! Don’t lose your head over her looks and ruin everything!’

Hwang In was beside himself with worry that Geom Mugeuk might be captivated by Geum Arin’s appearance.

But even without his warning, how could Geom Mugeuk not know it was her?

Geom Mugeuk had already realized that she was no ordinary martial artist.

‘Her achievements are impressive for her age.’

Whether it was through effort enough to kill a person, talent, enormous support, or some fortuitous encounter, hers was not a level of skill commonly seen around him.

And one more thing.

If someone like Jeon Nak had regarded her as terrifying, it meant she was certainly not the sort to show mercy with her hands.

On top of that, he sensed an expert hiding nearby her.

‘She is no ordinary person.’

Experts who had also mastered stealth arts were rare. Such people mostly served as protectors, and someone of this level was guarding her?

‘This woman must be blood kin of the head of the Golden Dragon Clan.’

Hwang In would never know just how much Geom Mugeuk had discerned in that brief moment.

As if only belatedly realizing who she was, Geom Mugeuk spoke with a surprised expression.

“Ah! So this is that person?”

Geum Arin reacted to those words.

“How did that man introduce me?”

Geom Mugeuk answered her.

“He said a very frightening person would be coming.”

Hwang In grew tense inwardly. She could have reacted irritably, saying, ‘He introduced me that way?’ His heart began to pound harder than usual.

‘Please, let today not become the day of my memorial rites!’

With heartfelt desperation in his eyes, he looked at Geom Mugeuk.

Geom Mugeuk introduced himself to Geum Arin.

“I am called Geom Yeon. Not the yeon of fate, but the yeon of smoke. After meeting you, I will vanish like smoke.”

“I didn’t ask.”

Geum Arin responded coldly without revealing her own name.

“They say you killed my subordinates?”

She asked directly, and Geom Mugeuk did not bother to hide it.

“Unintentionally, I had a conflict with your people. For that, I apologize.”

“There was more than one dead. This is not something that can end with an apology.”

“Is there something else you want?”

In a tone as cold as her gaze, she said,

“A life for a life.”

Geom Mugeuk looked at her and said,

“If you can take it, then try.”

For a moment, a taut tension filled the air.

Geum Arin struggled to suppress the desire to draw her sword and cut him down. She was not so lowly as to draw her sword at the moment her opponent desired. Her sword would be drawn at the moment she decided.

The one who broke that suffocating tension was Geom Mugeuk.

“I cannot give you my life, but I can buy you a drink.”

In this situation, without a trace of fear, he turned toward the waiter and ordered another bottle of liquor.

When Geom Mugeuk, having ordered the liquor, turned his head back, a sword was already aimed at his throat.

It was a truly swift move. There had not even been the faintest sound of wind.

To Hwang In, who was sitting beside them, the situation had unfolded in the blink of an eye.

Geom Mugeuk looked at her with an expression that showed no surprise at all. At that reaction, Geum Arin’s eyes grew even colder. He could have lost his life just now.

If he was an expert capable of killing Jeon Nak, he should have reacted somehow.

Could it be that he knew the sword would aim for his throat and still did not stop it? Because he was certain I would not kill him?

Or is he not as skilled as I thought? If so, then he is not the one who killed Jeon Nak and the Jeongwi.

She could not be certain which of the two possibilities was true. That made her feel even more unsettled.

‘This man is hard to read.’

Geom Mugeuk’s gaze followed the blade toward her.

“I must not die.”

“Why?”

After a brief pause, Geom Mugeuk said,

“It was not so in the past, but now my death… would become my father’s justification.”

The moment I die, Father will sweep through the entire murim. The grief of losing his child will become his justification, and the murim will turn into a battlefield.

Naturally, Geum Arin could not understand what he meant by that.

“Are you saying that if I kill you, your father will take revenge, so I should spare you?”

“Well, something like that.”

“Where is your father?”

“He is at the manor. Together with my uncle.”

She had heard from Hwang In that there were three of them in all. Instinct told her that this man’s father and uncle would be no ordinary figures either.

“I came out to buy groceries. Father said he would make a special dish today, you see. Miss, you are blessed with good fortune when it comes to food. Come with me. My father’s cooking is not something one can easily taste. You could boast about it for the rest of your life wherever you go.”

Geum Arin had to acknowledge at least one thing. Whether it was acting or reality, maintaining such composure at the tip of her sword was no easy feat.

With a light movement of her hand, Geum Arin withdrew her sword and said,

“I cannot kill a man who came out to buy groceries.”

Geom Mugeuk rubbed his neck and expressed his gratitude.

“Thank you for sparing me.”

At that sight, Hwang In inwardly felt a sense of dismay. His heart began to pound even more violently.

‘What is this? Why is he showing such weakness? Don’t tell me. Are you weaker than this woman?’

That could not be. He had certainly been there when Jeon Nak and all the Jeongwi were killed at once. Right? Stop joking around and cut this woman down in a single stroke, then tell me. Tell me I no longer need to worry.

But the situation flowed in the exact opposite direction from what he wished.

Whoosh, whoosh.

She sent out finger winds and sealed Geom Mugeuk’s inner energy.

Geom Mugeuk could not avoid them in time, but his composure remained unchanged.

“It is all right. I do not need inner energy to buy groceries anyway.”

Geum Arin looked at Geom Mugeuk with eyes that seemed to say, ‘What kind of lunatic is this?’

‘Surely you are not an expert skilled enough to undo the acupoints I have sealed.’

Now that she had sealed his inner energy, his life belonged to her. And yet he still showed such leisure? His actions went beyond her common sense.

“Father must be waiting.”

Geom Mugeuk rose from his seat and said,

“Come, let us go grocery shopping together.”

* * *

A single carriage was racing along.

Outwardly, it looked like an old and ordinary carriage, but it was made of a sturdy material that would not break even under blades, and it was drawn by fine steeds that would not tire even if they ran all day.

Naturally, the person riding inside was not ordinary either.

Extraordinary and exuding an air of nobility, he was none other than Yong Jamyeong, the guild master of the Eunha Merchant Guild.

Yong Jamyeong, the owner of the Eunha Merchant Guild.

He could be described in many ways, but the most effective and certain description would be this.

The person with the most money in the world.

That was why not only the coachman, but also the people riding in the carriage with him, were no ordinary experts. They were the strongest masters of the Eunha Merchant Guild, the ones Yong Jamyeong trusted most.

This was a secret journey by a man known to be so busy that one had to make an appointment months in advance just to see his face.

Including the coachman, there were only three experts accompanying him as guards. It was the first time such a thing had happened since the Eunha Merchant Guild became the number one merchant guild in the Central Plains. Even then, Yong Jamyeong had intended to bring only one person.

Of course, considering the skills of these three, this journey could be considered safe. They were the people Yong Jamyeong trusted most in the Eunha Merchant Guild, and at the same time, the three most skilled.

In particular, Baek Chong, who sat across from him, was a man who had shared life and death, hardship and joy with Yong Jamyeong his entire life and helped build the Eunha Merchant Guild.

“It is still not too late. Shall we turn the carriage around?”

Yong Jamyeong smiled and asked him,

“Are you afraid as well?”

Baek Chong answered honestly.

“I am.”

“That is the first time I have heard you say you are afraid.”

Baek Chong was not afraid that he himself might die. He was afraid that they might lose Yong Jamyeong on this journey.

“This is too dangerous a choice.”

“More dangerous than refusing the request of the Demonic Cult Leader?”

Baek Chong could say nothing. While growing the merchant guild together with Yong Jamyeong, they had weathered all manner of storms, but among them, the most dangerous opponent had always been the Demonic Cult.

“If it had been a simple request from the Demonic Cult, I would have found some excuse to buy time. But this time, the Demonic Cult Leader himself sent a secret letter.”

It had even been a letter written personally by the Demonic Cult Leader.

A handwritten letter from the Demonic Cult Leader.

It was truly the first time such a thing had happened.

“Then I urge you to secretly notify the Murim Alliance and the Sado Alliance. If they know, the Demonic Cult will not dare harbor any foolish thoughts.”

Yong Jamyeong understood better than anyone what Baek Chong was worried about. But this was not a matter that could be handled that way.

“The Demonic Cult Leader emphasized the word ‘secretly.’ That means we are not to contact them.”

“That is precisely why we must inform them. The Demonic Cult has never acted like this until now.”

This decision had not been made merely on instinct. It was a decision made by combining all his past experience with the vast amount of information collected recently.

Yes, how could he not know? How rough and brutal demonic practitioners were.

“We can only hope the information is true.”

“Are you speaking of the matter concerning the Young Cult Leader?”

Yong Jamyeong nodded. It was information that a new wind was blowing within the Demonic Cult. Because of it, even a tripartite conference between the righteous, unorthodox, and demonic factions had taken place.

That was why he hoped the reason he had been summoned this time was because of that Young Cult Leader. It felt safer than simply being summoned by the Heavenly Demon.

“You know this, do you not? Reality is always different from rumor.”

“Do not worry too much. Everything will turn out well.”

Yong Jamyeong was that sort of man.

A man who, at any moment, tried to overcome things with positive thoughts rather than negative ones.

He did not know whether this matter would become an opportunity for the Eunha Merchant Guild or whether he would die because of it, but as he always had, he would make his way through. He considered the Heavenly Demon’s handwritten letter a call of fate that could not be refused.

He believed he had not been summoned in order to be killed. The Demonic Cult Leader he had heard of and gathered information about was not that sort of person. He trusted the intelligence network of the Eunha Merchant Guild.

He would not return empty-handed. He would give what the other man wanted and receive what he himself wanted in return. A transaction was an exchange, after all.

Whenever such a moment of crisis came, he thought back to that time in his youth. To the moment when he had gone to the brink of death and survived. Others did not know how great a help that experience was in living his life.

A life he was living as a bonus anyway—let him live without regret.

Sometimes bravely, sometimes even more diligently.

Perhaps it was thanks to that that he had been able to become the owner of the number one merchant guild in the murim.

And so the carriage sped swiftly toward the appointed place.

* * *

‘He is really buying groceries?’

Geom Mugeuk began purchasing all sorts of ingredients at the market.

“For vegetables or fruit, you must choose ones with even, vivid colors. They must be firm, not soft, and you should avoid ones with spots like these.”

Geum Arin watched in astonishment as he shopped while even giving explanations.

At first, she suspected it was a ploy to stall for time. But he really was carefully buying ingredients for cooking. He even had a piece of paper on which he had written down what to buy.

“Now that we have bought the meat as well, that is everything!”

After finishing the shopping, Geom Mugeuk tried to take her back to the manor.

“Did you think I would obediently walk into the trap you dug?”

“Are you afraid?”

Geum Arin glared at Geom Mugeuk. Even after sealing his inner energy, she still could not come to a conclusion about him. What in the world was this composure? Or was he simply insane?

Geom Mugeuk lifted the ingredients he held in both hands.

“It will be a very delicious trap.”

Geum Arin said to Hwang In, who was with them,

“You go and deliver the message. If he wants to save his son, he is to come alone to the place I tell him.”

At that, Geom Mugeuk let out a sigh.

“My father will not come. He is not the sort of person to come just because someone tells him to.”

“His son will die if he does not come.”

“He still will not come.”

Geom Mugeuk said to Hwang In,

“If by any chance you meet my father, do not make eye contact. Behave respectfully. Tell him everything about the situation.”

“I will.”

Hwang In nodded, then flinched. It was because Geum Arin was looking at him with displeased eyes that seemed to say, ‘Whose side are you on?’

Geom Mugeuk said to her,

“Now then, since we have already bought all the ingredients, I will make dinner for you tonight. My cooking is also not easy to taste, so you are still a woman blessed with good fortune when it comes to food.”

Geum Arin looked at Geom Mugeuk with an incredulous gaze. In her entire life, no one had ever called her a woman blessed with good fortune when it came to food.

“Just what are you? Who sent you?”

With a smile that said she would soon find out, Geom Mugeuk began walking first.

“I am hungry, so let us hurry and make ourselves a meal.”

Then he stopped and asked her,

“By the way, where should we go? Let us go somewhere I can cook.”

Absolute Regression, Chapter 460

Chapter 460: Now Is the Time to Ask Yourself.
---------------------------------------------
Geom Woojin was in the rear courtyard, training in martial arts alone.

Hwi stood outside the wall, listening to the sounds of the Cult Leader’s training.

Because he had always been at the Cult Leader’s side, only he knew. He knew that lately, the Cult Leader had been more earnest in his martial training than ever before. He also knew that Geom Mugeuk had been the catalyst.

The Cult Leader’s breathing, his footsteps, the sound of him leaping, the sound of his sword cutting through the air, the sound of him releasing and withdrawing his aura.

They were sounds Hwi had heard for a very long time. Not once had he ever directly watched the Cult Leader while he trained. The Cult Leader had said it was fine for him to look, but Hwi, adhering to principle, had listened only to the sounds without watching him train.

Even from sound alone, Hwi could tell. No—perhaps it was because he had only ever listened that he could tell even better.

The Cult Leader’s realm had changed. He had believed the Cult Leader had nowhere higher to climb, but could there truly be another realm beyond that?

Without question, the Cult Leader’s sounds had changed.

Just as that bout of training came to an end, someone came to the manor.

When Hwi went out to see, it was Hwang In, who had visited once before.

“What brings you here?”

Hwang In spoke to Hwi with a frightened expression.

“May I speak directly to the Sect Leader?”

Hwi brought him to the rear courtyard.

“The leader of the Hwangdo Merchant Guild has come.”

From beyond the wall came Geom Woojin’s voice.

“Let him in.”

All the way here, Hwang In had been recalling what Geom Mugeuk had told him.

Do not meet his eyes, and be courteous.

“I greet the Sect Leader.”

After bowing politely, he spoke with his head lowered.

“Your son has been taken captive. By the person who came down to investigate this matter. He said the Sect Leader would cook for her, but she would not listen, so he said he would cook for her himself. No, that is not it.”

To Hwang In, who was rambling, Hwi said,

“Where is the place?”

“If you go about ten li west from the marketplace, there is an old manor.”

Originally, these were the words Geum Arin had told him to relay before he came here. Then Geom Mugeuk had interrupted.

—Wouldn’t it be confusing if you explained it only like that? From where in the marketplace is it ten li? Are there any forks in the road along the way? Are there any other manors in similar places? You must explain in detail.

—You said your father would not come.

At Geum Arin’s words, Geom Mugeuk had acted as though he were in anguish.

—He is a heartless man, you see.

Hwang In could tell. Geom Mugeuk was secretly hoping his father would come.

‘But your intuition was right.’

Just as Geom Mugeuk had said, Geom Woojin showed no sign of worry, nor even interest.

“I was told to tell you to come there. Alone.”

As if that was enough, Hwi said,

“Understood. You may leave now.”

Hwang In turned away, then stopped in his tracks. It was because he thought he had to bring Geom Woojin there somehow for Geom Mugeuk to survive. Only if Geom Mugeuk lived would he live too.

“Your son’s internal energy has been sealed.”

But that did not work.

“Your son told me again and again to explain the location of the manor properly so you would not be confused. He was truly desperate and earnest!”

Even earnest pleading, padded with exaggeration, did not work.

At the still blunt response, Hwang In bowed his head and turned away.

“Then I will take my leave.”

Hwi knew the Cult Leader better than anyone. He was not the sort to make a move over something the Young Cult Leader could escape from on his own.

But Geom Woojin once again showed Hwi, here and now, that the change in him was not merely in the sounds of his martial arts.

At that moment, Geom Woojin asked Hwang In, who had been walking away in the distance,

“Did he finish the shopping?”

At the unexpected question, Hwang In did not understand what he meant at first, but soon answered quickly.

“Yes! Even with his internal energy sealed, he shopped with both hands full. Vegetables, meat, and even fruit to eat for dessert—he bought everything.”

Was he imagining that sight? Geom Woojin smiled faintly.

“Lead the way.”

In truth, the one more surprised than Hwang In was Hwi.

As he silently followed behind Geom Woojin, Hwi had a premonition that protecting him would become more difficult from now on.

If the Cult Leader changed, then he too would have to change in order to protect him.

Even so, this change seemed to come as something welcome to Hwi. For on the lips of the man following behind him, there appeared a precious smile as rare to see as the Cult Leader’s own.

* * *

The appointed place was an old manor in a sparsely traveled area.

As soon as Geum Arin returned, she planted ambushes throughout the manor.

They were the people she had brought down with her this time. They were not many in number, but they were experts in surprise attacks.

They lay in ambush on all sides. Up in trees, on rooftops, behind buildings. Since they were all deeply experienced, they found their own positions.

Even if the opponent managed to defend against their surprise attacks, he would not be able to block her own attack in the opening that followed. The opponent would not leave this place alive.

Whether he knew the gravity of the situation or not, Geom Mugeuk came out of the kitchen carrying tableware.

‘He is really going to cook in this situation?’

Just as he had defied her expectations by going shopping, Geom Mugeuk began preparing to cook in the very place where the ambush was underway.

He lit a fire in the courtyard, brought out bowls and utensils, and washed them in clean water.

Then, with practiced skill, he began preparing the ingredients. Geum Arin watched him with the thought, Let us see how far you go.

As he peeled an onion, Geom Mugeuk said,

“Every time I cook, it feels as though I am learning martial arts. Lighting the fire, washing the bowls, studying what ingredients to use, trying this seasoning and that ingredient. Then, once the optimal method comes out, repeating it. What do you think? Isn’t it similar to our martial arts?”

“If you force the comparison like that, what wouldn’t be similar?”

At Geum Arin’s cold response, Geom Mugeuk smiled and admitted it.

“You are right. Playing an instrument, painting, even going out to work every day—all of them are alike.”

As he said that, perhaps because the onion was stinging, Geom Mugeuk closed his eyes. Seeing the tears well up, Geum Arin thought at least this one thing was certain.

‘A martial artist cries while peeling onions? This truly is the first time I have seen someone like this.’

Most martial artists were like those onions. People whose insides could not be known.

Some hid their outer selves, others their inner selves.

Some were weak but pretended to be strong, while others were strong but pretended to be weak.

But this man was a new type. He did not seem weak, yet he did not seem strong either.

That was why she could not make a decision. That was why she was watching this absurd scene that moved outside the bounds of her common sense.

“To be honest, I am better at cooking than my father. Know that thanks to this, you will be eating tastier food.”

It was right then.

Without realizing it, Geum Arin’s gaze turned behind her.

And it was not only her. The gazes of those lying in ambush there also naturally turned toward the main gate she was looking at.

How long had he been standing there? Outside the wide-open main gate stood a man with his hands clasped behind his back.

It was none other than Geom Woojin.

A silence flowed for an instant.

Geum Arin’s heart began pounding so violently that she could hear it in her ears.

The opponent had not revealed any particular aura, nor had he glared at her coldly, yet her entire body went rigid as though frozen.

Why was she like this? The opponent had done nothing.

She could tell that the gazes of those hiding themselves were also all fixed on that place. The presence of that man standing there at ease, hands behind his back, was setting everyone’s nerves on edge and drawing them in.

The one who broke the silence, in which not even the sound of breathing could be heard, was Geom Mugeuk.

“Father! You came to rescue your son! As expected! I knew you were not a heartless father who would abandon his child!”

The corner of Geom Woojin’s mouth curved into a sneer. This time, that sneer said this to his son: You? I came to rescue those people being toyed with by you.

Geom Woojin slowly walked inside. Behind him, Hwang In followed cautiously.

When Geom Woojin entered the courtyard, someone appeared between Geom Woojin and Geum Arin.

The white-haired masked man was Im Hyeok, the personal guard who had protected Geum Arin since childhood.

“Please leave at once, my lady.”

Having lived as a martial artist for a long time, Im Hyeok had experienced many martial artists. This was the first time he had faced an opponent who made him feel such primal danger. What was even more astonishing was the fact that the opponent had not revealed any aura at all.

Shwiiik!

Hidden weapons flew toward Geom Woojin. Under normal circumstances, this would have been the moment to shout, “Attack together harder!”

Clang!

Yet astonishingly, the one who threw himself forward and struck down the hidden weapon was Im Hyeok. He also hurled himself forward to knock away the second and third hidden weapons.

“Cease attacks! Stop!”

He halted the ambush. He had realized that their surprise attacks would not work on this opponent. It was an instinctive judgment that they must not provoke him.

‘If we touch him, we will all be slaughtered!’

His snow-white hair was a symbol of his years and experience, and he proved that with his excellent judgment.

“Everyone withdraw!”

At Im Hyeok’s command, everyone who had been lying in ambush withdrew.

Geum Arin launched herself forward. It was not because she had listened to him and fled.

She moved behind Geom Mugeuk and placed her sword against his neck. It was a declaration that if Im Hyeok was touched, she would kill Geom Mugeuk.

Geom Mugeuk said to her,

“There was a reason you have survived until now.”

It meant that Im Hyeok had protected her well.

At that moment, as taut tension filled the air.

“Put away the sword.”

Geom Woojin’s low words carried an absolute dignity that none could dare defy.

Im Hyeok’s judgment remained good until the very end.

He put away his sword without hesitation. Sometimes, one could protect someone by sheathing one’s sword.

Then he looked at Geum Arin. His eyes spoke. Hurry and do as he says.

Geum Arin withdrew her sword. But she did not move away from Geom Mugeuk.

As if nothing had happened, Geom Mugeuk said to his father,

“Today, I will cook for you.”

“Did you buy enough?”

“Yes. I bought plenty, just in case.”

The words just in case carried the meaning that he had anticipated his father might come. They also held his wish that his father would.

It was right then.

Geom Woojin rolled up his sleeves and approached Geom Mugeuk.

Then he quietly began trimming the vegetables. He was helping his son cook.

Geom Mugeuk looked at his father with a surprised expression, then quietly focused on the cooking.

He was so shocked and moved that he could not even think to crack a joke.

Cooking together with his father.

It was something he was doing for the first time since returning to the past. There were many things he wanted to do with his father, but he had never imagined there would come a moment when they would cook together.

He was all the more moved because this was not a moment he had created, but one his father had created.

‘Father, thank you.’

Geum Arin watched the scene with a frozen expression.

The fact that Im Hyeok had stepped forward like this meant that he had sensed a strength in the opponent that she herself had failed to perceive.

‘Just what are these people?’

She had never heard of a father and son like this existing in the martial world.

—You must absolutely not act rashly.

It was Im Hyeok’s sound transmission. This was the first time she had seen Im Hyeok so afraid and tense. She herself did not feel danger to that extent.

A short while later, Geom Mugeuk and Geom Woojin finished the food.

Finally, Geom Mugeuk called for Hwi.

“Uncle.”

At that, Hwi released his concealment and revealed himself between Im Hyeok and Geom Woojin.

“Gah!”

Im Hyeok let out a short cry. It was the first time Geum Arin had seen Im Hyeok so startled by someone’s appearance.

He was not startled because he recognized Hwi, but because of the level of his concealment technique.

Im Hyeok had not known that the other party had been hiding only a few steps away from him.

Others might vaguely think, How impressive, but he alone knew. He knew just how remarkable the concealment technique behind that single move of revealing himself had been.

It was a move that would shock the more one was an expert. Hwi’s concealment technique was that perfect.

That was not the only surprise. There was the air of a master he felt from Hwi’s appearance. The other man was clearly a guard warrior. A guard recognized another guard.

‘I have never in my life seen a guard warrior this strong.’

Just who was he protecting?

His ability befitted the presence of the man who had appeared before him.

His gaze naturally turned toward Geom Woojin.

This much was certain. Though he did not know who that man was, he was someone whose name anyone would recognize the moment they heard it.

Then Hwi looked at Im Hyeok. There were things that passed between guards who had devoted themselves to loyalty and service for a long time. What kind of guard the other was, what sort of life he must have lived.

That must have been why Hwi sent this sound transmission.

—Do you wish to protect your master? Then stop. Your thoughts, your actions. And keep your mouth shut.

Im Hyeok understood that it was advice to leave everything to them. It was not advice given to just anyone.

And so they all sat around the table. Hwi, Im Hyeok, and even Hwang In took their seats.

“Now then, let’s eat.”

Geom Ujin ate the food.

“How does it taste?”

Geom Mugeuk asked nervously. He had been the head cook today, while his father had merely assisted. The Young Cult Leader cooking, and the Heavenly Demon assisting. It was truly a day that would never be forgotten in the history of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult, nor in the personal histories of the two men.

“Not bad.”

Coming from his father, an assessment like that was practically praise on the level of, “What a delicacy!”

This time, he asked Geum Arin.

“Why are you not eating, young lady? Go on, eat. A dish made by my father and me is something you’ll have a hard time tasting again in your lifetime.”

Geum Arin had not yet picked up her chopsticks.

She was in turmoil. The situation had ended before she could even try anything. Now, it had slipped beyond her control. She had always believed herself to be someone who knew how to restrain herself at times like this, but this situation was…

“What on earth is this?”

The sigh escaped her before she knew it.

Im Hyeok told her with his eyes that she had to endure it. Of course, she trusted Im Hyeok’s judgment.

Even so, while she could endure a sword being drawn, she could not endure this question.

“What are you?”

A question she had asked and pondered several times, yet still had not found the answer to.

Geom Mugeuk looked at her with clear, deep eyes.

“By chance, I saw someone being forced into an unreasonable contract and helped them. When things went wrong, the ones doing the forcing tried to kill us. So I killed them. Were they such great men that you came all this way to avenge them?”

Because he spoke in such a calm, composed tone, and because until now he had mostly been saying frivolous things, Geom Mugeuk’s words struck her heavily.

“Why ask who we are? Is this not the time to ask yourself who you are? Are you the kind of person who deserves to die, like those dead men? Or are you someone worth saving?”

“!”

Geum Arin could not answer.

Rage surged up within her. But she could not show that emotion. Because another feeling, tangled together with that rage and holding it back from bursting out, was fear.

Geom Mugeuk was asking her now.

Are you a human being worth saving?

Amid the heavy silence, only the sound of chewing could be heard there.

Water being poured, chopsticks moving.

Geom Mugeuk and Geom Ujin ate naturally, as they usually did. Hwi and Hwang In ate cautiously, while Geum Arin and Im Hyeok could hardly eat at all.

It was then that a sound came from far away.

The sound grew louder and louder.

Dududududu.

It was the sound of a carriage racing toward them.

The manor stood in a secluded place, somewhere no one should have had any reason to come.

And so, like fate itself, the carriage came rushing in and arrived outside the gate.

Absolute Regression Chapter 461

Chapter 461: A Legend Came to Meet You?
---------------------------------------
“We’ve arrived at the estate.”

At the words of the warrior on the driver’s seat, Yong Jamyeong poked his head out of the window.

He could see the estate standing in a remote place.

Thinking of the person who would be waiting there, his expression was as calm as a lake, but a silent typhoon raged in his heart.

Having gone through all manner of hardships and storms, he had become the richest man in the world. He had prided himself on having reached a level where he wouldn’t bat an eyelash at most things by now, but seeing his heart race like this, it seemed he still had a long way to go.

But Yong Jamyeong never revealed his trembling outwardly. For Baek Chong’s anxiety, sitting before him now, was enough on its own.

“If a problem arises, speak the code word.”

There was a code word established between them. According to that code word, Baek Chong had dealt with various critical situations.

Then Yong Jamyeong asked with a smile.

“When was the last time we used it?”

It was so long ago that he truly couldn’t even remember when that had been.

Yong Jamyeong’s gaze turned back toward the window.

“That is how much we have lived without tension.”

Because tension had become the other party’s burden at some point.

And as if to punish that arrogance, tension suddenly returned.

Meanwhile, the carriage slowly came to a stop.

“We have arrived.”

At the report of the warrior on the driver’s seat, Baek Chong looked at the warrior sitting beside him. As if understanding without words, the warrior in the next seat nodded.

Since Yong Jamyeong had no intention of turning back, the three of them now had to protect their chief. Though each was an apex expert, they had staked their lives on this matter.

“Please trust only us.”

Speaking with force, Baek Chong was the first to descend from the carriage.

Next, Yong Jamyeong got down. His movements were composed and relaxed.

After him, the warrior who had been riding along and the warrior from the driver’s seat descended in succession.

The estate’s gate was closed.

They couldn’t know what traps lay beyond this door waiting for them.
