Demon Lords might be standing in a line waiting for them. The Poison King’s poison might be scattered, and the great blade of the Blood Heaven Demon Blade might fly toward their heads. The Extreme Evil Lesser Demon might look down at them as they died and smile coldly.

Baek Chong exchanged glances with the other warriors. They were of similar minds.

‘They asked to meet in a place like this?’

Judging from the surrounding trees and terrain, it was a good place for an ambush. Fortunately, the warriors who had been lying in ambush earlier had all withdrawn, so unnecessary misunderstandings were avoided.

What grated on Baek Chong’s nerves more was that the meeting place had been changed when they had almost reached their destination. It was a message from the Tongcheongak to meet at a different estate to the west instead of the original one.

Baek Chong was displeased by such things as well. Both the fact that the Tongcheongak was watching them and that they had changed the meeting place at the last minute.

Baek Chong slowly walked to the gate and slowly pushed it. The gate was not locked.

Creeeak.

The door opened, emitting the sound of olden years.

Then, an unexpected sight unfolded before their eyes.

A table was set up in the courtyard, and people were eating a meal.

Geom Mugeuk spoke while holding a ladle.

“Anyone for more soup?”

It was Geom Mugeuk’s own unique way of greeting visitors. Since we are at a meal, do not be tense!

Baek Chong stepped aside, and Yong Jamyeong walked forward from behind.

Though the people at the table didn’t have name tags, he could tell at a glance which one was the Heavenly Demon.

He felt he could have picked him out even if dozens had been crowded in the courtyard eating.

Yong Jamyeong’s gaze turned toward Geom Ujin.

It was the moment when the man known to possess the greatest martial power and the man known to possess the greatest wealth met. They might have had a chance to meet once before, but the two were meeting for the first time today. No, it would be the second time, but.

On the way in the carriage, Yong Jamyeong had imagined what his first impression of the Demon Cult Leader would be.

Would he be fearsome? Would he be trembling? Would he be more affable than expected? Would he be handsome?

But the first impression that came to mind was something he had never imagined.

‘He looks familiar!’

He truly looked familiar, as if he had seen him somewhere before.

But that was all. He couldn’t remember where he had seen him.

No. Since he had never seen the Heavenly Demon before, it would be more accurate to say he couldn’t remember who the person resembling him was.

Having reached the pinnacle of merchants, how many people must he have met in his life? So many people he couldn’t even remember one by one.

‘I have definitely seen someone who resembles that person.’

But his thoughts couldn’t last long. Because he couldn’t dare to keep staring at the Heavenly Demon’s face.

Geom Mugeuk could tell that he hadn’t recognized his father at once.

Well, after all, it had been during both his father’s youth and his own.

He must have never even imagined that the person who saved him could be the Heavenly Demon. Perhaps it was only natural.

Do not worry. I will fulfill your long-cherished wish. Before you leave, I will surely make you remember.

And at this moment, Geom Mugeuk wondered.

‘Did Father recognize him?’

But it was impossible to find that out from his father’s expression.

Geom Mugeuk set the ladle down and stepped forward.

“Welcome, Chief.”

Yong Jamyeong could tell at a glance. That this young man was the Young Cult Leader of the Heavenly Demon Cult. Because he resembled the Heavenly Demon sitting over there in some way.

“I am called Geom Yeon of the Seodo Sect.”

The reason they had called Yong Jamyeong to this place today was, after showing that they had a deep relationship with the Eunha Merchant Group, to use that Geum Arin to rapidly penetrate into their core.

They had considered pretending to be people from the Eunha Merchant Group, but his father’s presence made it impossible to pass him off as someone serving under another.

They had rather intended to reel her in with the story that the Arcane Martial Sect was trying to join hands with Yong Jamyeong.

“This is my father.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s introduction, Yong Jamyeong walked toward Geom Ujin. Baek Chong, who was behind him, had every nerve on edge.

“I am called Yong Jamyeong of the Eunha Merchant Group.”

At Yong Jamyeong’s greeting, Geom Ujin rose from his seat.

Geom Mugeuk and Hwi watched that sight with inwardly surprised hearts.

Originally, their father was not someone who got up to greet the owner of a merchant group. Even if that merchant group was the greatest under heaven.

But Geom Ujin received him with courtesy.

“Thank you for coming.”

He was expressing his gratitude to the one who had rushed here immediately upon receiving his letter.

The moment Yong Jamyeong heard Geom Ujin’s voice, it felt as if something struck his chest with a thud.

Excitement, anticipation, fear. A unique and intense feeling different from such emotions.

If fate were to speak to him, would it feel like this?

It wasn’t the first time. There had been several occasions in his life when he had felt this emotion. Each time, he had undergone a great change in life.

Meanwhile, when the name Yong Jamyeong appeared, everyone there was startled.

Especially the most surprised person was Geum Arin.

‘That man is the owner of the Eunha Merchant Group?’

She had never seen him in person. But she had heard far too many stories about him.

Once, her father had evaluated him like this.

—His power is not his wealth. He is a man whose very name, Yong Jamyeong, holds power greater than money.

It was an evaluation that he was a figure who, despite being a merchant, had surpassed the stage of dominating people and the world with money. At that time, she had felt it. Her father’s jealousy.

Unable to believe that such a Yong Jamyeong had appeared here, she looked at her bodyguard, Im Hyeok.

Immediately, Im Hyeok’s telepathic message flew to her.

—He is truly the owner of the Eunha Merchant Group.

Im Hyeok had seen him before. It was just that Geum Arin had been too young to remember; she too had seen him.

Im Hyeok’s expression hardened. Just Geom Mugeuk, Geom Ujin, and Hwi alone were people they couldn’t handle.

In such a situation, Yong Jamyeong had come here?

In the Upper Realm, Yong Jamyeong is the Martial Alliance Leader and the Heavenly Demon.

‘What should we do?’

As Im Hyeok was racking his brains, he suddenly locked eyes with Hwi across from him. The meaning contained in Hwi’s composed gaze was the same as the earlier telepathic message sent to Geum Arin.

—Stop thinking and acting, and close your mouth.

Although it was a dismissive remark, Im Hyeok had felt it was clearly well-intentioned advice.

The same was true now.

‘If you wish to live, do not step forward. Do not go against the current; entrust your body to the waves.’

Im Hyeok bowed his head very slightly to Hwi, then looked at the person he had to protect.

Geum Arin was looking at Geom Mugeuk.

‘That legendary man came to meet you? What are you people?’

Even the head of the Geumryong Clan she belonged to had to request an appointment in advance and go meet him at a time they set.

‘What are you, who even suppressed my internal energy?’

It was truly a sight difficult to believe even after seeing with one’s own eyes.

If the Eunha Merchant Group was the greatest in the Upper Realm, the Geumryong Clan she belonged to was also within the top three. They had a good relationship with the Eunha Merchant Group, but they were also fiercely competitive.

‘Something is strange. I have to find out why this meeting is happening and report it.’

Having finished greeting Geom Ujin, Yong Jamyeong’s gaze turned toward Geum Arin and Im Hyeok.

“Who might these people be?”

Geom Mugeuk introduced her.

“I have not yet heard the name of the young lady present here, but one thing is certain about her.”

She was inwardly anxious about what he might say. And as expected.

“This young lady is one with great fortune in eating.”

Geum Arin couldn’t hide her absurd expression. Introducing her like this to the chief of the Eunha Merchant Group?

Yong Jamyeong spoke with a smile.

“You possess a very joyful fortune among blessings.”

Though he spoke politely, he thought inwardly.

‘If she hasn’t even given her name yet, it means she doesn’t know who she is dealing with right now.’

He thought that the reason he had been asked to come here today might be related to these people.

Next, Geom Mugeuk introduced Hwang In.

“This is the chief of the Hwangdo Merchant Group. The one who dreams of becoming the owner of the greatest merchant group under heaven.”

Hwang In had truly never imagined he would meet the person he had dreamed of here today.

“It is an honor to meet you.”

“I have been admiring the rapid growth of the Hwangdo Merchant Group recently.”

Hwang In’s face flushed slightly. Because it was an achievement gained through improper methods. On the other hand, he was surprised that the Eunha Merchant Group had been paying attention to him.

“Our merchant group has been reborn. Please watch over us well from now on.”

Hwang In was quick-witted, as befitting a merchant.

“Then I shall take my leave now.”

He would have liked to spend all night talking with Yong Jamyeong and forge a connection, but he felt that this was not the atmosphere for it.

Leaving didn’t necessarily mean growing distant. Knowing when to step in and when to step back was, rather, taking one step closer to the goal.

Then, the bodyguard Im Hyeok sent a telepathic message to Geum Arin.

—Let us also get up now.

—We have to find out why these people met.

—You know, don’t you? They are not people who easily reveal themselves. If we stay longer, only our identities will be revealed further.

His judgment had been excellent from beginning to end.

Though he couldn’t lead the decision, Geum Arin’s judgment in accepting his decision was also good.

“Since a distinguished guest has arrived, we shall also take our leave.”

Geum Arin rose from her seat.

Geom Mugeuk spoke to her as she bid farewell.

“Next time, try the food properly. You won’t regret it.”

The Geom Mugeuk who spoke of food while parting felt like a different person from the Geom Mugeuk who had spoken these words earlier.

—Are you a human worth keeping alive?

Geum Arin had a premonition that she would soon have to answer that question.

She bowed politely to Geom Ujin and Yong Jamyeong, then left with Im Hyeok.

Walking toward the gate, she and Im Hyeok conversed via telepathic messages.

—This was the place we decided on, yet we are the first to be chased out.

—But are we not leaving with our lives?

Good judgment was the gift they had been given.

With even those two gone, Geom Mugeuk asked Yong Jamyeong.

“Have you eaten?”

Normally, he would have refused of course. Who would have a meal when arriving late to join someone else’s meal? No, it had been a long time since such a situation had even occurred.

But this occasion was different.

It was a table where the Heavenly Demon had been dining. He could not simply watch from the side after being told to join the meal.

“I haven’t eaten yet.”

“We cooked plenty, so there is more than enough to share.”

Thus Yong Jamyeong sat down together. The three guards including Baek Chong stood a few steps away from him.

“Please join us.”

When Geom Mugeuk attended to them, Baek Chong politely declined.

“We are fine.”

Geom Mugeuk didn’t press further. For if they were forced to sit in this situation, it would only be more trying.

Geom Mugeuk brought out the dishes that hadn’t been served yet, putting them in new bowls.

“This is a dish I made myself, with my father beside me preparing the ingredients.”

At those words, Yong Jamyeong was greatly surprised.

“This is truly a precious meal.”

“Indeed, you will never have it again.”

Yong Jamyeong bowed his head to Geom Ujin and spoke.

“It is an honor.”

“I wonder if it will suit your palate.”

Yong Jamyeong tasted the food. Baek Chong’s nerves behind him were stretched to the extreme.

What if there was poison? If not to kill, but to control Yong Jamyeong by even using a mild poison?

He wanted to run over at once and test it with a silver needle.

But if he stabbed a silver needle into food made by the Heavenly Demon and the Young Cult Leader, his own heart would be pierced by their swords.

Without such worries, Yong Jamyeong ate comfortably.

“It is truly delicious.”

It wasn’t empty praise. There was no delicious dish he hadn’t tasted, yet this dish was quite excellent.

“Next time, try a dish my father makes while I assist. And tell me whose food is more delicious.”

Yong Jamyeong merely smiled. He could never say he would. Because it was a request to evaluate and judge the superiority between the Cult Leader and the Young Cult Leader’s food.

After finishing the simple meal, Yong Jamyeong finally asked politely.

“I wonder why an esteemed person such as yourself has called a mere merchant.”

Geom Mugeuk thought he had already done everything he needed to do.

Because through this gathering today, Geum Arin now knew they had some kind of relationship with the Eunha Merchant Group.

But Father spoke unexpected words, as though he had not called him for a matter like that.

“I wanted to introduce my son.”

Absolute Regression: Chapter 462

Chapter 462: Can Wine Be Left Out of Money Talk?
------------------------------------------------
All the way here, Yong Jamyeong had pondered why the Demonic Cult Leader had summoned him.

In both good ways and bad.

But he had truly never imagined the reason would be to introduce him to his son.

Naturally, Yong Jamyeong’s gaze turned to Geom Mugeuk.

The next-generation Cheonma, and the man who had recently become the greatest topic of conversation in the murim. The many rumors and pieces of information about him were always contradictory.

Worthless, yet extraordinary.

The worthless deeds were so worthless they could not be believed, and the extraordinary achievements he had accomplished were so extraordinary they could not be believed.

And as if to prove those rumors were not merely rumors, at their first meeting he had been holding a ladle. The first words Yong Jamyeong heard from him had been, “Anyone want more soup?”

Geom Mugeuk bowed his head to his father.

“Thank you, Father.”

He did not know what hidden intention his father had in this matter, but he at least believed it was for his sake. He could feel it just from the way his father glanced at him.

Since Yong Jamyeong did not yet know what kind of people Geom Ujin and Geom Mugeuk were, such suspicions were only natural.

‘The Demonic Cult Leader arranged a meeting like this for the sake of his child’s connections? There’s no way.’

One conclusion came naturally.

‘In the end, is it money?’

A conclusion born of long experience.

No matter how they dressed it up, what martial artists ultimately wanted from merchants was money.

The Cheonma could not step forward personally, so he intended to put his son forward and demand money under some pretext?

Of course, the amount would be an astronomical sum befitting the names of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult and the Eunha Merchant Guild.

‘If that is the reason?’

A martial artist would be enraged by the other party’s unreasonable conduct and try to punish it, but a merchant must not do that. If one was a merchant, at such a time one had to ask oneself:

‘What can I obtain in return?’

Concealing those inner thoughts, Yong Jamyeong spoke to Geom Ujin with proper courtesy.

“Forming a friendship with the Young Cult Leader is something I, of all people, would earnestly wish for.”

He meant it. There was nothing bad about building a relationship with the Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult.

—If you come out like this, I’ll have to call someone I know too.

—Who would you dare call against us?

—The Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult.

It meant he could have such thrilling conversations. Provided, of course, that the other party had no hidden impure intentions.

Geom Ujin rose from his seat.

“Let us meet later.”

It meant he should spend time with Geom Mugeuk. And saying they would meet later meant that he should not leave right away, but stay for the day.

Yong Jamyeong was inwardly flustered, not having expected Geom Ujin to leave only the Young Cult Leader and himself behind.

‘What sort of situation is this?’

It was not something he, the leader of the Eunha Merchant Guild, could easily experience.

In any case, he had said they would meet later.

“I will see you later.”

Yong Jamyeong replied politely with a smile.

His flawless courtesy was not merely because the other party was the Cheonma. Yong Jamyeong the merchant was, by nature, not a man whose emotions became his attitude.

Geom Ujin slowly moved his feet.

Baek Chong, who had been standing behind Yong Jamyeong, grew even more tense.

When they had been sitting and eating, he had not realized it, but once Geom Ujin began to walk, the feeling was different again. Though he walked slowly, he did not seem slow, and it felt as if the air around him moved with him at its center.

Before leaving, Geom Ujin left Yong Jamyeong with a single remark.

“It will not be such a bad thing for you, either.”

What did that mean? Was it faith in his child? Or was it a hint?

Yong Jamyeong could not know. He had met countless people and gone through all manner of experiences, but today, that experience was of little use.

Meanwhile, once the Demonic Cult Leader left the place, Baek Chong actually became even more tense.

—There may be some trick, so do not let your guard down.

He sent that voice transmission to the martial artists with him. He, too, could not understand Geom Ujin’s actions. He did not focus on the fact that Geom Ujin had left Yong Jamyeong behind.

‘He’s leaving only the Young Cult Leader here?’

Since the Demonic Cult Leader had vacated the seat, the Young Cult Leader might act on his own.

Looking around, Yong Jamyeong asked Geom Mugeuk,

“Come to think of it, I don’t see a single guard. Where are they?”

“I left my guards at the main cult.”

Geom Mugeuk looked at the three martial artists standing behind Yong Jamyeong and said,

“I told them to train until they reached the level of those gentlemen there.”

No one present would believe it, but it was true. In reality, they would be completely absorbed in training right now. With the mindset that even if they could not protect him, they should at least not become a burden.

Neither of the two men was the type to allow an awkward silence, so the conversation continued naturally.

“We’ve eaten, so shall we go out and walk a little?”

“Let’s do that.”

The two left the manor. Yong Jamyeong first asked what he had been curious about.

“Why did you ask to meet at this manor?”

“Because of the woman blessed with good fortune for food whom you saw earlier.”

“What is your relationship with that woman?”

“She is someone who came to kill us.”

From Geom Mugeuk’s earlier remark that he did not even know the woman’s name, Yong Jamyeong had guessed that she did not know who they were. If she had known, there was no way she would have dared not reveal her own name. Because she had not known, she could have plotted such a thing.

The problem was this.

‘They let a woman who tried to kill the Demonic Cult Leader and the Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult simply go?’

That was something that could never happen.

The reason for his visit had been revealed.

“So you called me because of that woman.”

“I believed so too.”

Geom Mugeuk looked at Yong Jamyeong and said,

“But Father thought differently. You were surprised, weren’t you? I was surprised too.”

This private meeting between the two of them had not been discussed in advance? Was that really true?

Yong Jamyeong did not believe it. He was certain they wanted something.

As if he had read that thought.

“I have a favor to ask.”

Of course. There was no such thing as a good demon. Even those who called themselves righteous committed all kinds of schemes and trickery; how much more so would demons?

Concealing those feelings, Yong Jamyeong said gently,

“Say whatever it is.”

What do you want from the Eunha Merchant Guild? How much do you want?

But what came from Geom Mugeuk was not that sort of request.

“Please tell me your story, Guild Master.”

Yong Jamyeong stopped walking. Geom Mugeuk stopped as well.

“My story?”

“Yes. Any story will do.”

“May I ask why?”

“I thought carefully about why Father wanted to introduce you to me.”

Geom Mugeuk truly had pondered it. Before this was a matter between Yong Jamyeong and himself, it was a matter between his father and himself.

“‘Since you’ve met him, it would be nice if you became close.’ I don’t think he said it with that sort of meaning. My father is absolutely not that kind of person.”

“So? Did you find an answer?”

Geom Mugeuk nodded. The answer he had come up with was this:

“He must want me to learn from you, Guild Master.”

“What do you mean?”

After a brief pause, Geom Mugeuk said,

“The life of a man who rose to the very top in a single field.”

“!”

For a moment, Yong Jamyeong said nothing. He felt as if he had been struck in a blind spot. He had never imagined that the Cheonma had introduced him to his son with such an intention.

But soon, Yong Jamyeong doubted the other party’s intent.

‘No. That cannot be.’

He would use this as the beginning and then demand something. The gaze looking at him was so clear and deep that he had simply been momentarily confused.

“The Cult Leader thinks far too highly of a mere merchant. All I know is talk of money.”

Geom Mugeuk instead looked excited.

“Isn’t that the most interesting kind of story?”

Geom Mugeuk strode ahead and said,

“Let’s go. How could we talk about money without wine?”

* * *

The two sat facing each other at an inn on the market street.

It was the very inn where every meeting in this place had taken place.

Geom Mugeuk and Yong Jamyeong sat facing each other in a corner. The martial artists, including Baek Chong, were ordered to stay as far away as possible.

It was because Yong Jamyeong thought that when the Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult was asking about his life, having guards standing behind him did not suit the occasion.

After the two poured wine for each other, they emptied their first cups.

“It has truly been a long time since I drank in an inn like this. In the past, I used to come and drink occasionally.”

“For someone who could buy this entire inn with the cost of a single meal, you are quite modest.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s sweet words, Yong Jamyeong answered with a smile.

“I enjoy being evaluated that way.”

As Geom Mugeuk filled his cup, he said,

“A healthy amusement.”

“Healthy? Not vanity and arrogance?”

“It’s fine as long as that amusement isn’t directed at people.”

For an instant, Yong Jamyeong flinched.

“What do you mean?”

“I am someone with this much money. I am someone who earns this much money. If that sense of superiority is directed at people, problems arise. At first, it may only be enjoying their envy, but gradually one begins to look down on people, and in the end inflicts deep wounds. Just as problems arise when the tip of a blade is pointed at people.”

Yong Jamyeong listened silently to Geom Mugeuk’s words.

“On the other hand, a sense of superiority enjoyed alone in a shabby inn like this is a healthy amusement. So please come often and enjoy it.”

Yong Jamyeong sank into thought without speaking for a moment. He had never once thought of it in that way.

“Is that why you do it too?”

As the owner of the Eunha Merchant Guild, Yong Jamyeong naturally knew about the three-party talks between the righteous, unorthodox, and demonic factions. Along with the news that the talks had been held in a small tavern had come the information that the place was the Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult’s regular tavern.

In the end, his question was this:

So do you also enjoy a healthy amusement at that Pungryu Tavern?

Geom Mugeuk immediately understood what he meant. That was why there was certainty in his answer.

“No. I’m different.”

“How so?”

“I genuinely like the owner of that tavern. The wine tastes good, the food is delicious, and above all, he makes his guests feel comfortable. If you come to the main cult next time, I will be sure to take you there.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s bright smile, Yong Jamyeong also spoke with a smile.

“You are very good with words. That is a talent essential to a merchant. What do you think? Have you any thoughts of entering the world of commerce?”

“Do you suppose Father recognized in me the sprout of a money tree and introduced me to you, Guild Master?”

As such jokes went back and forth, the mood was good.

Unexpectedly, Yong Jamyeong liked this meeting. Though he did not know how much of the other party was genuine, nor when he might reveal his true colors, conversation with him flowed without obstruction. For some reason, it was enjoyable.

But when the sword and money met, a good atmosphere could not continue forever.

Rather, it would be better to ask while the atmosphere was good.

“What do you want from me? Money?”

Looking into Yong Jamyeong’s eyes, which did not fear death, Geom Mugeuk asked in return,

“If it were money, would you give it?”

A taut tension flowed between the two.

“How much do you want?”

A smile formed at the corners of Geom Mugeuk’s mouth.

“How much do I want? To hear those words from the richest man in the world? That is everyone’s dream.”

Despite Geom Mugeuk’s joke, Yong Jamyeong answered seriously.

“The amount is not what matters. What matters is what you can give me in return.”

Now, the playfulness disappeared from Geom Mugeuk’s face as well.

“I think the situation is one in which you could misunderstand enough, but you saw my father, didn’t you? Did he look like someone who would call you here because he needed money?”

Then came a meaningful addition.

“If Father needs money, he is a man who goes to get it, not one who summons someone.”

Yong Jamyeong understood. There was a double meaning in those words. At first glance, it sounded as if he meant he would pay a courteous visit, but that was not it. It meant he would storm in and take everything.

If he went so far as to say that, it did not seem he had called him because of money.

“I misunderstood. I apologize.”

“It would be strange if you didn’t misunderstand.”

No. If so, then the present situation was even stranger.

What on earth did the Demonic Cult Leader and this Young Cult Leader want from him?

“Do you know what question people ask me most often?”

Geom Mugeuk answered precisely.

“How can I earn a great deal of money?”

Yong Jamyeong nodded and asked,

“Why do you not ask that?”

Then came an unexpected answer.

“Because the answer will be in your life anyway, Guild Master. That’s why I said I wanted to hear your words.”

Yong Jamyeong himself did not trust the other party’s words. People were beings capable of lying at any time. What he trusted was the other party’s life. When seeing a person, he looked at the road they had walked.

“Are you truly curious about my story? If I talk about the past, it will become embellished, and in the end it will turn into boasting. Other people’s boasting—won’t it be tedious?”

“Boasting is tedious when someone who is not impressive acts impressive. The boasting of someone truly impressive is a magnificent adventure tale.”

Yong Jamyeong’s admiration naturally made him think of bloodline.

‘The Cheonma’s son is indeed his son.’

Otherwise, there was no explaining this intensity that came from such a young age.

“I always try to live while upholding two things.”

“What are they?”

“The first is that I try not to fear death. The second is that I try not to make the pleasure of earning money my highest value.”

“Neither seems to suit the principles of the richest man in the Central Plains. Could you explain in detail?”

The story of his life flowed out naturally.

“The reason I did not make the pleasure of earning money my highest value is because I confirmed that I am someone who feels more pleasure in earning money than in spending it.”

Most successful merchants possessed such a tendency.

“I have seen far too many people become obsessed with the pleasure of earning money and be devoured by that thing called money.”

This thought was likely the reason the name Yong Jamyeong carried a power stronger than money.

“It seems similar to martial arts. When one becomes too deeply obsessed with winning, one can end up being devoured by martial arts.”

“An apt comparison.”

The two drank together. Their cups clinked more forcefully than before.

“Then what is the reason you try not to fear death?”

Yong Jamyeong’s eyes deepened as he recalled that day he could never forget.

“When I was young, I once nearly died. I survived just before death. Ever since that day, I have lived with the thought that my life is a bonus, and that gives me courage in all things.”

“As a martial artist, I find myself interested in the ordeal of life and death you crossed.”

It is an opportunity to fulfill the long-cherished wish that remained your resentment until the day you died. So recall that day well.

“What happened that day?”

Chapter 463: Benefactor, Where Are You and What Are You Doing?
--------------------------------------------------------------
He does not know it on that very day.

Only when the years pass and he looks back does he finally realize.

Ah! That was the day my life changed.

That day had been such a day for Yong Jamyeong as well.

Not merely because he had saved a life on the verge of death, but because his heart toward life had changed.

"At that time, I was always full of confidence."

And rightly so. He was young, smart, and possessed such a gift for commerce that everything he touched succeeded time and again.

"I begrudged even the time I spent sleeping. Money was scattered all around—if I didn't pick it up first, it felt as though someone else would."

In his youth, he had been utterly intoxicated by the thrill of making money. No other emotion could surpass that joy.

"I succeeded time after time. It felt as though the world's fortune in wealth was concentrated solely upon me. Yet I was not arrogant. On the contrary, I was humbler and worked harder. Even so, the misfortune that was bound to come eventually found its way to me."

Yong Jamyeong silently gazed down at his wine cup. In its reflection, he saw the image of his younger self.

* * *

"My lord, you will become the God of Commerce!"

Manso said with a bright smile.

"Don't gild my face for no reason."

Yong Jamyeong shook his head and smiled. His smile, which put those who saw him at ease, had been the same even in his youth.

"It is no mere gilding. You know how many merchants I have met. Mark my words—you will surely become the greatest merchant."

He had known Manso for roughly three or four years. Manso had made his living by doing various jobs around merchants.

He would look up information on goods, arrange workers, or connect wanderers to the merchant group. Put nicely, he was versatile; put poorly, he handled menial tasks. Because he helped with the merchant group's affairs as if they were his own, everyone in the Eunha Merchant Group liked him.

Then one day,

"We've arrived."

The two men entered a small manor.

The reason they had come here today was that Manso was introducing someone to him.

"The merchant group has established its footing, so now we must recruit martial artists. This is no longer a matter to be delayed."

Before Yong Jamyeong had even formed a connection with Baekchong, at this time there had been no martial artists formally affiliated with the merchant group.

Goods transport could be handled through an escort agency, and there were people who had worked with the merchant group from the start. When guards were occasionally needed, he had dealt with the matter by having Manso introduce martial artists for the job.

However, as the merchant group's scale grew, the need for affiliated martial artists grew as well. The value and quantity of the goods they handled had increased. It was not something that could be solved by having sturdy men take turns standing watch in front of a warehouse.

But Yong Jamyeong could not easily come to a decision.

What if those martial artists harbored ill intentions?

That worry never left his heart.

It was due to his nature of not easily trusting others. In the midst of this, he had finally been persuaded by Manso to meet an expert.

"What sort of martial artist are you introducing?"

"He is someone you can trust. If he does not please you, my lord, you need not make a decision."

He figured that if Manso trusted the man, he was trustworthy. Never once so far had Manso disappointed him.

But he never imagined that disappointment would come this day.

The first impression of the awaited martial artist was not favorable. His sharp eyes exuded lust, and he bore an impression that was somehow cunning. Not to mention the scars on his face and his twisted lips.

"It is our first meeting."

The martial artist smirked at Yong Jamyeong's greeting. Seeing that smile, Yong Jamyeong felt displeased.

Yong Jamyeong looked at Manso, who stood beside him.

'This is the trustworthy person you introduced?'

Manso was smiling as always. Just as Yong Jamyeong was feeling that smile seemed strangely unfamiliar today, the martial artist opened his mouth.

"So you're the creditor?"

At those words laden with impure intent, Yong Jamyeong's heart sank.

The martial artist grabbed the wine bottle on the table and drank straight from it, then threw the documents beside it to the floor.

"Sign."

* * *

Yong Jamyeong returned to reality.

"It was the work of that bastard Manso, who had brought that martial artist in. Do you know what truly frightened me then?"

Geom Mugeuk poured wine into his cup and asked.

"What was it?"

"It was that I would die a dog's death."

Geom Mugeuk understood the exact meaning of those words.

"They were after a sum that was not even that large."

Yong Jamyeong nodded.

"The money they tried to seize amounted to merely a few thousand nyang. Of course, to some it might have been a large sum, but to me, who was solidifying the foundation of the merchant group at the time, it was not such a large amount. It must have been a plan to embezzle goods that had arrived at the warehouse. With documents bearing my signature, it would have been possible—after all, Manso had frequently helped with our affairs."

"That bastard Manso should have roughly known your financial situation, shouldn't he?"

"He would have known."

"Why didn't they aim for more?"

"The martial artist who schemed with him was no ordinary person. He was the notorious Yin Blood Ghost of that time."

Yin Blood Ghost.

He was a notorious lecher in the martial arts world at that time. A man who had ravaged and murdered women regardless of age, listed as a public enemy of the martial world by the Martial Arts Alliance.

"At that time, the bastard was urgently fleeing from someone. He had joined hands with Manso right when he was trying to secure funds for his escape. The two had known each other from before."

All of that was revealed later.

"Can you believe it? I had lived a life completely unrelated to lechers, yet I became entangled with one like that."

Geom Mugeuk silently listened to him. For now, his role was simply to listen.

"I asked Manso. Don't you roughly know how much wealth I have? And yet you committed such acts for a mere few thousand nyang?"

As if recalling that moment, Yong Jamyeong's face flushed.

"And so that bastard brazenly smiled and said this: 'Then give me more.'"

Rage gathered on Yong Jamyeong's face.

"I was truly furious. Not angry at him—angry at myself for being deceived by such a scoundrel. He was an unsavory fellow who associated with lechers from the start, yet I had failed to notice even the slightest sign."

"What happened then?"

Yong Jamyeong drank his wine, and Geom Mugeuk refilled it. These were words he could not speak without alcohol.

"First, they beat me to a pulp."

* * *

Thud! Thud!

Under the continuing blows, Yong Jamyeong cried out.

"I'll sign! I will!"

The one beating him was not the Yin Blood Ghost, but Manso.

He ignored Yong Jamyeong and simply rained down blows. Yong Jamyeong realized he was trying to break his spirit first.

Merchants, too, sometimes have to break their counterpart's spirit when making a deal. He had chosen the most extreme and fastest method.

Someone who has never once fought will easily succumb when first exposed to violence. Surprised, in pain, and afraid—'Will I truly be beaten to death?' The heart inevitably collapses in an instant. It was a terror that those who haven't experienced it could never know.

"Please! Stop!"

Yong Jamyeong crouched, covering his head and face.

He despaired. Not because of the beating, but because they had shown their faces and carried out their deeds openly.

'In the end, they will kill me.'

Manso stopped the beating and abruptly pulled Yong Jamyeong to his feet.

"Now, sign."

Yong Jamyeong knew that the moment he signed, he would die.

"I'll give you more money. A truly large amount..."

Thud!

Manso's fist flew in again. A beating more severe than before began. Manso knew Yong Jamyeong well. He knew a smart man would not sign easily. That was why he was trying to beat the defiance out of him.

Even as he screamed, Yong Jamyeong had but one thought.

'I have to live. I won't die at the hands of bastards like these!'

The bastard pulled Yong Jamyeong up again.

Meanwhile, the Yin Blood Ghost was looking out the window. As if being chased by someone, he appeared anxious.

"Hurry up and finish this!"

At his words, Manso urged Yong Jamyeong.

"Sign."

"I'll give you my entire fortune. So please..."

Thud! Thud!

The pain could not overcome the fear of death.

Tears streamed from Yong Jamyeong's eyes. Tears from the pain, and tears from the injustice.

How hard he had lived. Working while cutting back on sleep. He had done his best to treat the people of the merchant group well.

Was the end of that effort to be a death like this?

He shouldn't have trusted people. He had said he didn't.

'You will become the God of Commerce!'

It was the result of being intoxicated by those sweet words. Coming to his senses after being deceived, the situation was already over.

What if it had been a choice between losing all his fortune or not?

Would he have felt somewhat better?

'Please save me.'

He prayed to the heavens. If there was a god of merchants, he prayed to that god as well.

'If you save me! If you save me!'

He hadn't even finished his promise when the door opened.

* * *

With an ecstatic face, Yong Jamyeong spoke.

"That man entered through there."

Geom Mugeuk recalled that moment. The image of his father in his youth.

"When that man entered, I felt the air in the room change."

Yong Jamyeong closed his eyes and recalled that time. If the earlier memory was pain, from here on it was a memory of joy and longing.

"How should I describe that man?"

Watching Yong Jamyeong agonize over it, Geom Mugeuk smiled.

"It would not be easy to describe from such a brief meeting."

"First, I thought he was handsome."

"More than me?"

At Geom Mugeuk's question, Yong Jamyeong nodded firmly.

"You seem to have forgotten who I am for a moment."

"I apologize, but he was my savior."

"I shall take it that my debt of gratitude has increased."

At his jest, Yong Jamyeong smiled. Now that it was a pleasant story, he was at ease.

"He was handsome, yet gave off a powerful feeling. Cold somehow, and haughty. I don't know how to express him."

The sight of my father in his youth—I too want to see it terribly. To the point that I wish I had been there then.

"The moment he saw him, the Yin Blood Ghost trembled in terror. That was the first time I had ever seen such extreme terror on a person's face. I realized that the reason the bastard had been anxious the entire time he was there was because of that man. And what followed was instantaneous. A brilliant light flashed, and that lecherous bastard was shattered beyond recognition, collapsing into pieces. As a martial artist, you might enjoy such tales, but in truth, my eyes could see nothing."

Seeing him smashed to pieces, it seemed Father had been enraged. He must have raped and murdered a woman from the Heavenly Demon Cult.

"So what did you do?"

"At first, I froze and couldn't say a word. When that man looked this way, I shouted. Please save me. I am not one of these bastards."

Yong Jamyeong returned again to that day, to that very spot.

* * *

He still remembered it. That gaze which had looked at him.

The gaze was asking for a reason. Why should I believe you? Why should I save you?

Yong Jamyeong had an intuition that his life or death hinged on his answer. What should he say? Though his mind pondered, his mouth was already speaking. He hoped his instincts were working properly.

"That man who tried to kill me always said it. That I would become the God of Commerce. I want to achieve that."

He could not know why he said those words. They had escaped him without his awareness.

The man did not handle matters by feeling alone. He went to the table and looked at the contract placed there. From the contract, he confirmed that it was a situation where they were trying to force a signature.

This time, the man looked at Manso.

"He is lying. He is..."

The man waited, but the next words did not come. Manso possessed the eloquence to concoct plausible words even in such situations, but today was different.

The moment he met the gaze of the man silently staring at him, strangely, words failed him.

He had lived his entire life in hypocrisy and lies, but in this final moment, he could not utter a single lie and died truthfully.

With a flash of light, his body was bisected.

"Please tell me the name of my benefactor."

But the man left that place without a word.

"I will become the greatest merchant under heaven and surely repay you!"

He made to that man the promise he had failed to make to the heavens.

It was regrettable. If only he had said a word to boast of his deed, or at least smiled at him.

Just as he had come without warning, he left in the same manner.

"That is what happened that day."

Geom Mugeuk raised his wine cup to him. Yong Jamyeong toasted his cup against it. The two men drank in silence.

For a moment, they thought of the same person.

“You kept your promise to become the greatest merchant under heaven.”

“But he does not know that, does he?”

That was what he found so regrettable. He wanted to meet that man and tell him.

Do you remember me? I am the one you saved back then. Just as I told you then, I became the greatest merchant.

If only he could say those words, he felt he would have no lingering regrets. It was something he could have simply brushed off with, “I was lucky,” and moved on, yet for some reason, that man kept coming to mind. To the point that he felt as though they were bound together by the chains of fate.

“I tried every possible means to find my benefactor, but I could not. There was no one who had seen him. At times, I even think this: was it all a dream?”

It was so regrettable. Year after year, he was growing older like this. That man, too, must have aged. How had he changed? What was he doing with his life?

“Do you happen to have a portrait of him?”

At that, Yong Jamyeong took a sheet of paper from inside his robes. He took it out with such care, as though it might crumble at the slightest touch.

“I always carry it with me.”

The paper, folded carefully inside a silk cloth, was old and discolored, its edges looking as though they might tear at any moment.

“This is the original that was first drawn back then. I had hundreds of copies made and was about to use them to search for him, but I stopped. I thought it might cause him trouble.”

Geom Mugeuk looked at the face drawn on the old paper. It was so faded that someone seeing it for the first time would not be able to recognize it, but Geom Mugeuk saw his father within it.

Father.

So this is how I come to see you in your youth.

“I can no longer remember his face clearly.”

He had thought he would never forget it. No, he had been certain he would never forget it. But like the paper on which that portrait was drawn, as the years passed, it had gradually faded away.

Yong Jamyeong watched Geom Mugeuk as he gazed at the portrait.

In his deep, clear eyes, he saw longing.

‘Why is he looking at it like that?’

The next moment, Yong Jamyeong flinched.

The face of the man that had been growing indistinct, as though shrouded in mist, began to surface.

It was a face that had only grown hazier of late, yet now it came to mind as clearly as if that incident had happened yesterday.

‘Benefactor!’

When that face surfaced so vividly, he was overwhelmed with joy. It felt so lifelike, as though the man were standing right before him.

‘Why on earth?’

What did the reason matter? What mattered was this joyful feeling.

‘I have grown this old, yet you remain the same. Benefactor, where are you now, and what are you doing? You are not suffering because you have no money, are you? I have plenty of money. Hurry and come find me, and tell me to repay the kindness from back then!’

Then, without thinking, he looked at Geom Mugeuk.

“!”

Goosebumps rose all over his body. It was because he felt that the benefactor from back then and Geom Mugeuk before him now gave off a similar impression. As though that very man from that time were sitting in front of him now. Though their appearances were certainly not that similar.

At that very moment!

“……Surely not?”

Over the face of his benefactor in youth, another person’s face began to overlap.

The face of the person who had summoned him here.

The face of the absolute ruler of this era.

And so, the face of the Demonic Cult Leader, Geom Ujin, crossed the long years and overlapped with the face of his young benefactor.

The two faces became exactly one.

Absolute Regression, Chapter 464

Chapter 464: The Son Who Is Greed Incarnate.
--------------------------------------------
“Benefactor!”

Yong Jamyeong sprang to his feet. A thrilling shudder ran through his entire body. Never in his life had he been so shocked.

‘My benefactor was the Demonic Cult Leader?’

His heart pounded wildly. Of all people, the Demonic Cult Leader? It was truly unbelievable.

‘Could I be mistaken?’

But he was not. The figure of his benefactor he had seen that day and the figure of the Heavenly Demon he had met today fit together perfectly, as if they were one person. They were most certainly the same man.

Geom Mugeuk, who had been looking at the portrait sketch, looked up at Yong Jamyeong.

Geom Mugeuk could tell. Yong Jamyeong had realized that the person who had saved him in the past was his father.

Yes, there was no way he would fail to recognize someone like Father.

Geom Mugeuk carefully folded the paper on which his father in his youth was drawn and wrapped it back in silk. Then he held it out to Yong Jamyeong again.

“I have seen it well.”

But Yong Jamyeong did not take it.

“Young Cult Leader, you should keep it.”

Geom Mugeuk pretended not to know and asked him.

“Why are you giving this to me? This drawing must be precious to you, Guild Master.”

Then came an answer that told him his guess had been exactly right.

“From now on, it may become something even more precious to you, Young Cult Leader. Now, let us return to the residence.”

* * *

Geom Woojin was drinking tea in his quarters.

“Father, we have returned.”

Geom Mugeuk and Yong Jamyeong stepped inside.

Geom Woojin sensed that the way Yong Jamyeong looked at him had changed from before. He knew full well where the intense emotion filling those eyes came from, but Geom Woojin feigned ignorance and asked his son.

“Did you have a good talk with the Guild Master?”

“Is there any conversation more interesting than one about money? On top of that, I even heard stories about the Guild Master’s younger days.”

Geom Woojin looked at Yong Jamyeong and spoke calmly.

“How was it for you, Guild Master?”

“It was a time more precious than any other.”

Ever since entering the room, Yong Jamyeong had been staring only at Geom Woojin’s face. When they had met earlier, such a thing would have been absolutely unthinkable.

“Cult Leader.”

Yong Jamyeong’s voice trembled. In the process of raising the Eunha Merchant Guild to the very top, he had gone through all manner of things, but never had he trembled as much as he did at this moment.

“Do you remember me?”

His father looked at him in silence for a moment.

From that reaction, Geom Mugeuk could tell.

‘Father knew.’

Thinking about it, of course he would have known. Yong Jamyeong could never have imagined that the person who saved him was the Heavenly Demon, but his father was different.

When he had heard what happened that day from Yong Jamyeong, he had said that Father had seen the contract that Eumhyeolgwi and Manso had tried to force him to sign.

At that time, Father must have realized that the person he had saved was the young guild master of the Eunha Merchant Guild.

Since that day, the Eunha Merchant Guild had grown while maintaining balance among the demonic, orthodox, and unorthodox factions.

If he had wanted to, Father could have revealed that he was the one who had saved him and turned the Eunha Merchant Guild into a merchant guild that followed the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult, but Father had never revealed that fact at all. He must surely have received reports that Yong Jamyeong was desperately searching for the benefactor who had saved him.

The richest man in the world had been searching to repay a debt of gratitude, yet he had not contacted him?

How very like Father. How could one not respect him?

Yong Jamyeong asked about that day long ago.

“By any chance, do you remember the day in your youth when you punished Eumhyeolgwi, who had been committing evil deeds?”

Even at his trembling question, Geom Woojin still gave no answer.

Rather, because of that, Yong Jamyeong became certain that the benefactor from that day was the Cult Leader before his eyes. If he had not been, he would have said, “I have never done such a thing,” or reacted with, “I do not remember.”

But Geom Woojin merely listened to him without the slightest change in expression.

And those eyes gazing at him in silence.

‘Those are the eyes.’

They were unmistakably the eyes from that day. Now that he thought about it, even on the day he had saved him, the man had not said a single word. He had left behind only those eyes.

“That day, Cult Leader, you saved me when I was being held there. If not for you, I would have died that day.”

It would have been an unbearably unjust death. It was not as if all his wealth had been stolen; he would have lost his life because one warehouse had been plundered for a lecher’s escape funds. Even in death, he probably would not have been able to close his eyes.

Yong Jamyeong slowly prostrated himself on the floor and offered a bow.

“First, please accept my bow.”

He bowed with utmost sincerity. Since the Eunha Merchant Guild had risen to the highest position, had he ever lowered his head to anyone?

“To think I only meet you now… I truly wished to see you all these years.”

In truth, until now, he had been bewildered, afraid before the Heavenly Demon, and still held a sliver of doubt that perhaps it might not be him. His feelings had been indescribably complicated.

It was right then.

Geom Woojin rose from his seat and walked over to him.

Then he bent down before him, took Yong Jamyeong by the arm, and helped him up.

Geom Mugeuk, watching, was startled. He had never imagined that his father would help him rise.

Then how shocked must Yong Jamyeong have been?

But his shock began now.

“I remember you.”

“!”

At that single sentence that flew into his heart and lodged there, Yong Jamyeong’s eyes widened.

‘It really is the Cult Leader! He remembered me!’

His entire body trembled. The person holding his arm and helping him up was the one who had saved his life, and at the same time, the Heavenly Demon of this era.

“Then did you know it was me from the beginning?”

Geom Woojin nodded.

“You knew that I was the one you saved back then!”

Then, from Geom Woojin’s mouth came the words Yong Jamyeong had wished to hear all his life.

“I have been hearing news of your successive victories in the world of commerce.”

“Ah! You knew!”

Yong Jamyeong’s heart swelled, and his throat tightened.

He did not ask why he had not come to find him. Was the Heavenly Demon an ordinary person? Just because the person he had saved had become the owner of the greatest merchant guild under heaven, would he be someone who went there to boast of his favor? What mattered now was this.

‘My benefactor remembered me. He knew that I became the greatest merchant in the world.’

Only then did true joy surge in. The clear, moving relief of having everything revealed with certainty wrapped around Yong Jamyeong’s entire body.

Most of all, what made him glad was that the years past had not simply flowed away in vain. He knew that he had kept his promise. Because the man had been watching his life.

Geom Woojin gave him a precious sentence.

“You have endured much.”

At those words, he could bear it no longer.

Tears welled in Yong Jamyeong’s eyes.

He had lived a ruthless life without showing tears, but he could not suppress the passion surging up from deep within his heart.

No matter how much praise he received from those around him, a corner of his heart had always felt empty. Yes, now he knew for certain that it had been because he wanted this man’s acknowledgment.

“…I truly wished to see you.”

You cannot know how much I wanted to tell you that I kept the promise I made that day.

“Now I have no regrets, even if I die.”

In the end, tears streamed from Yong Jamyeong’s eyes.

He had two long-cherished wishes.

To become the owner of the greatest merchant guild under heaven, and to find his benefactor and repay his grace.

Now was the moment he fulfilled the second wish.

Yong Jamyeong took something from his robes. The thing he had carefully kept was an ornate key.

At a glance, it was no ordinary key. It was a key made of ten-thousand-year cold iron. The object itself possessed tremendous value.

“It is the key to a secret vault where I gathered the things I intended to give you once I found my benefactor.”

Yong Jamyeong offered it respectfully to Geom Woojin.

His father refused at once.

“No repayment is necessary.”

The reason for his refusal was also very much like Father.

“The heart with which you have tried to find me until now has been enough repayment.”

Because of that, Yong Jamyeong could give it to him with even greater pleasure.

“Because you are the Cult Leader, my heart is actually at ease in giving this to you. After all, you are someone who can obtain anything you desire in this world. So please accept it comfortably. To you, Cult Leader, these are insignificant things.”

Just from this alone, Geom Mugeuk could tell that Yong Jamyeong was no ordinary person.

How many people would try to repay a debt of gratitude for so many long years? Moreover, someone who had prepared like this for the day they might meet again would be even rarer.

Because he was such a person, he must have been able to achieve such great success.

“If Father does not want it, I will accept it in his stead.”

When Geom Mugeuk moved to take it, his father accepted the key one step faster.

“No, I said give it to me. Your son, the incarnation of material greed, is right here!”

With an expression that said there was no chance of that, Geom Woojin placed the key in his robes.

Watching this, Yong Jamyeong smiled faintly. He could tell that Geom Mugeuk had deliberately stepped in to help Geom Woojin accept the key.

“If you go below Yuhua Mountain in Chongqing and find a herb gatherer named Zhong Song, he will guide you to the secret vault.”

“I will gladly accept your sincerity.”

Geom Mugeuk looked at Geom Woojin. Chongqing was between Shaanxi and the main cult anyway. They could stop by on the way there or back.

What could be in the secret vault opened by that key? Judging by the atmosphere, it seemed to contain gifts prepared over a long period of time.

‘Father, I really do seem far more worldly than you. I want to run there right now and see what is inside.’

Then again, as the master of the Heavenly Demon Treasury, what would Father be curious about?

And the greatest gift to Father was probably not that key.

It was the fact that Yong Jamyeong had learned Father was his benefactor.

Until now, the Eunha Merchant Guild had walked a tightrope well among the demonic, orthodox, and unorthodox factions, but now it had become a Eunha Merchant Guild that followed the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.

Yong Jamyeong did not deliberately state such resolve aloud. Just as Geom Woojin had not come to him and demanded something, he did not reveal his heart with words.

In the future, he would still operate the merchant guild as if he were walking a line among the demonic, orthodox, and unorthodox factions, but he would treat Father with a deep loyalty unknown to anyone.

Geom Mugeuk looked at his father.

‘Father, did you perhaps foresee this result? Is that why you introduced me to this man? Because you hoped that man’s loyalty would be passed on to me.’

After all, his words that he would introduce him had been truly unexpected.

Maintaining a good relationship with Yong Jamyeong would be a great strength in leading the cult. When Father introduced him, he had said this to Yong Jamyeong.

—It will not be such a bad thing for you either.

Because he hoped that the connection between Yong Jamyeong and him would continue. Because he knew this situation would come to pass.

“Father, would you take a look at this?”

Geom Mugeuk took out the paper with the portrait sketch he had received from Yong Jamyeong and showed it to his father.

“This is you from back then.”

His father rarely revealed his feelings, but at this unexpected image of his own youth, a strange light flashed through his eyes.

Yes, how could Father not miss his past youth?

“Guild Master Yong seems to have mistakenly remembered Father as more handsome than I am.”

At that, his father said firmly,

“As expected of the greatest merchant, his memory is excellent.”

“He may have presbyopia.”

At the exchange between the two, Yong Jamyeong laughed and looked at the faded drawing.

“I miss those days so much.”

For a long while, his father’s gaze did not leave the drawing.

A moment later, Geom Mugeuk received the drawing back from his father and folded it carefully.

“This is mine. I may yield that key, but not this.”

A faint smile formed at the corners of Geom Woojin’s mouth. To Geom Mugeuk, it was a smile more precious than the treasures of a secret vault.

* * *

That night, Yong Jamyeong was walking in the front yard.

His heart was restless, and sleep would not come.

“Can you not sleep?”

When he turned, Geom Mugeuk was walking out.

“Perhaps because my heart is stirred up, I cannot sleep.”

The look with which he regarded Geom Mugeuk was incomparably gentler than when they had first met.

Now, with his mind much more at ease, Yong Jamyeong asked,

“May I ask why the Cult Leader summoned me this time?”

He clearly felt that he had been called for reasons unrelated to the past.

Geom Mugeuk revealed the reason they had come here.

“The reason Father and I came here this time was because of the Golden Dragon Family.”

“As expected.”

Yong Jamyeong was not surprised at all, as if he already knew something.

“We also obtained information that the Golden Dragon Family has recently been operating a secret organization in concealment, and have been keeping an eye on them.”

The information wars waged in the commercial world were no less fierce than those of murim. As the greatest merchant guild under heaven, their intelligence on rival guilds would surely be extraordinary.

“Yes. We detected signs that the money earned through that organization was flowing into a murim organization that threatens murim, so we came to investigate.”

And where would their intelligence be limited to the commercial world alone?

“The tripartite meeting was connected to them as well, wasn’t it?”

At Yong Jamyeong’s low question, Geom Mugeuk nodded.

“It seems the woman we saw during the day is the one leading that secret organization.”

“So that woman was the reason you called me here.”

“That’s right. They will consider our meeting with you, Guildmaster, to be major intelligence. The leader of the Eunha Merchant Guild came in person, after all. I intend to use that to enter the heart of the Golden Dragon Family.”

“Is there anything more I can do to help?”

“You have already helped enough simply by coming in person.”

“I would like to help more.”

He was clearly reluctant to leave just like this.

“It may be dangerous.”

“Do you think I only walked safe roads in building the greatest merchant guild in the Central Plains?”

Yong Jamyeong did not want to return like this. How long had it been since he had met his benefactor again? He wanted to spend even a little more time with him. If he truly were not the Heavenly Demon, Yong Jamyeong would have asked him to come with him to the guild and wanted to stay with him for a month, even a year.

“Very well. Then please stay three more days and help me. It will be of great assistance to me.”

Yong Jamyeong’s expression brightened radiantly.

In truth, Geom Mugeuk was confident he could deal with that woman and create an opening even without Yong Jamyeong.

That was why telling him to stay was consideration for Yong Jamyeong. He fully understood the desire to spend a little more time with one’s father.

You waited for decades, so enjoy three more days.

As befitted someone who had lived as a merchant for a long time, Yong Jamyeong was quick-witted.

“Thank you.”

“What do you mean?”

“I know this decision was made for my sake.”

Geom Mugeuk answered with a smile.

“To be honest, it is for my sake. When else would I ever get to spend time with the greatest merchant under heaven?”

Yong Jamyeong wanted to say this to him.

‘From now on, as much as you like.’

Because he was no longer the Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult, but the son of his benefactor.

The fact that his benefactor was the Heavenly Demon made him even happier. It made him feel that he had not become the owner of the greatest merchant guild under heaven simply through good fortune.

It felt as though he had become another destiny created by a great destiny.

“Now then, just say the word. What should I do now?”

Three days with the Heavenly Demon and the Young Cult Leader—this would be a time he would never forget for the rest of his life. As if to prove that already, the Young Cult Leader before him asked with a smile.

“Pardon me, but do you have some money?”

Absolute Regression, Chapter 465

Chapter 465: We Must Find Out the Amount.
-----------------------------------------
Had he ever laughed this heartily in recent years?

Yong Jamyeong truly laughed out loud.

Did he have some money?

Asking such a question of the richest man in the Central Plains was no different from asking the leader of the Demon Cult, “Are you any good at martial arts?” And the person making such a joke was the Young Cult Leader of the present Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.

While building up the Eunha Merchant Guild, he had always been cautious of martial artists. Among them, he had been most wary and guarded against the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult. If something happened, he had felt negotiations would not work with them. They were like a being that would surge in like a tidal wave.

Then new news from the Demon Cult reached him.

This young man standing right before his eyes. It was news about the Demon Cult’s Young Cult Leader.

The information that came up in secret was truly astonishing. Stories so difficult to believe they seemed unreal.

Even as he heard one report after another that the Young Cult Leader was different from the demonic practitioners of the past, he had thought, Different? How different could he be? In the end, a demonic practitioner was still a demonic practitioner.

And yet, the Young Cult Leader he had met in person was different. How different could he be? As one could see, he was far too different. He was someone even more extraordinary than the rumors said.

“How much will you need?”

At Yong Jamyeong’s question, Geom Mugeuk smiled and said,

“The more, the better.”

“That is the one thing I can do well.”

At his modest words, Geom Mugeuk said something unexpected.

“However, you don’t actually need to give it to us.”

“What does that mean?”

“You only need to pretend to give it to us.”

Yong Jamyeong guessed Geom Mugeuk’s intent.

It must mean they only needed to leak information that the Eunha Merchant Guild had given a huge sum to the Seodo Sect.

“And the question of how much it will take is something we should hear not from you, Guild Master, but from the other side.”

Yong Jamyeong was impressed by Geom Mugeuk. It had been only moments ago that he had asked to be allowed to remain here. That was why they had gained three days.

In other words, in that brief instant, Geom Mugeuk had devised some sort of plan.

The most extraordinary Young Cult Leader in history.

He was certainly showing himself worthy of that rumor.

“Young Cult Leader.”

“Yes, Guild Master.”

Now it was time to offer him his thanks formally.

“Thank you.”

If not for Geom Mugeuk, he would never have realized that Geom Ujin was his benefactor.

If Geom Mugeuk had not asked to hear the old story, if he had not asked to see the portrait sketch, if he had not seen Geom Ujin’s likeness in Geom Mugeuk’s appearance.

He would never have known that Geom Ujin was his benefactor.

Though he knew Yong Jamyeong meant it in that way, Geom Mugeuk smiled and said,

“When I’m with the richest man in the world, I ask for money and still get thanked for it.”

Yong Jamyeong liked Geom Mugeuk. The words of the Demon Cult Leader—that it would not be such a bad thing for him either—now made perfect sense. No, it was more than not bad. If he continued to form a good relationship with a Young Cult Leader like this, it would be a great help not only to himself but to the Eunha Merchant Guild as well.

Perhaps that was why these words came out so naturally.

“Those three days will begin from tomorrow.”

Even a single day was precious to him.

* * *

“The Eunha Merchant Guild is plotting something.”

At Geum Arin’s words, her personal guard, Im Hyeok, silently nodded.

The fact that the Guild Master of the Eunha Merchant Guild had appeared here in person was proof of that. For now, circumstantially, it looked as though the Eunha Merchant Guild and the Seodo Sect were joining hands.

“The Seodo Sect are not ordinary people.”

Im Hyeok recalled Hwi, who had appeared after releasing his concealment.

It was the first time he had seen such incredible concealment arts. It was concealment that had truly reached the pinnacle, something only one who had learned concealment arts could recognize.

At that moment, the words he had spoken came to mind.

—Do you wish to protect your master? Then stop. Your thoughts, your actions. And close your mouth.

He had meant that Im Hyeok should not act rashly in that manor.

Yet somehow, he kept thinking it was a warning that ought to be applied to this entire matter.

“That must be why the Guild Master of the Eunha Merchant Guild came in person.”

The man called Geom Yeon was no ordinary person, and neither were the figures shown by his father and uncle. In particular, the presence his father had displayed was difficult to describe in words.

“If they’ve joined hands, then in the end, my subordinates were done in by the Eunha Merchant Guild.”

That was a different matter from simply getting caught in a dispute with a murim sect by chance. It meant the Eunha Merchant Guild might have been behind this incident. Of course, the possibility was slim.

If that had been the case, they would not have let them leave the manor so easily that day.

“The clan will contact us.”

They had already reported to the clan that the Guild Master of the Eunha Merchant Guild had moved on his own.

“We can’t just sit and wait until they contact us.”

Before that, the situation here might already be over.

She had to step forward and find out herself. What sort of relationship the Eunha Merchant Guild and the Seodo Sect had, and why the Guild Master of the Eunha Merchant Guild had come here in person.

“What do you intend to do?”

“I need to find out how things are unfolding through that man called Geom Yeon.”

Im Hyeok looked at her with concern. She had done well in every task she had taken on so far. For someone so young, she certainly had exceptional qualities. But this time, his instincts did not feel good.

Geum Arin sensed Im Hyeok’s concern.

“I know. He isn’t an easy opponent.”

She thought of Geom Mugeuk. When she thought of him, the first image that came to mind was him smiling and joking. She knew. Since ancient times, it was the strong who smiled.

That smile of Geom Mugeuk’s came from composure. And that composure came from strength.

Just then, a subordinate came and reported.

“Geom Yeon and the Guild Master of the Eunha Merchant Guild have moved.”

“Just the two of them?”

“Yes, that is correct.”

“Where?”

“To the Daeryuk Money House.”

Geum Arin immediately guessed the reason.

If a merchant and a martial artist went to a money house, what other reason could there be? The Eunha Merchant Guild must have handed money over to the Seodo Sect.

Geum Arin ordered her subordinate,

“Find out discreetly how much money changed hands. You must learn the amount.”

* * *

That afternoon, Geum Arin came to Geom Mugeuk’s residence.

“What brings you here?”

“I came because I have something to say to you.”

“Come in.”

“It’s stuffy. Let’s walk for a bit.”

Geum Arin refused to enter the house. She knew that the Guild Master of the Eunha Merchant Guild was still staying here. On top of that, this man’s father was there as well. Inside was a tiger’s den.

Geom Mugeuk readily accepted her suggestion.

“Very well, let’s do that.”

It was precisely because of this attitude. For some reason, it made her feel she could find something out from this man.

She had to use this man’s confidence. She had to stick a straw into that confidence and suck out only the humility inside it. Once humility disappeared from confidence, it became arrogance.

The two slowly made their way toward the marketplace. She walked a little distance away from Geom Mugeuk.

“Why are you walking so far away?”

At Geom Mugeuk’s question, Geum Arin answered,

“Have you forgotten? The last time we met, we were trying to kill each other.”

“Let’s be precise. It wasn’t each other. It was you trying to kill me.”

“The blood of my subordinates is probably still on your sword.”

“They tried to kill me first.”

“You survived, didn’t you?”

She was cautious with each word and focused her entire mind. She had never dealt with this type of person before. Even if she made a single joke, she had to keep her wits about her.

“But there’s one thing I find strange.”

“What is strange?”

“As you know, I ran into the Guild Master of the Eunha Merchant Guild that day. Then the martial artists of his guild should have investigated me afterward, yet there has been no movement at all.”

It was a probing remark to find out whether the Eunha Merchant Guild knew of her existence.

In reality, the Guild Master of the Eunha Merchant Guild’s protection was so airtight that she knew if some unknown person made contact with him, they would always investigate that person afterward.

“I told them about you first.”

“What did you say?”

“You heard it that day, didn’t you? That you were a woman blessed with good fortune when it came to food.”

When Geum Arin looked dumbfounded, Geom Mugeuk smiled and added,

“I said you were someone with a personal connection to me. So, out of consideration for me, they will not investigate you.”

Geum Arin stopped walking for a moment. Naturally, Geom Mugeuk also stopped.

“We are ill-fated.”

She deliberately did not say anything nice to Geom Mugeuk. He had said she had a personal connection with him. In other words, it meant he bore goodwill toward her. At times like this, it was better to treat him coldly instead.

Sure enough, the other party did not take it as an ill-fated relationship.

“Isn’t it still too early to know that? Our meeting is not over yet.”

The two began walking again. She quietly inserted the straw into him and took one sip of humility.

“For the Guild Master of the Eunha Merchant Guild to come in person... You must be more impressive than I thought.”

At that, Geom Mugeuk looked at her with a peculiar smile. That gaze at times like this felt as though it saw straight through her heart.

“I think you are the more impressive one.”

“What do you mean?”

“You came to see such an impressive person without fear, did you not?”

Such an impressive person?

It seemed her earlier words had properly sucked away some humility.

“Why did you come to see me?”

“To be honest, dealing with someone like you is burdensome for me too. But since this has happened, it must be concluded one way or another.”

While they spoke, the two entered the marketplace.

Geom Mugeuk was the first to make a suggestion.

“Let’s have a drink.”

It was exactly the suggestion she had been hoping for, but she pushed back once. If she wanted to draw information from him, she must not show impatience. She had to approach this strategically to the end.

“We are not on terms where we can sit across from each other and drink.”

This was also her confidence.

A woman’s confidence that a man would not refuse just because she pushed back like this. The men she had dealt with until now could be handled with a sword, so she simply had never needed this charm.

“That does not mean we are on terms where we cannot drink, does it? Come, let’s go.”

Geom Mugeuk took the lead and entered an inn.

A faint smile formed on Geum Arin’s lips as she followed him in. So far, the plan was going smoothly.

“There are plenty of other taverns, yet you seem to like this place.”

“Did I not meet you here? It is a meaningful place for us.”

At the unexpected words, Geum Arin flinched.

She stared at Geom Mugeuk, but she could not tell whether he truly thought so or whether he was saying it like a joke.

“Bring us wine.”

Geom Mugeuk ordered one dish for her and one dish for himself.

After drinking a cup of wine, Geom Mugeuk asked with a curious expression,

“Let me ask you one thing. Where do you belong?”

“Why do you think I belong somewhere?”

“To be doing this kind of work...”

Geom Mugeuk cut himself off for a moment, and Geum Arin supplied the rest.

“You think I’m too young.”

At that, Geom Mugeuk shook his head.

“No. I was going to say you are not someone suited to this kind of work.”

“What kind of person is suited to this kind of work?”

“Someone who can seize another person’s family business without batting an eye. Someone who does not hesitate to kill those who try to stop it.”

The corner of Geum Arin’s mouth curled up slightly as he struck the mark. Quick-witted as she was, there was no way she would fail to understand.

‘He’s saying that to me.’

He said she was not suited to it, but in the end, he was rebuking her.

Though she was inwardly angry, she pretended not to notice and changed the subject. She had not come to fight, but to extract information.

“Your father truly seems remarkable. I had thought that a martial artist would have to be the leader of one of the major sects to have a private meeting with the Guild Master of the Eunha Merchant Guild.”

She deliberately praised his father. There was no better way to strip humility from someone than by provoking jealousy.

Sure enough, it was as she expected.

“I was the one who proposed calling the Eunha Merchant Guild.”

“For what reason did you call them?”

As if it were not some great secret, Geom Mugeuk told her,

“The Eunha Merchant Guild was secretly looking for an expert. An expert whose name was not known in the murim. In that regard, my father was perfect. After all, it has truly been a long time since he entered the murim.”

It was not a lie. His father truly had emerged into the murim after a very long time.

At the unexpected major piece of information, Geum Arin’s mind began turning rapidly.

‘The Eunha Merchant Guild is secretly looking for experts? Why on earth?’

The Eunha Merchant Guild was already filled with countless experts.

As if he had seen through her thoughts, Geom Mugeuk said,

“I was curious as to why they needed experts in secret, so I asked them directly.”

Geum Arin waited for his answer, inwardly tense.

But Geom Mugeuk did not answer, instead drawing it out. He called the waiter and ordered more wine, and also ordered a new dish.

As though he had forgotten what he was saying, he drank and shifted the topic to talk about the food.

In the end, after a few cups of wine, she asked,

“Why did the Eunha Merchant Guild say they were looking for experts?”

“Ah, did I not tell you?”

Because he unfolded the conversation in this way, her suspicion—Why is this man telling me so easily?—was dispersed.

“They said there is an organization moving secretly in the merchant world. They called them people who ignore commercial ethics and disturb the order. They asked us to eliminate them.”

“!”

Geum Arin was truly shocked, but she tried hard to remain calm. That secret organization was undoubtedly her own organization. She had expected the Eunha Merchant Guild to notice someday, but she had not expected them to respond like this.

“Then why are they bringing in outside forces instead of dealing with it themselves?”

“That is obvious.”

Geom Mugeuk spoke confidently, as though he had seen even that far.

“They must intend to take over the empty space left after eliminating them.”

“That can’t be. They are the greatest merchant guild under heaven.”

After emptying his wine cup, Geom Mugeuk said,

“That is precisely how they became the greatest merchant guild under heaven.”

After a brief silence, Geum Arin asked,

“Why are you telling me such secrets so readily?”

“Because you seemed curious. At this point, may we call this a connection rather than an ill-fated relationship?”

Geom Mugeuk stared intently at Geum Arin.

She simply could not understand this man’s true thoughts. In any case, whether it was true or not could be confirmed again later. And since words were flowing so smoothly now, she thought she had to ask everything she needed to ask.

“How much did they offer you?”

If Geom Mugeuk’s words were true, she had to stop them from joining hands. Otherwise, they would suffer losses far greater than the money used to hire these people. No, more than that, her own life would be in danger.

Geom Mugeuk, who had been unreserved until now, held back his answer this time.

“Why do you ask?”

“If I give you more money, you could come over to our side.”

Geom Mugeuk laughed openly.

“I apologize. I was not mocking you.”

It must have looked as though she were trying to deal with the richest man under heaven.
