Listen, we are in the top three too.

“Tell me. How much will it take?”

Absolute Regression Chapter 466

Chapter 466: But Why Does It Feel Like We Lost?
-----------------------------------------------
Geom Mugeuk smiled as he looked at Geum Arin.

“I apologize for smiling again.”

Though he spoke an apology, the smile was unmistakably meant to provoke her. She failed to realize that every careless remark, every single smile, contained Geom Mugeuk's calculation.

“I’m not playing around.”

When her expression turned grave, Geom Mugeuk finally wiped the smile from his face.

“Do you ask sincerely?”

“Yes, I am sincere.”

From Geom Mugeuk’s mouth as he stared at her came an unexpected sum.

“Thirty thousand nyang.”

Geum Arin asked back with a puzzled expression.

“Did you say thirty thousand nyang?”

Had they sold themselves for a mere thirty thousand nyang?

Geum Arin thought these martial artists—strong only in martial arts and ignorant of worldly affairs—had been deceived by the Eunha Sangdan. Learning this fact in advance was a heaven-sent opportunity.

“Did they say you were worth no more than thirty thousand nyang? You’ve been deceived by them!”

Geom Mugeuk didn’t waver in the slightest; instead, a strange smile crossed his face.

From that expression, Geum Arin realized she had misunderstood something.

“Then you didn’t contract with them for thirty thousand nyang.”

“Of course not.”

“Then what is the thirty thousand nyang?”

“It means that if you wish to hear how much they offered, you must put up thirty thousand nyang.”

Geum Arin’s face hardened in an instant.

Meanwhile, Geom Mugeuk calmly explained the reason for the thirty thousand nyang.

“You spoke as though you could offer more money than the Eunha Sangdan. Should you not first show me you can produce thirty thousand nyang as the price of a question? Only then could I trust you, could I not?”

Geum Arin was at a loss for words. She could have argued that thirty thousand nyang was too much for a single question, or she could have lost her temper and said he should simply refuse if he didn’t want to answer.

But that would have been an unseemly reaction. To try to obtain such important information without any price was wrong from the very start.

The man before her seemed so approachable, yet he was truly not to be trifled with. To see how calmly he drove people into a corner.

As if to say, “Now, now, let’s just drink,” Geom Mugeuk emptied his cup. As she watched him, Geum Arin sent a telepathic message to Im Hyeok.

‘—Go to the field and bring thirty thousand nyang.’

‘—Miss, I’m investigating the Continental Battlefield anyway, so I’ll find out the amount.’

‘—I know. This isn’t payment for the question; it’s payment to show your mettle. Bring it.’

‘—Understood.’

Shortly after, Im Hyeok arrived and handed her a promissory note.

He had brought thirty thousand nyang from the field. The funds for operating the secret organization were supported by the Geumnyong Sega. That money could not be used freely without permission, so what she had here was entirely her personal fortune.

“Here. Thirty thousand nyang.”

Geom Mugeuk apologized with a remorseful expression.

“I seem to have wounded your pride. My apologies.”

“It’s not a matter of pride.”

“Then what is it?”

“It’s to show my sincerity in wanting to bring you to my side.”

Her instincts whispered to make this man her ally.

Because she felt a sense of crisis that if this utterly unreadable person became an enemy, no expert of the Sega could stand against them.

Because he was the sort of man who would smile so comfortably and then plunge a blade into her, saying, “It can’t be helped, can it?” and kill her.

“Now tell me.”

Geom Mugeuk, who had been quietly watching her, revealed the figure.

“Two million nyang.”

Truly, an astronomical sum.

“If you wish to hire us, you must give us two million nyang.”

It was a startling amount, yet Geum Arin wasn’t surprised, as if she had expected as much.

“You are not surprised.”

“Because I believe you are worth that much.”

She did her utmost to humor Geom Mugeuk.

“Did the Eunha Sangdan offer two million nyang? Or is that what you demanded?”

“No. The money received from the Eunha Sangdan was one million nyang.”

“Then why two million nyang?”

“To break our deal with them and make a new deal with you, wouldn’t we need the justification to ask for double?”

Geom Mugeuk added, looking at her,

“Can you give us two million nyang?”

Geum Arin couldn’t answer.

If it had been something she could decide herself.

‘—I would give it to you. From now on, you are mine.’

She might truly have spoken those words.

Even if they didn’t prove worth the money, she would certainly have said so. Because her instincts demanded it.

But the one with the authority to decide would view it as an absurdly large sum. No matter how much she stressed their importance, it was an amount that would never be approved.

As if he had known this would happen, Geom Mugeuk returned the thirty thousand nyang.

“Why are you returning it?”

“I wished to see your resolve; money was not my aim.”

Having returned the money, Geom Mugeuk rose from his seat first.

“Then let us meet again sometime.”

He even paid for the drinks before leaving the place.

Left alone, Geum Arin drank the liquor before her.

The instinct that she had to secure him and the reality that she could not still tangled her heart.

Sensing her darkened mood, Im Hyeok sat in the seat Geom Mugeuk had occupied.

“The Eunha Sangdan invested a full million nyang.”

While Im Hyeok was surprised, Geum Arin was calculating differently.

“No. Those people were deceived by the Eunha Sangdan.”

“Deceived?”

“If it were one person handling a single mission, it would be an unprecedented fortune. But there are three of them.”

The other two were no ordinary men either. His father, for whom words were insufficient to describe, and his uncle, a master of stealth techniques.

“I don’t know if there are others, but let’s assume it’s just those three. Then it’s roughly 330,000 nyang per person, is it not?”

“That is correct.”

“And you heard what he said earlier, right? They think they’ll eliminate the secret organization and take its place. In other words, they’re thinking about what comes after. They’re planning in terms of years. If it takes at least three or four years, then each person gets less than 100,000 nyang. They’ll be putting experts of that caliber to full use, and earning the money we used to earn in our stead. Seen that way, they were truly hired cheap.”

Im Hyeok nodded. Hearing her explanation, he realized it wasn’t such an expensive price.

Im Hyeok asked cautiously,

“The secret organization commissioned by the Eunha Sangdan… is it… us?”

The question was whether it meant their organization.

Geum Arin nodded.

“Yes. It must be us.”

He had expected it, but facing the fact that they themselves had become targets, Im Hyeok grew grave as well.

He thought of Hwi’s stealth technique. If Hwi approached silently and tried to kill Geum Arin, could he truly stop him?

“Fire has fallen upon our feet.”

For the first time in a while, Im Hyeok wanted a drink.

“What do you intend to do now?”

Her deliberation was not long.

“We have to draw them to our side. If we leave them be, it’s only a matter of time before we suffer.”

The words, “Don’t worry, I’ll protect you,” didn’t leave Im Hyeok’s mouth.

“It is a staggering two million nyang. The Family Head will never approve.”

“I’ll have to negotiate it down as much as possible. I’m still a bloodline of the merchant house, after all.”

At those words, Im Hyeok knew she was serious. She truly intended to recruit them.

“There is one more problem. Even if the Family Head permits it and we succeed in bringing them to our side, what about the Eunha Sangdan? Will they sit idle after having them stolen away?”

“And if they don’t? Will they try to kill them?”

“The Eunha Sangdan values its pride.”

But she was certain that the Eunha Sangdan could do nothing about it.

“But they value money more. How much would it take to eliminate those three? Since they were valued at one million nyang, eliminating them would cost at least that much, would it not? And if the elimination fails? If experts of that level come seeking revenge? How much more would it cost to stop them?”

She shook her head resolutely.

“The greatest merchant lord under heaven would never make a losing deal. In the end, it means nothing will happen even if we recruit them.”

That was why Im Hyeok trusted her. Because she calculated so wisely.

His final worry was this.

“Doesn’t the Eunha Sangdan know that the Geumnyong Sega stands behind us?”

That was something even she couldn’t help.

“Do we have the luxury to worry about the Sega right now? Let’s save our own lives first.”

Geum Arin poured Im Hyeok a drink.

“Just one glass. We should have a toast, after all. We drank their liquor and extracted all the information we wanted.”

Though they had gotten the money back, gained the information, and clinked cups in good spirits.

The drink Im Hyeok hadn’t tasted in a long time was bitter.

‘But why does it feel like we lost?’

* * *

Yong Jamyeong paced the courtyard.

Walking a few steps, he would look at the building; walking a few more steps, he would look again.

What he gazed at was the room where the Demonic Cult Leader was staying.

He wanted to see Geom Woojin. He wanted to meet and talk with him, but he didn’t have the courage to seek him out.

‘I have only three days left.’

As he was wandering the courtyard like that, Geom Woojin came out of the building.

“Cult Leader!”

“Branch Leader Yong.”

Seeing Geom Woojin’s face, Yong Jamyeong’s heart surged once more. Who would have known he would feel this way about someone at his age?

“Are you going out somewhere?”

“I thought I’d get some air. Will you join me?”

“Would I not be a hindrance?”

“Not at all. Let us go.”

Yong Jamyeong happily followed Geom Woojin out.

He told the three experts of the merchant house not to follow and to wait at their lodgings. Normally, this wouldn’t be permitted, but because his long-sought benefactor had been revealed to be the Heavenly Demon, the martial artists obeyed without protest.

The two walked slowly toward Jeoja. It was a situation that could have been endlessly awkward, but unexpectedly, the two conversed well on various matters.

“How many children do you have?”

“Three sons.”

“That must not be easy.”

Yong Jamyeong guessed what Geom Woojin meant. That the struggle for succession would be fierce.

“I do not intend to pass the family business to my children.”

Geom Woojin looked at him with a rather surprised expression.

“None of them possess the aptitude to lead the merchant house. And even if they did, I had no intention of passing it on.”

Though they lacked aptitude, it was unlikely they lacked ambition.

Perhaps the estate was rather large? Knowing full well how those around him would scheme and stab at his sides, whether his sons or others, he intended to declare from the outset that he wouldn’t hand it over to anyone.

“I plan to divide wealth enough for them to live comfortably for generations, and prevent them entirely from meddling in merchant house affairs. The merchant house will be entrusted to someone who understands its affairs better than anyone and can be trusted. Because the livelihoods of the countless people employed by the merchant house and their families hang in the balance.”

Geom Woojin looked at him and spoke calmly.

“Admirable.”

There was sincerity in the praise. How could he not know how difficult a decision this was? Yong Jamyeong was every bit as excellent in judgment as his reputation suggested.

Then Yong Jamyeong revealed his honest feelings.

“If I had a son like the Young Cult Leader, I would have passed all my wealth to him. He would handle it well enough on his own. In that regard, I greatly envy you, Cult Leader.”

When had he ever shown his honest heart like this? He had never spoken easily to anyone about his children.

“He is still a reckless, naive child. So please look after him well in the future, Branch Leader.”

Though he said that, Yong Jamyeong sensed that Geom Woojin took pride in his son. Being a father himself, how could he not sense such emotion?

Just then, a cart was approaching from up ahead.

Both of their gazes naturally turned toward the cart.

An old man held the reins of a donkey-drawn cart. Sitting in the back cargo area were an adult and a child. The adult was Geom Mugeuk, and the child was the old man’s granddaughter.

“Here, it’s all finished.”

What Geom Mugeuk handed over was a small wooden doll he had carved.

For something he had hastily made with a dagger on the way here from Jeoja, it was quite well done.

“I love it!”

The child happily received the wooden doll.

When the cart passed by Geom Woojin and Yong Jamyeong, Geom Mugeuk dismounted.

“Go safely!”

Geom Mugeuk bade farewell to the child. As the cart moved away, the child waved while holding the doll.

After sending off the cart, Geom Mugeuk explained to the two,

“Ah, the cart had fallen into a ditch, so I helped pull it out.”

Yong Jamyeong looked at Geom Mugeuk with fresh eyes.

Pulling out the cart was one thing, but it was no easy matter to come here while making a doll for the child who had been riding in it.

Yong Jamyeong glanced at Geom Ujin. He was looking at his son with an expression that said, 'He certainly meddles in everything,' yet Yong Jamyeong could sense it. That he was watching with pleasure. That was why he envied him.

"Where are the two of you going?"

"Going out for some fresh air with the Cult Leader."

Geom Mugeuk could imagine how much Yong Jamyeong had wanted to arrange this meeting. And how difficult it must have been.

"How did the errand you went on turn out?"

He had handled it as though he had accompanied Yong Jamyeong to the Continental Battlefield and received a million nyang. When he leaked word of that, a woman came to him.

"I asked for two million nyang. Though if I consider Father, I should have asked for a billion."

Yong Jamyeong was inwardly surprised. Did those words not mean he already had the other party wrapped around his finger?

"It seems you know how to earn money better than I do."

"Unfortunately, this money must be divided."

Geom Mugeuk spoke to his father.

"The money earned this time will be divided into exactly three equal shares."

From the empty air came Hwi's answer.

"I am fine."

Given his personality, he would never dare try to receive the same amount of money as his father.

"Because to them, this is money for selling the three of us as a bundle, so you must take it."

Once more, Hwi's firm refusal rang out.

"I do not need it."

"I know. That you are the most expensive bodyguard in all of Murim."

Hwi had likely never heard such words from anyone before.

That was why Geom Mugeuk had said it deliberately. For him, who possessed supreme skill, yet had never once been able to boast as he deserved. For him, who had never even had the opportunity.

Offering to divide the money like this was a gesture of gratitude for him. For it was the only chance to give material compensation to the man who had devoted his life to Father.

"In the bodyguard field, mister, you are the Cheonmago, the Eunha Sangdanju, are you not?"

"Please do not say such things."

Hwi's flustered voice was a sound long unheard.

A faint smile formed on Geom Ujin's lips as he watched Geom Mugeuk carry on like that. It was a smile that said, 'You brat, stop teasing him.'

"You have saved up a great deal of money, and I know full well that you have no time to spend it. That is why you must accept it. Though you have no time to spend it, you live with the pleasure of knowing you have so much money you could spend. That is the joy you live for, is it not? Ah, and when Father steps down from his position as Cult Leader, you must retire as well and live spending money freely."

After a brief pause, Hwi spoke.

"I will serve the Cult Leader until the very end."

Meaning that even if Father stepped down as Cult Leader, he would serve him until death. Hwi was truly a man akin to the very symbol of loyalty.

Father, who had only been listening, finally stepped in.

"Take it."

No persuasion was needed. At those words, Hwi could refuse no longer.

"Understood."

Afterward, he gave his son a mission. This too was the father's consideration for Hwi.

"So extract as much as possible."

Absolute Regression Episode 467

Chapter 467: Take Good Care of Them and Get Back What You Gave.
---------------------------------------------------------------
Hwi was sincerely moved.

He was grateful that Geom Mugeuk was willing to share the money with him, and grateful as well to the Cult Master, who had ordered him to accept it.

He was even grateful that Geom Mugeuk had called him the bodyguard with the highest price tag. He had thought he was not a vain man, but seeing how good those words made him feel, perhaps he had possessed some vanity after all.

But of all these pleasant things, what he was most grateful for was the Cult Master’s final remark.

‘So make sure you get as much as possible.’

It was a statement utterly unfitting for the Cult Master. Asking someone for money—and telling them to get as much as possible—was not something one would expect from a man like him. Yet the Cult Master had said it for his sake.

Yong Jamyeong felt the same sentiment.

If the man had been greedy for money, he would have come looking for him several times by now.

Yet this same man had boldly told him to get as much money as possible for his subordinate.

Yong Jamyeong looked back and forth between Geom Ujin and Geom Mugeuk. Every moment spent with them was new and astonishing. The Young Cult Master’s answer alone proved that. Before meeting them, could he ever have imagined that the Young Cult Master would give such an answer?

“Don’t worry. Who am I? Am I not the embodiment of avarice? I’ll make sure to get every last penny.”

After Geom Ujin nodded, he spoke to Yong Jamyeong.

“Let’s go.”

“Yes, Cult Master.”

Leaving Geom Mugeuk behind, Geom Ujin and Yong Jamyeong began walking again.

Yong Jamyeong turned around.

Before he knew it, Geom Mugeuk was walking in the opposite direction. Unlike the playful figure he had shown moments ago, his back looked calm and composed.

When Yong Jamyeong turned forward again, Geom Ujin had already walked some distance ahead.

Yong Jamyeong quickened his pace and walked alongside him.

“I must have seen more incarnations of avarice than anyone else in my life. But that’s the first time I’ve seen one with such clear eyes.”

At Yong Jamyeong’s joke, Geom Ujin said nothing and simply continued walking, but Yong Jamyeong could feel it. The man was proud of his son. He had sensed it once during their earlier conversation, and now he sensed it again.

That meant Geom Ujin did not hide such feelings from him.

Yong Jamyeong thought that this emotion must be much the same as the honest sentiment Geom Ujin had revealed when he said he would not pass his family business on to his child.

Who could he show such feelings to?

To someone who would not carelessly repeat whatever he said, of course.

The trust required to share private and secret matters had little to do with how long one had known the other.

There were people one could trust after meeting them for only a day, while there were others to whom one could hardly open one’s mouth even after ten years.

Yong Jamyeong was happy to know that the Cult Master trusted him.

It was a trust they could feel without having to speak of it.

It was the kind of connection one might encounter only once or twice in an entire lifetime. He had liked the Cult Master from the beginning, but after feeling this as well, Yong Jamyeong came to like Geom Ujin even more.

Perhaps he had been lonely.

In all his years as the greatest merchant under heaven, he had never had anyone to whom he could comfortably voice his worries apart from matters of profit and connections, nor anyone on whom he could comfortably rely.

He had lived a life in which, whenever he looked at someone, his first thought was always, What does that person want this time?

And this Heavenly Demon had come to him—not merely as a benefactor anymore, but like an elder brother.

As they walked, the two men talked about this and that.

They had each reached the pinnacle of their respective fields. And not just any fields—the ends of all human desire: power and money.

The pinnacle of pinnacles.

Perhaps that was why they conversed so well.

He had thought the Demonic Cult Master would be nothing but taciturn, but that was not so. Whenever he had something to say, he said it readily, and he even asked questions.

They spoke of children, the merchant world, the martial world, and how Yong Jamyeong had felt when he received the Cult Master’s personally written letter. They talked without noticing the passage of time.

Yong Jamyeong thought this would be the last time in his life he would have the chance to take a walk while exchanging idle conversation with the Heavenly Demon. So he treasured this moment.

In the meantime, they arrived at the marketplace, and Geom Ujin stopped for a while to watch the people coming and going.

“Do you know what my son wants most from me on this journey?”

“What is it?”

“To stand here and watch the people.”

Yong Jamyeong looked at Geom Ujin with an expression asking what he meant, but Geom Ujin offered no answer. After a long while, Yong Jamyeong heard Geom Ujin murmur quietly.

“Indeed…”

He could not tell what words had been left unsaid after that “indeed.”

* * *

The next day, Geum Arin came to see Geom Mugeuk again.

“Let’s talk.”

“Very well.”

Geom Mugeuk did not invite her inside. He came out instead.

The last time, she had walked some distance away from him. Today, she walked a little closer, showing that her wariness had lessened.

Instead of heading toward the marketplace, they went in the opposite direction, toward the fields.

The fields stretched through the wide-open view all the way to the distant horizon.

The two of them stood silently for a long time, watching the grass and crops sway in the wind.

The first to speak was Geum Arin.

“Two million nyang is too much.”

She was asking him to lower the price. Geom Mugeuk could tell that she had made up her mind. Things were proceeding according to plan.

“It’s too much for anyone, not just you. That’s also why we joined hands with the Eunha Merchant Guild.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s words, Geum Arin reluctantly spoke. It was something she did not want to say, but there was no helping it now.

“If you lower it, we can manage.”

Geom Mugeuk smiled.

“You mean you want me to lower it by about one million nine hundred seventy thousand nyang?”

He was joking about the thirty thousand nyang he had returned to her last time.

“Do it for one million nyang.”

One million nyang. She had come in asking him to cut the price in half. If he considered only the plan to infiltrate the heart of their organization, he could lower it, but unfortunately for Geom Mugeuk, he had been given the supreme mission of extracting as much as possible.

“Didn’t I tell you? We need a justification for refusing the Eunha Merchant Guild. If we say we’re going elsewhere for the exact same one million nyang, what do you think they’ll assume?”

“That we found someone better to work with.”

Geom Mugeuk turned to look at her.

“Am I really better?”

She did not avoid the gaze with which he stared at her.

“I don’t know. I don’t know what kind of person the master of the Eunha Merchant Guild is. You’ve met him, so you should know. Who seems like the better person?”

Though phrased as a question, it was a confident question from someone who had already decided on the answer. This was her momentum.

“At the very least, you’re more shameless.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s response, Geum Arin thought things might go more smoothly than expected. She realized that he clearly had a favorable impression of her.

“Whether it’s one million or two million nyang, you’re not the decision-maker anyway, are you?”

“Why do you keep assuming I’m someone’s subordinate? Because I’m young? Because I’m a woman?”

Geom Mugeuk shook his head.

“That’s not why.”

He revealed an unexpected reason.

“I don’t get the feeling that you’ve staked everything on the organization.”

Geum Arin flinched at his words. It was true. She had not staked everything on this secret organization. No—if anything, she wanted to finish this assignment as quickly as possible. She had hated the reason she had accepted it in the first place.

‘He saw through that?’

This man was exceptionally perceptive.

He was smarter and more eloquent than anyone she had ever met.

Geum Arin felt that it was time to stake everything on a single move. It was the biggest gamble of her life, one on which her very life and death depended.

“The organization you’re trying to destroy is the organization I lead.”

It was her life-or-death gambit.

If her honesty failed to get through to him, she might die right here.

She had expected him to be shocked, but Geom Mugeuk was not. In the end, she was the one who was surprised.

“Don’t tell me you already knew?”

Geom Mugeuk nodded.

“How did you know?”

“The Eunha Merchant Guild had already conducted a fairly extensive investigation into your group.”

He attributed what he knew about her to information obtained through the Eunha Merchant Guild.

Geum Arin watched Geom Mugeuk with trembling eyes. If he had learned it through the Eunha Merchant Guild, then that meant he had known even when they met last time.

“Why didn’t you kill me? This was a one-million-nyang job.”

She acted calm, but she was tense.

To her, Geom Mugeuk was an unpredictable man who might draw his sword at any moment and say, I was going to kill you anyway.

At least in that respect, she had judged him correctly. Once again, he gave an answer she had never expected.

“Because I thought you might be able to pay more.”

It was one surprise after another.

At the very least, she had never expected him to say that he wanted to extract more money.

“Why are you so surprised?”

“I didn’t think you’d say something like that. You seemed somewhat noble. Ah, I’m not being sarcastic. It’s a compliment, so don’t misunderstand.”

Geom Mugeuk countered her honest statement.

“Is someone noble simply because he has no interest in money? Then does that mean someone who cares greatly about money is vulgar and low-class? That’s not something a merchant should say.”

“That’s not what I meant. I only meant that it was unexpected.”

“If I ask for more money, will this person dislike me? If I ask for more money, will he think me lowly? I think that kind of thinking is mere annoyance and an escape from reality. You have to summon even the courage you never had when it comes to making money. Even if the coin has fallen into a pool of filthy water, you should roll up your sleeves and retrieve it.”

Geom Mugeuk spoke like the very embodiment of avarice, but in truth, the life he had led before his regression had been far removed from money. It had been a life devoted solely to martial arts and the materials needed for the Great Rebirth Art.

“Take that back.”

“I should. I’m negotiating instead of drawing my sword even though I’m facing a one-million-nyang enemy. Isn’t that the height of nobility?”

It was not a situation in which she should laugh, yet Geum Arin found herself laughing despite herself. Soon, she straightened her expression and changed the subject.

“If you consider me someone’s subordinate, can you really extract more money from me?”

“Because you have another identity besides being a subordinate.”

After a brief pause, Geom Mugeuk continued.

“I believe you’re a blood relative of the head of the Golden Dragon House.”

She was not surprised that Geom Mugeuk knew about her. He seemed like the sort of person who knew everything.

“Because if I weren’t a blood relative, I couldn’t have proposed this deal in the first place?”

“That’s part of it, but there’s a greater reason.”

Once again, Geom Mugeuk saw through her perfectly.

“I can’t sense any longing for money in you.”

“!”

That, too, struck precisely at the heart of the matter. How could a blood relative of the Golden Dragon House possibly yearn for money?

He had seen that she had grown up in abundance, without ever having to worry about money.

“How can you see that?”

She did not say “know,” but “see.” There was something about the gaze in this man’s eyes that made her feel as though he could look directly into a person’s heart.

“Give us two million nyang and join hands with us. I’ll take care of severing ties with the Eunha Merchant Guild cleanly.”

She truly wanted to give it to him. That was why she wanted to make someone like him her ally.

“I told you I can’t.”

“If two million nyang is impossible, doesn’t that mean one million nyang was impossible from the beginning?”

It struck the heart of the matter. Whether it was one million or two million nyang, the same storm of trouble would descend upon her either way.

“Please understand my circumstances.”

Despite her plea, Geom Mugeuk remained firm.

“Do you think the circumstances of the person giving the money matter any less than those of the person receiving it? So don’t appeal to the recipient’s emotions. I think that’s the worst thing you can do in a transaction.”

Geum Arin pressed her lips together. She thought the same. It was only because such a large sum of money was changing hands that she had no choice.

Regardless, she had to bring this man over to her side. Today’s conversation had made that even clearer. If she made him an enemy, she would certainly die. He was the kind of person who made her think that without even drawing his sword.

“Take proper care of me, and then collect as much as you give.”

Enter into a contract to pay two million nyang?

How would her father react? Surely he would not kill his only daughter.

The problem was her brothers. In particular, her second elder brother, who regarded her as an eyesore, would use this matter to drive her all the way to hell. Could this man really keep her second elder brother at bay?

Her gaze turned toward Geom Mugeuk’s clear, deep eyes.

Could I really trust you?

Perhaps he had read that question in her eyes.

“For the duration of the contract, I will unquestionably be on your side.”

“That’s only natural, isn’t it?”

“Isn’t the failure to uphold that natural expectation what causes all sorts of trouble?”

He said it not merely as a promise, but with genuine sincerity.

“Can you keep your promise to be firmly on my side?”

“Don’t trust my words. Trust your eyes as a merchant.”

Her gaze turned toward the fields.

I’m doing this because I trust you too much.

How long had she known him? How much did she really know about him? And yet this man was giving her his trust. He stirred her instincts and drew out her premonition. Choose me.

The grass swaying in the wind rippled like waves. Her heart, too, rose and fell with those waves. The last words that came to her mind before making her decision were these:

―Take proper care of me, and then collect as much as you give.

You—did you even know what I was planning to take care of?

At last, Geum Arin made the decision that would change her life.

“Let’s send the contract as soon as possible.”

Chapter 468: He Told Us to Clean It Up
--------------------------------------
“Will you be alright?”

Im Hyeok asked worriedly.

Having returned from meeting Geom Mugeuk, Geum Arin immediately began drafting a contract. A contract with clean terms, hiding no intentions whatsoever, stating that she would pay two million nyang and join hands with them.

“Will you be okay? You’re doing something crazy.”

At Geum Arin’s answer, Im Hyeok thought it was rather fortunate. At least she had a proper grasp of the situation.

“Why do you trust that person so much?”

The answer was exactly one word.

“Instinct.”

The hand with which she had been writing paused for a moment.

“I didn’t know either. That I would decide my fate by instinct.”

Contrary to her words, she began finishing the contract with eyes full of certainty.

The moment Geom Mugeuk signed the contract, she had to give him two million nyang.

In the upper realm, trust is life. No matter how secretive an organization, since its head was the daughter of the Geumryong Clan head, it was the same as a contract with the Geumryong Clan.

“To pay two million nyang, we would have to combine this year’s operating funds as well as all the profits our organization has gathered. It may still not be enough.”

“I’ll pay it even if I have to borrow money from the battlefield.”

Im Hyeok spoke worriedly.

“The Second Young Master won’t let this slide.”

“I suppose so.”

Objectively speaking, not just her second brother, but anyone would consider this decision rash.

She raised her head, looked at Im Hyeok, and asked.

“Do you think so?”

Though he hadn’t yet seen even the slightest bit of their martial arts, Im Hyeok thought of Hwi. That gaze with which he had looked at him, and his calm advice, could not easily be converted into money.

“No.”

So Im Hyeok respected her instinct. She must have felt the same from Geom Mugeuk.

“Yes, me neither.”

Geum Arin revealed that this choice was aimed at a place further away.

“I made up my mind when that person said he would become a sure ally of mine. That I would use them to become the successor.”

Im Hyeok finally felt that what had to come had come. She had stepped back from the succession struggle of the Geumryong Clan. Until now, she had merely watched the fight between her two brothers from across the river. Now she was trying to jump into the full-fledged succession struggle.

She had expressed it as instinct, but this was following the pull of fate.

“Take this, get it signed, and pay the money,” she said, holding out the finished contract. “What I bought with two million nyang is not our lives, nor the Seodopa. It’s the successor position.”

* * *

“I’ve earned money!”

Geom Mugeuk set three pouches down on the table.

“We received two million nyang and divided it into three. Six hundred and sixty-six thousand nyang each.”

Geom Ujin was the first to take a pouch containing the draft, as if to boast.

“Well done.”

At that, Hwi released his concealment and revealed himself. He truly couldn’t stand receiving the same amount as the Cult Leader, but above that was the Cult Leader’s order to take the money.

“Here, this is Uncle’s share.”

Hwi carefully tucked the pouch into his chest.

“Father, let’s come out often, if only for Uncle Hwi’s sake. What good is money if we have no time to spend it?”

At Geom Mugeuk’s words, Hwi gave an awkward smile. Even without this money, he had saved a great deal having spent his whole life in the Divine Cult. But as Geom Mugeuk said, he had no time to spend it.

Geom Mugeuk spoke to Yong Jamyeong, who was beside him.

“Would you say a word about how to spend money?”

At the sudden question, Yong Jamyeong held off answering for a moment. He could have talked about money for three days and nights without rest, but since the Cult Leader was listening, he answered modestly.

“There are many people in the world who earn money well, but few who spend it well. I belong to those who earn well, not those who spend well.”

Then he asked Geom Mugeuk’s thoughts.

“Does the Young Cult Leader have any separate thoughts on this?”

“What choice do I have? I’ll have to find someone who spends money well later and take them as a subordinate. That subordinate, I should treat with the best of care. About that much.”

“So you already knew the answer to spending money wisely.”

The two looked at each other and smiled.

Geom Mugeuk conveyed his gratitude to Yong Jamyeong.

“This matter was easy thanks to the Leader.”

Because everything had proceeded from the fact that the Leader of the Eunha Merchant Group had come to find him himself.

But the fact that things had gone well also meant it was time for him to leave. Having received money from them, it was now time for his side to depart.

Several more days had passed since the three days he had initially planned. He had happily spent a few more days because Geom Ujin wished for him to stay longer. It had truly been a dreamlike time.

Geom Ujin had made much time for him, so they had talked a great deal. The time spent with him had truly been enjoyable.

“It was an unforgettable time in my life. If you need me again, please call on me anytime.”

“Let us do so.”

Geom Ujin’s last farewell to him.

“Contact us anytime if you need us.”

Where in the world was there a more reassuring word than this?

With gratitude filling his face, Yong Jamyeong said his goodbyes.

“Then until we meet again, take care.”

Geom Mugeuk came out to the main gate to see off the departing Yong Jamyeong.

“Thanks to you, I’ve fulfilled an old dream. Truly, thank you.”

“It is the Leader’s good fortune. It was also the Leader who remembered my father.”

He had wanted to resolve his long-cherished wish, and fortunately things had worked out well. But it wasn’t as if he had only received.

“Thank you truly for the teachings you have imparted.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s request, Yong Jamyeong had lectured extensively on matters of the upper realm. Since the enemies they had to face were great houses of the upper realm, the more they knew, the more advantageous it would be in battle. Thanks to that, Geom Mugeuk had learned much listening to his discourse.

Giving, and receiving. Now it was time to give again.

“Please contact my father often.”

“To the Cult Leader?”

“I noticed he likes being with the Leader.”

“If so, that is truly fortunate.”

“For your reference, my father likes baduk. If you bring a good baduk board and stones the next time we meet, he will be very pleased.”

“Thank you for telling me.”

Yong Jamyeong looked at Geom Mugeuk for a moment.

He had seen the Young Cult Leader of the rumors, and now he could speak about the rumors. The rumors had been understated. The Young Cult Leader had been underestimated.

Yong Jamyeong, having boarded the carriage, gave his last greeting.

“Stay well.”

The Young Cult Leader, who had shown a relaxed smile the whole time, was no exception at the end either.

“Is there no final lecture?”

Though it must have been said in jest, Yong Jamyeong left him with words he wanted to say.

“Until the very moment of death, money will try to reveal your true nature. Don’t let that fellow catch on.”

Thus, Geom Mugeuk disappeared from sight.

Baek Chong, who was riding together, saw Yong Jamyeong lean his head out of the carriage to bid farewell for the first time. He had always sat solemnly whenever he was in a carriage.

“I’m glad it ended safely.”

Until they came here, they had truly staked their lives with a grim resolve. But the meeting with the Demonic Cult Leader had truly been a rest they hadn’t had in a long time. Yong Jamyeong had, and so had Baek Chong.

“I should meet all the kids when I return.”

He couldn’t remember the last time he had talked with his sons. He was busy with merchant group affairs, and when they actually met, it was awkward. Thus, putting it off, even seeing their faces once became a task. But after meeting this Heavenly Demon father and son, he missed his children.

Yong Jamyeong suddenly thought this.

Perhaps this time, he had met an old benefactor and at the same time gained a new benefactor.

* * *

“Prepare to return to the Great House.”

Geum Arin, having given the order to Im Hyeok, felt her heart pounding already. She had carried out the plan with great momentum, but her father’s scolding was inevitable. She had even lost sleep at night, having nightmares. They were dreams of them taking the money and disappearing. She had another nightmare as well. They had displayed their skills before her father, and they had been truly hopeless.

“I’ll meet that person and set the departure time.”

Just then, someone entered and spoke.

“There’s no need.”

Surprised, she turned to see a man entering.

Seeing him, Geum Arin’s expression hardened.

He was Geum Arin’s second brother, Geum Ajong.

He was the one she had worried about most while executing this matter, yet he had appeared here first. He had quite a handsome appearance resembling Geum Arin, but his personality didn’t match his looks. He was selfish, greedy, and filled with ambition for success.

“How did you get here?”

“How else? I came to stop the house’s money from bleeding away.”

That he had come this quickly meant he had been watching her every move.

“Our older brother is someone who always waits for me to make a mistake.”

Among her two brothers, Geum Ajong was especially wary of her. He suspected that one day their father might pass the Great House to his daughter.

“Two million nyang? You! Are you out of your mind?”

As expected, her brother had a precise grasp of the situation.

“My business is mine to handle. Older brother should mind his own business.”

“Not even knowing you’ve been played by a swindler.”

Geum Arin flinched at the word swindler. It was something she had never imagined hearing.

“A swindler?”

“You spent such a large sum without properly investigating the other party, didn’t you?”

She had nothing to say to that. This decision had been an instinctive one relying entirely on instinct.

“Did you know that the Seodopa produced a winner at the Soryong Tournament hosted by the Murim Alliance?”

It was something she hadn’t known, so Geum Arin flinched. Until now, it had been an obscure sect, but now it was becoming a true sect that had participated in the Soryong Tournament.

Just as she was thinking that since it was a competition to determine late-stage adepts, of course they would have won—

“They barely won after a fierce battle with the Murim Alliance Leader’s granddaughter.”

At those words, Geum Arin’s heart sank.

*It was a close match with the Alliance Leader’s granddaughter?*

No matter that she was the Alliance Leader’s granddaughter, she was only a late-stage adept.

Worry reared its head in one corner of her heart.

*Could it be that they just talk big but their skills don’t match?*

Suppressing the rising worry with effort, she spoke.

“They won, didn’t they? They might have been hiding their strength.”

As if he had been waiting for exactly this moment, Geum Ajong raised his voice with a gleeful face.

“What are you saying? The one called Geom Yeon, to whom you gave two million nyang, did not appear in the Soryong Tournament. The one who won the Soryong Tournament is a man named Seoryong.”

“Then what about Geom Yeon?”

Words like a thunderbolt from a clear sky flowed from Geum Ajong’s mouth.

“Geom Yeon is Seoryong’s servant.”

“!”

Geum Arin sprang up from her seat.

Her pupils trembled.

That person was a servant?

“It’s already a well-known fact that at the time, the Alliance Leader’s granddaughter caused a commotion saying she would marry a servant she had brought with her. She must have been deceived, just like you.”

He treated him like a swindler who went around deceiving women. But if those words were true? If he was really only a servant?

Geom Mugeuk’s figure surfaced in her mind. Had he been unreadable because he was a swindler? Was her nightmare becoming reality?

“I have to meet him.”

Geum Ajong stopped her, who was about to rush out immediately.

“Don’t rush. I’ve already sent people to recover the money.”

Geum Arin was shocked.

“Who?”

“The Money Leeches have gone.”

“It can’t be!”

The words burst from her lips on their own.

The Money Leeches’ original name was Hwanggeumchong. They were shadow experts raised by the Great House, deployed for rough tasks. They were mainly deployed for affairs that must not be revealed to the world—exterminating those who attacked merchant groups, or struggling for commercial rights when pioneering new territories. They truly handled rough and dangerous matters.

Therefore, they were insufferably arrogant within the Great House. Workers and martial artists alike feared and despised them. The decisive reason the Money Leeches were terrifying was that they did not hesitate in means or methods to achieve their objective. Moreover, they did not even cherish their own lives. That was why they were hated, and at the same time, absolutely necessary to the Geumryong Clan.

“They will all die.”

“To whom? To a servant?”

She was confused, but her heart still trusted Geom Mugeuk greatly.

“Yes, to a servant.”

“Are you ill? Well. You wouldn’t have done something like this in your right mind.”

Geum Arin flew out, leaving the quarters.

Geum Ajong ran after her using lightness skill.

“You’ll fall completely out of Father’s favor over this.”

Geum Ajong’s words did not reach her ears. Several scenes rose in her mind.

She pictured Geom Mugeuk truly being a fraud, dead after having been brutally beaten by the Money Grubs. Next, she pictured the Money Grubs reporting that he had already fled and was nowhere to be found.

Conversely, she also pictured all the Money Grubs lying scattered as corpses.

If he killed them all, her father would never forgive Seodopa. In other words, her own plan to use them to become the successor would also be ruined.

Every imagining was the worst possible outcome.

“Sending them without even consulting me was your mistake, Brother.”

“You made the first mistake by giving him money without consulting anyone.”

The two, who had raced like the wind, arrived at the manor where Geom Mugeuk was staying.

Perhaps the situation was already over, for it was quiet inside.

As they cautiously approached the main gate, they heard a sound from within.

Scrape, scrape.

What was that sound?

It sounded like something sweeping the ground.

Swipe, swipe.

There was also the sound of something being wiped.

Just then, someone’s whispering voice came through.

Don’t touch that.

Not only Geum Arin, but even Geum Ajong, who had come with utter confidence, wore a puzzled expression. For a moment, they wondered if they had come to the wrong place.

“Is this the place?”

“I’m certain.”

Geum Arin carefully opened the door.

When the door slowly swung open, a sight unfolded within the grounds that truly surpassed anything they could have imagined.

The Money Grubs were holding brooms and sweeping the courtyard. Other Money Grubs were wiping the walls with rags. They were clearing away broken furnishings and filling in gouges in the ground.

Dark bruises ringed their eyes, and some were limping as they carried loads. One was retrieving a hammock hanging from a tree, his forehead wrapped in a bandage where blood had trickled down. Another was carrying belongings out from inside the building.

The Money Grubs’ movements came to a halt, and all their gazes turned at once toward the two people who had entered through the gate.

Awkward, embarrassed expressions filled the Money Grubs’ faces.

Geum Ajong felt as though he were seeing things.

“What in the world are you doing right now?”

At Geum Ajong’s question, a Money Grub standing near the entrance bowed and said,

“He said he would be leaving soon and told us to clean everything up.”

This was the first time the Money Grubs had ever worn such kind faces.

“Who? Who in the world!”

At that moment, Geom Mugeuk emerged from the building ahead. Looking at Geum Arin, he said cheerfully,

“Thank you for sending people to clean.”

In that instant, emotion surged within Geum Arin. All the terrible imaginings she had carried on the way here were washed away by that emotion.

She was immensely grateful that he had only beaten the Money Grubs and not killed them.

Whether he was an attendant or not no longer mattered. She would not doubt him anymore. Yes, my choice was right.

With a bright expression, Geum Arin said to Geum Ajong, who stood beside her,

“Thank you, Brother. I couldn’t very well make a two-million-nyang attendant clean.”

Absolute Regression, Chapter 469

Chapter 469: Bring Me Proof He's a Fraud!
-----------------------------------------
Geum Ajong could not make sense of this situation at all.

Fine, even if he conceded a hundred times over that the other party was more skilled than the money-grubbers.

But he had made those fierce, rough, arrogant bastards this docile? How on earth?

The one over there clearing away the shattered stones scattered on the ground was someone who normally did not even greet him. He truly was an arrogant fellow who lived as if there were no tomorrow. Geum Ajong had only learned today that he was capable of making such an innocent expression.

Meanwhile, Geom Mugeuk walked up to the two of them.

Just then, one of the nearby money-grubbers dropped a stone he had been clearing.

When Geom Mugeuk picked it up and handed it to him, the money-grubber thought he was about to be hit, flinched in terror, and fell on his backside.

Watching that, Geum Ajong was dumbfounded.

‘What a load of shit. Seriously.’

What he could not understand was this.

If the money-grubbers had had their arms and legs cut off, he would have understood. They would have been through something truly dreadful. Forget cleaning—if they were told to crawl across the floor like insects, they would have to crawl.

But they had merely been beaten up, and they were this docile? Those foul-tempered bastards? Just what kind of beating had he given them?

“This is my elder brother.”

Geum Arin introduced her brother to Geom Mugeuk.

“Pleased to meet you. I am Geom Yeon.”

The look Geum Ajong gave Geom Mugeuk could not possibly be a kind one. But in this situation, with the money-grubbers cleaning, he could not very well interrogate him by saying, “I know you’re a fraud.”

“I heard about you from my younger sister.”

“What did your sister say?”

Geom Mugeuk’s gaze naturally turned toward Geum Arin. Her eyes as she looked at him held gratitude.

‘Thank you. For not running away, and for not killing the money-grubbers.’

The fact that he had not run away meant that, at the very least, he was not a fraud as Geum Ajong had claimed.

Geum Ajong, selfish by nature, was someone who valued himself above all else. Naturally, he also chose carefully when and where to throw his temper around.

“She said you were a person of remarkable skill.”

“Since ancient times, a martial artist risks his life for the one who recognizes his worth.”

It’s because of the money, isn’t it! Geum Ajong struggled to swallow the words rising in his throat. As if that were the only thing he wanted to pour out.

A lowly servant bastard!

He was surely a fraud. How had a mere servant forced the money-grubbers to submit? Had he used some trick? Was the man said to be his father an expert? Yes, that must be it. The person inside that house had to be an expert. He probably wasn’t even his father. He must have hired him with money.

The one who answered his curiosity was Geum Arin. She did not hold back.

“You said the Seodo Sect produced the winner of the Little Dragon Tournament?”

It was something she had to ask at least once anyway, and because she had prepared herself to accept whatever the result might be, she could ask calmly.

“They did.”

That was the reason Geom Mugeuk was using the Seodo Sect again. Because the Seodo Sect had such a history.

When judging someone, one could only first look at outward appearances or facts.

The Seodo Sect produced a winner?

Geom Yeon was that winner’s servant?

One could not help but focus on such facts.

But facts were nothing more than shells.

The truth that Geom Yeon was Geom Mugeuk would only be visible once all those shells were peeled away and one looked inside.

But it would not be easy. The prejudice that he was a servant was wrapped around their attention. The more provocative the surrounding facts, the more they became a powerful shield that hid the truth.

“I heard the winner was someone named Seo Ryong.”

“That’s right. He is my senior brother.”

“Senior brother? I heard you were Seo Ryong’s servant.”

From Geom Mugeuk’s mouth came an answer she had never imagined.

“The Martial Alliance Leader’s granddaughter liked me, so I had no choice but to pretend to be a servant.”

Since Jin Haryeong had once taken him to the Alliance Leader, it would seem plausible enough.

“Ah! The greatness of love that transcends even status!”

Geum Ajong and the nearby money-grubbers who were listening wore expressions of utter disbelief.

In particular, one of the money-grubbers muttered without realizing it, “Crazy bastard!”

Then he started in fright. Realizing he had spoken rashly, he froze, and the money-grubbers beside him took him and withdrew backward.

Watching this, Geum Ajong found the money-grubbers’ reaction even more absurd. They were practically performing tricks in front of Geom Mugeuk. And he was paying such expensive monthly wages to bastards like these!

Geum Arin asked Geom Mugeuk.

“Is that true?”

The Martial Alliance Leader’s granddaughter liked this man? Then again, if she liked him, that must be why she had taken him to the Martial Alliance Leader. The fact that had bewitched her had changed like this.

From “Geom Yeon is a servant” to now “The Martial Alliance Leader’s granddaughter liked him.”

“I’ll introduce you to Miss Jin next time. Since then, we have been getting along like friends.”

She felt half-convinced and half-doubtful, but soon her gaze turned toward the money-grubbers.

Yes, if it was this man.

Geum Arin decided to believe him. She had already staked her fate on him, so she would believe him.

Geum Ajong’s thoughts, however, were different.

‘He’s definitely a fraud.’

Was it not the way of frauds to put forward their connections with famous people?

The reason the Martial Alliance Leader’s granddaughter had taken him to the Alliance Leader must have been as an act of rebellion against the Martial Alliance Leader.

Otherwise, why would he not marry the Alliance Leader’s granddaughter and instead be here extorting two million nyang? With such remarkable skill, no less.

That was why he was a fraud. Perhaps he had even extorted money from the Alliance Leader’s granddaughter.

While Geum Ajong was indulging in all sorts of delusions, Geom Mugeuk called the money-grubbers over.

“Now, if you’re done cleaning, gather here.”

At that, the money-grubbers gathered there. They must have been ordered to look kind and innocent. All of them were making the kindest expressions they could.

Seeing that sight up close made Geum Ajong even more dumbfounded.

That money-grubber’s forearm over there was as thick as this man Geom Yeon’s waist. Weren’t they ashamed?

Could it be? Had they all been poisoned? Unless that was the case, he could not believe they had been defeated by this young man.

Geum Arin, seeing those who had behaved so arrogantly now cowed, felt inwardly exhilarated. It was fine for her to feel exhilarated. Geom Yeon was now her own hands and feet.

Geom Mugeuk said to the money-grubbers.

“Now then, you’ve all worked hard. You may go.”

At that, the money-grubbers bowed deeply and left the place.

Geum Ajong glared at them.

‘They’re really leaving just because that bastard told them to?’

But they truly left without even looking back. It may have been because their pride was hurt, but because of that, Geum Ajong’s pride was hurt as well. No matter how much they followed his father, he was still his father’s flesh and blood.

Geom Mugeuk said to the two of them.

“Come to think of it, both of you sent people to kill me. It seems your bloodline is fated not to leave me alone. How many siblings are there? Surely you’re not nine brothers and sisters or something, are you?”

At Geom Mugeuk’s joke, Geum Arin’s face flushed slightly. Geum Ajong maintained a brazen expression, as if he had not been the one who sent the money-grubbers.

“When will we depart? We are all ready to leave.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s question, Geum Arin answered.

“Then let’s depart right away. I’ll come here in half a shichen.”

“Let’s do that. If you lead the way, we’ll follow your carriage.”

“Then I’ll see you later.”

After saying farewell and turning to leave, Geum Arin turned back again and said,

“Thank you.”

She did not say why she was grateful, but Geom Mugeuk smiled at her with eyes that said he knew everything. At that moment, Geum Arin realized anew.

‘So that was it.’

Now she knew for certain why she had been drawn to Geom Mugeuk. It was because of that composure. The reason she had invested two million nyang and even staked her own fate was because of that relaxed smile. Because of the composure of this man, who seemed to know everything.

Thus Geum Arin and Geum Ajong left the place. Geum Ajong said,

“That bastard is a fraud.”

After a brief pause, Geum Arin asked,

“And you aren’t, Brother?”

The rebuke that struck the startled Geum Ajong was sharp.

“You act as if you’re doing it for me or for the clan, but you’re not, are you? If you had been doing it for me, you would have discussed it with me beforehand before sending the money-grubbers. You were sure I had made a mistake, and you were going to fix it and score points with Father, weren’t you?”

“What nonsense are you spouting?”

Geum Ajong, struck where it hurt, flatly denied it. If things had gone according to his expectations, he would have answered those words like this: Serves you right. You’re completely out of Father’s favor now.

“I’ll see you at the main estate. Oh, and if you get the chance, make sure to thank the servant. If you had lost all the money-grubbers, Brother, you would have been the one to fall out of Father’s favor.”

Geum Arin left first.

Geum Ajong, watching her retreating back, looked toward the estate behind him.

Through the half-open gate, he could see Geom Mugeuk. Geom Mugeuk was looking up at the sky, and strangely enough, he seemed lonely.

Then Geom Mugeuk sensed Geum Ajong’s gaze, looked over at him, and waved.

Geum Ajong pretended not to see him, whipped around, and quickened his steps.

His older brother had once said to him: you are not only greedy, you even have the makings of a fraud. Yes, he admitted it. A fraud recognized another fraud. That bastard was definitely hiding something.

‘I’ll expose your true identity.’

And to do that, he had a subordinate more outstanding than anyone else.

“I Chu!”

At that, a masked man appeared.

“Find proof that bastard is a fraud as quickly as possible!”

“Understood.”

I Chu was a man specialized in digging into people’s backgrounds and gathering information, and among Geum Ajong’s subordinates, he commanded the highest price.

“Don’t fail under any circumstances! I’ve had enough failure for today.”

* * *

The carriage raced toward Shaanxi, where the main headquarters of the Golden Dragon Clan was located.

The two carriages traveled some distance apart, and Geum Arin told Im Hyeok, who was driving the carriage, not to worry even if Geom Mugeuk’s carriage did not follow closely behind.

Inside the carriage, Geom Mugeuk reported to his father what he knew about the Golden Dragon Clan. His father would also have heard all the information from Tongcheon Pavilion, but this information had come from Eunwol.

“The Golden Dragon Clan has three children. Geum Ajong and Geum Arin, whom we met earlier, are the second and third, respectively. The eldest son, Geum Ahyeok, is currently the most likely successor. If one had to express the possibilities numerically, they analyzed it as six-tenths for Geum Ahyeok, three-tenths for Geum Ajong, and one-tenth for Geum Arin.”

Geom Mugeuk had accurately grasped Geum Arin’s intentions.

“The ultimate reason Geum Arin brought us in this time is probably to use us in her fight for succession. It’s a fight she’s bound to lose, so she’s dreaming of a last-minute reversal.”

Geom Ujin simply listened in silence to Geom Mugeuk’s report.

“Once we arrive at the Golden Dragon Clan, I intend to investigate the clan head first. I believe he is connected to the force behind this.”

“Why?”

The reason was Geum Arin.

“The money earned by the secret organization flowed to the force behind it. And since the leader of this secret organization is the daughter of the Golden Dragon Clan’s clan head, it is certain the clan head sent the money.”

If the leader had been someone else, there might have been a slight possibility that one of the leadership had schemed without the clan head’s knowledge. But since it was Geum Arin, the head of the Golden Dragon Clan was definitely involved.

“The problem is how he is connected. Whether he is completely their subordinate, whether he is a sympathizer, or whether he might be under threat. He might even have been deceived.”

There was also a concrete plan.

“However, because they gave us two million nyang this time, the payment has been disrupted. In a situation where they have to hand money over to those people, a major problem has arisen. I intend to focus on that point. From the clan head’s reaction, we should be able to infer his relationship with them.”

A strange light flashed through Geom Ujin’s eyes.

“Did you take the money with all that in mind from the beginning?”

Geom Mugeuk’s shining eyes had already answered yes.

Then Geom Ujin said,

“Is there really any need for me to go as well?”

He meant that if Geom Mugeuk could see through the situation so thoroughly, did he truly need his help?

“There is a need to show them once.”

“Show them what?”

Geom Mugeuk said calmly,

“That Father exists in this murim.”

“!”

Geom Ujin looked at his son without a word, then turned his head toward the window of the racing carriage.

Geom Mugeuk added one more sentence to his father.

“Of course, Father, I know you would prefer to make that known in a different way.”

* * *

They stayed at an inn that day.

At dawn, when everyone was asleep, a masked man quietly appeared. He was none other than I Chu, whom Geum Ajong had sent.

He slipped not into the guest rooms where people were sleeping, but into the stables.

His footsteps made no sound, and even when he opened the carriage door, there was no noise. His movements were such that one could believe he was a thief, or an assassin.

I Chu did not rashly enter the guest room where they were sleeping.

First, he examined the carriage.

After carefully inspecting the compartment inside the carriage, he next examined the driver’s seat. Perhaps their luggage had been moved to the guest room, for there was nothing noteworthy.

In this utterly ordinary carriage, I Chu felt an inexplicable sense of incongruity.

‘Something is strange.’

After looking around for a moment, he realized the source of that incongruity.

‘The carriage is too old compared to the horses.’

The horses were truly fine steeds, but the carriage was far too old.

I Chu slowly examined the carriage. He looked here and there, tapped on it, and inspected it.

As a result, he realized that the exterior of the carriage had been newly covered with a thin plywood panel. It had been deliberately disguised to look like an old carriage.

I Chu scraped the edge of the plywood with a dagger and pried it open slightly.

When he peered into the gap, he could tell that a picture had been drawn on the carriage.

Looking closely, it was shaped like a crescent moon.

“A crescent moon?”

He murmured softly to himself. Was there a sect in the murim that used a crescent moon?

It seemed he might be able to recognize it if he peeled away a little more. But if he did that, it looked as though he would leave traces of having removed the plywood. He thought he should bring materials to reattach the plywood and then peel it off completely.

Before that, just as he was looking into the gap to check the picture once more—

“!”

I Chu was startled out of his wits. It was not because of the picture. Before he knew it, someone had come silently to his side and was peering into the gap in the plywood with him. It was none other than Geom Mugeuk.

“It looks more like a horn than a crescent moon.”

Absolute Regression, Chapter 470

Chapter 470: I Told You I’m a Scammer!
--------------------------------------
Ichu felt as if his breath had stopped.

Had he ever been so surprised in his life? Had he ever allowed someone to approach this close?

Instinctively, Ichu tried to flee, flinging his body away.

The moment his body soared into the air, he felt it. The man who had been standing with him was flying up right beside him. He clung to his side like a shadow.

The moment Geommugeuk grabbed his shoulder, Ichu—who had been trying to escape through the roof—descended back to where they had originally been standing, together with Geommugeuk.

The movement of being caught and brought back down was so smooth that it looked as though the two had taken flight in perfect harmony before descending once more.

Ichu felt his opponent’s martial arts overwhelming his own. The move shown just now was a technique far more difficult than silently approaching and suppressing his acupoints.

“Since we’ve already stripped it, let’s strip it all.”

Geommugeuk grabbed the plywood attached to the carriage and pulled it hard.

Screeeeech.

With that, the wall of the carriage was revealed. The carriage exclusively for the Heavenly Demon, made of a unique metal, revealed itself. It was the sturdiest carriage in all of Murim.

“Honestly, considering the passenger, there was no need to make it this sturdy.”

Ichu couldn’t properly hear Geommugeuk’s words. He stared at the carriage with wide eyes, as if entranced.

The picture drawn on the carriage.

As Geommugeuk had said, it was not a crescent moon but horns.

The terrifying face of an evil ghost with two horns like a demon. Truly, it was a fearsome evil ghost that sent a chill down one’s spine just by looking at it.

Ichu was completely frozen. There was only one organization in Murim that would openly display such a picture. If this was a carriage from that place he was thinking of?

‘I’m dead.’

Truly, when he had entered this stable, he had never dreamed that today would become his death anniversary.

Whether or not Geommugeuk knew what he was thinking, he walked back to his side and stood beside him, speaking nonchalantly.

“Isn’t it frightening? Was there really a need to draw it this realistically and frighteningly? I get chills every time I see it. What about you?”

Geommugeuk looked at him.

Ichu answered in a trembling voice.

“It’s my first time seeing it.”

“That’s right. It is not a picture one can easily see.”

Meaningful words continued from Geommugeuk’s mouth.

“Not just anyone can draw this picture on a carriage. Only a truly high-ranking person can.”

He should have said “only one person,” but he did not reveal that much.

Then, doubt suddenly sprouted in Ichu’s heart.

‘Huh? Are these guys really con artists?’

He thought it might be possible. The truly high-ranking members of the Demonic Cult traveling with just three people like this? Somehow, he couldn’t believe it.

‘Anyone could draw this picture if they just paid a painter, right? And the way they deliberately tore it off for me to see.’

And then, right at that moment.

When he came to his senses, he was standing in the sky. The sky was blue, and his surroundings were blue. It felt as if all this might be a dream. Yes, praying it was a dream, he touched the blue floor.

Splash. It was not the sky, but incredibly clear water. What he was seeing was the sky reflected in the water.

It was Geommugeuk’s aura. The aura released in an instant had pulled him into a new world.

But Geommugeuk’s true aura was beneath his feet.

Invisible energy pulled him into the water.

He kept sinking. The terror of the deep sea, with no visible bottom, assailed him. The fear of that abyss was endless and monstrous.

“Uwaack!”

When Ichu screamed, he was still standing in front of the carriage.

Geommugeuk was looking at him with profound eyes.

Only then could Ichu understand.

‘They are not con artists.’

But he soon corrected that thought.

‘No, they are con artists. The greatest con artists in history.’

He had been cheerfully taking them to the family, not knowing they were demons, and not knowing they were absolute masters of the Demonic Cult. And he had ordered them investigated without knowing any of this.

Ichu squeezed his eyes shut.

“Kill me.”

His voice trembled.

“Have you no last words?”

“Who would I blame? I can only blame my fate for getting entangled in such matters.”

Even with his eyes closed for a moment, his opponent did not kill him. When Ichu opened his eyes, Geommugeuk was still looking at the carriage wall.

“Why won’t you kill me?”

“Why would I kill you for some great wrong? All you did was rummage through the carriage a bit.”

“You people are……”

“Why? Do we look like evil demons who kill anyone who meets our eyes?”

Come to think of it, his acupoints hadn’t even been suppressed yet. He briefly wondered if he should flee even now, but soon gave up.

“Are you sparing me?”

Of course, there was a condition attached.

“Just as the young lady Geum hired us, I too shall hire you. Only until this matter is finished and we depart.”

Ichu was surprised. He had thought he would die without a doubt, yet a path to survival had opened.

“Are you perhaps loyal to Geumajong to the point of giving your life?”

“No.”

“I thought as much.”

The Geumajong known to the public was not a figure capable of earning such loyalty from his subordinates.

“Anyway, his fate will likely have nothing to do with your decision now. So there is no need to feel guilty. I will pay you triple what you are currently receiving.”

“I am only working for him for as much as I was paid anyway. I feel no guilt.”

The problem was this: how this new opponent would treat him. Because this opponent was not a merchant from a rival firm offering more money.

“Will you feed me poison?”

“That will not happen.”

“If I expose your identities? How can you trust me?”

“I don’t trust you.”

Geommugeuk’s gaze turned toward the carriage wall.

“I trust that.”

Ichu’s gaze also turned toward the evil ghost on the carriage wall.

Geommugeuk did not threaten him at all. He did not utter a single common threat about imprisoning and torturing him for life if he betrayed them, or killing him brutally, or killing his family.

That was why it was even more terrifying.

“What must I do?”

* * *

“I intend to intervene in the succession struggle of the Geumryong Family.”

From the running carriage, Geommugeuk’s words were thrown out like a declaration of war, and Geomujin answered.

“Isn’t that your specialty?”

“After all, instigating others’ fights is the most enjoyable thing, is it not?”

Of course, that was not the reason he was intervening.

“If a succession struggle breaks out, everyone’s attention will be on that fight rather than on us. We must use that struggle to find and deal with the ones behind this, then escape. Please leave it to me.”

Geomujin nodded silently, as if telling him to do as he pleased.

Then Hwi spoke from the driver’s seat.

“The horses are tired; we will rest.”

The running carriage slowly came to a stop.

The carriage of Geumarin, which had been running ahead, was waiting where the carriage stopped.

After releasing the horses into the field, Hwi prepared the meal.

“I will prepare this meal.”

“I will help.”

Imhyeok rolled up his sleeves and stepped forward, but Hwi refused.

“Just rest. Take charge of the meal during the next rest.”

“Understood.”

Was it because he recognized him as a fellow bodyguard? Imhyeok was most conscious of Hwi among the three. He felt a strange sense of kinship, and also felt as though he had met a great senior in the bodyguard profession.

Then Geumarin spoke to Geommugeuk.

“Let us talk.”

“Let us.”

Geommugeuk and Geumarin slowly moved their feet.

Imhyeok tried to follow, but Geommugeuk spoke to him.

“When you are with me, you may simply rest.”

“I have never once left the young lady alone.”

Looking at his eyes full of loyalty, Geommugeuk said,

“This time, try it once. If there is ever a time you must do so, today would be the opportune moment.”

Geumarin simply watched as Geommugeuk did this. She thought that when this man acted like this, there must certainly be a reason.

“Are you perhaps going to have a conversation I should not hear?”

Geommugeuk shook his head.

“No. I purely mean for you to rest. I know you won’t rest well because you’ll be worried, but still, I am telling you to rest.”

Geommugeuk’s gaze turned to Hwi standing beside him. These words were also for Hwi—to rest a bit on this journey. These loyal bodyguards were proficient in all matters, but truly, they had never learned how to rest.

Geumarin, who was beside him, nodded with an expression telling Imhyeok to do so.

Imhyeok no longer refused.

“Understood. Please go and return.”

Thus, Geommugeuk and Geumarin left that place.

Perhaps because he felt uneasy seeing them off, Imhyeok stared at their retreating figures for a long time.

Imhyeok circled around Hwi, unable to leave his side.

“Do you have something to say?”

“No.”

In the end, the hesitating Imhyeok brought up what he had been holding back.

“I do. May I ask just one thing?”

“Go ahead.”

He wasn’t sure if they were close enough for such a question.

“It is about your niece. Did you think that my always guarding the young lady’s side is rather hindering her growth as a martial artist? I ask because I wondered if that is why you told me to rest.”

He had used Geommugeuk as an excuse, but this was his own worry. He had truly cherished Geumarin like a daughter, so he had always tried to protect her. But could that be ruining her? He had agonized over this.

He wanted to ask Hwi about this matter. Because he felt Hwi would give him an answer.

“I do not know your niece’s thoughts. Perhaps it could be so, or it could simply be words meant to let you rest.”

Having spoken as if to brush it off as unknown, Hwi raised his head and looked at Imhyeok.

“Whatever it is, what does it have to do with us?”

“Pardon?”

“Isn’t growth as a martial artist a separate matter from us? Those who will grow well will do so regardless of whether they have bodyguards or not. Isn’t it overstepping our duties for us, mere bodyguards, to worry that far?”

It was a simple answer telling him not to become too emotionally invested in his charge, but he felt his pent-up heart loosening.

How could he not have thought that himself?

But even after worrying a hundred times, he couldn’t reach an answer.

Yet when someone else said it, he felt refreshed. And since it was someone who felt like a great senior in the bodyguard profession, he thought he could simply follow these words.

“Yes, you are right. It is not a matter for me to involve myself in. Thank you truly for your teaching.”

Today Hwi had spoken more than usual. Just as Hwi felt special to Imhyeok, Hwi also felt it was a special connection. Because he had met a junior from the martial world who worried about his bodyguard duties. That was why he said this much.

“Your niece’s words are likely correct. That it will be fine when she is with you. So think of it as your last vacation and enjoy it.”

Imhyeok felt a strange feeling.

With these people, it was difficult to refuse. Geommugeuk, this bodyguard, and that man standing with his back turned up ahead.

They all felt like people he could not handle.

Imhyeok’s gaze turned in the direction Geumarin had disappeared.

‘So the young lady must choose well.’

Geommugeuk and Geumarin stood side by side on the hill.

The scenery viewed from the hill was beautiful.

The reeds rippled like living things, and beyond them, the river water sparkled in the sunlight, flowing with elegant grace.

“I have something to say to you.”

Geumarin had worried about one matter the entire ride in the carriage. And she had reached a conclusion. She was trying to convey that conclusion to Geommugeuk.

“The reason I made a contract with you is not because you made a deal with the Eunha Merchant Guild and tried to kill me.”

“Then what is it?”

“I wanted help in the succession struggle.”

She spoke honestly. This decision was also instinct. Choosing Geommugeuk was instinct, and being honest was instinct. If this instinct was wrong, she would be ruined.

“Make me the successor of the Geumryong Family.”

Geommugeuk was not very surprised.

“You knew.”

“I had roughly guessed.”

Geumarin thought she had spoken well. Even if she perished, the sense of stability this man gave her felt truly good. That must be why she could ask such a question.

“Can you make me the successor?”

“That depends on you.”

Geommugeuk’s gaze toward her was serious.

“How far can you trust me?”

* * *

Geumajong was also returning to the family.

He was traveling even faster than Geommugeuk and Geumarin’s carriage. He had returned to the family first and sent Ichu to investigate Geommugeuk.

And Ichu returned, having been awaited.

“Did you find anything?”

“Yes, I did.”

Ichu spoke in a lowered voice.

“The young master’s prediction was correct. I was fortunate enough to overhear their conversation; I heard them say they would pull off another big score at the family.”

“Right? I told you they were con artists! Those damned con artist bastards!”

Geumajong was overjoyed that his prediction was correct.

“Evidence?”

“I haven’t found any yet.”

Geumajong’s expression darkened. Without clear evidence, it was useless. Even if Ichu had heard them, they would insist it was a frame-up since he was Geumajong’s subordinate.

“We must find evidence. Only then can we get rid of Arin.”

Then Ichu spoke cautiously.

“May I share my thoughts?”

“Speak.”

“Use them to entangle not the Third Young Lady, but the Great Young Master.”

It was an unexpected remark.

“My brother?”

“Isn’t the Great Young Master the young master’s true competitor?”

It was a valid point. He had worried that his father might make his younger sibling the successor, but that was close to paranoia. The likely successor right now was his older brother. Perhaps he was tormenting his younger sibling as an escape from reality, lacking the courage to confront his older brother.

“It’s a good idea, but how?”

“They are con artists, are they not? Throw the Great Young Master to them as bait.”

Geumajong’s eyes widened. It was a striking statement. If he could connect these guys to his brother? He could thoroughly shatter their father’s and the family martial artists’ trust.

“Will my brother fall for it easily?”

“We have to make him fall for it.”

“How?”

“I do not know beyond that.”

He had been told to only speak up to this point, because speaking too perfectly would instead arouse suspicion. And that the rest would be handled on its own. Since Geumajong was someone with considerable skill in scheming, he would figure it out well.

As expected by Geommugeuk, Geumajong began to rack his brains.

“Pull my brother into those con artist bastards’ trap?”

His eyes gleamed cunningly as he sought a method.

For the scions of upper-class families, money scandals were indeed a fatal thing. Since his younger sibling had caused a two million nyang scandal…

“What size scandal should I make my brother cause?”

Absolute Regression, Episode 471

Chapter 471: Can You Collect the Debt?
--------------------------------------
The carriage passed through Chongqing and headed for Shaanxi.

Since they were traveling with Geum Arin, they decided to stop by the secret vault the head of the Eunha Merchant Guild had given them as thanks on the way back.

“Aren’t you curious what’s in there?”

Even at Geom Mugeuk’s question, Geom Ujin merely gazed out the window, as if he had no interest whatsoever.

“Please give me thirty chances to choose too!”
