At the words “thirty chances,” Geom Ujin turned his head and looked at his son.

“I figured I’d have to start around there if I wanted to get even three later.”

Geom Mugeuk saw it—the corner of his father’s mouth wavering, unsure whether to rise or not. Yes, he’d at least give him three.

Geom Mugeuk followed his father’s gaze and looked out the window.

“How long has it been since you were last in Chongqing?”

“A long time.”

Geom Ujin thought of the Chongqing of the past, while Geom Mugeuk thought of the Chongqing of the future. Long ago, Geom Ujin had roamed through this place with the Fist Demon, and Geom Mugeuk had wandered here searching for materials for the great ritual.

Just then, Geom Ujin looked at an inn up ahead and said,

“Let us rest there today.”

At his father’s words, Hwi answered.

“Understood.”

Until now, his father had silently followed the schedule as Hwi and Im Hyeok led it. If they went to an inn, he went; if they camped outdoors, he camped.

But today, he was the first to say they should rest at that inn.

After sending word to Geum Arin’s carriage ahead, Hwi brought the carriage to the inn.

Geom Mugeuk was curious. Had there been some meaningful memory here in the past? Perhaps the innkeeper might remember his father. Thank you so much for helping me back then.

But no such thing happened.

‘Then why did he suggest staying here?’

After they unpacked, Geom Ujin took Hwi and went outside. He did not bring Geom Mugeuk.

“You stay here and wait.”

“Please take me with you. Where are you two going by yourselves?”

“You need not know.”

Leaving only that question behind, Geom Ujin and Hwi left the inn.

After the two had walked for quite some time, Geom Ujin asked quietly,

“This is the place, is it not?”

At Geom Ujin’s question, Hwi asked with a surprised expression,

“You knew?”

Geom Ujin nodded. This was Hwi’s hometown. Even Hwi himself had not imagined that the Cult Leader had known it was his hometown and had stopped here for that reason.

“I left when I was young, so I feel nothing in particular. My parents passed away, and I left right afterward.”

“Even so, since it lies along our path, you should visit at least once. Where is your old home?”

“It is a mountain village beyond that ridge.”

Geom Ujin moved first, and Hwi quietly followed behind him.

He was surprised that the Cult Leader remembered his hometown, and even more surprised that he had stopped there and was now going with him like this.

‘The Cult Leader truly has changed.’

Hwi had watched him more closely than anyone, right at Geom Ujin’s side. The Cult Leader was a man with deep affection within him, but he was not someone who expressed it like this. Hwi knew better than anyone where this change had come from.

Hwi thought of Geom Mugeuk. Throughout this journey, the Young Cult Leader had kept pushing him to take time for himself.

—Why are you going this far for me?

—These days, a wave of change is sweeping through our cult, isn’t it? I’m trying to make sure you’re not left out, Uncle.

Yes, he understood better than anyone what kind of change that was. That was why he had been able to ask.

—Where is this change leading us?

The Young Cult Leader, who had created so many changes, had answered like this.

—I do not know either.

When he heard that he did not know, Hwi had instead felt relieved.

And so the two arrived at the place where Hwi had been born.

Surprisingly, the house was still there, though other people were living in it.

No one remembered Hwi. It would have been stranger for anyone to remember a boy who had left the village as a child, or the middle-aged martial artist who had returned.

He had said he felt nothing for his hometown, but the house where he had lived was different.

His childhood came back to him, and he was immersed in memories.

What he especially remembered was his mother calling out for him to come eat when he was playing with his friends in the alley in front of the house. The memory of the children scattering one by one and running home came back to him faintly.

After looking around the house and the alley before it for a long while, the two ate noodles together at a small noodle shop in the village.

Geom Mugeuk kept telling him that he had to change, that he should change, but Hwi himself had already changed a great deal. After all, was he not sitting in the same place as the Cult Leader, eating noodles together?

Without a word, they finished a bowl of noodles and returned to the inn.

They did not exchange any special conversation. But even with just that, Hwi could fully feel Geom Ujin’s heart. In his own way, the Cult Leader had shown that he cherished him.

At the entrance of the inn, Hwi said,

“Thank you.”

Then Geom Ujin replied,

“I am the one who should be grateful.”

It was Geom Ujin’s heart for the man who had always protected him. He had now begun expressing himself to those closest to him.

At that time, Geom Mugeuk was using the Space-Time Transposition Art to train in martial arts through the Sicheon Secret Art. Throughout the journey here, whenever he had time, he had focused on martial training.

He always thought that his strength lay not in his techniques, but in his relationships with people—but such a thought was only possible when he could block the enemy’s attacks as they came flying at him. For the days spent together with others to shine, he had to shed blood and sweat through the nights.

And so Geom Mugeuk’s nights were long.

* * *

The carriage ran and ran, finally arriving at the Golden Dragon Clan in Shaanxi.

Geom Mugeuk parted with his father at a place where the clan’s buildings could be seen in the distance.

“I will go ahead as the scouting party.”

He had judged that there was no need to go to the Golden Dragon Clan with his father from the very beginning.

“The main character is supposed to appear later, is he not?”

No matter how one tried to hide it, how could his father’s presence be concealed?

Moreover, since they did not know how the head of the clan would react, it was the wiser choice for him to go alone first and gauge the atmosphere.

Geom Ujin also guessed his son’s intention, and so he accepted it without objection. Besides, he also had to receive the report from Tongcheon Pavilion and take care of piled-up matters.

“I will wait at the safe house.”

There was a safe house of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult not far away, so if help was needed, he would be able to come quickly.

“I will send word.”

The carriage carrying his father departed in the opposite direction.

Geom Mugeuk rejoined Geum Arin’s carriage.

Geum Arin nearly said that it might be better for the two of them to come as well, but stopped herself. Trust him. Trust this man. He must have thought it through.

The carriage arrived at the entrance of the Golden Dragon Clan.

Above the magnificent main gate hung a golden signboard, its calligraphy powerful, as if a dragon were sweeping through the air in flight.

The gate opened, and the carriage entered.

Those guarding the entrance were imposing like martial artists of a martial household, with the eyes of men well trained.

Inside the gate, the broad courtyard, gardens, and pond were arranged in harmonious beauty. The buildings were grand, and golden dragons symbolizing the Golden Dragon Clan were carved here and there.

There were many intentions visible everywhere, all meant to overwhelm those who entered, but they had no effect on Geom Mugeuk, who had grown up looking at the enormous demon statues of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.

As they drew closer to the clan head’s hall, he could feel Geum Arin growing even more tense.

“Are you nervous?”

At Geom Mugeuk’s question, Geum Arin nodded that she was. Now was not the time to hide her feelings, but to prepare for her father’s reaction.

“My father may roar at you.”

“You need not worry about that. When it comes to ‘terrifying fathers,’ I have more experience than anyone.”

“That’s not what I meant!”

Are you truly not worried? How could he be this calm?

Waiting in front of the clan head’s hall was the second son, Geum Ajong, who had arrived first.

“You have come?”

Having completely misunderstood Geom Mugeuk’s identity, he looked at him with a different feeling from before.

‘You little swindler! You adorable bastard who will change my fate!’

His heart was filled with the desire to quickly connect this swindler with his elder brother and bring his brother to ruin.

“Was the journey here difficult?”

“It was fine.”

“Let us share a drink when there is time. Since you are a guest of our house, I will treat you.”

“Let us do that.”

Geom Mugeuk readily accepted his offer.

After parting from Geum Ajong, Geom Mugeuk and Geum Arin entered the clan head’s hall.

As they walked down the corridor, Geum Arin said,

“This is suspicious.”

“What is?”

“There is no way my elder brother would be kind to you. He is overdoing it right now.”

She certainly was quick-witted.

“There is no harm in becoming close, is there?”

“You just saw him. Someone there is harm in becoming close to.”

And so the two entered the clan head’s hall.

There, the clan head, Geum Cheonbang, was waiting.

Unlike ordinary magnates, who tended to have pleasant faces and impressive bearing, Geum Cheonbang had a small build and a somewhat irritable appearance. Fortunately, it seemed both Geum Arin and Geum Ajong had taken after their mother.

Considering that he had made the Golden Dragon Clan one of the top five in the Central Plains with such an impression, one could say his ability was all the more extraordinary.

Geum Arin had thought her father might thunder at her as soon as he saw her, but unexpectedly, her father was calm.

“I have returned, Father.”

“You have come?”

Geum Cheonbang’s gaze turned toward Geom Mugeuk.

“I am Geom Yeon of the Seodo Sect.”

When he greeted him politely with a fist-and-palm salute, Geum Cheonbang also received the greeting gently.

“It is good to meet you.”

Geum Arin grew even more tense at the sight of her father smiling as he looked at Geom Mugeuk. He was not the kind of person to smile like that at someone who had taken an enormous sum of two million nyang.

Moreover, this man’s father and the man said to be his uncle had not even come with him. In other words, her father might think that after swallowing two million nyang, they had sent a young fellow in their place.

But her father did not reveal the slightest displeasure. That was why she felt even more uneasy. Her elder brother, and now her father as well. They were both acting outside her expectations.

“I have heard the story. You have decided to help our Arin?”

“That is correct.”

“My daughter has a good eye for people.”

“Seeing as she chose me, it certainly seems that way.”

Even Geom Mugeuk’s joke was met with a smile by Geum Cheonbang.

“But I heard there were three of you?”

“My father and uncle had somewhere to stop by briefly, so they decided to come later.”

“I see.”

This was precisely the reason Geum Cheonbang was being cautious.

‘The head of the Eunha Merchant Guild personally went to meet these people?’

He knew better than anyone what kind of position the head of the Eunha Merchant Guild held. He was a man who summoned others to where he was, no matter who the other party might be.

If such a man had moved personally, then they must surely be special people.

As if to prove it, they had even dared to break the contract they had made with him and enter a new one with his daughter.

‘Just how great are their skills?’

Geom Mugeuk was also observing him closely.

That was because Geom Mugeuk considered this Geum Cheonbang the core of the matter.

‘Do you know to whom your money is going?’

And so their probing of one another came to an end.

The first to speak was Geum Cheonbang.

“I am a merchant. My principle is that goods must be bought at their proper price and sold at their proper price.”

And a man like that ran a secret organization and tried to absorb the Ju Clan Sword Family?

“It must be because you possess such excellent principles that you have built such a great enterprise.”

“I am grateful you say so. In that case, may I ask one favor of you?”

It meant that since he had received two million nyang, he should prove his worth.

“What favor?”

“The Golden Dragon Clan conducts countless transactions. Even at this very moment, while I am speaking with you, transactions are surely being made somewhere. There are good transactions, but naturally there are bad ones as well. There are several places that have caused us great losses and then washed their hands of it. Among them, there is one that has tormented us for a long time.”

Geum Cheonbang looked at Geom Mugeuk and narrowed his eyes.

“Could you go there and collect the debt for us?”

“Who are those shameless people?”

Geum Cheonbang let out a deliberate sigh and answered.

“The Baixian Gang.”

The Baixian Gang. Though its name meant a gang where a hundred immortals gathered, if it had truly been a place where such people gathered, they would not have named it that in the first place.

The Baixian Gang was, quite literally, a den of demons. Geom Mugeuk knew of them as well.

Only now did Geum Arin understand why her father had been so kind. He did not intend to let it end with merely getting angry and shouting.

‘He is trying to kill this man.’

The Baixian Gang was more than an old headache for the Golden Dragon Clan—it was an utterly wicked and dangerous existence.

Even they, the Geumryong Family, had yet to recover the money that had been extorted from them. Telling these people to collect it was a request laced with malice far beyond a mere test of skill.

“Father, entrusting that task to them is…”

At that moment, Geum Cheonbang looked at her with icy eyes. Geum Arin could say no more. She could feel just how furious her father was.

Geom Mugeuk, on the other hand, was at ease.

“How much money is owed?”

“The amount has accumulated over the years. The sum to be collected is three million nyang.”

It would be more accurate to say it was money they had extorted under all sorts of pretexts. It was money they did not even recognize as having borrowed, and for that very reason, collecting it was impossible. Baekseonbang was crawling with experts, and they were men whose viciousness had reached its peak.

Geum Cheonbang wondered how Geom Mugeuk would react.

‘Will he accept even in a situation like this?’

This was a kind of probing exchange, and a test as well. A test to see how he would react.

Geum Arin hurriedly sent Geom Mugeuk a voice transmission.

—Refuse! No matter how capable you are, you can’t deal with them!

She had staked everything on this man. She could not lose him like this. She had to stop him, even if she had to throw herself in his way.

Of course, Geom Mugeuk was not someone who moved according to her wishes.

—When you asked me to make you the successor, what did I ask you?

She remembered the question Geom Mugeuk had asked her.

How far can you trust me?

Even so, to be put to such a test already…

While she hesitated to answer, Geom Mugeuk gave his reply first.

“I’ll collect it.”

Geum Cheonbang smiled strangely.

‘He’ll do it? Is that a decision he made because he doesn’t know who Baekseonbang is? Or because he does?’

Of course, Geom Mugeuk was not the sort to accept such a demand meekly.

“Then what share will you give me in return?”

“What?”

To the startled Geum Cheonbang, Geom Mugeuk explained calmly.

“I am not your subordinate, Family Head. I am someone who has joined hands with Miss Geum here, am I not? Right now, I am about to do something my leader is trying to stop me from doing, so naturally I should receive appropriate compensation. Am I wrong in what I’m asking?”

Geum Cheonbang could not refute him. It was true enough. Strictly speaking, he was not a subordinate Geum Cheonbang had hired.

But he had already taken two million nyang, and now he was asking for more? Under normal circumstances it would have been an absurd demand, but the present situation was special.

“How much do you want?”

“You said it has been a long time since you were able to collect the money, so it is a bad debt. You must have already assumed you would never receive it anyway. And since even you, Family Head, could not collect it, they must be that dangerous.”

After laying down one premise after another, Geom Mugeuk made a bold move.

“I will need half.”

Absolute Regression, Chapter 472

Chapter 472: Whether Money or Blood Will Pour.
----------------------------------------------
“Half, you say?”

Geumcheonbang could not hide his surprise. It was an excessive demand, just as excessive as his own unreasonable request.

“In such cases, the usual commission is ten percent. Even if they offer a generous sum, the matter is handled for twenty percent of the money.”

“That would be true for an ordinary opponent.”

Rather, Geommuguk went a step further.

“Truthfully, even if you gave me the entire sum, it would still be a gain for the Family Head.”

“And what sophistry is that?”

At that, Geommuguk raised his voice and spoke with vigor.

“If you do business with the Geumryong Sega, you must pay back the money.”

Those pleasant words echoed loudly throughout the hall.

“No one can ever cheat the Geumryong Sega out of its money.”

Thinking of all the money he had failed to collect for this reason and that, how wonderful would it be if things could truly be as those words said? It was the kind of statement that brought a smile to one’s lips just thinking about it.

Having captured his interest with such pleasing words, Geumcheonbang naturally inclined his ear to what followed.

“What do you think of this rumor? They say even the Baekseonbang paid back the money.”

What more needed to be said? It was a tremendous rumor indeed.

“If it truly comes to that, no one going forward will even think of cheating the Geumryong Sega out of its money.”

Geommuguk gazed directly into Geumcheonbang’s eyes and asked.

“How much do you think that value is worth?”

Even Geumcheonbang, who was quick with calculations, could not answer easily. Considering not just the present but the future as well, it was not something that could be converted into money.

“That only applies once the money has actually been collected from them, does it not?”

To Geumcheonbang’s words, Geommuguk answered with a gaze full of conviction.

“I will collect it, and I am saying I will take only half of that money.”

Though “as much as half” had become “only half,” Geumcheonbang felt no sense of incongruity whatsoever.

On top of that, it even became an act of favor.

“I have made a special concession, thinking of your daughter.”

Curiosity flickered in Geumcheonbang’s eyes. *He’s quite something*, he thought. Yes, this was how one negotiated. Bold to the point of shamelessness, yet persuasive.

*Indeed, the Eunha Merchant Group Leader would not go to meet just anyone.*

Could it be? For a moment, he thought that perhaps the money might actually be collected.

But he soon dismissed the notion.

He knew well what the Baekseonbang was.

Ordinary people inevitably perceived them as cruel, emotional, and unreasonable. Truly ignorant brutes with whom reasoning was impossible.

At first, Geumcheonbang had thought so too. But now he knew. The Bangju who led them was a clever person. Their revenge, too—their legal plunder—all sprang from thorough calculation.

*Can you really handle such a difficult person?*

Even while thinking him no match, the fact that curiosity arose meant this young man’s display was impressive indeed. Such spirit was rare to see these days.

“What will you do if you cannot collect it?”

He had expected him to say he would return the two million taels received from his daughter, but.

“If I cannot collect it, then I cannot collect it. Would I easily collect money that even the Family Head could not?”

Drawing him in like that, Geumcheonbang could offer no rebuttal. He admired Geommuguk’s response.

“Will you give me half?”

It was money he could not collect anyway. If he could secure even 1.5 million taels out of three million, it was profit.

Moreover, if the man truly collected it, he would gain a reputation more precious than money, just as this fellow had said.

*If he fails?*

The Baekseonbang would likely let him off with moderate injuries before sending him back. Since they owed a debt to the Geumryong Sega, they would not make a major incident of it.

If this fellow’s spirit was broken, it would be easy to collect the two million taels from him going forward.

Thus Geumcheonbang’s calculations concluded.

“Very well, I shall give you half.”

Geum Arin, who had been watching, could not hold back and interjected.

“You cannot!”

She had tried not to step forward knowing her father was angry at her, but if she did not stop this now, it would be too late later.

“Are you making this promise knowing how dangerous this matter is?”

At that, Geommuguk looked at her and spoke calmly.

“I know how large a sum two million taels is. I also know that 1.5 million taels is a large sum. To earn large sums of money, must one not naturally take risks?”

Geumcheonbang liked those words. He detested people who tried to earn money without cost. To earn big money, one had to embrace big risks.

Geommuguk’s gaze turned from Geum Arin back to Geumcheonbang.

“As expected, to earn money, one must jump into the major leagues. The moment I arrive, money pours in.”

To Geommuguk’s composure, Geumcheonbang responded with composure of his own.

“Whether money pours or blood pours, we shall see when it is over.”

The two men faced each other. At their first meeting, which could have ended as a mere probing bout, a grand bargain was struck.

“Then I shall see you again after I have collected the money.”

As Geommuguk politely bowed and made to leave, Geum Arin spoke.

“Please wait outside for a moment. I have something to discuss with my father.”

“Let us do so.”

Once Geommuguk exited the Patriarch’s chamber, only Geumcheonbang and Geum Arin remained.

Geumcheonbang had a guess as to what his daughter was going to say.

“You know he will absolutely fail, do you not? Yet you are having him do this to punish me, are you not? That is too much.”

Geumcheonbang responded coldly.

“It is you who has gone too far.”

“What do you mean?”

“It means you brought a source of future trouble to the main house.”

“The Eunha Merchant Group was trying to bring them in to strike at my organization. I could not simply stand by and watch.”

Strictly speaking, it was a matter of her own life.

Her life was on the line, yet he flew into a rage over money!

She forcibly swallowed the words that had risen to her throat. After all, she had been in the wrong for spending two million taels without permission.

“When you learned of that fact, you should have returned to the family immediately and discussed it with this father of yours.”

Geum Arin also had something to say about that.

“Then my older brothers would have seized upon it as a pretext. They would say I could not handle my own affairs and was hiding in my father’s embrace.”

“It was still rash. What if that man is a spy the Eunha Merchant Group planted among us?”

“Is there such a loud and conspicuous spy in this world?”

Geumcheonbang said nothing more.

Geum Arin left those final words and departed.

“He is not a source of trouble, but a blessing. Though you have thrown that blessing into a cesspit.”

Geum Arin exited the Patriarch’s chamber.

Her chest felt tight. And of all places, it had to be the Baekseonbang.

Geommuguk was waiting for her outside the building.

“Are you really going to do this?”

Geommuguk nodded and moved his steps. The two walked side by side, conversing.

“Do you know what kind of place the Baekseonbang is?”

“What kind of place is it?”

With an expression that said *I knew you would ask this*, Geum Arin explained the people she did not even want to think about.

“It is a gathering of human trash. And that trash is not local ruffians, but experts who soar and crawl. Especially the Bangju who leads them is known as a peerless expert.”

Geommuguk merely walked in silence.

“That is not all. What makes them truly terrifying is that if one of them dies, the rest swarm and take revenge without fail. They are cruel people who kill even friends and family.”

That extensive vengeance was the greatest reason the Baekseonbang had become a symbol of terror. At the same time, it was also a reason they deserved to die.

“How have such people survived until now? Has the Murim Alliance simply let them be?”

“They never leave behind evidence. I heard that if someone does leave evidence, they eliminate that person themselves to cut off the tail. In the end, there are only abundant rumors about them.”

In his life before regression, the Baekseonbang had eventually been subjugated by the Murim Alliance. The Alliance had found evidence of their evil deeds and attacked; the brutes had resisted fiercely but were annihilated. In that process, a considerable number of the Alliance’s experts had been injured or killed, he knew.

Seeing that in this life, the Baekseonbang was forming a connection with him, he could truly feel anew the fate that those who must die would die.

“Did you hear me? They kill even innocent families.”

“My family is fine.”

Geommuguk added with a smile.

“So fine that it is problematic.”

Geum Arin knew where that confidence stemmed from.

“I know well that you trust my father, but……”

She walked in silence for a moment, then made a great resolve.

“Fine, then I shall go with you.”

Though it was a life-risking resolve, she was refused instantly.

“Do you wish for them to extort more money?”

“What do you mean?”

“If you visit, do you think they will simply let you be? They will pick a fight somehow, capture you, then demand from your father. ‘Bring money if you want your daughter back.’”

They were exactly the type to do such a thing, so she could not argue.

“Then what about you? Will you be alright?”

“I will be fine. My father will not pay a single penny.”

A joke at a time like this? She thought Geommuguk’s composure was excessive. If only he would calmly explain what he was thinking, but Geommuguk headed in a different direction from where he needed to go.

“Where are you going? The guest quarters are this way.”

“I have somewhere to be. See you later.”

Geum Arin watched Geommuguk’s retreating figure as he strode away and let out a sigh she had been holding in. Now she understood. Why he had said those words.

How far can you trust me?

How far will you make me worry?

* * *

That night, Geum Arin came to Geommuguk’s quarters.

Though it was late, Geommuguk was awake.

“Did I disturb your sleep?”

“It is fine, please come in. We are people who are busy at night.”

“Pardon?”

Geommuguk merely wore an inscrutable smile.

Geum Arin entered. Seeing the liquor bottle on the table, it seemed he had been drinking.

“Will you have a drink?”

“Please.”

She was truly in a state where liquor came to mind unbidden.

After returning to the family, she had never dreamed she would experience such things.

“Have you contacted your father and uncle?”

“What contact do you mean?”

“The matter of the Baekseonbang. You should inform them of it.”

She asked with a feeling of *surely not*.

“Surely you are not thinking of going alone?”

She had thought it dangerous even if the three of them went together.

“I am going alone.”

Geum Arin was startled. Seeing Geommuguk’s calm eyes, she could tell it was not a joke.

She downed the drink she had received earlier in one go and set down the empty cup with a sharp clack.

“This is a two-million-tael drinking session.”

It was a joke that this would be the last time she saw him. A joke, yet not a joke.

“Fine, let us say you are a peerless expert unknown to me.”

It was possible. He had killed Jeonnak and made those money-grubbers so obedient.

“No matter what, you must formulate a plan. The opponent numbers a hundred strong. Whom to seek out first among them, what to say when you arrive, what to do if they do not hand over the money willingly. No, since they will definitely not give the money, you must think in advance about how to respond.”

“I am formulating one.”

“Or at least find their weakness, right? What did you say?”

“I said I was formulating a plan.”

Only then did the papers stacked on the table enter her eyes. On the topmost paper, the characters “Baekseonbang” were visible.

“What is this?”

“Materials on the Baekseonbang.”

“Where did you get them?”

“I bought them from an information broker.”

In truth, he had received these materials through Eunwol. The Eunwol Seomseo Branch had naturally conducted investigations on the Baekseonbang, an organization of interest in this region.

“When?”

“I went out and bought them earlier.”

“May I look?”

Geommuguk nodded, and Geum Arin examined the materials.

They were truly materials about them. Who the Bangju was, what martial arts he possessed, who his subordinates were and how much they trusted each other. What their personalities were like and what affairs they handled. All the evil deeds each had committed were recorded.

That was not all. It detailed which merchant groups and martial sects they had borrowed money from, how much they had swindled, and what businesses they were running with that money. They were involved in all manner of vested interests, reaping enormous profits.

“Information brokers sell even such detailed information.”

“There is no information that cannot be obtained if one has money.”

She felt embarrassed. She had come to someone so thoroughly prepared and told him to make a plan with mere words. If she had truly been worried, she should have bought this information and given it to him. She was not an intellect that failed to act, but a worrier who failed to act.

“So have you made a plan?”

“I have.”

Truly, no one could match such a refreshing answer.

“Tell me.”

“It is a secret.”

Seeing him like this, a vain hope arose that perhaps he really would collect the money.

“Instead, I have a request for you.”

“What is it?”

It was truly something she had never imagined.

“Go to the merchant groups and martial sects written there and tell them you will collect the money swindled by the Baekseonbang on their behalf. In exchange, say you will take half as a commission.”

“What?”

So surprised was Geum Arin that she sprang to her feet. Truly, when one is too surprised, one leaps up.

“They would trust you going more than me.”

Geum Arin was dazed, not knowing what to say. While she stood thus, Geommuguk’s words continued.

“I could take all their money for myself. But this is the better way. You will gain the reputation of having recovered their money, they will retrieve money they thought lost, and I will gain half the profit.”

Geommuguk pointed out that, at least for her, this matter held a different meaning.

“This matter will play a great role in you becoming the successor. Your reputation in Seomseo will rise greatly.”

Geum Arin could tell Geommuguk was sincere.

“You truly intend to collect all that money.”

All of it? All the money from those who were victimized by the Baekseonbang?

“That is right. I will collect the money for all who wish it.”

“Even collecting our money is impossible……”

In an instant, a single thought struck her mind like lightning.

“Surely you do not mean……?”

It was the most surprising moment since meeting Geommuguk.

“You intend to kill them all.”

Absolute Regression, Chapter 473.

Chapter 473: I'm Here to Collect the Money I Lent You.
------------------------------------------------------
Geum Arin experienced it for the first time.

That such calm killing intent could exist.

“So you’re going to kill them all?”

Now she understood.

Why Geom Mugeuk had accepted her father’s request and asked for half. And why he had told her to tell the other merchant guilds and martial sects that he would collect their money.

“If it were a paltry sum, perhaps, but surely you know there’s no way they would hand over such a massive amount willingly.”

Then Geom Mugeuk spoke.

“This matter is not about collecting the money. The problem is what comes after receiving it.”

“What do you mean?”

“Didn’t you say it yourself? They are people who take revenge by killing even family members. If they are robbed of such a huge sum, how do you think they will react?”

“They’ll surely take revenge.”

Geom Mugeuk calmly nodded.

“It is not I who kill them. The way they have lived their lives is what kills them.”

She could tell that her premonition was certain. Now what she wondered was this.

‘How?’

Surely, he couldn’t possess enough strength to annihilate Baekseonbang all by himself?

Geom Mugeuk said to her.

“Now, go and bring in as much as you can. Persuading them is up to you. Think of it as the process of becoming the successor.”

Geom Mugeuk placed a paper with the names of the merchant guilds and sects written on it into Geum Arin’s hand and ushered her outside.

“Leave right now. Start with the ones with the largest sums.”

“No, don’t push me. Let’s talk more.”

“I have much to do. I’m a man who is busy at night.”

And so she was chased out of the room as if pushed.

Outside the closed door, she raised her voice.

“Your leader is me. I give the orders.”

Have you ever been chased out by a subordinate worth a whopping two million nyang?

Then Geom Mugeuk’s answer came from inside.

“A leader who just sits and gives orders has no charm. Now, Leader, please be on your way.”

* * *

For several days, Geum Arin busily traveled back and forth to each merchant guild and martial sect.

Just as Geom Mugeuk had ordered, she secretly visited the guilds and sects and obtained their permission.

“I intend to collect the money extorted by Baekseonbang. I will receive it together with you when the time comes, so please give half of it to us as a service fee.”

If she had required a contract, they would not have agreed. They would have feared Baekseonbang’s retaliation. But she did not ask for a contract. She merely checked the IOUs to see how much had been lent.

A verbal promise was easy. Because they could simply deny it later if they claimed otherwise. Because they could insist that the Geumryong Sega had dragged them in just to create a justification.

If someone else had come and said this, they would never have promised even verbally.

But if it were the Geumryong Sega? If it was a promise made in person by a blood descendant of the Geumryong Sega, the richest family in Seomseo and one of the top five—no, top three—in the Central Plains? Everyone pinned their hopes on it. Perhaps because it seemed feasible, they had refrained from stepping forward themselves.

Thus, Geum Arin obtained permission from the places that had massive sums extorted from them. Among them were places whose livelihoods had declined because they had continuously had money taken by those bastards. They placed their hopes on her promise.

Geum Arin returned and handed over the paper with the amounts that each merchant guild and martial sect had lent written on it.

“Now then, what are you planning to do?”

Geom Mugeuk rose from his seat and said.

“I shall go and return.”

Geum Arin blocked his path.

This man was truly planning to go alone to face a full hundred martial experts, and no less, a den filled with nothing but villains.

“Even if I try to stop you, you’re going, aren’t you?”

She wanted to go with him too. But how could she not know that she would only be a burden if she went along?

“Making money is a difficult thing.”

Leaving those words behind, Geom Mugeuk departed lightly.

She couldn’t say anything to him. She should have said “Be careful,” but those words kept sticking in her throat.

‘You might as well run away down this road.’

Surprisingly, she wanted to say this to him.

If he did, she would never be able to become the successor, and a label of being scammed out of two million nyang would be attached to her for life, yet even so.

But why had she wanted to say such a thing?

Yes, because she had thought that this person, at least, was someone who shouldn’t die at the hands of Baekseonbang’s villains. Because she had thought that that bright face smiling so leisurely should not be torn apart and killed by such lowly villains.

Geum Arin sighed and looked out the window.

She had a premonition that the time spent waiting for him to return would truly feel long. She should have at least said this.

‘You don’t have to bring the money, just come back safely.’

* * *

“A visitor from the Geumryong Sega has arrived.”

At the subordinate’s report, Yeom Je (廉制), the Bangju of Baekseonbang, raised his head.

With a long beard and a white changsam robe, he truly looked like a seonsin.

But neither his dignified white beard nor his snow-white clothes could hide the glint in his eyes filled with coldness and cunning.

If he truly possessed a heart like that of an immortal, he would not have been able to keep the villains gathered in this great hall as his subordinates.

And had he died by any of their blades, it would have happened many times over. This place was hell, a place where only evil spirits could survive.

“Who has come?”

“A young man, green behind the ears; a face I had never seen.”

Yeom Je tilted his head slightly. They had sent someone he’d never seen before from the Geumryong Sega?

“What did he say he came for?”

“He said he wished to meet you and speak in person.”

“Make up a suitable excuse and send him back.”

“Yes.”

Yeom Je was displeased. Would he even meet the Gaju if the man came in person? And they send some greenhorn whose face he doesn’t even know? Regardless of the business, he felt offended.

“It seems I need to discipline the Geumryong Sega once.”

Then Ilseon, who was below the high seat, received his words.

“I shall have the Gaju summoned shortly.”

Nine seonsins were lined up beside Ilseon. They were precisely the Sipseon (十仙) who always guarded Yeom Je’s side. The ranks first through tenth in Baekseonbang were them.

Those present here today were not only them. The vast great hall was filled with the seonsins of Baekseonbang.

“Sipchilseon has finished the accounting!”

Today was the monthly accounting day, when they reported the money they had earned and presented it to Yeom Je. When the accounting finished, they would all drink and hold a banquet.

Yeom Je deliberately created such occasions to make them compete against each other.

Baekseonbang was an organization strictly comprised of a ranking system.

Below the Bangju were the Sipseon, and below them were fixed ranks from Sipilseon to Gusipguseon.

Yeom Je had firmly established this ranking system. This ranking was the very thing that protected Baekseonbang and was the source of increasing profits.

The desire to climb higher.

Because the higher the rank, the more profit one took, they all strived somehow to achieve merit and climb higher. Thus, the ranks changed once a year.

It was precisely then. The first words that would overturn the fate of everyone present rang out.

“You have a lot of money.”

At the unfamiliar voice resounding through the great hall, everyone’s gazes turned toward it.

A young man had spoken while looking at the promissory notes stacked high at the accounting station.

“You have so much money, so why don’t you pay your debts?”

A nearby seonsin asked in surprise.

“Who are you?”

No one had noticed until this first-time stranger entered their midst. Of course, it was the hectic accounting day, but given their martial arts prowess, it was an impossible occurrence. Even so, at least one out of a hundred should have seen him.

The young man walked to the center under everyone’s gaze.

And he looked at Yeom Je seated on the high seat and bowed politely.

“I greet the Bangju. I am called Geom Yeon, from the Geumryong Sega.”

At the words “Geumryong Sega,” everyone relaxed. Exclamations of “Whew, you startled us!” came from here and there.

For it was the moment the opponent was confirmed as a mere martial artist sent by the Geumryong Sega, rather than an unfathomable mysterious master.

But Bangju Yeom Je did not relax. No matter how bustling the atmosphere was, the fact that even he himself had not perceived the man’s entry weighed on his mind.

‘What if that man were an assassin?’

Did that not mean he could have been assassinated?

After stopping Ilseon, who was about to step forward, with a raised hand, Yeom Je personally spoke to Geom Mugeuk. The Geumryong Sega was worth dealing with directly.

“A face I haven’t seen before?”

“I recently formed a connection with the Geumryong Sega.”

Yeom Je was inwardly puzzled. They sent someone to him, yet they sent a recent recruit? It was an unheard-of occurrence of late.

“What have you come for?”

Everyone’s gaze focused on Geom Mugeuk. And the words that came from Geom Mugeuk’s mouth were unexpected by any of them.

“I’ve come to collect the money you borrowed.”

Silence flowed.

Is he crazy? Did I mishear?

In that place filled with these two thoughts, someone burst into laughter. That laughter spread, and laughs erupted here and there.

It had been a long time since someone had come directly to them saying he was here to collect money. Could there truly be someone so unlucky? To come on the accounting day of all days, when they were all gathered.

“The money you haven’t paid back until now amounts to three million nyang. Though I should collect interest as well, I shall graciously take only the principal.”

Once more, laughter burst from all around.

Yeom Je found himself thinking the unthinkable.

Had they sent him here to die?

Could it be that the Geumryong Sega Gaju had sent him because he didn’t want to bloody his own hands? If not, that man would have had no reason to do something so reckless. Just who in the world was he?

“Who are you?”

“I am Geom Yeon. The character yeon is not from inyeon (fate), but from yeongi (smoke). When a fate ends, one must disappear like smoke, yet sometimes lingering attachments remain. Like the drunken air of one who loves alcohol, there are times one wishes to linger nearby. But today, it seems I will disappear quickly without any lingering attachment.”

What nonsense was this?

Everyone turned their heads and exchanged glances. Many emotions were contained in those eyes, but the most prevalent was this:

A madman has come!

Therefore, their eyes were filled with amusement. How shall they toy with this guy?

Though it seemed like nothing, Yeom Je felt a strange sense of unease.

It was because this was unfamiliar. Why did this happen on the accounting day of all days, when everyone was gathered?

“Do you know where this place is?”

“Is it not a place where one hundred seons play?”

Geom Mugeuk lowered his voice slightly.

“However, this is just between us, but isn’t it rather odd to call yourselves seons when you aren’t? I heard you’ve done many wicked things.”

Seeing him speak such spiteful words, it seemed true that he had been sent to be killed.

Yeom Je gave a signal with his eyes to one man.

He was Gusipgu Seon, the last in the ranking. Yeom Je always gave orders strictly according to rank on such occasions.

“What man talks so much?”

Gusipgu Seon strode forward. It was the perfect opportunity to look good before the Bangju. A chance to escape being the lowest ranked.

Of course, things did not go as he wished. Geom Mugeuk asked his name.

“Might I ask our seonsin’s honorable name?”

“Why do you ask my name?”

“There is something I need to confirm.”

Geom Mugeuk took out a promissory note from his bosom. It was a note for one hundred thousand nyang.

“If you answer, I shall give you this.”

One hundred thousand nyang for saying his name? There was no way he would naively believe that.

“You intend to mock me with a fake note?”

Gusipgu Seon’s hand naturally went to his sword hilt.

“It’s a genuine note. What have you got to lose? If it’s real, you say your name once and gain one hundred thousand nyang. If it’s fake, you gain a justification to punish me.”

Gusipgu Seon alternated his gaze between Geom Mugeuk and the note in his hand, then finally looked back at Yeom Je. Yeom Je nodded as if to say, “Try doing as he asks.”

“I am Jo Gung (曺弓).”

Then Geom Mugeuk took out a paper from his bosom and checked him against it.

“Jo Gung, Jo Gung. Ah, here you are. You borrowed money from a friend in your hometown, failed to pay it back, and instead killed your friend and fled. You wandered as a vagabond, received private tutelage from a master wanderer, then became active in the Heukjeong Samho, killed two brothers, and fled. Our seonsin seems to specialize mainly in stabbing close acquaintances in the back.”

Jo Gung’s eyes turned ice-cold. His opponent had come with precise information about him.

“What are you trying to do now?”

Killing intent surged in Jo Gung’s eyes.

“Is what is written here correct? Though I trust my information, I wanted to confirm at least one person as a representative. That way, my mind is at ease too.”

Then one of the Sipseon standing in the back spoke.

“You’re wrong. He didn’t just kill the brothers. To eliminate future troubles, he killed their entire families too before coming out.”

Jo Gung was not ashamed in the slightest. Everyone here was that sort of person. In fact, the more wicked and despicable one was, the easier it was to live.

Jo Gung drew his sword and strode toward Geom Mugeuk.

“You ran your mouth, so your tongue and that hundred thousand nyang are mine.”

Jo Gung unleashed a killing move. As if he truly meant to cut out his tongue, he thrust his sword straight at Geom Mugeuk’s mouth.

But Geom Mugeuk’s tongue was not something Jo Gung’s sword could cut.

Avoiding the incoming blade, Geom Mugeuk lightly counterattacked.

Jo Gung, his heart pierced by a single sword strike, collapsed and died on the spot.

The smell of blood burst into the air, and silence settled over the place.

The move Geom Mugeuk had just shown was utterly ordinary. He had not killed him with some special technique; he had merely dodged the incoming attack and stabbed with his sword.

So their reactions were split according to each person’s skill.

To be taken down by an attack like that!

To kill Jo Gung with such an ordinary attack?

Of course, that did not mean anyone here was frightened. After all, only the Ninety-Ninth Immortal, the very bottom of the hierarchy, had died, and there were ninety-nine of them gathered here.

Geom Mugeuk cupped his fist toward the Flame Emperor and said,

“My apologies. As you saw, I had no choice.”

The Flame Emperor spoke with a sunken gaze.

“It’s all right. When people who carry blades gather, blood is bound to be spilled.”

“You are a man of great tolerance. If you simply return the money you borrowed, I will leave as I am.”

But the Flame Emperor was not the sort of man to hand over the enormous sum of three million nyang just because one of his subordinates had died.

“I did intend to repay the money. However, our sect’s circumstances have not been good, so I have been putting it off. Go back and tell your family head. I will repay the money soon.”

Of course, he had not the slightest intention of doing so. This bastard would be killed one way or another, and the Golden Dragon Family would have to pay for today’s incident with money. They would have to prepare a far greater sum than what they had paid until now.

It would have been nice if he had said, Very well, and turned to leave.

Geom Mugeuk turned his head and looked at the piled-up promissory notes.

“There’s plenty of money over there, isn’t there? Let’s see. If this much money comes in every month, there must be an incredible amount piled up in the vault.”

At the mention of the vault, the Flame Emperor’s face twitched.

“Why not repay it?”

The order “tear that bastard to pieces” rose all the way to the Flame Emperor’s throat, but he forced it back down. His instincts were telling him not to. Not now. If he was going to kill him, kill him later. No matter how he looked at him, wasn’t that bastard strange?

The Flame Emperor smiled and looked around at his subordinates.

“As you can see, I have many mouths to feed.”

The Immortals looked at Geom Mugeuk with mockery and sly smiles filling those mouths.

Geom Mugeuk, looking at them, offered a solution.

“That can be solved easily.”

Geom Mugeuk slowly drew his sword and said,

“Let’s reduce the number of mouths.”

Absolute Regression, Chapter 474

Chapter 474: If the Money Is Short I’ll Take This Too
-----------------------------------------------------
Once again, the reactions split in two.

The far larger group reacted as if they were dealing with a madman. Buoyed by the absolute reassurance of the number ninety-nine, they wore sneers at the corners of their mouths.

Some grew tense.

One worry rose in their minds.

What if he wasn’t mad?

The moment that assumption became reality, those words about reducing the number of mouths would be no different from the Grim Reaper opening his ledger.

The Flame Emperor was among those who worried. He stared piercingly at Geom Mugeuk, trying to gauge his hidden strength, but he could reach no conclusion.

He was young—far too young—and no matter how he looked at him, he did not seem to be that much of an expert. And yet, in this situation, he was saying such things.

“Since the Gang Leader must have grown fond of them, for better or worse, I’ll handle it for you. Which mouth should I take care of first? From what I can see, they all look like mouths that could disappear.”

The Immortals all frowned at once. There were more than a few who wanted to charge out immediately and kill this insolent bastard.

For the Flame Emperor, this was truly a first. No expert in the world would dare spout such madness before them. When all of them were gathered, even the Murim Alliance would not dare barge in recklessly.

‘Could he be aiming for that? Trying to pass himself off as an expert with such arrogant words?’

That was how far his thoughts went. Truly, the more he looked at him, the more confusing the man became.

‘Fine. First, I need to see his skill.’

Before the bastard attacked, they would move first.

The Flame Emperor smiled leisurely and said,

“With lives as tenacious as ours, reducing the mouths won’t be easy.”

At the Flame Emperor’s glance, nine men stepped forward, from the Ninetieth Immortal to the Ninety-Eighth Immortal. It would not be too late to decide what came next after accurately assessing the opponent’s strength.

They surrounded Geom Mugeuk.

After slowly looking them over, Geom Mugeuk said,

“Are these the youngest?”

He asked as if guessing, but Geom Mugeuk had accurately grasped their skill. Judging by their presence, they were weaker than the others.

Amid this crowd of people, Geom Mugeuk had understood their abilities precisely with just a brief glance. And that was not all.

“It doesn’t look like you’ve ever fought in a combined attack before, either.”

He had seen that accurately as well. They had never had the chance to fight in a coordinated assault. Most of the time, they simply swarmed in for revenge and carried out one-sided massacres. Even that had hardly happened in recent times.

‘You want to assess my skill?’

Of course, Geom Mugeuk was not someone who would play along with his opponent’s intentions.

With his sword hanging low, Geom Mugeuk seemed to slowly walk toward the opponent standing directly in front of him.

Whoosh.

In an instant, he changed direction to the right and rushed forward. The movement was so fast and so sudden that the man on the right had his throat cut before he could even swing his sword.

Puaaak!

As blood sprayed in every direction, Geom Mugeuk’s figure was already charging toward another Immortal. The opponent instinctively thrust out his sword as well.

The two swords brushed through the air. Because he had thoroughly hidden his true skill, Geom Mugeuk’s sword was ahead of his by exactly half a move.

Thud!

That was why, when he killed the second Immortal, sighs of regret burst out here and there. It was from the disappointment that, had he been just a little faster, that Immortal could have won.

The sword that had pierced the second opponent’s heart now spun in a wide arc and flew toward the man rushing in from the opposite side.

Paat!

The blood on Geom Mugeuk’s sword flew through the air and entered the Immortal’s eyes. Whether it had been intentional or accidental was impossible to tell, but he closed his eyes for an instant. He would never open those closed eyes again. Once again, sighs erupted from all directions. Of all things, blood had to get into his eyes?

When he cut down the third man, a surprise attack came from behind.

The attacker was almost certain he had succeeded, but the strike missed by a hair.

Because it was such a pity, the thought, “One more time,” naturally sprang to mind, but he had no second chance.

The blood he spewed sprayed in every direction once more, blocking the vision of the remaining Immortals.

Surely he wasn’t deliberately scattering blood like this to obstruct their sight? There was no way, right?

Those watching found themselves thinking such thoughts. That was how much blood was spraying like a fountain. Geom Mugeuk’s fighting was not elegant. It was rough and ferocious. It was a battle steeped in the reek of blood.

Of course, there was a reason Geom Mugeuk was fighting this way.

The battle was close. If only they had been a little faster. If only they had attacked from a slightly different angle.

Leaving behind such regrets, the remaining Immortals fell one after another. Sighs of disappointment continued, and when the ninth sigh ended, they had all collapsed as corpses.

The smell of their spilled blood filled the hall.

The silence hanging over the hall was, of course, heavier than when the Ninety-Ninth Immortal had died earlier. No one had expected nine experts to die like this without leaving so much as a scratch on their opponent.

And yet, it did not make them think the opponent was extraordinary.

Rather, most of those watching felt, ‘What a lucky bastard!’ In their eyes, it had been a fight lost by the narrowest of margins.

On top of that, Geom Mugeuk’s swordsmanship was far too ordinary.

‘He should defend like that, and attack like that.’

It was the kind of fight that would be perfect to show beginners just starting to learn martial arts. A battle that could be drawn exactly as it was and published as a manual.

Of course, in the Flame Emperor’s eyes, this fight looked different.

‘It looked as though he was fighting purely by instinct.’

His nine subordinates had not managed a proper combined attack even once.

If two or three of them had attacked at the same time, they surely could have led the fight more easily. They must have intended to coordinate, but the opponent’s movements did not allow it, and in the end, they were each defeated individually.

‘What if this fight was intentional? What if this narrowly won, lucky-looking battle was thoroughly calculated?’

The Flame Emperor knew. He knew how difficult it was to kill them with fundamentals. Far more difficult than killing them with dazzling techniques.

‘This bastard has a hidden move.’

After shaking the blood from his sword, Geom Mugeuk looked at the Flame Emperor and said,

“Now then, I’ve reduced the mouths for you, so pay back the money.”

The Flame Emperor gave no answer.

‘Where on earth did they find and send a bastard like this?’

Geom Mugeuk saw the unconcealable greed revealed on the Flame Emperor’s face.

Through prior investigation, Geom Mugeuk had already known that the Flame Emperor was such a man. He valued money more than his subordinates.

If he had to hand over all the money he had earned, he would rather give up his life.

That was why, in order to open the Flame Emperor’s vault, he absolutely could not rush.

He could wipe them all out at once, but he had to draw blood like this and gradually heighten the mood, slowly breaking down the man’s heart. The primary goal today was not to kill them, but to recover the money.

“You kill a few errand boys and get carried away.”

Of course, they were not such people, but the Flame Emperor forced himself to keep up his momentum and suppressed an unknowable unease.

‘Whom should I send?’

Just as he was considering whether to send out the Ten Immortals and cut the sprout at the root—

Geom Mugeuk did something completely unexpected.

“Let me pass for a moment.”

Geom Mugeuk suddenly began walking toward the Immortals standing on the right.

At the sudden action, the Immortals on that side drew their swords and stepped back. As they spread left and right, a path naturally formed.

The Immortals on either side glared with fierce momentum, but Geom Mugeuk passed between them with a face that showed no fear at all.

Openings showed in his natural stride. They wondered whether to attack or not, but because the openings were too obvious, they could not bring themselves to strike.

But there was one Immortal who could not hold back. He had already calculated that this single move would raise his rank by ten places at once.

Shh.

It was a surprise attack that truly looked, to anyone watching, as though it had landed properly.

Puk.

Geom Mugeuk twisted his body to evade and drove his sword in.

This time as well, he dodged the attack by luck and barely succeeded in counterattacking.

Even at that moment, several Immortals flinched. They were tormented by the desire to take advantage of the opening and attack, but the man who had just died was the Forty-Fifth Immortal. Among those present, a fighter above the middle ranks had failed in a surprise attack.

When the Ninety-Eighth Immortal attacked, it was close. When the Forty-Fifth Immortal attacked, it was close.

Then they should have drawn a wise conclusion from this result, but right now, Geom Mugeuk gave them no time for that.

“Is this painting genuine?”

Holding a sword dripping with blood, the place he had walked to was the painting hanging on the rear wall.

The Flame Emperor asked with a puzzled gaze,

“Why are you asking that?”

“If the Gang Leader comes up short on money, I was thinking of taking this too.”

The Flame Emperor’s expression hardened. The opponent was openly disregarding him. There was certainly dignity he had to maintain before his subordinates.

But for now, the strange unease this young opponent gave him was greater.

“Do I look like the sort of person who would hang a fake in a place where people come and go?”

Such pride, in a situation thick with the smell of his subordinates’ blood? Geom Mugeuk felt a strange sense of dissonance.

But Geom Mugeuk was the only one who found it dissonant.

Rather, his subordinates felt no sense of incongruity at those words whatsoever. For all of them, their own face and money were more important than their comrades.

Geom Mugeuk began taking down the frame from the wall.

Was he really taking the painting? The moment the Flame Emperor’s expression stiffened sharply—

Shiiing!

An Immortal nearby did not miss the opportunity and launched a surprise attack. It was the very moment he took down the painting and lifted it. Moreover, when Geom Mugeuk held the painting in his right hand, he could not restrain himself. Of course, in the end, he should have.

Puck!

The sword missed by a hair, and Geom Mugeuk, turning around, struck his jaw with his left fist.

If he was the Thirty-Ninth Immortal, he should have been able to withstand a single punch, but he collapsed dead on the spot. It had not even looked like such an impressive fist technique.

The Flame Emperor’s gaze was directed not at his dead subordinate, but at the painting. One question had taken hold of him.

‘Why take the painting now, of all times? If he really needs it, he could take it down later.’

Geom Mugeuk carried the frame over and placed it on the table piled with bank drafts. It looked as if he were saying that everything here belonged to him.

“What are you doing right now?”

At the Flame Emperor’s question, Geom Mugeuk replied calmly,

“Because you people have lived this kind of life, there aren’t many ways to deal with you. So don’t resent me.”

The Immortals stood there staring at Geom Mugeuk with expressions that seemed to say, What nonsense is he spouting?

In that instant, an ominous thought rose in the Flame Emperor’s mind.

‘No way. Surely not?’

It was right then.

Shiiiiiiing!

The Flame Emperor saw a single line.

Not only him—everyone saw it. A single line was drawn out in a clean sweep, then vanished.

The line appeared to Geom Mugeuk’s right. The very side where Geom Mugeuk had walked over and taken down the painting.

The line passed through the Immortals standing there. It passed through the Immortals in the first row, and through those in the second and third rows as well.

A moment later, they saw Geom Mugeuk extending his sword toward them.

Only when they saw that did they finally understand.

That the line they had just seen had been a sword line created by Geom Mugeuk’s blade.

‘Can sword qi fly out as a single line like that?’

It had not flown like a brilliant light, nor had it left afterimages behind.

A single line, as if drawn straight with a ruler.

It was the first time in their lives they had seen such clean sword qi.

Silence flowed through the place.

Clack.

The moment Geom Mugeuk’s Black Demon Sword entered its sheath—

Srrrrk.

The bodies of the Immortals standing on the right began to slide apart.

The Immortals whose bodies had been cut in two all spurted blood at once and collapsed where they stood.

“Uaaaaaagh!”

One Immortal watching the scene from the front screamed. No matter how bold these experts were, the sight of over twenty men having their torsos sliced apart in a single move was both shocking and terrifying.

And now they understood why he had taken the painting from the back. The sword qi that had cut through the Immortals left a long line on the rear wall.

The Flame Emperor’s eyes widened.

He had thrown himself into the murim and overcome many crises, but had there ever been a time when he had been this shocked?

He had known the opponent had a hidden move, but he had not imagined he would kill over twenty of his subordinates in one stroke.

The other Immortals were the same. Now there was no one left there who was sneering.

The hot, rising stench of blood filled the great hall.

“He used up a great deal of internal energy, so he must have none left!”

Had he looked inside Geom Mugeuk’s dantian, such words could never have come out, but at the Flame Emperor’s shout, the Immortals all drew their swords at once.

They all revealed their killing intent, but Geom Mugeuk was not frightened in the slightest.

“I told you not to draw your swords, but to repay the money.”

Even in this situation, with over thirty of his subordinates dead, the words that he would pay did not leave the Flame Emperor’s mouth. Even with their lives, they had yet to touch so much as a hair of the three million nyang.

“It seems the number of mouths needs to be reduced further.”

This time, Geom Mugeuk looked to the opposite side, the left, and quietly said to the Immortals standing there,

“Fortunately, there’s no painting on this wall.”

Absolute Regression Chapter 475

Chapter 475: Smile When Borrowing, Draw the Sword When Repaying.
----------------------------------------------------------------
The immortals on Geom Mugeuk's left tensed simultaneously.

The remark that there were no paintings on this wall—did that not mean he would unleash sword qi here as well?

Moreover, Geom Mugeuk's bearing had grown even colder. He had elevated the terror to the next level by cutting down twenty people in a single move.

"Stop!"

Yeomje stepped forward and stopped him. Still, he harbored a doubt.

'Could he possibly have the internal energy to unleash such a devastating sword qi again?'

It seemed unlikely, but what if he did? The subordinates on the left would be swept away as well. He didn't care about their lives.

He thought about the money they brought him. No matter how he calculated it, it was a loss. The money they currently earned was one thing, but the cost of raising or acquiring such experts couldn't be ignored either.

Trying to save three million taels would mean losing far more money in the long run.

But he also didn't want to hand over three million taels obediently.

'First, I need to buy time.'

Yeomje's gaze turned to the Ten Immortals standing before him. From first rank to tenth rank.

It would have been nice if one or two of them stepped forward voluntarily to check. But no one did. Well, after seeing that sword qi earlier, it wouldn't be easy to step up.

Yeomje sent a voice transmission to Il Seon.

—Il Seon.

—Yes, Hall Master.

—Have him expend more of his internal energy.

—Understood.

Il Seon sent a voice transmission to Yuk Seon. One who leads by example and steps forward himself, or one who possesses sincere loyalty to the Hall Master.

There was no such person here. They all knew that well. He hadn't expected it from the start. They were people who moved only for desire and profit.

Since they were such a group, Il Seon's choice was naturally based on emotion. Yuk Seon was the person he liked least among the Ten Immortals.

—Yuk Seon, gauge that man's strength and make him expend his internal energy.

Yuk Seon didn't want to step forward.

'Damn it!'

The sword qi his opponent had shown moments ago was a technique he had never seen in his life. The image of lines being drawn with a sharp crack like shattering mirrors, and bodies being sliced apart, surfaced in his mind.

But if he defied Il Seon's order, he would be the first to be made an example. Yeomje and Il Seon were more than capable of that.

Yuk Seon didn't step into the swamp alone. As he stepped forward, he sent a voice transmission to Sip Il Seon.

—Help me, and I'll get you into the Ten Immortals.

For Sip Il Seon, entering the Ten Immortals was a fervent wish that could overcome even the fear of death. He knew it was possible if Yuk Seon helped him.

Moreover, if he distinguished himself in this situation? Perhaps he could even take a position above Yuk Seon.

Sip Il Seon also stepped forward with Yuk Seon.

As the two stepped forward, Geom Mugeuk first asked Yuk Seon,

"Who are you?"

"I am Yuk Seon."

Geom Mugeuk took out some documents from his bosom and read them again.

"So you are Yuk Seon."

As he read about Yuk Seon for a moment, Geom Mugeuk's expression furrowed.

"Just how many people have you killed?"

This time, Geom Mugeuk asked Sip Il Seon,

"Who are you?"

"Sip Il Seon."

This time, he frowned even more.

"Your ranks don't seem to be determined by who killed more people."

Rather, Sip Il Seon was the one who had committed more slaughter. But Geom Mugeuk's frown wasn't solely due to the large number of people killed.

"You even killed children."

Sip Il Seon neither confirmed nor denied it, but his eyes acknowledged the fact. So? What about it? That was the look in his eyes. He had originally been treated as a madman even within Baekseon Hall.

Yuk Seon asked Geom Mugeuk,

"Just who in the world are you?"

Instead of answering, Geom Mugeuk slowly walked toward Sip Il Seon.

As he walked toward him, Sip Il Seon tensed up and prepared to draw his sword at any moment. Yuk Seon also prepared for the worst.

If you draw, we draw too. With the two of us?

He had clearly been this confident.

Shk! Shik! Shk!

Thud! Thud! Thwack!

Geom Mugeuk's sword consecutively pierced Sip Il Seon's stomach, chest, and neck.

It was so fast that Sip Il Seon had no time to dodge or block, let alone counterattack.

The moment Yuk Seon beside him instinctively tried to counterattack, his eyes met Geom Mugeuk's. Geom Mugeuk, who had already stabbed Sip Il Seon three times, was looking at him.

'So fast!'

He had tried to face someone like this with two people? What kind of confidence had that been?

As Sip Il Seon toppled backward, having perished on his feet, only then did Geom Mugeuk ask,

"Ah, what was it you asked a moment ago?"

His courtesy made it all the more horrifying.

Yuk Seon only poured internal strength into his sword-wielding hand, saying nothing. His opponent had already answered his question by killing Sip Il Seon with that sword.

That he was a grim reaper who had come to kill them.

That was Yuk Seon's only thought.

'Only the first strike is the path to survival.'

When they reached striking distance, Yuk Seon's sword flew first, and Geom Mugeuk's sword followed.

But the sword that flew later arrived first.

Srrk!

Blood sprayed in all directions from Yuk Seon's severed neck. With the deaths of two people, the bloody stench in the hall grew even thicker.

Everyone's gaze was focused on Geom Mugeuk.

Villains were villains. Even as their comrades died one after another, none of them felt the slightest regret over their deaths.

Then the one who jumped out was Sam Seon.

"Stop talking about money and fight me!"

He was the one who loved fighting the most among the immortals of Baekseon Hall. Like a moth leaping into flames, he couldn't suppress his killing intent that surged at the thick scent of blood. If Sip Il Seon was a madman, this Sam Seon was an even bigger madman.

"Isn't this a world overflowing with things I want to kill?"

Without hesitation, Sam Seon unleashed sword qi.

Shwaaaaak!

Rough sword qi flew forth to tear Geom Mugeuk apart.

As Geom Mugeuk dodged the sword qi, it swept toward the immortals behind him.

Two of the immortals standing in the back were swept away before they could dodge.

Shwaaaaaak!

Even though a comrade had died, Sam Seon unleashed sword qi again. This time, it was even rougher and more powerful.

Geom Mugeuk could have nullified it by unleashing his own sword qi, but he let it be. More immortals in the back were swept away by his sword qi. Curses erupted from all directions.

But Sam Seon unleashed sword qi like a madman. He didn't care if his comrades died or not.

Shwak! Shwaaak!

Kwaang! Kwong!

Sword qi was unleashed to block sword qi. Then more sword qi flew to block that sword qi.

The place instantly became a scene of carnage. Geom Mugeuk moved this way and that, exquisitely dodging the flying sword qi.

Puwuk!

Sam Seon's figure stopped.

The sword piercing through his heart belonged to Yeomje.

"You insane bastard."

As he spat out the cold curse and pulled out his sword, Sam Seon sprayed blood and collapsed on the spot, perishing. With his death, the commotion in the hall finally ceased. In this mad onslaught, over ten people had died by the hands of their own side.

Yeomje said to Geom Mugeuk,

"I lost. I'll pay the money."

He couldn't endure any longer.

'To kill this bastard, I have to bet everything I have.'

That everything included his own life. Charging at an opponent who had shown such divine might with a life-or-death attack was tantamount to asking everyone to die together. Even if he won, it would be a losing trade.

'I have to send him away first. Revenge can be considered later.'

Hiding his anger, Yeomje spoke calmly,

"With our numbers reduced to this extent, even if I pay, the main hall can be maintained."

Geom Mugeuk smiled.

"That's a relief."

"But to give such a large sum as three million taels, I need time. I have to settle my affairs. Take that over there first and give me some time."

The place Yeomje looked at was the table where they had been settling accounts. Promissory notes were piled there.

Of course, there was no way it would work on Geom Mugeuk.

"Isn't the money in the safe?"

"I don't have that much money. How could I keep such a large sum in a safe?"

Yeomje shot back.

Geom Mugeuk stared at him and said,

"What if I open it and it's there?"

Yeomje flinched.

"May I take it all?"

Geom Mugeuk saw it. Though he put on a troubled expression, a glimmer of joy flashed in Yeomje's eyes.

"Fine, I promise."

Yeomje was confident there wasn't three million taels in the safe.

"The main hall's safe is right here in the great hall, where I spend the most time."

Yeomje rose from his seat and walked to the back wall.

He manipulated the ornaments attached to the back wall. Since he blocked them with his body, no one could see what or how he was touching them.

"If you try to force the door open, mechanisms will activate and kill the intruder."

Rumble.

Shortly after, a secret wall opened, revealing the safe.

"Now, come and look."

Geom Mugeuk walked up to it.

The remaining Ten Immortals followed together and stood around Yeomje, guarding him.

Yeomje took out the promissory notes and gold ingots from the safe.

"There are a little over two million five hundred thousand taels in promissory notes. Even if I sell all the gold pieces, it's far short of three million taels. Do you still think I deceived you?"

Yeomje was brazen, as if he hadn't lied.

"Bring the money that was being settled over there."

At that, the immortals brought the promissory notes from the table. Those who hadn't settled their accounts yet also put money there.

Gathering all the money like that, it came to three million taels.

"Fortunately, this is enough to pay you. Take it and go. Today was also my fault for not repaying the debt, so I shall forget about this cleanly."

"Truly! You are a magnanimous man."

Of course, as if that could be true.

He was planning his revenge starting now. He would kill not only this bastard, but his family, friends, and neighbors too. He didn't plan to let Geumryongse off either. He would kill ten times as many as had died today to show the martial world what kind of place Baekseon Hall was. Even if he had to buy every assassin in the martial world.

It would have been truly nice if he took the money and left.

"Ah, I forgot. Sorry, but there's more money I'm owed."

Geom Mugeuk took out a sheet of paper from his bosom and held it out.

It was a paper densely packed with numerous names and amounts. It was a record of the money Baekseon Hall had extorted from each merchant guild.

"I've been asked to collect this money as well."

Yeomje's eyes widened to slits.

'These bastards!'

Killing intent surged within Yeomje. Hand over all the money he had extorted until now? It was an amount he absolutely couldn't repay, even if it killed him. Suppressing his boiling anger, Yeomje said,

"As you can see, the safe is empty. Come back next time to collect."

Then, words that pushed Yeomje to the edge of a cliff flowed out.

"Doesn't our Hall Master have a private safe?"

Only now could Yeomje know for certain.

'He came fully prepared.'

If he had known this, he wouldn't have opened this safe either. No, it didn't matter. Even if it killed him, he didn't plan to hand over this money now either.

Yeomje glared at Geom Mugeuk with cold eyes.

"You came to take all my money."

"There are two things wrong with that statement. First, it's not the Hall Master's money. Second, I won't be taking it all either. Since you've borrowed that money without paying interest, it must have grown greatly by now."

He was right. Because he had built all sorts of businesses using that money.

"Pay back what you borrowed and conduct your business honorably from now on."

Of course, Geom Mugeuk was certain Yeomje wouldn't do that. If he were someone capable of that, he wouldn't have engaged in such matters in the first place.

For a brief moment, Yeomje deliberated.

'Should I just hand it all over and aim for another day?'

But he didn't make that decision.

It wasn't merely because he had to pay back borrowed money. Once this young bastard saw inside the safe, he would surely be greedy and take everything. Because Yeomje himself would definitely do the same.

In the end, this final choice was fitting of his life.

"To whoever kills him, I shall give the position of First Immortal and those three million taels!"

The immortals simultaneously revealed their killing intent. Even having seen the opponent's strength, the reward of becoming First Immortal and three million taels made them fearless of death. And anyway, this was not a situation where one could defy the Hall Master's orders.

"You borrow money with a smile, and when it's time to pay, you draw your sword. Isn't this too much?"

Ignoring Geom Mugeuk's words, Yeomje shouted,

"Kill him!"

The immortals rushed in from all directions. The momentum was tremendous as sixty experts charged simultaneously. The thought that as long as they didn't die, they'd be fine dominated them. They only thought that if they survived by luck, they could take the three million taels.

For some reason, Geom Mugeuk was pushed back by them. At that sight, Yeomje shouted,

"His internal energy is depleted! Kill him!"

Even so, he did not step forward. To him, only money and his own life mattered.

Thus, Geom Mugeuk was driven to one wall, and all the remaining immortals gathered before him. If it was truly the case that his opponent's internal energy had been depleted?

The one who killed him first would become the owner of three million taels.

Il Seon stepped forward to protect his position, and the rest stepped forward to seize both money and position.

Gathered like a pack of hyenas, they only watched for an opportunity before Geom Mugeuk.

For someone cornered, his eyes were far too clear and deep, making Il Seon, who stood in the front row, want to step back. An ominous premonition rose in that instant!

'Don't tell me? Did he gather us in one place on purpose?'

Geom Mugeuk calmly spoke to those filled with greed.

"I hope you have no next life."

The moment those words ended.

The most terrifying evil ghost appeared before Geom Mugeuk.

At that horrifying sight, those at the forefront screamed. They had never seen such a terrifying evil ghost.

And that terrifying evil ghost split into twelve.

Through recent blood-soaked training, the number had increased from ten to twelve—none other than the Great Annihilation Form of the Nine Transformations Demonic Art.

The moment they faced the evil ghosts, their entire bodies froze.

Kwakwakwakwakwakwakwakwakwakwakwakwak!

The evil ghosts showed neither mercy nor hesitation. They swept away everything blocking their path and surged forward. No technique, no protective astral qi was of any use. They were all torn to pieces and annihilated.

Thus, over sixty villains left only pools of blood.

Yeomje, left alone, opened his eyes wide in terror. Overcome with fear, he trembled like an aspen leaf. He had never seen such a fearsome martial art. That evil ghost seemed like something he could never forget even after death.

"J-just who are you?"

His fear-filled voice trembled.

Splash. Splash.

"You know, don't you? I'm just a man who came to collect a debt."

Geom Mugeuk walked over the pools of blood and spoke politely.

"Pay your debts."

Absolute Regression, Episode 476.

Chapter 476: It's Your Retirement Fund, Mister.
-----------------------------------------------
Yeomje sank down beside the grand tutor’s chair and squeezed his eyes shut.

He hoped that when he opened them again, he would be in his own bedchamber. He hoped all of this was nothing more than a nightmare from the night before.

But when he opened his eyes, the nightmare had come right up before him.

The gaze with which Geom Mugeuk looked down at him was calm. Yet because it was calm and courteous, it was all the more terrifying. With those clear eyes, he had killed every one of his men. Speaking politely all the while, he had killed them all.

Now it was his turn to fight. But he could not summon the will to do so.

‘So this day has come after all.’

Yeomje raised his voice in resentment.
