“What grudge do you have against me? You’re going this far just because I cheated you out of some money?”

As if anyone had killed them merely for cheating him out of money.

The evil deeds they had committed until now were so great that, from the moment he came here, Geom Mugeuk had already resolved to kill them all.

If he left them alive and let fate flow as it originally had, then far too many warriors of the Martial Alliance would die in the process of wiping them out.

Those who had to die would die, and those who should not die would live. The conviction of his regression was being applied here in Baekseonbang as well.

“It’s money you can’t even take with you when you die. Why be so greedy over it?”

Yeomje looked down at the blood pooling thickly on the floor and clenched his teeth. Those were the sort of words only losers said. Words spoken by bastards who couldn’t make money. What value in the world could possibly be greater than money?

In the end, he hurled abuse at him.

“You damned son of a bitch! It’s my money! It’s all my money! Whether I scatter it in the marketplace or fill my coffin with it, what business is it of yours?”

Geom Mugeuk saw the madness in his eyes. He had lived his entire life this way; how could a few words bring repentance?

Suddenly, he recalled what the head of the Eunha Merchant Guild had said.

—I’ve seen far too many people get swallowed by the creature called money after falling in love with the fun of making it.

The one who had reached that extreme was none other than Yeomje. And right now, he had no right to stand there so brazenly.

“It isn’t your money, is it?”

Struck where it hurt, Yeomje clenched his teeth. There was only one thing he could say in reply.

“Who the hell are you?”

The answer came at once.

“How many times must I tell you? I’m the man who came to collect the money.”

The reason he kept asking was probably this: he was a man who had no concept whatsoever of repaying money he had borrowed.

“Open your personal vault.”

If not for that purpose, he would not have been worth even a single word of conversation.

“I told you, there’s no such thing.”

Even after all ninety-nine of his subordinates had died, he still would not open his personal vault. He valued money more than his own life. That was the kind of man he was.

‘Do you know what I did to earn that money?’

He had truly done every manner of thing to make it. Beaten people, kidnapped them, sold them off, killed them. Even if he was bound for hell in the end, he had done anything and everything to eat well and live well in this life.

He had been determined never to hand it over, even if he died.

“I know your vault is in this main hall.”

Yeomje tried to feign composure, but who could he fool? To Geom Mugeuk, the sound of his heart pounding was as loud as thunder.

“Did you not say it yourself when you opened that vault? That Baekseonbang’s vault was in this place where you spend the most time. Then your personal vault must be here as well.”

Geom Mugeuk gave him no time to think and pressed him.

“I’ll find the money myself.”

Geom Mugeuk drew his sword. He was just about to cut him down in one stroke without hesitation when—

“I’ll open it! Spare me!”

Geom Mugeuk’s sword stopped in midair.

Even then, Geom Mugeuk did not miss it. Yeomje spoke as though he had given up, but in his eyes there flashed a venomous will that knew no surrender.

“You’re right. The vault is here.”

Yeomje rose from his seat and walked toward the back. He entered the empty vault he had opened earlier and felt around the ceiling. It was a plain, featureless wall with no visible mechanism, yet a hidden device was concealed there.

Ziiiiiing.

One wall inside the vault opened, revealing an abacus.

This vault had been a disguise meant to conceal the real vault.

A double vault.

The money and gold ingots in the first vault had served as the mechanical devices and guards protecting the true vault.

Anyone would be dazzled by the money and gold there, and would never think that another vault lay behind it.

Yeomje flicked the abacus beads. It was an intricate mechanism that would only open the door if the exact numbers were entered.

“Not a single number can be wrong.”

For some reason, he let Geom Mugeuk watch as he entered the numbers on the abacus.

Three, two, four.

And before raising the final number, Yeomje suddenly spoke with his back turned.

“I know you won’t spare me even if I open this.”

Yeomje looked back at Geom Mugeuk, his eyes smiling meaningfully.

He raised the last abacus bead.

The number was four.

At that very moment—

Chiiiiiiik.

Smoke sprayed down from the ceiling where the two men stood. Because he had intentionally entered the wrong number, the mechanism prepared for intruders had released poisonous smoke.

Within the poison smoke, Yeomje spoke with a smile.

“The last number wasn’t four. It was three.”

His eyes were incomparably vicious as he said,

“It’s the most agonizing poison in the world. You’ll suffer the pain of every organ in your body melting away. Anyone who comes after my money ought to die feeling the torments of hell!”

He burst into laughter, only to let out a scream of agony a moment later. Truly horrific pain surged through him, but it was fine. He was going to die anyway, and now he could take that bastard with him.

‘Let’s die together, you son of a bitch!’

Writhing in pain, he looked at Geom Mugeuk. The final sight of this world that he would take with him would be Geom Mugeuk writhing in agony.

But the sight he had expected was not there.

‘What?’

Geom Mugeuk was still standing in the same place, silently looking at him.

‘Why?’

He had clearly been enveloped in the poison smoke along with him. It seemed as though he was looking at Yeomje with contempt, or perhaps with pity.

He was confused. Even amid the excruciating pain of his entire body melting away, he was truly curious. Why was he not dying?

Then, just before his breath was cut off, Geom Mugeuk’s voice reached his ears.

“The last number was three?”

Hearing those words, Yeomje screamed.

‘No!’

He had shown him the numbers because he had thought they would die together anyway. But if this happened?

‘It’s my money! That’s my money!’

More than the pain of his organs melting, he suffered at the thought of his money being taken from him, and then his breath ceased. Until the very moment of his death, he died as a slave to money.

Leaving his collapsed corpse behind, Geom Mugeuk entered the correct numbers on the abacus.

Then the hidden vault opened.

Inside the vault, there were only promissory notes stacked in piles.

All of them were high-denomination notes issued by the Three Great Banks of the Central Plains. They had been prepared so that he could take them and leave at any time.

Geom Mugeuk gathered them all and left the place.

He did not spare even a glance for Yeomje’s corpse. As always, he attached no meaning to the deaths of villains.

Splash, splash.

In his life, he let them flow away like the blood beneath his feet.

* * *

Geum Arin was anxiously pacing back and forth through the courtyard.

Waiting in her room had been so stifling that she simply could not stay still.

“Should I go now, even at this point?”

At her words, Im Hyeok, who was standing a little distance away, answered firmly.

“No.”

As the man whose duty was to protect Geum Arin, there was no way he would accept such a thing.

“He might die.”

“Even so, no.”

After a brief pause, Im Hyeok added,

“Young Hero Geom will be fine.”

Yes, she thought so too. If not for one thing.

“But the opponent is Baekseonbang.”

The answer that rose in Im Hyeok’s heart was:

‘Yes, even if the opponent is Baekseonbang.’

Even so, he did not say it aloud, perhaps to protect Geum Arin’s heart. If she got her hopes up for nothing and then…

Just then, the rank smell of blood drifted in on the wind.

Im Hyeok sprang forward and blocked Geum Arin’s path.

The smell of blood grew thicker and thicker.

Then the gate opened, and someone entered the inner courtyard.

“You?”

For a moment, Geum Arin could not continue speaking.

The person who had entered was Geom Mugeuk. His clothes and body were soaked entirely in blood.

“Are you hurt?”

At her startled question, Geom Mugeuk staggered over, clutched his chest, and sat down on a rock.

“I have one last request. If I die, please be sure to tell my father. This son of his, at the request of a woman, went into the jaws of death to collect money and…”

“It’s not funny, so please stop.”

“Was it obvious?”

Of course. Because his acting was exaggerated, she could tell at once.

But even if his acting had been truly realistic, she felt she would not have been fooled. This man did not seem like someone who would leave any words behind when he died. He seemed like someone who would lean against a small rock, look out over a windswept plain with those clear eyes, and quietly die.

She had never once thought about such a thing before, but seeing him pretend to die made that thought occur to her.

Only belatedly did she feel relieved.

“You came back safely.”

“Were you worried?”

Geum Arin did not answer, but he could feel how worried she had been while waiting for him.

“Here, take these.”

Geom Mugeuk pulled out a pile of envelopes from the leather pouch he was carrying.

“What are these?”

“The money that had to be recovered from Baekseonbang. Go return it.”

The envelopes had the names of merchant guilds and sects written on them. Among them was an envelope with the name of the Geumryong Clan written on it as well. When she checked inside, there was a promissory note for one million five hundred thousand nyang.

Geum Arin’s eyes widened.

“You really recovered all the money?”

“It’s the money after taking half, as promised.”

She had wished for Geom Mugeuk to return safely.

Please, just come back safely. You don’t have to bring the money back, just come back safely. She had prayed so earnestly. And yet he had even recovered all the money?

“What happened to Baekseonbang?”

To her trembling question came a shocking answer.

“Baekseonbang no longer exists in this world.”

Aghast, Geum Arin could say nothing. Im Hyeok, standing behind her, also stared wide-eyed in shock.

Of course, that must have been why he had recovered the money. But hearing it directly made her heart pound.

He destroyed Baekseonbang all by himself? Is that truly possible?

Then Geom Mugeuk held out one more envelope.

“This is money I’m giving you out of my own share.”

When she saw the amount, it was an enormous sum she had never expected.

“I don’t need it.”

“I’m not giving it to you for you to use.”

“Then?”

“There must be merchant guilds and residents whose lives have become difficult because of Baekseonbang’s tyranny.”

Of course, such people were everywhere.

“Use this money to help them.”

Geum Arin was startled. After staring at Geom Mugeuk for a moment, she asked carefully,

“Why are you asking me to do it? You could do it yourself.”

Then he could monopolize all that good deed as well.

“Didn’t you ask me to make you the successor? I’m keeping that promise.”

“What do you mean?”

“By recovering the money for each merchant guild this time, your reputation will rise greatly. If you also help those in need on top of that, you’ll take another step closer to the position of successor.”

For a moment, Geum Arin was unable to speak.

“Even I, the person directly involved, had not thought that far.”

Then Geom Mugeuk gave an unexpected answer.

“That is why I think you are worthy of becoming the successor.”

“What?”

“Because that desire is still pure.”

After looking at Geom Mugeuk for a moment, Geum Arin spoke her honest thoughts.

“That desire will soon be sullied.”

“If your connection with me continues, then at the very least, it will not be sullied.”

“Why are you so certain?”

“Because I’ll introduce you to my friends. I have some friends who are quite pure for the positions they hold. If you spend time with them, you’ll think, If even those people are pure, then what about me?”

For the positions they hold? Who on earth was he talking about?

From the moment she first saw him, Geom Mugeuk had been no ordinary person, but the more she learned about him, the more mysterious he seemed.

“You paid a high price to hire me, so I should at least do this much for you.”

Even though he had done something that would not have been excessive to boast about all day, Geom Mugeuk soon left that place.

Looking down at the envelopes Geom Mugeuk had given her, Geum Arin muttered as if to herself,

“I thought so too.”

But now, this thought came to her.

Had she not hired him for far too cheap a price?

* * *

I placed two envelopes on the table.

“I’ll divide the money I earned from this matter equally as well.”

After checking the amount of money in the envelopes, Father looked at me. He was surprised by how much money it was.

“You had amassed quite a lot. There was more originally, but I had some of it used for those who had suffered until now.”

Hwi spoke cautiously.

“The money I received earlier is already enough for me.”

Of course, I had expected Hwi to react this way.

“If you keep it, there will surely come a time when you need it.”

“No, I do not need it. Please give it to the Cult Leader.”

I persuaded him, still looking troubled.

“You have to prepare for the day when your blades no longer have strength. Travel a little, too. You’ve spent your whole life as a guard, so when that time comes, Uncle, you should have guards of your own. Very expensive ones, at that.”

From Hwi’s expression, I could tell it was something he had never once considered in his life.

Father looked at me as if to say I was getting cheeky, then said to Hwi,

“Take it.”

“Yes, Cult Leader.”

Hwi put the envelope away as well.

“Then I will accept it gratefully. I will use this money for my juniors.”

He meant he would use it for the younger guards of the Heavenly Demon Hall.

“You can’t! That’s your retirement fund, Uncle!”

At that, Hwi smiled faintly and asked me,

“Young Cult Leader, you do not intend to use that money on yourself either, do you?”

Hwi knew. The money I had earned this time would also all be invested in Eunwol and the Ghost Shadow Unit.

“I’m still young, aren’t I? Retirement planning can wait……”

“It begins when you are young.”

Seeing Hwi say something like that made me feel good.

In your past life, you lived as rigidly as ten-thousand-year cold iron, so in this life, please live more gently and joyfully.

Then Father suddenly asked me,

“Are you all right?”

It was a single sentence that struck deep into my heart.

Father had asked me the same thing last time as well.

Even after staining your hands with so much blood, is your heart all right?

Though I had washed clean and changed my clothes, it seemed I had not been able to erase all the scent of blood clinging to me.

For a moment, I examined my own heart, my own feelings.

“I think I’m all right.”

Father probably did not know how much strength that single question gave me.

And in that moment, I thought the important thing was not whether the answer was that I was all right or not.

“If it becomes difficult later, I will be sure to tell you, Father.”

Conveying this feeling—that his son was relying on his father—was surely far more important.

“For this matter, I am pushing ahead while trusting only in you, Father.”

“And what will you do if they find out we came?”

I thought that would be fine in its own way.

“Well, if things get tangled, you will untangle them for me, Father. Did I not tell you on the way here? Those people who only plot from behind the scenes need to be shown at least once. That you exist in this murim.”

It was not mere flattery, but my sincere feeling.

So, on my first journey through the martial world with Father, this was the message I would deliver to them.

“If they’re confident, let them come out and try fighting us.”

Absolute Regression, Chapter 477

Chapter 477: Even Cooking Requires Knowing the Ingredients.
-----------------------------------------------------------
My confidence was not directed at my enemies.

This was something I said to my father.

That I trusted him this much.

I didn't know how much strength these words would give Father.

Because he had never heard such words from his child before.

Would he ever have the chance to hear such words from his child in this life?

“Father, I trust you, so I will handle matters comfortably and freely, just as I please.”

That was the core of this Shaanxi operation.

But that was my thinking; Father's thoughts were different.

“Go and clean up the aftermath.”

“Understood.”

The expression of Hwi, who had received the order, brightened.

The order to clean up the aftermath meant completely erasing any traces left at Baekseonbang.

The command was to handle it as though they had vacated Baekseonbang and left.

Hwi's mission was, above all, Father's safe return to the sect.

Quietly, without drawing as much attention as possible—that was what he desired, so this was likely something he had wanted to request first.

I wanted to make this joke:

‘No, why is someone who wants to fight all of Murim being so careful!’

My itched to say it, but I held back.

Because it was best not to say things related to the Murim Unification.

Instead, I spoke to Father about this matter.

“The results of this matter will draw out the masterminds. They’ll make a move soon. They can’t afford to give up the Geumnyongsega financial line.”

They might attack me or Father, or make an unexpected move. There was no telling how they would act.

“Move carefully.”

“Yes!”

Even though he was the one who possessed the greatest power in all of Murim.

“Surely you’re being this careful because of me? Because you’re worried about your son?”

And I saw it.

That scornful smirk forming at Father’s lips.

These days, ever since we left the sect together, the mood had been good, so I hadn’t seen it often.

So I had forgotten. How good it felt to see that scoff.

“You’re too much! Worry about your son a little, would you!”

Father walked to the window without a word and gazed at the sky outside.

I stared at Father’s back like that, and the cloudless blue sky stretched out above him.

It was an unremarkable sight, but the two solitudes shared a resemblance.

‘I know well that it’s for me.’

Father wasn’t someone who would clean up traces out of concern for someone else.

Because if it were the kind of situation that required that, he wouldn’t even have drawn his sword in the first place.

So I will be careful. Don’t worry.

* * *

Geumcheonbang silently looked down at the 1.5 million nyang promissory note. This was not merely money. It was a signal that everyone’s destiny was beginning to change.

“Did he really collect this money from Baekseonbang?”

To her surprised father’s question, Geum Arin calmly replied,

“Why are you so surprised? You entrusted this to me because you expected success, didn’t you?”

She felt sorry speaking to her father like this.

Because she herself had been many times more surprised.

“What about Baekseonbang?”

Toward his disbelieving gaze, an astonishing answer flowed out.

“He said they disappeared from this world.”

Geumcheonbang couldn’t believe it. He had entrusted her with the task because of a slight hope that she might pull it off as she said. The benefits of success were too great to not entrust her with it.

But to think he really annihilated them and brought back the money?

“That can’t be.”

He must have pulled some trick.

“What about him? Why hasn’t he come?”

He wanted to see him and ask directly.

“He is my subordinate. This matter is entirely mine. If you have questions, ask me.”

Geum Arin had come alone on purpose.

Because the fight with her father over the successor position was beginning now.

“Did you verify what happened to Baekseonbang?”

For a moment, Geum Arin flinched.

“You didn’t.”

“I trust my subordinate.”

That wasn’t the reason.

She hadn’t even thought to verify it.

Because she trusted Geom Mugeuk?

No. This wasn’t a matter of trust.

She was so astonished by the results, so distracted by the man named Geom Mugeuk who had accomplished this, that she had neglected to send people to Baekseonbang to confirm what had happened.

Since he had said they disappeared from the world, she had thoughtlessly assumed he killed them all.

What if those words were a lie? What if all of this was a scheme orchestrated with Baekseonbang and the Sword Alliance?

It probably wasn’t the case, but not verifying was clearly a mistake.

‘I can’t be like this from now on.’

The reason she was reflecting so properly was that she felt she had been bewitched by the man named Geom Mugeuk. The entire way to meet her father, she had thought only of him.

“There is more I must tell you.”

Geum Arin told her father about the task entrusted by Geom Mugeuk. The fact that they had taken money from the merchant companies that had been extorted by Baekseonbang and kept half. Already yesterday and today, she had gone around to all the merchant companies and returned the money.

Geumcheonbang wasn’t surprised at all. From that reaction, she could tell her father had known beforehand. After all, among the merchant guild leaders who had asked for the money to be retrieved, there must have been those who checked separately with her father.

“Why didn’t you tell me this in advance?”

“I was going to tell you once the results came out. I had resolved to take full responsibility even if problems arose later.”

She spoke resolutely, but the truth was she hadn’t told him because she was afraid he would stop her. If this matter succeeded, she would be the one to gain the most. She had wanted to show her father after achieving results.

That was why she had come to her father last, after returning all the money to each merchant company.

When she had entered this place, she had come like a triumphant general.

Even if not quite a ‘Well done, my daughter,’ she hadn’t expected this interrogation.

“Through this matter, many merchant companies and sects that suffered at Baekseonbang’s hands will hold great goodwill toward the main family. If you make good use of that, you will see enormous profits. I didn’t spend money in vain, did I?”

Still, her father’s reaction was cold.

“What mistake did I make?”

She had thought he was angry because she hadn’t told him in advance, but that wasn’t it.

“Do you want to become the successor?”

Geumcheonbang saw through why she hadn’t told him in advance.

She answered honestly.

“Yes, I want to become the successor.”

Then, words like a bolt from the blue fell from her father’s mouth.

“Give up on becoming the successor.”

“!”

She had never imagined her father would say such words himself.

“Why?”

Geumcheonbang did not give a reason.

“Leave now.”

“Father!”

Geumcheonbang looked coldly at his daughter. Normally, when he was this angry, it was frightening, so she would quietly retreat. But not today.

“Are you angry that I didn’t tell you in advance? Or are you angry that I’m coveting the successor position?”

Geumcheonbang made no answer at all.

“Did you not consider me as a successor from the beginning?”

She raised her voice, but Geumcheonbang said nothing.

To her, his silence sounded like affirmation. Then, naturally, a sense of disappointment washed over her.

‘Is it because I’m a daughter? Or because I lack ability? Because you favor my older brother?’

Because of her disappointment, she couldn’t judge rationally.

“I will definitely become the successor.”

She declared as if making a declaration of war, then left the room.

Geumcheonbang’s gaze deepened as he stood with his back turned. It was a gaze that, had she seen it, would have made her think, ‘There must be a reason for this.’

Having come out of her father’s quarters, Geum Arin stood in the middle of the courtyard for a moment, trying to compose her emotions. She was so disappointed and angry.

Then, she thought of someone. She didn’t know why he came to mind at a time like this, but her feet naturally moved toward his quarters.

* * *

When Geum Arin arrived, Geom Mugeuk was in his room.

“Do you have a moment?”

“Of course. If the chief calls, I must make time even when I have none.”

Geom Mugeuk’s martial arts uniform was soaked with sweat.

“Please wait a moment. I shall wash and change my clothes.”

Geum Arin was inwardly puzzled.

‘What did he do in his room to sweat like that?’

Then she flinched, startled.

‘Could it be?’

Standing in the courtyard, she couldn’t contain her curiosity and entered the building.

Walking silently down the corridor, she arrived at Geom Mugeuk’s room. She peeked inside through the window gap. There was no one in the room. Seeing the empty bedding neatly arranged, she felt a needless sense of relief.

Just as she was about to turn around, she was startled. Someone was standing behind her, she knew not when.

It was Geom Woo-jin.

She was so surprised by his figure staring intently at her that she nearly screamed.

“Ah, I wasn’t peeking. I came to meet Young Hero Geom. Ah, he went to wash. No, that’s not it….”

Flustered, she rambled incoherently.

“Go in and wait.”

“No, I can wait outside. Then, I’ll see you next time.”

She bowed and ran outside as if fleeing.

Coming out into the courtyard, she sighed. She didn’t know why it was so difficult to deal with Geom Woo-jin. Besides, she had been caught peeking into the room.

After a short while, Geom Mugeuk returned.

The sight of Geom Mugeuk in a clean martial arts uniform overlapped with the sight of him returning then in blood-stained clothes.

‘Which is your true appearance?’

Not knowing what kind of person he was, with her heart in turmoil and angry, she had ended up coming to find Geom Mugeuk.

“You were sweating profusely?”

“I did some martial arts training.”

“In your room?”

“Training in a narrow place is actually more difficult and consumes more mental strength. You should try it next time, young lady.”

Of course, that wasn’t the reason.

While training in his room, he had unfolded the Space-Time Displacement Technique and even used the Sicheon Secret Art.

He saved even spare moments. For Geom Mugeuk, there was no such thing as spare time.

“You look downcast; has something happened?”

She was grateful to Geom Mugeuk for accurately reading her mood. For allowing her to speak so easily of the matter she had come for, without worrying how to broach the subject.

“Our efforts were in vain. Father has no intention of making me the successor.”

Geom Mugeuk showed no particular reaction to her words.

“Why aren’t you surprised? Surely you didn’t also think I wouldn’t become the successor, did you?”

She snapped, then realized her mistake. She realized she had reacted neurotically out of needless insecurity. Geom Mugeuk, on the other hand, was composed.

“Would you have hired me for such a large sum if this were an easy matter?”

Hearing Geom Mugeuk’s answer, Geum Arin calmed down. ‘Yes, he’s right. It wasn’t easy from the start. I had been thinking as if I had already become the successor.’

“You speak like someone who has experienced a fierce succession struggle. Even though you’ve never been through one.”

Geom Mugeuk smiled. If she knew what the fight with his older brother had been like, she would know that her struggle didn’t even qualify as a mere sparring match.

“Can you tell me why I can’t become the successor?”

Geum Arin shook her head.

If seeing that he had entrusted a secret organization to her meant he surely had faith in her, then why couldn’t she become the successor?

Geom Mugeuk began to think that the reason might be related to the masterminds behind the scenes.

Meeting Geum Arin, he had come to know. She didn’t know where the money she had earned drained away to. Geum Ajong, the second son, also seemed preoccupied only with the succession struggle.

Then the mastermind must be somewhere around the head of Geumnyongsega or the first son, Geum Ahyeok.

‘Where are you hiding?’

At that moment, a new visitor arrived there.

It was Geum Ajong, the second son.

“What brings you here, Brother?”

When he came to Geom Mugeuk’s quarters, Geum Arin showed wariness.

“There are matters between men that you wouldn’t know about.”

Geum Ajong said, looking at Geom Mugeuk.

“Surely you haven’t forgotten our plan to have a drink?”

Geum Ajong was determined to somehow introduce this swindler to his older brother. ‘If you ruin my younger sister and my older brother as well, I’ll carry you around Shaanxi on my back.’

However, before proceeding in earnest, there was one question that needed answering. Today, he had come determined to resolve that question.

“How about today? Let’s have a drink.”

“Very well. Let’s go.”

Geom Mugeuk readily accepted his proposal.

“I’ll go too.”

She didn’t want to leave Geom Mugeuk alone with her older brother. Because he was an older brother who might use some dirty trick.

But Geum Ajong wouldn’t include her.

“This is a prior engagement.”

She pleaded for help with her eyes, but Geom Mugeuk pretended not to notice. Because it was easier to handle him when it was just the two of them, Geum Ajong and himself.

* * *

A drinking table for just the two of them commenced.

Since both men had clear objectives, the atmosphere at the drinking table was truly excellent.

Geommugeuk knew very well that Geum Ajong regarded him as a swindler.

Some time ago, his subordinate I Chu had come to inspect the carriage and been caught by him. Geommugeuk had turned that around and used him to make Geum Ajong think he was a swindler.

Back then, I Chu had reported this to Geum Ajong: he had overheard them saying they should go to the clan and make one big score.

So of course he regarded him as a swindler through and through.

Conversely, I Chu had relayed Geum Ajong’s intentions to Geommugeuk.

—He means to introduce you to the Eldest Young Master and ruin you.

Geum Ajong raised his cup high and said,

“If you bear any old grievances against me, forget them.”

At Geum Ajong’s concern, Geommugeuk spoke as if he did not mind in the slightest.

“If there are old feelings, let us build new ones and bury them beneath.”

“Splendid words!”

“Now, let’s drink!”

And so the two drank merrily.

Geommugeuk boasted of his capacity and drank cup after cup. He knew the other man was trying to get him drunk and learn something from him, so he played along.

After they had drunk like that for a while and Geommugeuk began slurring his words in intoxication, Geum Ajong asked casually,

“What did you do to those Money Grubs back then?”

This was the very question he had to unravel.

There had to be a reason those ferocious men had become so polite and done the cleaning.

He could not find out through the Money Grubs either. Their pride had been wounded to the limit, and they were the sort who might draw blades if he brought it up. Perhaps if his father asked, it would be different, but they were not people who would readily tell him.

Were they truly swindlers with such remarkable skill, or had they used some other trick?

Geum Ajong was determined to find out somehow. Even when cooking, one had to know the ingredients.

“You are curious about that?”

“Of course I’m curious. It’s all in the past now, so surely you can tell me?”

With his face flushed from drink, Geommugeuk raised his fist.

“This was it. What other reason could there be?”

If that were the case, there was one problem.

What if, after making a score off his elder brother, they did not leave? What if they tried to swallow the entire clan whole? What if they truly were swindlers with that level of ability?

It was just then. I Chu’s sound transmission reached him. While Geommugeuk had left his quarters, I Chu had gone to search them.

—I found empty medicine bottles in his quarters. I checked the medicine left inside, and it was one I know.

—What medicine?

—Have you perhaps heard of Heukbi?

Geum Ajong had heard of it. There was an incredible internal energy-dispersing poison made by the Evil Dao Alliance, and its name was Heukbi.

That was it. Since all their internal energy had vanished, the Money Grubs had been unable to move an inch.

Now the secret was revealed!

Everything was going smoothly. As if fate itself were pushing him to become the successor, Geommugeuk casually asked,

“What sort of person is the Eldest Young Master?”

At the thought that he could comfortably proceed with the next plan, Geum Ajong broke into a broad smile.

‘You adorable little swindler!’

Absolute Regression, Chapter 478

Chapter 478: Just How Many Outfits Do You Have?
-----------------------------------------------
Ichu was faithfully following Geom Mugeuk's orders.

“He will ask if you can obtain the antidote for the Heukbi now. Answer that you can.”

“Understood.”

Just as Geom Mugeuk had predicted, Geum Ajong sent a voice transmission.

“Can you get the antidote for that Sangongdok?”

“I should be able to obtain it.”

“Get it as quickly as possible, no matter what it takes!”

“Understood.”

“What are you doing? Why aren’t you going quickly!”

Ichu, who had been about to leave that place, looked back at the two people.

The sight of them drinking looked as though Geum Ajong were dancing atop the palm of a demon painted on the carriage wall.

That didn’t mean he was worried about Geum Ajong. It wasn’t that he usually treated him carelessly.

‘Him and I are the same.’

It was because he thought that perhaps he too was dancing along. On the massive palm of a demon so huge that he couldn’t even tell he was on its palm right now.

“Did you ask what kind of person my older brother is?”

Geum Ajong poured alcohol into Geom Mugeuk’s cup.

Having discovered the secret behind the swindle, Geom Mugeuk now seemed manageable.

Sangongdok was a poison that prevented one from using internal energy for a certain time, not a poison that killed the opponent immediately. This meant that as long as the antidote was prepared in advance, one could sufficiently defend against it. A poison befitting a swindler.

“My older brother is an excellent man. Clever, diligent, and handsome. The one who will inherit the main family.”

Geom Mugeuk sensed it. While spouting words he did not mean, a hint of emotion seeped out when he particularly mentioned that he was handsome. Could it be that he felt an inferiority complex about his looks compared to his brother?

“If you speak so highly of him, I would like to meet him. Could you introduce me?”

Geum Ajong thought his prediction had been correct.

‘As expected, their target was my older brother!’

Those bastards had come to the family to pull a big scam, and their target was his older brother.

‘If my brother falls for these bastards?’

He would be the one catching the swindlers who robbed both the younger sibling and older brother at the last moment.

Rather than catching swindlers, he would be capturing the successor position.

At the request for an introduction, Geum Ajong subtly stepped back.

“Is my introduction really necessary? If you want to see him, just go meet him. We’re family now, after all.”

Then Geom Mugeuk refilled his cup and spoke.

“Would he welcome me if I went to meet him?”

“Indeed, given my brother’s personality, he will not like you. He will think you swindled two million nyang from the main family. Ah, please don’t misunderstand. That is not my thought.”

Now Geum Ajong slowly began to show his true colors.

“I am saying this because we were talking about my brother. If I were you, I would have helped the older brother rather than the younger brother.”

“May I ask the reason?”

“Shouldn’t a true man accomplish great things? The younger brother has no chance of becoming the successor anyway.”

Geom Mugeuk stared at Geum Ajong and asked.

“Then what about you? Do you not have confidence in becoming the successor? You seem like an ambitious man.”

Quick-witted as befits a swindler.

“I am a good-for-nothing. Overshadowed by my older brother, overshadowed by my younger brother. Reality is not kind.”

“If the ambition is great, wouldn’t the wings be large as well?”

Geom Mugeuk looked up as if he could see massive wings behind his back.

At that sight, Geum Ajong smiled. It was a trivial reaction, but he did not feel bad. If he looked ambitious, what was wrong with that?

“What satisfaction is there in helping someone who would become the successor anyway and making them the successor? It is also a problem to stand beside someone who cannot become the successor at all. I wish I had met you sooner.”

Geum Ajong was tempted by Geom Mugeuk’s regret. So he felt like revealing everything to this bastard and joining hands to scheme together. Somehow, he felt he could succeed with this guy.

But soon Geum Ajong flinched.

‘What am I thinking right now!’

Indeed, a swindler is a swindler. Without realizing it, he had entertained the absurd thought of joining hands with this bastard.

He felt that this bastard was born with his face, voice, and expression. Listening to him, his guard kept dropping.

“Let’s go together tomorrow. I will introduce you to my brother.”

“Thank you.”

“I will let you in on one thing in particular.”

It was not merely because he was in a good mood. If this bastard was to properly bring his brother in, he needed to know him well.

“There is something my brother absolutely cannot stand.”

“What is it?”

Geum Ajong emptied his last cup and spoke.

“Losing with money.”

* * *

At the same time, at the patriarch’s residence of Geumryong Sega, the awaited report was being delivered.

“Baekseonbang was completely empty.”

At his subordinate’s report, Geum Cheonbang asked in surprise.

“Are you saying there was not even a single corpse?”

“Yes. Not a single one.”

“Were there traces of a fight?”

“They remained everywhere. Only the corpses had all been cleared away, so it was impossible to know how they fought or what the result was.”

When he had first sent the subordinate, he had never dreamed he would ask such questions upon his return.

“Is there a possibility that Baekseonbang staged the whole thing?”

It was the doubt that arose from the news that all corpses had disappeared. If they had pretended to be defeated and vanished?

“The possibility remains open. They may have all died, or they may have left behind traces of a fight and fled.”

Seeing Geum Cheonbang frown at the obvious remark, the subordinate quickly added.

“If it is true that they were all defeated, one thing is certain. They could not have had much time to clear them, yet seeing how cleanly it was organized, the one responsible for the cleanup either possesses considerable martial arts prowess or is an expert in such matters.”

That was the end of the subordinate’s report.

“You may withdraw.”

“Yes.”

Geum Cheonbang felt that this matter was no ordinary one.

“A staged act by Baekseonbang? Or the work of Eunha Sangdan?”

He still found it hard to believe that Geom Mugeuk had done it.

It was then.

“It is neither.”

The one who entered was the eldest son, Geum Ahyeok.

He possessed the appearance befitting an heir of a prestigious family.

An appearance emanating the bearing of a great righteous hero.

If the second son, Geum Ajong, was the type to reveal his cunning and selfish nature through his face and actions, this Geum Ahyeok was someone who thoroughly hid himself behind the demeanor of a great hero.

“Everyone, withdraw.”

Geum Cheonbang dismissed even the hidden subordinates.

Now only the two of them remained in the patriarch’s residence.

“Neither? What do you mean?”

“A man named Geom Yeon killed all the immortals of Baekseonbang.”

“How do you know that?”

Geum Ahyeok did not answer, but Geum Cheonbang realized the reason.

“Did you hear it from that person?”

He could tell from his son’s expression.

“Did I not tell you to avoid contact with him as much as possible? I said I would handle it personally.”

Then Geum Ahyeok asked with an expression of incomprehension.

“Why do you dislike him?”

The fact that his son called him ‘that person’ rather than just ‘him’ in this atmosphere angered Geum Cheonbang.

So his voice naturally rose.

“Because he is dangerous!”

He had not given off this dangerous feeling from the start. At first, he had been gentle and calm. For a long time, he had consistently given the trust that one could gladly join hands with.

Like clothes getting wet in a drizzle, he had slowly seeped into his clan.

Only after the clothes were soaked did he realize. It was not rain, but blood.

“We must endure him! He has brought us greater profit than anywhere else under the heavens. For a merchant, what clearer reason is needed?”

Geum Cheonbang had lived his entire life as a merchant. He had experienced enough of the world and human affairs that he could roughly see and get a feel for things, roughly assess their value, and roughly know what was what. He had reached that age.

Yet lately, there were two things he could not figure out. No, two people.

His son and that person. He could not understand his son, and he could not understand him either, the one his son called ‘that person.’ When they first met, he had thoroughly analyzed and figured him out, but as over ten years passed, he now felt he understood him even less. Like the heart of his grown son.

“If we speak of danger, the Seodopa bastards that Rin brought are more dangerous. They have undoubtedly come targeting him.”

“How can you be certain?”

Geum Ahyeok did not answer.

“It was what he said again.”

Geum Ahyeok stared straight at Geum Cheonbang. At some point, his son had begun looking at him with such eyes. Such unfamiliar eyes.

Geum Cheonbang had always been proud of his eldest son from a young age. How had such a son come from someone as small and shabby as himself? He felt this satisfaction every time.

When he went out with his son, everyone praised and envied him. So he had boasted quite a bit. Enough to feel sorry for his second son.

And he had lived believing this.

That his son’s nature would be like his appearance that bore the demeanor of a great hero.

He had truly believed so. And in reality, his son had grown up well without major problems.

But at some point, he changed. He wished it had been since meeting him, but he knew well that it was not. He had felt something unfamiliar about his son even before that.

This cold?

This self-centered?

This greedy for money?

That was why he thought that he might have approached him.

Even so, he believed in his son.

He had not answered the question just now, but he believed in him. Because he was his son. If he grew a little older, he would judge situations more calmly.

“Father, did you not say you wanted to become the greatest tycoon in the Central Plains? Did you not say that was your dream?”

“I did, I did. However…”

“What are you afraid of? Are we not achieving that dream?”

He did not deny that Geumryong Sega had grown faster thanks to joining hands with him. He had earned tremendous money with his help.

But the money he demanded was snowballing as well.

“One day, he might swallow us whole.”

“Even if he swallows us, he will make us the greatest before he does.”

His son’s eyes said this.

If you are scared, step aside.

It was because of those eyes. That was why he had told his daughter she could not be the successor. Because of those eyes that would remove even a younger sibling if they obstructed his path.

“The Seodopa bastards my brother brought will all disappear before tonight passes. Just as Baekseonbang disappeared.”

“You must not!”

Geum Cheonbang’s heart raced. Killing people meant they could suffer retaliation and die themselves. How could he make such a decision so easily? You fool, we are merchants!

“If they are the ones who eliminated Baekseonbang, as you say, they are not people we can recklessly touch!”

“Did you not always say, Father?”

Geum Ahyeok added with a relaxed smile.

“That there is nothing in the world money cannot do.”

The reason Geum Cheonbang was rendered speechless was because he himself had often said those words.

No matter what… he had not known such a day would come.

* * *

After finishing the drinking session with Geum Ajong, I went to see my father.

Father and Hwi had moved their residence from the main house to Geumryong Sega here.

As we had discussed at the main house, I judged that handling Baekseonbang would cause them to move in earnest soon. Because they would try somehow to protect Geumryong Sega, this massive source of money.

I hesitated for a moment in front of Father’s room.

Was it because of the dizzying intoxication? I had come because I wanted to see Father, but thinking about it, it was already his bedtime.

Father, it is your second son!

It was a day when I wanted to bang on the door and throw a drunken tantrum, but I did not dare.

‘The martial artists will never know. That the Heavenly Demon goes to bed this early. Surely a Heavenly Demon should be awake even at the break of dawn, don’t you think!’

As I was quietly turning around without making a sound, the door opened.

I turned around in surprise, and there stood Father.

Rather than asking if he had not slept yet, rather than apologizing for waking him, these words came out first inevitably.

“Another floral pattern? Just how many of them do you have?”

Father was wearing floral pajamas.

Would the Heavenly Demon really wear such pajamas? I mean, those ultimate assassin-proof pajamas.

He had not worn them when camping, so I did not know, and when staying at inns, I had not seen him at night, so I did not know.

He should have been somewhat awkward and embarrassed, but Father was not ashamed of these pajamas at all.

Could he really wear these pajamas confidently even in front of the Martial Alliance Leader or the Demonic Path Alliance Leader?

“What is it?”

At his blunt question, I did not know what to answer. I had really just come on impulse because the alcohol made me want to see him.

“I just came to wish you a good night.”

“You woke a sleeping person to tell them to sleep well?”

I bowed my head deeply to my dumbfounded father. This was the only thing I could think of to say right now.

“Thank you.”

What I felt during this trip was that Father was indulging me. After staying only at the main headquarters, how could this trip, taken after such a long time, be purely pleasant?

Moreover, I was acting willfully, trying to do everything I had not done with Father. Waking Father tonight was surely demanding his patience.

I thought he might strike the back of my bowed head.

Father, who had looked down at me for a moment, closed the door and went inside.

Although I had woken Father, I was happy to see his face.

It was a brief, insignificant encounter, but even this would become a memory for me. The title would be something like “A Small Drunken Folly at Midnight.”

The true victory in my life was not killing enemies, but moments like these.

‘Even if Father finds it bothersome, I will keep making these small memories.’

Even if Hwamugi draws his sword right before my eyes, I will banter with Father. I will build memories with my demonic followers and my subordinates as well.

And so I will console my past self, who fell asleep alone in terrible loneliness, waiting only for this moment. Though it is a vow I always make.

‘Not for Father, but for myself.’

Because I know well that only then is it truly for the sake of the other.

Standing before the window in the hallway outside Father’s room, I looked up at the dark night sky. It was a night when both stars and moon were hidden by clouds.

Then my gaze fell like a meteor, glaring at the courtyard shrouded in darkness.

I slowly placed my index finger over my lips and spoke in a low voice.

“Shh! Father is sleeping.”

Absolute Regression, Episode 479.

Chapter 479: I Tried to Handle It as Quietly as Possible.
---------------------------------------------------------
The intruders were assassins.

Having faced many assassins before my regression, I knew the aura unique to them.

A killing intent devoid of emotion.

Was it because they did not kill out of resentment or hatred toward their targets? Or was it because, upon becoming assassins, they first trained themselves to eliminate emotion?

Their killing intent was subtly different.

If I had noticed them, then Hwi naturally would have noticed them as well. No—since he was the one who discovered the intruders, he must have noticed them even sooner than I did.

Since I had stepped forward, he was probably hiding somewhere around Father’s residence, watching the situation unfold.

Of course, if I had noticed them and Hwi had noticed them, then Father must have known about the intruders as well. The Heavenly Demon Body-Protecting Art, which had reached the Great Perfection, would have naturally warned him of the danger.

Just trust me and go back to sleep. Blood splattered on that nightclothes of yours wouldn’t suit you, would it?

I quietly opened the door and stepped into the courtyard.

Standing there, I stared into the darkness, then once again put a finger to my lips and whispered.

“Father’s sleeping, so let’s fight quietly.”

Yet no one revealed a sign of their presence, as though there was no one here at all. The only sound around us was the chirping of insects in the grass.

If Father had been awake, he certainly would have said this:

You’re the noisiest one here!

The thought brought a smile to my lips.

At that moment, as though responding to my words, someone concealed in the darkness directly ahead revealed the faintest trace of their presence.

It was no mistake.

The moment they realized I had detected them, they had revealed their position and made themselves bait.

The other assassins nearby would be waiting without even breathing for me to attack him.

That was what made assassins so frightening.

Assassins were taught that even if every one of them died, all that mattered was for a single person to succeed. Thus, although there were many of them, they were also a single assassin.

Since my opponent had thrown out bait, I should take a bite at least once, if only to respect his sincerity.

I launched myself toward the assassin who had revealed his presence.

I flew forward swiftly, yet no sound of wind could be heard. Like movement in a world where sound had vanished, my footwork was smooth and silent.

My opponent did not retreat. He revealed himself from within the darkness. A black mask, black clothes, and even black shoes. He was disguised in black from head to toe.

The saying that an assassin whose hiding place had been discovered was an assassin who had exhausted his life did not apply to this man.

He was bait, but not merely bait.

His movements were exceedingly fast and precise. He possessed enough skill to kill most experts in a head-on confrontation. His martial arts were so soundly trained that I even felt it was a waste for someone like him to work as an assassin.

That was probably why he had revealed his presence as the representative. For the other assassins to wait for an opportunity while we fought, he had to be capable of enduring a direct confrontation.

Of course, I gave him no such opportunity.

To prevent the sound of sword striking sword, I decided the match with a single blow.

At the instant his sword flew toward my face, my figure vanished from that spot.

Having employed the Shadow Step, I was behind him.

Thud!

Even with the sword thrusting through his back and protruding from his chest, he desperately held back his scream. He was someone who had made it second nature never to make a sound, no matter the situation. This alone told me for certain that they were exceptionally well-trained assassins.

‘They must have paid a fortune to hire these men.’

Because the first assassin had died too quickly, no one else stepped forward. They had not obtained the chance to launch a surprise attack.

They were probably waiting for their next opportunity.

For the moment when I let my guard down, thinking that only this one man had entered.

If I searched the corpse’s clothing to find out who they were, a fatal attack would rain down upon me.

Of course, I did not move as they wanted me to.

I caught the assassin as he collapsed into a corpse, lowering him gently to the ground without making a sound.

At the same time, I looked toward the darkness ahead and put a finger to my lips.

I had not done so at random. The energy that had already spread from my body like a spider’s web had seized control of the entire area.

This time, my gesture was directed at the place where the web’s vibrations were strongest.

Realizing that his position had been exposed, the assassin immediately burst forth and flew toward me. He had abandoned defense entirely, delivering a killing blow meant only to take my life.

Shiiing.

The sword that flew at me grazed past my face. The reflection of my eyes in its blade held not a trace of emotion.

Swish! Slash!

The Black Demon Sword drew a straight line of sword light and cut across his chest. He instinctively twisted his body to evade it, but two-thirds of his chest, along with his heart, had already been severed.

The cut had been so fast and sharp that he died without even having time to feel pain.

Once again, I was slicing silently through the air.

Thud!

My sword pierced through a tree as smoothly as sinking into tofu—yet faster than any swift sword.

The assassin hiding behind the tree was impaled, but once again, he made no sound.

Instead, even as he died, he gripped the blade with both hands.

How could a dying man possibly hold my Black Demon Sword?

I pulled out the sword and sprang upward along the tree. The assassin’s fingers, still clutching the sword, were severed and scattered in all directions.

Sword light flashed through the darkness.

Thud! Thud!

There had originally been three assassins clinging to the tree. Who would have imagined that three men were attached to a single tree? It was a feint concealed within a feint, but they could not evade my energy spread out like a spider’s web.

By the time the two assassins clinging to the tree like cicadas had fallen and landed atop the first corpse, I had already touched down before the wall more than a dozen paces away.

As expected, the Black Demon Sword sank smoothly into the wall.

Shraaak!

With the sword embedded in the wall, I slid along it.

Like skating across ice, my figure raced sideways along the wall.

At the same time, the Black Demon Sword tore straight across the wall.

The bodies of the assassins who had been holding their breaths while leaning against the other side of the wall were cleaved in two and collapsed.

I could sense the assassins’ shock and confusion.

Judging by their skill at concealing themselves, they must have been assassins who had earned recognition by killing countless people. Whenever they accepted a contract, they must have killed their targets and returned without fail.

Yet today, they were falling one after another without even managing to properly raise a hand.

‘There’s a traitor among us!’

Their positions had been exposed with pinpoint accuracy. They were probably thinking something like that.

I dispatched them swiftly.

They had come here with the purpose of killing me, Father, and Hwi. They were men who killed people for money. I had no reason to hesitate.

When I climbed onto the roof, concealed weapons came flying at me.

Swish! Swish! Swish! Swish!

I ran between the flying weapons. Though I was racing across the roof, my footsteps made no sound.

In the blink of an eye, I reached the far end of the roof, and the assassins who had thrown the weapons fell to the ground.

Despite falling from such a height, they made no thudding sound. Just before their corpses struck the ground, I used Empty-Catching to prevent any noise.

As I continued to find them despite their concealment, the assassins could endure no longer. They attacked first.

Two assassins attacked simultaneously, one from atop a tree and the other from the darkness to my left.

This time, I employed the Underworld King’s Step. My target was the assassin rushing in from the left.

The assassin who had sent countless people to their deaths could not stop this step toward death, just as all those who had died by his hand had been unable to.

As he fell with blood spurting from his throat, the assassin dropping from the tree was already throwing concealed weapons.

Fwoosh! Thud!

The concealed weapon and the throwing dagger passed through the air. The former disappeared into the distant darkness, while the dagger I had thrown vanished into the black-clad man’s body.

The assassin who appeared from behind the stone lantern held a concealed weapon in his hand. It was not a weapon meant to be thrown like a dagger. It was a weapon that launched poisoned needles with tremendous force from a round cylinder.

Flash!

I rushed toward him with the Flickering Step.

Slash! Slash!

Before he could fire the weapon, his wrist and neck were severed in succession.

“We agreed to fight quietly, didn’t we?”

If he had fired it, there would have been an explosion.

Assassins of this caliber rarely used concealed weapons that made such a loud noise. That they had resorted to one meant the situation had become that desperate.

One of the assassins spread out a net and threw it over me.

It was an iron-steel net used by assassins. Densely woven like a web, this special net could not easily be cut by blades—or even by sword qi. If it were fixed in place and cut with sword qi, it could be severed, but having it thrown over one during an actual fight would cause a moment of confusion and make one flail helplessly.

At that moment, another assassin flew overhead, cutting through the darkness.

Shraaak!

What he scattered was Bone-Melting Acid. It was a vicious move meant to trap me in the net and melt me away. Their coordination was so perfect that the movements of the two assassins seemed like the movements of a single person.

With two slashes, the net split into four pieces, and by the time the Bone-Melting Acid poured down, I was no longer there.

Saaaash!

The instant I slit the throat of the assassin who had thrown the net, the assassin who had passed overhead landed on the ground in the distance. A dagger I had thrown was already lodged in his throat. Naturally, Empty-Catching allowed him to fall silently as well.

That was when it happened.

Shrieeek!

A sword sprang up from the ground and passed within a hair’s breadth of my face.

It was the assassin’s desperate final move from concealment.

He had dug his way underground and waited at precisely this spot.

The power behind that strike told me everything I needed to know. He was the most skilled of all the assassins I had faced so far. This attack had been the core of today’s ambush.

But that was their concern.

To me, he was merely an assassin who had failed to kill me—and perhaps the assassin who had killed the most people of them all.

My sword was already buried deep in his shoulder, extending downward.

At the last moment, he tried to scream. I could not tell whether he intended to ruin the end of this silent battle, or whether he simply could no longer endure the pain of being stabbed from his shoulder into his vitals.

But my hand was faster. I covered his mouth with my left hand and pulled out my sword.

As the sword slid free, he breathed his last while vomiting blood. Half of his body was still in the pit in the ground.

I released my qi and surveyed the area. There was no assassin left alive or hiding in this courtyard.

I slowly moved toward the building and entered.

* * *

The assassin crawled across the ceiling.

There were only a few rooms left before he reached the target. The situation outside was clearly not normal.

Even beneath the ceiling he was crawling along, the sounds of swords cutting through flesh and tearing it apart could be heard. He could not see what was happening, but instinctively he knew. The men who had come with him were dying one after another.

He instinctively realized that the success or failure of today’s contract rested on him.

‘Even better.’

He would be able to keep all the money that would otherwise have been divided among them.

‘I’ll be the one to kill him.’

He moved little by little, ever so slowly.

An assassin’s true skill lay in infiltrating without revealing his presence, enduring unseen for hours if necessary, and determining the precise moment when the enemy could be eliminated with certainty.

In that regard, he was the most patient of all the assassins who had come today. Once he reached the spot directly above the target’s head, success was assured.

No one could block a poisoned needle fired silently from the ceiling while asleep.

Moreover, it was not an ordinary poisoned needle. This needle, thin as a strand of hair, was a special concealed weapon that killed its victim without them even realizing they had been pricked.

It was so thin that even an autopsy afterward would not reveal where the victim had been struck and poisoned.

To check how far he had come, he made a hole in the ceiling.

The instant he looked down through it—

“!”

He was terrified.

Someone below was looking up through the hole.

The next moment—

Thud!

A sharp sword pierced upward and passed through the assassin’s neck.

The one who killed him was Hwi.

Blood trickled down from the ceiling pierced by the sword. Then a voice came from behind.

“It’s raining heavily in here.”

Geom Mugeuk entered the room and gazed at the blood running down from the ceiling.

Corpses were strewn across the corridor as well. Every assassin within the residence, including those who had approached from behind, had been eliminated.

“Are you all right?”

“I’m fine. What about you, Uncle?”

“I’m fine as well.”

I was certain that even if I had not been here, Hwi alone would have stopped today’s intrusion.

An assassin and a guard warrior.

Within that natural enemy relationship, Hwi stood at the very pinnacle of an assassin’s natural enemies.

A martial artist might be able to kill me, but an assassin could never kill me.

Hwi possessed that kind of pride.

At that moment, a presence stirred inside the room.

“Father’s awake. Let’s go in.”

Geom Mugeuk and Hwi entered the room.

Geom Ujin had risen from his bed and was drinking cold tea poured from the kettle on the table.

“I tried to deal with things as quietly as possible. I’m sorry.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s words, Geom Ujin turned his head and looked at his son.

“All I heard was your voice.”

Geom Mugeuk smiled. This was why he had been talking so loudly. He had wanted to hear those words.

“That was me restraining myself as much as I could.”

A faint smile appeared at the corner of Father’s mouth.

Hwi reported to Father.

“The intruders were assassins.”

The faint smile that had appeared on Father’s lips vanished. Father disliked the Poison King and the Soul-Subduing Demon Venerable—how could he possibly like assassins?

Geom Mugeuk asked Hwi.

“Did you happen to determine where they came from?”

As the Heavenly Demon’s chief guard, Hwi was the person most knowledgeable about the assassins of the martial world. The more famous they were, the more he knew about them.

As expected, Hwi knew who their opponents were.

“Judging by their methods of attack, these men were assassins from the Underworld.”

The Underworld.

It was one of the Three Great Assassin Organizations of the martial world, infamous as a symbol of dread and terror.

The moment Geom Mugeuk heard the name Underworld, one man came to mind.

He had many titles.

The Supreme Assassin, born with an innate talent for killing.

The Ruthless Assassin who killed his father, the leader of the Underworld, and seized the organization for himself.

The man who would later unify the assassin world and become the king of all assassins.

The God of Death, said to be capable of cutting even Yama’s head from his shoulders.

But the title with the greatest meaning to Geom Mugeuk was this one:

The Fourth King among the Twelve Zodiac Kings—the Killing King.

He could not know what the situation was yet, but he instinctively sensed that the Killing King was behind the matter involving the Golden Dragon House. Where there was money, blades were sure to follow.

The Killing King.

He could not let down his guard. None of the Twelve Zodiac Kings were easy opponents, but the Killing King in particular had to be approached with caution.

He was a man born with the greatest talent for killing others, regardless of the means or methods required.

The wind blowing in through the window carried the hot scent of the dead assassins’ blood.

With his gaze deepened, Geom Mugeuk calmly said,

“It seems we’ll have to see a great deal of blood this time.”

Chapter 480: Sometimes Parents Do Win Against Their Children.
-------------------------------------------------------------
“The mastermind we’re looking for seems to have deep ties to an assassin organization.”

First, he had to inform his father and Hwi of the situation accurately.

“Why do you think so?”

Hwi was asking whether it might simply have been a commission placed by the Geumryong Family.

“As far as I know, large assassin organizations like Myeongbu prioritize a thorough investigation of their targets. Especially for a large-scale killing like today’s.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s words, Hwi nodded and replied.

“That is correct.”

“Considering the date we arrived here, they wouldn’t have had time to investigate properly. Nor does it seem they sent them knowing our identities.”

This time, he added while looking at his father, who was standing by the window.

“If they’d known the target was Father, they never would have accepted the commission. If they had still forced it through, every top-class assassin in the Central Plains should have come.”

Would his father possibly enjoy getting entangled in a fight with assassins? Even the smell of blood wafting in from the window right now must be irritating him. That was why he had said it. So that he might feel a little better, at least like this.

“Therefore, this was not an ordinary commission. The mastermind must have deep ties to an assassin organization. Whoever it is, they must be someone in a position to skip the investigation and mobilize Myeongbu’s assassins in a short time.”

Hwi nodded as if he agreed.

“When it comes to assassins, you’re the expert, Uncle, so I’ll be counting on you from now on. In particular, please educate me about assassins.”

If he had learned about the merchant world from the head of the Eunha Merchant Guild, he intended to learn about assassins from Hwi.

Normally, Hwi would have politely declined, but given the situation, he accepted readily.

“Very well.”

Geom Mugeuk did not miss this chance and threw a joke at his father, who was standing by the window.

“Father, Uncle Hwi will take care of everything. Let’s go fishing.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s joke, Geom Woojin spoke without turning around.

“If you’re going to put Hwi in front, then you take responsibility and protect him.”

Both Geom Mugeuk and Hwi were surprised by the unexpected words.

There were times when his father startled people like this.

“No, please take a joke as a joke! How am I supposed to protect the person who’s best at protecting others in this entire world?”

His father kept looking out the window as if it had not been a joke, and Hwi gave Geom Mugeuk an awkward smile.

This was the first time the Cult Leader had expressed concern for his safety in such direct words.

The heart of a man like Hwi could not be moved even if one divided a huge fortune into three and gave him a share—no, even if one gave him all of it. But a single remark like this from the Cult Leader made his heart sincerely happy.

The person he was grateful to was the Cult Leader, but he had to acknowledge this much.

This is all thanks to the Young Cult Leader.

“Uncle, now you’ll have to teach me not only about assassins, but also about escort warriors.”

Looking troubled, Hwi excused himself.

“I will clear away the corpses.”

Then Geom Mugeuk stopped him.

“Leave the corpses as they are.”

Geom Mugeuk’s gaze went out the window, far toward the Family Head’s Hall.

“The person who sent them should be made to clean them up.”

* * *

“You sent assassins?”

Geum Cheonbang was truly dumbfounded.

His son had learned martial arts, yes, but it was only the level of martial arts the eldest son of a thriving merchant guild might learn. If anything, Geum Arin’s martial skill was stronger.

“How could you…….”

There was no need to hear the answer. That absurd incident surely had “him” involved. But he had not imagined his son had been influenced to the point of calling in assassins without hesitation.

“You did something this important without even discussing it with your father?”

Geum Cheonbang shouted, but his son showed no sign of apology.

“It was to protect him.”

Geum Cheonbang was stunned, but what mattered now was not the reason, but the result.

“Fine. Let’s say that is so. But do you even know what kind of people assassins are? They are the sort who use the fact that you commissioned them as leverage to extort money from you for the rest of your life.”

“That is only third-rate assassins. The people I sent are not third-rate.”

“You have no experience using assassins, do you?”

Geum Ahyeok looked into Geum Cheonbang’s eyes and asked in return.

“Why do you think I don’t?”

“You wretch!”

Geum Cheonbang roared. He was so furious he did not know what to say. He was a man who had not been easily agitated even by all manner of disputes in the merchant world. After all, he had spent his life fighting battles in which the side that lost its composure first was the loser.

But now he had lost. Against his own child, it was a complete defeat.

Unlike the agitated Geum Cheonbang, Geum Ahyeok was calm.

“By now, they should be dead.”

As if the matter were already over, Geum Ahyeok rose from his seat.

“Please rest, Father.”

The moment his eyes met those of his son, who was bowing politely, Geum Cheonbang’s heart sank.

Eyes colder and more frigid than anything. Recently, he had seen that unfamiliar look several times, but today it felt the most unfamiliar of all.

With that, Geum Ahyeok left the Family Head’s Hall.

Crash!

The teapot and teacups on the table fell to the floor and shattered. It was the first time he had ever broken something out of anger.

He was a man so good at enduring things. Yet when it came to his child, enduring was difficult.

Geum Cheonbang brought out liquor kept in a cabinet on one wall and gulped it down straight from the bottle.

Whenever he heard that the hardest thing in the world was raising children, he had thought it had nothing to do with him. He had been too proud raising children others envied.

But as if to make him pay the price for every moment he had boasted of his son to others, that son had suddenly changed.

He restrained the urge to throw even the liquor bottle and set it down on the table. Now was not the time to smash things, but the time to handle the aftermath.

‘What am I to do about this?’

Whether the assassination succeeded or failed, it was a problem. If this assassination succeeded, then from now on, whenever his son faced difficulty, he would try to solve it this way. No, perhaps he was already in such a state.

And if it failed? If it was revealed that his son had hired assassins?

Both possibilities were things he did not want to imagine.

Just then, the door to the main hall opened.

Creak.

Looking at the door, Geum Cheonbang hoped.

He hoped his son had come back and would say, “I am sorry for handling it without consulting you, Father.” If he did, then somehow they could join forces and devise a plan.

“There’s no one standing guard outside.”

The person who stepped inside was Geom Mugeuk.

Seeing him, Geum Cheonbang’s eyes widened in surprise.

‘The assassination failed.’

Geum Cheonbang struggled to calm himself and received Geom Mugeuk.

“I had dismissed my men for a while.”

It was a situation in which he had sent all his subordinates away to have a private talk with his son.

‘Surely not? He didn’t overhear everything we said, did he?’

If he had heard that his son sent assassins? If so, his son’s life was in danger.

“I happened to see someone on my way here. A young gentleman was leaving the Family Head’s Hall. Was that perhaps the eldest young master?”

“That’s right. I had a talk with my eldest.”

When Geom Mugeuk mentioned his son, Geum Cheonbang’s heart began to pound.

‘Is he probing me? Or is he hinting that he heard our conversation?’

Because he had a guilty conscience, all sorts of needless thoughts came to him.

“As I’d heard, he was truly tall and handsome. He really had the bearing of a great hero.”

Those were words that had always made him feel pleased, but not today. The unfamiliar look with which his son had gazed at him came back to mind.

“So? Did you exchange greetings?”

“No, I only saw him from afar.”

“And yet you saw his face in such detail?”

“I have good eyes.”

Geom Mugeuk looked around, then pointed at a distant wall.

“Can you see the leaves in the flower pattern on that wall?”

“They’re too small. I can’t see them.”

“There are nine. Go and check.”

This man had collected money from Baekseonbang and made the assassination fail.

And now he was telling him to go count flower petals.

This incomprehensible specialness truly seemed to exist in a realm that was, quite literally, beyond comprehension.

“Never mind the petals. But what brings you here at this hour?”

“I came because I had something urgent to tell you.”

“What is it?”

“Assassins entered my quarters.”

Geum Cheonbang pretended to be shocked.

“Assassins? Is that true?”

He acted like someone who truly knew nothing. He was a man who had experienced all sorts of things in the merchant world, where deceiving and being deceived were everyday matters. If nothing else, he was more confident than anyone in hiding his true feelings.

“Were you hurt anywhere?”

“Fortunately, I’m fine.”

“And the assassins?”

“I dealt with them all.”

“I’ll send people at once to clear away the corpses. I’ll also prepare a new guest hall for you.”

“Thank you.”

Ordinarily, he should have said that he would investigate the corpses and find out where the assassins came from. But he omitted those words. He was in a situation where he had to hide even the evidence that existed.

Geum Cheonbang called his subordinates and sent them urgently to the guest hall.

“Clear away the corpses and move those staying there to a new guest hall.”

“Understood.”

Before the subordinates left, Geom Mugeuk stepped in.

“But shouldn’t you not simply clear them away, but examine the corpses to investigate which organization they came from?”

Geum Cheonbang was inwardly flustered, but he forced himself to speak calmly.

“Of course we should.”

“And you should sound the alarm and strengthen the guard around the Family Head’s Hall as well.”

“I was going to do so.”

As though that were truly the case, he repeated Geom Mugeuk’s words and gave the order to his subordinate.

A short while later, the sound of bells rang noisily outside.

Geum Cheonbang felt as frantic as those bells that kept ringing without rest. He knew full well it was something his son had done, and yet.

‘Assassins entered the main house?’

Just the fact that assassins had entered made his whole body tremble. Thinking that men who made a living killing people for money had roamed inside the Geumryong Family made his chest feel tight.

Rage and bewilderment. At least those two emotions required no acting.

“You should be careful from now on as well, Family Head.”

“What do you mean?”

“There were quite a number of assassins. If no one allowed them to infiltrate, doesn’t that mean the Geumryong Family was breached?”

Was he probing? Or was it a warning? Geum Cheonbang could not tell what Geom Mugeuk was thinking. It seemed as though he knew nothing at all, and yet also as though he knew something.

“From now on, I will strengthen the guard thoroughly.”

Geum Cheonbang had always had a pathological fear of martial artists.

No matter how difficult matters with merchants were, he was confident he could overcome them somehow, but once martial artists became involved, he grew deeply uneasy.

Reason did not work on them, calculations did not work on them. Those people who drew their swords first had frightened and repulsed him since childhood.

He was even afraid of the martial artists of his own family. What if someone recruited by a rival merchant guild suddenly drew a sword and stabbed him? This life he did not want to lose even if he died would end with that single thrust.

It must have been because of this fundamental fear.

That he had wanted his sons to handle affairs with martial artists in his stead.

That he had attached an expensive martial arts master to his daughter, who had the greatest martial talent among his three children.

That he had entrusted matters concerning him to his eldest son.

Even though he knew well that escape always demanded such a costly price.

“Are you all right?”

“I’m fine. I was only a little startled.”

Geom Mugeuk asked as if speaking to himself.

“Who in the world tried to kill me?”

At Geom Mugeuk’s meaningful question, Geum Cheonbang forced himself to answer calmly.

“Wouldn’t you know that better than I?”

“True! I have lived a life with many enemies.”

The reason Geom Mugeuk had come here at this hour was to confirm whether Geum Cheonbang was involved in sending the assassins.

If he showed up alive and well on the very day the assassins were sent, and right after the killing had been carried out, something would surely reveal a clue.

And Geom Mugeuk was able to tell who had sent the assassins.

It was not because of Geum Cheonbang’s bewilderment, which he had failed to hide completely no matter how experienced he was.

It was because of that.

Geom Mugeuk’s gaze went to the teacups and teapot shattered to pieces on the floor.

“Did you throw them?”

“No. I dropped them by mistake.”

“It isn’t easy for them to break this completely, though.”

From those broken teacups, he could tell. The one who had sent the assassins was Geum Ahyeok, his son. Geum Cheonbang must have realized that fact only a short while ago and revealed his rage.

“Raising children isn’t easy, is it?”

Geum Cheonbang was flustered by the sudden question.

“What do you mean by that all of a sudden?”

He must think I threw them because I was angry at my son!

Unlike Geum Cheonbang, whose urgent desire was to keep the topic from focusing on his son as much as possible, Geom Mugeuk turned the subject toward himself.

“I was talking about myself. I’ve caused my father quite a lot of grief.”

“A son who truly causes grief doesn’t even know that he does. Still, what can one do? No matter how much trouble they cause, they are still one’s children, are they not? They say there is no parent who can defeat their child.”

At that, Geom Mugeuk suddenly said,

“Are there not times when a parent must become one who can best their own child?”

“!”

The moment he heard those words, Geum Cheonbang was certain.

‘He seems to know something. He thinks Ahyeok sent them.’

All this pressure had been deliberate on Geom Mugeuk’s part. Mistakes always came when one was hurried and flustered. And that mistake would guide him to the one behind it all.

“In that regard, my father is the best there is.”

“Your father?”

“You should ask him for advice sometime.”

Geom Mugeuk added with a smile,

“When it comes to parents who can beat their children, he’s the foremost expert.”

* * *
