The next day, Geum Ahyeok greeted the two people entering his office with a stiff expression.

“I know you’re usually displeased with me, but is there any need to glare like that?”

It was not because of his younger brother that Geum Ahyeok’s face had hardened. It was because of Geom Mugeuk, who had come in with Geum Ajong. A man who should have been dead had arrived.

Up until the emergency bell rang last night, he had been in a good mood. He had been thinking that soon, news would come that the Seodo Sect staying in the guest hall had been annihilated.

But he had truly never imagined hearing that all the assassins had been killed.

“This is the expensive gentleman my younger brother brought in for two million nyang. You know him, right?”

At Geum Ajong’s introduction, Geom Mugeuk greeted him politely with a cupped-fist salute.

“Pleased to meet you. I am Geom Yeon.”

As if nothing had happened in the night, Geom Mugeuk was facing Geum Ahyeok calmly.

“I have heard much about you, Eldest Young Master. That is why I wished to meet you at least once.”

“What did you hear?”

“That you are the most likely to become the next family head.”

Geum Ahyeok looked at his younger brother. There was no way that bastard had said anything good about him.

“That is something we’ll only know once it happens. I’m busy today.”

It was an order to leave as soon as they had exchanged greetings.

Geum Ahyeok lowered his gaze back to the desk and did not raise his head again.

Geum Ajong knew his elder brother’s temperament well, so he understood that saying anything here would be pointless. So he sent Geom Mugeuk a look telling him they should leave for today.

“Then let us meet again another time.”

Geom Mugeuk, who had been about to turn after saying farewell, turned back as if something had just occurred to him.

“Ah, our Eldest Young Master does not ask, I see.”

Geum Ahyeok looked at Geom Mugeuk with eyes that asked what he was trying to say.

“Did you not hear the news that assassins entered the guest hall where I stayed yesterday?”

“I heard.”

The emotion contained in Geum Ahyeok’s cold gaze was this: And what, am I supposed to worry about you?

“Aren’t you curious? What sort of assassins they were, why they came to kill us. What kind of fool sent them.”

Seeing Geum Ahyeok’s eyes sharpen in that instant, Geom Mugeuk leisurely cast out his fishing line, baited with curiosity.

“I think I know who it was.”

Absolute Regression, Chapter 481

Chapter 481: A Life Where Nothing Happens.
------------------------------------------
Assassins who never failed had failed.

He had been confident that a martial artist’s fight and an assassin’s fight were different. That no matter how great an expert, they could not withstand a hidden blade.

Why had that hidden blade failed to pierce the belly of that smirking bastard?

In any case, he had judged that remaining with that man was disadvantageous to him, and had coldly issued an order of dismissal.

As if saying he seemed to know who had sent the assassins?

This was bait he could not resist biting.

“Who is it?”

Geom Mugeuk stared at him and spoke.

“It seems you are finally curious.”

Geum Ahhyeok responded calmly.

“I believe this assassination attempt is related to your grudge. So I am not curious about the client. I am trying to see what kind of person you are. How honest a person you are.”

It was not easy to be so shameless and confident when someone you had tried to kill was standing alive before your eyes. Moreover, in a situation where the opponent’s martial arts were overwhelming. In other words, it was proof that he had someone he trusted.

‘So this guy meets with the backer directly.’

Thus, Geom Mugeuk inferred the relationship with the mastermind from Geum Ahhyeok’s reaction.

“So tell me.”

Between the two, the second son, Geum Ahjong, was caught in a misunderstanding.

‘This cute little swindler, going so far as to stage a false flag?’

Geum Ahjong had regarded the assassins’ intrusion the night before as Geom Mugeuk’s staged act.

Assassins suddenly entering the Geumryong Clan, which had been perfectly fine all its life—wasn’t that impossible?

But he had never imagined that his father or brother had called the assassins. He thought he had the worst temper in the family, but as it turned out, he was the most naive.

‘In any case, he succeeded in catching my brother’s attention.’

Geum Ahjong was curious how this swindler would manipulate his brother.

Geum Ahhyeok pressed strongly.

“If you do not wish to speak, leave. And never seek me out again.”

Geom Mugeuk spoke with a smile.

“I am someone who wishes to become close with the Eldest Young Master. Could you not tell from my visit today to pay my respects?”

Geom Mugeuk sat at the reception table in the office.

“Ah, this is the kind of story that should be shared over a drink at a street tavern. You people must drink at expensive taverns or pleasure houses, but among the shabby taverns on the street, there are hidden gastronomic gems like Jangbyeongdo.”

“If you’re going to spout nonsense, leave!”

Geum Ahhyeok glared at his younger brother. His eyes told him to take him and get out immediately.

‘Ah, so this guy isn’t easy for my brother either.’

Lately, his brother had changed a lot. He had never liked him to begin with, but these days he even seemed repulsive and strange.

“Don’t press him so hard. That man must have said that because he knows something.”

Then, Geom Mugeuk suddenly spoke.

“You.”

Silence fell for a moment.

“The client is you.”

Geum Ahhyeok had never imagined he would be accused. And to his face, at that.

Geum Ahjong, who had been listening, burst into laughter.

“As expected, an interesting fellow.”

But Geom Mugeuk and Geum Ahhyeok did not laugh. The smile vanished from Geum Ahjong’s face as he laughed aloud.

“What? Surely you were not speaking in earnest?”

Geom Mugeuk nodded. The word “Madman!” involuntarily escaped Geum Ahjong’s lips.

“Why do you suspect me? Choose your words carefully. You are currently treating the Eldest Young Master of the Geumryong Clan as the one who commissioned the assassins.”

Geom Mugeuk stared at him. His turmoil could be felt from his trembling pupils. No matter how hard he tried, it would not be easy to remain calm in this situation.

Then, another shocking statement flowed from Geom Mugeuk.

“One of the assassins confessed your name as he died.”

Geom Mugeuk lied without a change in expression.

On the other hand, Geum Ahhyeok flustered at the word “confession.” The corners of his eyes trembled.

Geum Ahjong’s heart sank as he watched that sight. His brother’s eye corners had always trembled like that when he was struck in a vital point or when his lie was exposed. He had been like that since childhood.

“If you heard such words, why tell them to me?”

To the calm question, Geom Mugeuk replied with a smile.

“I believe our Eldest Young Master has been framed. Would the future successor of the Geumryong Clan possibly commit such an act? I am now a member of the Geumryong Clan as well; should I not provide such information to the one who will inherit the clan?”

Geum Ahhyeok said nothing.

As the conversation could continue no further, Geom Mugeuk rose from his seat.

“You must be busy; I have taken too much of your time. I shall take my leave for today.”

He added one more thing before leaving.

“Think carefully about who might have framed you.”

Leaving those words behind, Geom Mugeuk left the place first.

Geum Ahjong looked at his brother. Geum Ahhyeok went to the window and did not look back.

His heart was half-convinced, half-doubting now.

‘Did he really commission the assassins? No?’

If this were true, it was the perfect opportunity to drive his brother out of the successor position.

But he could not simply be happy.

‘This madman could have commissioned them against me too, couldn’t he?’

* * *

That night, in a small smithy, a middle-aged man placed heated iron on an anvil and struck it with a hammer.

Clang! Da-clang!

The hammering was cheerful and powerful. The shirtless man was skinny and frail, yet it was perplexing how such powerful blows could come from that bony, emaciated body.

Then, Geum Ahhyeok entered.

He quietly waited for the hammering to end. This man forgave anything else, but he absolutely could not tolerate being disturbed while working.

Clang!

With the long, resounding hammer blow as the finale, the man raised his head and looked at Geum Ahhyeok.

“You’ve come?”

“Elder, have you been well?”

The middle-aged man’s name was Myeongsin.

This was the very man Geum Ahhyeok referred to as “that person” before his father.

Geum Ahhyeok truly respected Myeongsin.

A strong and smart person. Someone different from ignorant martial artists.

It was pleasant being with him. He learned about the martial world, and about human affairs. Especially, when they were together, he felt the relief that no one could kill him.

Blood cannot be denied; the pathological fear of martial artists that his father, Geum Cheonbang, harbored had passed down to his son, Geum Ahhyeok.

“You seem to have grown thinner. Please take care of your meals.”

Now he looked like a common laborer in the smithy, drenched in sweat, but if he donned white robes at Haksageon and held a fan, he would truly look like the smartest person in the world. And he actually was smart.

Occasionally making swords and daggers as a hobby, his skill was truly remarkable.

“The assassination failed?”

“How did you know?”

Instead of answering, Myeongsin simply nodded once with an inscrutable expression.

The reason Geum Ahhyeok had invested a great sum to commission through the registry was for Myeongsin.

“Though we failed this time, I will sell all my possessions if I must to protect you, Elder.”

His father would faint in shock if he heard these words, but they were Geum Ahhyeok’s sincere feelings. Geum Ahhyeok believed in and followed Myeongsin like someone possessed.

After declaring his resolve, Geum Ahhyeok carefully broached another subject.

“But there is one thing I find puzzling.”

“What is it?”

“I was told that one of the assassins confessed, as he died, that I had placed the commission.”

“Who said so?”

“A man named Geomyeon said so.”

Myeongsin fell silent for a moment at the name Geomyeon. This expression was the first Geum Ahhyeok had seen from him.

It was unfamiliar because he felt an inexplicable tension. At this moment, he felt toward Myeongsin the same strange emotion his father had felt toward him.

“That is a lie. An assassin who carries out an assassination does not know who the client is.”

Geum Ahhyeok made an expression as if to say, “As I thought.” He had thought the same.

“Then why would he lie to me?”

Myeongsin looked at him. It was a look that asked if he truly did not know, but Geum Ahhyeok could not know. Myeongsin knew the answer.

“I have a guest to meet today, so return for now.”

A guest at this hour? How could that be? He probably did not want to see him much after the failed commission.

Geum Ahhyeok was disappointed. He had wanted to stay together longer on a day like today.

With this uneasy heart, unable to cleanly resolve the problem, he would not sleep a wink.

“I will visit again.”

Geum Ahhyeok courteously bowed and turned around.

Then, turning back, he said.

“Please do not worry about this matter. I will handle it well.”

“That is reassuring.”

Myeongsin showed a bright smile.

As soon as Geum Ahhyeok left, someone entered.

It was Geom Mugeuk, who had tailed Geum Ahhyeok to this place.

He had taken many detours to shake any tail, but how could he evade Geom Mugeuk’s eyes?

“Please forgive me for seeking you out with such shallow tricks.”

“How can a technique that accurately reads its opponent be called a shallow trick?”

It was something that would have truly hurt Geum Ahhyeok if he were here, but Myeongsin spoke without showing much emotion.

Their gazes met.

Geom Mugeuk could tell one fact from his eyes.

“You know who I am.”

A smile formed at Myeongsin’s lips.

“Indeed. Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult, Geom Mugeuk. Welcome.”

Though he had not introduced himself, Geom Mugeuk had already guessed who his opponent was. That was partly because of this place.

Myeongsin.

The right hand of the King of Assassins.

An assassin ranked second in the martial world.

Originally, he should have contended for supremacy under heaven alongside the King of Assassins, but for some reason, he had made the decision to become a subordinate to the King of Assassins.

No one in the martial world knew what had passed between them. There were only speculations that Myeongsin might owe the King of Assassins a debt of life-saving grace.

But Geom Mugeuk knew the reason. In his life before regression, it was revealed after Myeongsin died. The one who killed Myeongsin, who had been loyal his whole life, was the King of Assassins.

“You do not ask who I am?”

“Do assassins even have names? By what number are you called?”

Despite the provocative words, Myeongsin maintained his composure.

That Geom Mugeuk had immediately realized he was an assassin caused a strange light to flash in his eyes.

Myeongsin was an assassin of a type Geom Mugeuk had never experienced. In truth, his bearing was different from ordinary assassins.

Emotion could be felt in his bearing.

Geom Mugeuk looked around at the items hanging inside the smithy.

“Did you make all of these yourself?”

“Indeed.”

“May I touch them?”

“By all means.”

Myeongsin had exceptional skill in crafting concealed weapons, and even the King of Assassins frequently favored those he made. The reason the King of Assassins liked his concealed weapons was not solely because of their lethal power. It was said that among the weapons Myeongsin made, there were many that were bizarre and extraordinary.

Unfortunately, the items hanging here were merely ordinary daggers and blades. Weapons with perfect balance, as if flaunting his skill.

“Do you sell them?”

“I do not sell them, as this is but a pastime.”

“That is a shame. I was planning to buy everything here if they were for sale.”

Myeongsin formed a strange smile.

“Just as the rumors say.”

“What rumors of me have you heard?”

Myeongsin slowly walked toward Geom Mugeuk.

The man who was the second-best at killing people in the martial world was approaching.

Geom Mugeuk tensed every nerve and maintained the calmness of his mind.

Gazing quietly at Geom Mugeuk, he spoke calmly.

“I shall have to forget all the rumors.”

It sounded like words saying he would judge for himself by seeing directly, so Geom Mugeuk spoke with a disappointed expression.

“There must have been many decent rumors. A shame.”

The distance between the two was close enough to reach with a swung sword.

“Amazing. That you show yourself like this instead of fleeing when we have come.”

“Rather, I am amazed. That they would leave you so unattended.”

It was a statement implying that he could kill the Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult, who traveled alone.

“That is because my father raises his children so harshly. He is the type to throw one off a cliff and tell them to devise a movement technique on the way down.”

Myeongsin received the joke with a laugh. He was certainly composed. The composure given by strength and confidence.

“You must have expected that I would track Geum Ahhyeok here. Why did you wait?”

Myeongsin spoke, gazing at Geom Mugeuk.

“I waited to tell you to leave.”

Precisely because it was calm, it was a threatening warning.

“If you know who I am, you must also know who came with me this time. Would you leave if he told you to? Go tell him yourself.”

With an expression that said that would be troublesome, Myeongsin made a persuasion for the sake of persuasion.

“Be persuaded. Then nothing will happen.”

Of course, there was no way it would work.

“I am still young. A life in which nothing happens is far too dull.”

With an expression that said he had expected as much, Myeongsin walked toward the opposite wall and said,

“We intend to resolve this matter with money.”

He gripped a handle protruding from the wall beside the brazier.

“Do you know what this is?”

“What is it?”

“A device that issues an order to every assassin in the murim to spread a certain piece of news. The moment I pull it, my subordinates will begin delivering the message all at once.”

“What news?”

Then came words he had not expected.

“The news that a bounty has been placed on your head.”

Geom Mugeuk’s gaze deepened.

“You would have to put up quite a sum to make them want to kill the young cult leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.”

“So we did put up quite a sum.”

An astonishing amount flowed from Myeongsin’s lips.

“Ten million nyang.”

“!”

It was a sum that even startled Geom Mugeuk.

“The highest bounty in the history of the murim. Not merely enough for the young cult leader of the Demonic Cult—enough to make them leap into hell in search of the target.”

Myeongsin said with a cold gaze,

“The moment I pull this, you will be busy starting today. Will you still not leave?”

A taut tension flowed between the two of them.

Geom Mugeuk slowly began walking toward him.

Myeongsin grew tense at Geom Mugeuk’s reaction.

“Come any closer, and I’ll pull it.”

But as if daring him to pull it if he could, Geom Mugeuk did not stop.

At last, when he reached Myeongsin’s side, Geom Mugeuk did something completely unexpected.

He also seized the handle that Myeongsin was holding. Because of that, their hands overlapped.

It was a moment in which a fierce battle could have erupted in an instant, but Myeongsin simply watched what Geom Mugeuk did.

Both their actions and reactions defied common sense, yet they were calm and tranquil.

Geom Mugeuk looked at Myeongsin, grinned, and pulled the handle with all his strength.

Clank.

Then the door of the brazier swung open. This handle had been the mechanism for opening that door.

“How did you know?”

Geom Mugeuk answered with a faint smile.

“Aren’t you people staying with the Golden Dragon Clan because of money? And yet you’d spend that much just to kill me?”

Myeongsin showed his admiration honestly.

“You’re rather clever.”

“It should have been in the rumors about me. Not just clever, but brilliant; not just brilliant, but genius.”

Myeongsin grinned.

“Genius young cult leader, then we shall meet again.”

The handle was not only a mechanism for opening the brazier. After a short delay, another device activated.

Clank.

The floor beneath Myeongsin opened, and he dropped downward. At the same time, steel needles poured down on the place where Geom Mugeuk stood.

Swish swish swish swish swish swish swish swish swish!

Clang clang clang clang clang clang clang clang clang!

By the time he drew his sword and struck down all the steel needles, Myeongsin had already escaped. For some reason, Geom Mugeuk did not pursue him down the hole.

Instead, he shouted into the hole through which Myeongsin had vanished.

“You don’t look like you have ten million nyang—let alone a thousand!”

From far within the hole, it seemed as though Myeongsin’s laughter echoed back.

Absolute Regression, Chapter 482

Chapter 482: We Are Not in Hell.
--------------------------------
Myeongsin emerged from the Iron Chamber and arrived at a market street some distance from the Geumryong Family.

The street, lined with a couple dozen buildings on either side, was the entirety of the village. It was the sort of place that served as a rest stop for travelers between villages.

Walking down the unlit street, Myeongsin arrived at a shabby tavern.

The tavern had closed for the night, and a man who appeared to be the owner was wiping down the tables with a cloth as he cleaned up.

The tavern owner was a thoroughly ordinary man. His appearance, his height, his features—all of them. He had the kind of face one might encounter at least once in a lifetime. Whether one met him as a merchant, a waiter, or even a martial artist, one would likely just think, “I suppose that’s what he looks like,” and pass him by. He had no moles, scars, or distinguishing features.

When Myeongsin entered the tavern, the owner, who had been wiping the table, said,

“Guest, we’re closed for the night.”

“I’d like to have a quick bite and a drink before I go.”

The owner looked troubled as he examined the man’s appearance. Apparently deciding that his guest was not a martial artist, he relaxed.

“Judging by the look of you, you seem to have skipped a meal. What were you doing that you couldn’t even eat until this late at night?”

“I was struggling to make a living, and it turned out that way.”

The owner set down the cloth and gestured for him to come in.

“Thank you. I’ll eat quickly and be on my way.”

When Myeongsin sat down by the window, the owner went into the kitchen and brought out some liquor and a few simple dishes.

“They’ve gone cold, but they should still be edible.”

“Thank you.”

After serving the liquor and food, the owner picked up his cloth and resumed wiping the table he had been cleaning.

Then he suddenly asked,

“How was anything awkward?”

At the unexpected question, Myeongsin answered,

“It was perfect.”

The owner put down the cloth and looked at Myeongsin.

“It’s been a while since I played the owner of a tavern. When was the last time?”

“Four years ago, when I killed the Jeongmu Samgaek.”

“Ah, right.”

Amazingly, this tavern owner was none other than the Salwang—the current head of the Myeongbu, and the man who would later become the King of Assassins.

A man who had reached the pinnacle of extraordinariness within ordinariness.

The Salwang was a man who could imitate a hundred different lives and become each life itself. Today, his life was that of a tavern owner.

The Salwang began wiping the table again, swish after swish. This way, then that way. He was astonishingly studying and practicing, trying to determine what would look natural.

“As you predicted, Geom Mugeuk came.”

“What was he like in person?”

“He was a remarkable man, just as I had heard.”

“In what way?”

Myeongsin recalled the moment Geom Mugeuk had strode over, taken his hand, and pulled on the handle. He remembered those clear, deep eyes that had looked at him then.

“Confident, cheerful, and free.”

“The Young Cult Master scored quite a few points with you.”

The Salwang looked at Myeongsin and smiled broadly.

One might wonder whether such a smile suited him, but the Salwang was a man of many expressions. His emotional displays were just as abundant. When he saw something sad, he could shed floods of tears, and he could shout and rage like a man consumed by fury.

But Myeongsin knew. Every one of those emotions had been practiced. Manufactured.

There was only one word that could express the man Myeongsin believed the Salwang to be.

Emptiness.

There was nothing inside him. No longing for slaughter, no vulgar greed.

No—perhaps it was more accurate to say that he had been born with an interior that nothing could fill.

He was like an ice cliff. Anything that entered his heart simply slid down. Down into the darkness below, where no end could be seen.

That was why Myeongsin believed that the man before him would one day become the King of Assassins.

Because no matter the situation, no matter the moment, he would never hesitate.

Myeongsin looked around the tavern. Devices for ambushes had been prepared everywhere. The wooden container holding chopsticks in the corner, the old noren hanging at the kitchen entrance, the pig figurine in front of the counter, the hole in the broken wall…

And countless other things were all devices meant to kill people. Since none of them carried the metallic killing intent characteristic of mechanisms, an ordinary person could never have noticed them. Most of them had been made by Myeongsin himself.

“Are you really going to kill the Young Cult Master?”

In truth, there was no need to ask.

This tavern was not the only place where preparations were under way.

Myeongsin’s gaze shifted toward the building across the street.

The interior was visible through its windows. Men were wiping blood from the walls.

When a cart arrived in front of the building, assassins carried corpses out and began piling them onto it.

The man standing on the roof with his arms crossed was Hyeolla, the most promising young assassin in the Myeongbu these days.

He had made no mistakes in thirty-nine assassination missions. Of the masters he had killed, most were men whose names alone were enough to make anyone recognize them.

He was Myeongsin’s direct junior, but his eyes held none of the regard one would expect from a junior looking at his senior.

Corpses were also being carried out of another building.

The masked man standing in front of it gave him a slight nod.

He was not an assassin of the Myeongbu.

Eop-hwa. A special-grade assassin of Salcheon.

For this operation, the Salwang had even borrowed the finest assassins from other organizations.

In the building beside it, two assassins were looking up at the sign of a cloth merchant’s shop and speaking to each other. They were the Heukyeong Ssangs al, the twin assassins of the Black Shadow, who were not tied to any organization yet had recently achieved the greatest fame.

The entire street had been massacred today.

Now, these finest assassins would take their place.

An assassination operation involving dozens of assassins whose names alone were enough to identify them.

Never before in the history of assassins had there been an operation on such a scale. Whatever the outcome, it would remain in the annals of assassination forever.

“If we kill the Young Cult Master, we’ll all die too.”

“Are you afraid of dying?”

Myeongsin said nothing. He simply watched the cart carrying the corpses depart.

Then the Salwang shouted,

“Wait!”

The Salwang strode out to the roadside.

He walked over to the cart piled with corpses.

With an indifferent gaze, he grabbed one of the corpses by the neck. At that instant, the corpse opened its eyes. He had not yet died. Even from the building some distance away, the Salwang had detected that faint trace of vitality.

Crack-crack-crack.

The sound of neck bones breaking echoed through the quiet night street.

The Salwang returned inside and continued what he had been saying as though nothing had happened.

“If we die, wouldn’t it be nice to see all the people we’ve killed until now? Don’t you want to see them?”

After a brief pause, Myeongsin answered,

“I see them every day.”

The Salwang’s eyes shone white. The coldness of them made the hairs on the back of Myeongsin’s neck rise.

“The decision has already left our hands. Those above have made their decision. So we’ll kill only the Young Cult Master and get out.”

Myeongsin spoke worriedly.

“What could those above possibly be thinking? The Heavenly Demon won’t simply let the loss of his son go. He’ll chase us to the ends of hell.”

“We’re not in hell. We’re here in this world. If we wish to hide, they’ll never find us.”

Yes. The Salwang could do that. He was the kind of man who could even live undetected as the very pursuer sent to hunt him.

“Why me?”

Myeongsin’s role was to bring the Young Cult Master here.

There were assassins who specialized in such tasks—those who lured the target of a contract to the place of execution. Yet the Salwang had entrusted the task to Myeongsin.

The Salwang gave an unexpected answer.

“The Young Cult Master resembles you.”

Myeongsin turned to look at him.

“I heard he imitates the righteous sects despite being a fiend?”

Myeongsin knew the words were meant as a reproach toward him, but he could offer no rebuttal.

Despite being an assassin.

He did not kill children. He did not kill those who had never learned martial arts. He did not kill those who were not evil.

Those were the conditions Myeongsin had imposed on himself throughout his assassination career.

The Salwang slowly walked over and stood beside Myeongsin.

“Sin-ah.”

“!”

It was a name he had not heard in a long time.

“Stop putting on an act of hypocrisy. If you keep assigning meaning to killing people for money, you’ll only make things harder for yourself.”

Myeongsin said nothing. No—he could not say anything.

“If we weren’t friends, you would have died long ago.”

Myeongsin was now naked and clinging to the Salwang’s smooth ice cliff. If he tried to force himself away, the skin of his entire body would be torn off.

Even so, he was the one and only person whom the Salwang had allowed onto that cliff.

“I’ll be going now. It probably won’t be easy to bring him here.”

At Myeongsin’s farewell, the Salwang once again began wiping the table with his cloth.

“Would killing him really be easy?”

Myeongsin turned and left the tavern. The gazes of the assassins standing nearby naturally focused on him.

From the building across the street, Hyeolla said to the Salwang,

“How much would the contract pay to kill an assassin who no longer likes killing people?”

He was speaking of Myeongsin. Recently, rumors had been circulating that Myeongsin was avoiding contracts. There were even rumors that he wanted to retire.

The Salwang stopped wiping and answered quietly,

“If an assassin no longer wishes to kill people, then isn’t he already a dead man?”

At the Salwang’s answer, Hyeolla burst out laughing.

It was at that moment.

Whoooosh!

With the Salwang’s outstretched hand, Hyeolla, who had been standing on the roof of the building across the street, was sucked toward him in an instant. It was a display of divine might backed by tremendous internal energy.

Even when the Salwang seized him by the neck, Hyeolla did not dare resist.

“P-Please, forgive me!”

Despite his pleas, the Salwang drew a dagger without hesitation and cleanly sliced off his tongue.

Splash!

Blood sprayed in every direction from the severed tongue.

Hyeolla did not dare scream and staunched the bleeding himself.

The assassins of the Myeongbu, as well as those from the other organizations, held their breaths.

Myeongsin and the Salwang, standing in the street, met each other’s gaze.

Myeongsin silently turned and walked away, while the Salwang looked for a rag.

“Our Young Cult Master likes drinking in shabby taverns.”

The Salwang crouched down and began wiping the blood from the floor with the rag.

“So we should send him off from a place he likes.”

* * *

When Geom Mugeuk returned to his residence, Geum Arin was waiting for him.

“Where on earth have you been wandering around this late at night?”

She had rushed over after hearing that assassins had entered Geom Mugeuk’s residence, only to find that he had gone out. She came again and again, but he was never there. In the end, they met this late at night.

“Did you get hurt anywhere fighting the assassins?”

“No. It seems they sent assassins who cost less than my own bounty.”

Judging by his unchanged jokes, he seemed to be fine.

“Whose doing was it?”

Geom Mugeuk did not answer her honestly.

“I don’t know. I have so many enemies, after all.”

Geum Arin wondered whether the one who had placed the contract might be her own family. No matter how many enemies he had, would they really have ambushed him while he was in the Geumryong Family?

“Don’t die. You have to keep the promise you made to me, remember?”

When she reminded him of his promise to make her his successor, Geom Mugeuk suddenly asked,

“Can you kill your older brothers?”

“!”

The unexpected question left Geum Arin flustered.

“Do you think your older brothers will easily give up the position of successor? If they refuse to surrender it even at the cost of their lives, I’m asking whether you can take those lives.”

It would be a lie to say she had never thought about it.

But when Geom Mugeuk actually asked her, no easy answer came. Neither “I can’t” nor “I can.”

After a long while, she said,

“I don’t know.”

With her head lowered, she failed to see Geom Mugeuk’s faint smile.

She did not know that, because she was the kind of person who answered that she did not know, Geom Mugeuk considered her the best of the three by far.

“Think about it seriously.”

It was no longer time to gaze at the stars. It was time to look at the ground beneath her feet.

Whether that ground would be solid earth, a filthy sewer, or a thousand-foot precipice depended on her choice and her fate.

Geom Mugeuk let her leave, then stood in the courtyard for a while, gazing up at the night sky.

Then he heard Hwi’s voice behind him.

“You missed a chance to leave your name in the history of the martial world.”

He was referring to the bounty story from earlier.

Geom Mugeuk laughed and answered,

“I may be the embodiment of a desire for fame as much as I am of a desire for wealth, but I’ll pass on having my name appear in the history of assassins.”

Hwi smiled and walked over to stand beside Geom Mugeuk.

“The assassins are behind this incident.”

Geom Mugeuk told Hwi that his suspicions had become certain.

It was something Hwi needed to know before his father, who was sleeping at the moment.

Once the enemy was confirmed to be assassins, a fierce light flashed in Hwi’s eyes before disappearing.

“The threat that they would place a bounty on you didn’t sound like a mere joke. If they choose a target, they’ll probably choose me.”

They would not dare consider attempting to assassinate his father.

“You asked me to tell you about assassins, didn’t you?”

There was something he absolutely had to tell Geom Mugeuk.

“When you think of assassins, what comes to mind?”

Geom Mugeuk answered without hesitation, listing whatever came to him.

“Ambushes, sneak attacks, cowardice, suicide, poison, viciousness, despicability, the greatest bounty in history, and what a shame it is that I didn’t receive it. Things like that.”

Hwi smiled and answered the same question.

“When I think of assassins, these are the things that come to mind.”

And not one of them was among the things Geom Mugeuk had mentioned.

“Patience, planning, investigation, preparation, and study.”

Until now, Geom Mugeuk had never thought of these things first when he thought of assassins.

“It’s easy to think that assassins are impulsive and emotional, but that’s not true at all. There are no people more rational and methodical than assassins. They are people who endure, study, and prepare. That is how they overcome differences in ability.”

This was the crux of what Hwi wanted to say.

“They will have finished investigating everything about you, Young Cult Master. What kind of personality you have, what you like, how you react in certain situations, and what you say in others. What food you prefer, and whom you care about. They may know you better than you know yourself.”

As Geom Mugeuk listened, a thought suddenly occurred to him. Perhaps those who did not know themselves well were the ones most likely to be killed by assassins.

“All of an assassin’s investigation and preparation exists solely for this one moment.”

“What moment is that?”

The moment everyone knew existed, yet forgot for a single instant.

“The moment you let your guard down.”

Hwi’s eyes gleamed as he asked calmly,

“When is the moment you are most off guard, Young Cult Master?”

The Absolute Regressor

Chapter 483

Chapter 483: What Comes When You Let Your Guard Down.
-----------------------------------------------------
It was a lesson passed down by Hwi.

‘The moment you let your guard down, you die!’

A simple truth passed down by the greatest bodyguard of the era.

Geom Mugik knew. He knew how difficult it was to uphold that single line. He knew that truths which are always difficult to keep are simple.

“Ah! This is a disaster.”

Geom Mugik made a half-joking fuss.

“There are far too many moments in my life when I let my guard down. Playing around and drinking! My life is one of even dancing!”

He spoke as if that were the case, but there were only exactly two instances when he let his guard down.

“When I’m with my father, I’m the very definition of letting my guard down. Whether assassins come or not, Father will block them for me!”

The truth was, even when he was with his father, he was tense. Not tense about someone’s ambush, but tense because of his father.

The better their relationship became, the tenser he was. Because it meant he was due for a mistake.

The only moment he completely relieved all tension was when he was inside the Spacetime Migration Technique.

Ah, that place was wonderful just to think about.

The comfort of lying on that hot sandy beach. The cries of seagulls and the sound of waves, a single crab crawling across the sand.

Of course, now there was one more place. Soaking in a hot spring surrounded by snow on all sides, he could forget every worry in the world.

With assassins on his mind, thinking of that place made him feel anew how precious it was.

“Ah, even now, listening to you, I’m letting my guard down.”

Hwi laughed. He had smiled faintly many times before, but this was the first time he had smiled so cheerfully.

“You’re letting your guard down right now too, aren’t you?”

At that, Hwi nodded readily.

“That’s right. If I were ambushed at this moment, I would die.”

“We would have died together, then.”

At that, Hwi spoke carefully.

“I would have died first.”

There was one thing a true martial artist bodyguard never wished to see.

Even after witnessing all the horrific and fearsome things in the world, even if he were to see a sword piercing his own heart, there was one thing he absolutely never wished to see.

Namely, the sight of the person he was meant to protect dying.

Just as I-an had thrown himself for him, Hwi was also a man prepared to die first.

“Perhaps that is the only blessing given to a bodyguard.”

What more words were needed? He had already proven himself through death.

He liked this man who had protected his father all his life and was with him until death.

That is why he could promise.

In this life, such a thing would never happen.

Because the reason he had regressed through all that hardship was for this.

That is why he would never let his guard down.

“Sir.”

“Yes, Young Leader.”

“I will definitely keep today’s words in mind and deliver this message for certain. I survived thanks to what you told me, Hwi!”

After killing the Murder King and returning, he would surely pass on these words.

Whether his sincerity was conveyed, Hwi smiled.

“You must be tired. Please go in and rest.”

“Yes, you rest too.”

As he entered the building and walked down the corridor, he saw Hwi still standing in the courtyard through the window.

With a face lost in thought, Hwi looked up at the night sky.

Come to think of it, this was the first time he had seen Hwi standing in the moonlight.

* * *

Geum Cheonbang arrived at Geom Mugik’s residence.

Recently, he had been unable to sleep properly due to worries about his son. The fact that his son had commissioned assassins was a great shock to him.

He could never forgive whatever had influenced his son to commission assassins.

Of course, he did not know.

That the outcome had already been decided regardless of his son. That from the very beginning, they were nothing more than grass-eating beings caught in a fight between fierce beasts.

He had even thought of going to confront him.

But he had left matters with him to his son until now, and meeting him at this point felt burdensome.

So he decided that even if he were to meet him, he would meet Geom Mugik first and then decide.

‘I have to settle this somehow.’

Even if his son had commissioned them because of that man’s influence, his son could not avoid responsibility for this commission. This was the core of today’s visit.

‘Does he really know who commissioned them?’

When Geom Mugik had come to him, he had seemed to know something.

He had come to confirm that for certain. If he did know? The reason he had come alone like this, without even bringing subordinates, was because he had come prepared for that very possibility.

‘If it doesn’t work, I’ll have to settle it with money.’

He was in a situation where he had not even exchanged greetings with the father and uncle of the one called Geom Yeon. He was curious what kind of people they were.

When he knocked on the door, Hwi welcomed him.

“How did you come to be here?”

“Are you by chance Geom Yeon’s father?”

“No. I am Yeon’s uncle.”

“Ah, I see. It is an honor to meet you. I am the head of the Geumryong Merchant Guild.”

They were subordinates his daughter had brought. Strictly speaking, there was no need to be this polite, but...

The moment he saw Hwi, he involuntarily felt intimidated.

“I wish to see Geom Yeon’s father.”

“Please wait a moment. I will go ask inside.”

“Do so.”

If the head of a merchant guild had come, shouldn’t they be brought inside unconditionally? Did they mean to turn him away if he was sleeping?

As he was thinking this and that, Hwi came back out.

“Please come in.”

Hwi guided him to Geom Woojin.

“The head of the Geumryong Merchant Guild has arrived.”

“Show him in.”

Geum Cheonbang entered the room. Hwi followed him in as well. Right now, he was not here as a bodyguard but as an uncle.

Geom Woojin, who had been standing by the window looking outside, turned around and looked at Geum Cheonbang.

The moment their eyes met, all the hair on Geum Cheonbang’s body stood on end. He felt a presence incomparable to the oppressive aura he had felt when meeting Hwi earlier.

“I am called Geum Cheonbang, who leads the merchant guild.”

Geum Cheonbang greeted him with a cupped-fist salute, more politely than his usual greetings.

Though he had a false identity as the leader of the Seodoh Sect, Geom Woojin introduced himself like this:

“I am Yeon’s father.”

Geum Cheonbang had met countless people while leading the merchant company, but he had never met someone who gave off such an intense feeling.

“Unfortunately, something that should not have happened occurred at the main house recently. I apologize for that.”

After apologizing first, he asked after his well-being.

“Are you well?”

“I am well.”

“Is there anyone you suspect might be behind this?”

“There is not.”

He could learn nothing from the curt answers, so he tried to discern the truth from the other’s expression.

But far from discerning the truth, the moment their eyes met, he involuntarily lowered his head and averted his gaze.

‘He is no easy opponent.’

His son Geom Yeon was the same. They were no ordinary father and son.

Could these people really have come as his daughter’s subordinates?

An awkward silence flowed. He was adept at leading conversations, having met many people, but strangely, he could not get his mouth to open.

He had not uncovered anything. At this rate, he would be shown the door at any moment.

Then, Geom Mugik’s words to him suddenly came to mind.

“Your son recommended that I visit my father and seek advice.”

“Me?”

He seemed to be hearing this for the first time.

“He told me to learn from you how to become a parent who overcomes their child. He said you are the best in that field.”

Only then did a strange smile form on Geom Woojin’s face.

“How can one overcome their child?”

After a brief pause, Geom Woojin answered.

“I am losing too.”

Hwi slightly lowered his head. He could not dare show a laughing expression at the Cult Leader’s words. A declaration of defeat from the Cult Leader?

It was an answer mixed with jest, but Hwi knew it was not entirely a joke. Lately, the Cult Leader had been losing often, and he had even lost at fishing.

Seeing Hwi smiling with his head lowered, Geom Woojin asked him.

“What do you think?”

“How would I know, having no children?”

“That is why I ask.”

It sounded like having children would rather make one unable to find the answer.

Though flustered by the unexpected question, how could he be negligent in answering when the Cult Leader had asked?

“I do not know about the matter of winning or losing against one’s child. However, I have heard this saying before.”

The Cult Leader had asked the question, but the answer was directed at Geum Cheonbang. Because it was what he needed to hear.

“Children grow by watching not words, but actions.”

Geum Cheonbang’s heart grew heavy. How could he not know the meaning contained in those words?

If you cannot overcome your child, that means you cannot overcome yourself.

In that regard, Geum Cheonbang was deeply ashamed.

Because his son was answering the question of what he had shown him with the conviction that there was nothing money couldn’t solve.

Geom Woojin spoke to Geum Cheonbang.

“So it is.”

“I have gratefully received your good words.”

He had not been able to confirm whether they knew about his son’s commission.

But that did not mean today’s visit had been solely filled with regret over raising his child poorly.

As he was about to bid farewell and turn around, Geom Woojin said something unexpected.

“It seems my son has seen you all favorably.”

“!”

To Geum Cheonbang, those words sounded like this:

His son was being given a chance to be forgiven.

The moment he heard those words, he felt as if a small breathing hole had opened in his suffocated, stuffy heart.

If only that were the meaning.

Geum Cheonbang slightly lowered his head, greeted him once more, and left.

To Hwi, who had seen him off and returned, Geom Woojin said,

“Do you have some hidden child somewhere?”

It was praise for having answered the earlier question well.

Hwi smiled without a word.

After looking out the window with Geom Woojin for a moment, Hwi spoke carefully.

“The assassin behind this does not seem to be an ordinary person.”

Geom Woojin silently nodded.

Hwi knew. Though the Cult Leader seemed to be perfectly at ease, he knew many things. Because he received reports from the Chief Strategist every time.

He was fully aware of which assassins were moving and how. Even that the best of the best were moving.

But despite knowing that, the Cult Leader was merely watching.

What did the Cult Leader see when he looked at the Young Leader?

Perhaps he saw even deeper things that Hwi himself could not see?

Because Hwi could not see that far, and because of that...

“I have a request, Cult Leader.”

Geom Woojin looked at Hwi with an expression of surprise for a moment.

“Have you ever asked anything of me?”

To Geom Woojin’s question, Hwi answered respectfully.

“It is the first time.”

“What is the request?”

“It is something I should not dare to say. Please forgive my rudeness.”

Geom Woojin quietly waited for Hwi’s words.

“For the time being, I wish to take charge of the Young Leader’s protection.”

He was none other than the Heavenly Demon’s guard. A person who must never avert his eyes from the Heavenly Demon for a single moment. Even if the Heavenly Demon was eating rice here, and the Young Leader met death over there, he was someone who should not go there.

He had truly lived with that mindset, yet now he was making such a request.

Geom Woojin said firmly to Hwi.

“I shall refuse.”

Hwi politely lowered his head.

“Yes, Cult Leader. I am sorry for speaking out of turn. I will gladly accept punishment for this great rudeness.”

Then, unexpected words came out.

“Hwi, I command you.”

“Yes, Cult Leader.”

Hwi cupped his fists with a crisp sound and tensed. It had truly been a long time since he received a formal command.

“Until this matter is concluded, protect the Young Leader.”

The surprised Hwi looked at Geom Woojin.

Geom Woojin looked into his eyes and said,

“I cannot let you waste your first-ever request on something as trivial as protecting my son.”

“!”

“Save it and make a request for yourself instead.”

Hwi looked at Geom Woojin with heated eyes and answered more powerfully than ever.

“I accept the Cult Leader’s command!”

* * *

“From now on, you’re going to be my bodyguard?”

I was startled by Hwi’s words.

“It is the Cult Leader’s command.”

“Father suddenly gave such an order? That can’t be. You know it too, don’t you? He is not the type to cast his son off a cliff while laying a net below.”

I stared quietly at Hwi.

“You asked to protect me, didn’t you?”

“No. It was the Cult Leader’s command.”

Since he was not someone skilled in lying, I could tell from his voice and eyes. That this was something Hwi had volunteered for.

So that’s what he was worrying about under the moonlight that day—he was worried about me.

“If it were not a command, I would never have left the Cult Leader’s side.”

Hwi denied it to the end. He was trying to make it seem like Father had done this for me out of consideration.

“You need not be conscious of me. From now on, I am someone who is with the Young Leader yet not with him.”

Of course, that would be the case. The number of people who could find Hwi when he concealed himself could be counted on one hand.

Would the Murder King even notice?

Then, Father’s words suddenly came to mind.

—If you’re going to put Hwi forward, you take responsibility and protect him.

Father sent Hwi to me not only to protect me, but because he believed I would protect Hwi.

Hwi, you’re probably only thinking of protecting me, but we have to protect each other.

“Sir, I look forward to working with you.”

“I as well.”

Of course, a joke couldn’t be missing at a time like this.

I raised both hands and rejoiced.

“Then from now on, I’ll let my guard down with peace of mind!”

It was the very moment Hwi smiled.

Our gazes turned toward the door simultaneously.

Hwi silently disappeared, showing what the greatest concealment art of the era was.

Soon after, a knock on the main gate was heard.

When I opened the door, surprisingly, Myeongsin was standing there.

With eyes transparent yet betraying nothing of his inner thoughts, he said,

“I don’t have ten million nyang, but I have enough money to buy a drink. How about it? Will you have a drink with me?”

You were right, sir. The moment you let your guard down, an assassin comes.

Absolute Regression, Chapter 484

Chapter 484: Because I Don't Have a Single Assassin Friend.
-----------------------------------------------------------
Geom Mugeuk stared at Myeongsin in silence.

Myeongsin likewise met that gaze calmly.

They felt it simultaneously. That beginning with this meeting, the fates of the two of them—and indeed, the fates of many others—would be decided.

“Surprised?”

To Myeongsin’s question, Geom Mugeuk replied with an expression that said it was obvious.

“Have you forgotten already? The last image I have of you is your back as you fled after dousing me in strong liquor.”

At that, Myeongsin spoke with a smile.

“And have you forgotten? You were the one who grabbed that handle.”

This time, Geom Mugeuk smiled.

“How about it? Shall we have a drink?”

He had asked without missing the pleasant atmosphere, but Geom Mugeuk did not immediately accept.

“What does your organization’s report say about a man like me? Does it say I’m the sort of fool who would follow a terrifying assassin who tried to kill him just because he asked me out for a drink?”

“That’s exactly what it says. That’s why I came.”

Geom Mugeuk, who had been blinking his eyes, let out a deliberately long sigh.

“It’s vexing, but they’ve investigated me properly.”

Had he ever seen someone give such an answer at a moment like this? Would he ever see one again?

Myeongsin was certain. It would be the first and last time. The only person who would entertain this absurd meeting was the young cult leader.

This time, Geom Mugeuk calmed his eyes and spoke softly.

“But what if I kill you here and create an unexpected variable?”

Myeongsin reacted without the slightest hint of panic.

“My report on you included that you might make such a threat.”

It was a jest. Surely it couldn’t have contained such detail.

Of course, Myeongbu had investigated Geom Mugeuk thoroughly, and they knew much about the young cult leader of the Demonic Cult. Myeongsin had fully memorized those contents, but much of it was still hard to believe. There had never been such a demonic young cult leader in the world.

“Very well, let’s go.”

Geom Mugeuk readily accepted the proposal and stepped out past the main gate.

Rather, it was Myeongsin who asked in surprise.

“You’re really going? It could be a trap.”

“Of course it could.”

“Then why accept?”

“Surely I cannot spend my whole life cooped up inside? And you cannot wait forever for me to come out, either. Especially for your lot, time is money, is it not?”

Geom Mugeuk took the first step, and Myeongsin walked alongside him.

Hwi, who was concealed, would be following secretly. As he had said, from now on Hwi was a person who both existed and did not exist. The moment he revealed himself would be the moment when everyone’s life and death was decided.

The two walked toward the tavern district a few paces apart. Though they were separated, it was a distance from which either could launch a fatal surprise attack upon the other if they so wished.

Myeongsin imagined ambushing Geom Mugeuk.

Being an assassin, it was natural to have such thoughts when meeting a master. Could that man block my ambush? If I struck like this, he would block like that, then I would follow up with another attack. He could roughly picture how it would unfold.

But for some reason, no matter how much information he had read, he could not picture such a scene with Geom Mugeuk.

Then, Geom Mugeuk abruptly asked.

“Why you?”

It was a sudden question cutting away all context, yet Myeongsin knew what he meant.

Myeongsin recalled a conversation with the King of Assassins.

—Why me?

—The young cult leader resembles you.

Myeongsin did not relay those words.

“I suppose I’m a thorn in the organization’s eye.”

Geom Mugeuk immediately seized upon his self-mocking jest.

“Or perhaps because you’re the strongest.”

Myeongsin did not deny it. After the King of Assassins, the strongest assassin in Myeongbu was indisputably himself.

“I thought the Upper Realm would be involved in this matter. I didn’t know you were the ones behind it.”

Myeongsin knew the reason. The organization had entrusted Myeongbu with the money management because they knew the King of Assassins had no greed for wealth.

What had been promised to him?

To Myeongsin, moving the King of Assassins’s hollow heart seemed like the most difficult thing in the world.

“By the way, I saw the other day that your skill at crafting weapons is that of a master artisan?”

“Just a pastime.”

“It’s a shame to waste such dexterity on killing people.”

Had it been a thoughtlessly blurted remark, he would have been angered, but the young cult leader sincerely held his craft in high esteem.

“If Master Kwak of our cult’s Smithy had seen it, he would have begged to kidnap you and bring you back.”

“He might have asked to kill me out of jealousy instead.”

At the pride in Myeongsin’s voice, Geom Mugeuk said with a laugh.

“Not to that extent.”

Rather, because Geom Mugeuk spoke that way, Myeongsin could tell his praise was not merely empty words meant to flatter him.

“Sell me a few of those throwing daggers you’ve made next time. I happen to have run out of them.”

“Are you going to kill me with them?”

“I don’t appear weak enough to be killed by daggers I’m unaccustomed to, do I?”

Myeongsin felt not the slightest discomfort conversing with the young cult leader like this. The young cult leader certainly had a gift for guiding conversations comfortably. Even unpleasant topics did not feel so unpleasant when he brought them up.

Thus, the two exchanged all manner of disjointed talk as they arrived at the tavern district.

The place prepared as a trap was not among the taverns there. It was a rest stop situated between this village and another.

“Where shall we drink?”

Myeongsin gave Geom Mugeuk the choice of tavern.

Geom Mugeuk surveyed the several taverns there and chose one.

“Let’s go there.”

The place Geom Mugeuk had chosen was the shabbiest tavern among them. Myeongsin meekly followed his lead.

The interior of the tavern had a similar atmosphere to the place the King of Assassins was preparing.

‘So this young cult leader truly enjoys this kind of atmosphere.’

Myeongsin asked Geom Mugeuk.

“I heard the trilateral talks were also held in a tavern like this.”

“Is it not tasteful and pleasant?”

Geom Mugeuk looked at the innkeeper.

“The food and drink here should be good as well.”

“How can you be sure?”

“I can tell the taste just by looking at the innkeeper’s face. It is an innate ability.”

But shortly after, Geom Mugeuk set down his chopsticks with a crestfallen face.

The food served as a side dish was far too bad. Moreover, the liquor was mediocre as well.

With a dejected face, Geom Mugeuk said.

“It seems the innkeeper has changed his face. This might be a place where a former assassin washed his hands and opened shop.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s jest, Myeongsin smiled faintly.

Bitterness tinged that smile as he accepted the jest. The words “former assassin” weighed heavily upon him.

Until now, he had carried out his assassinations according to his principles.

He did not kill children. He did not kill those who had not learned martial arts. He did not kill those who were not evil.

But at some point, the King of Assassins had begun to touch upon these principles.

Especially the last one.

—Who can determine good and evil? If you conduct yourself as an assassin with such principles, are you a good person?

Myeongsin could not answer yes to that question.

—I acknowledge not killing children. I acknowledge not killing those who are not martial artists. But I cannot acknowledge that you will not kill those who are not evil.

When an order came down from the organization, it meant kill without question.

—This is for your sake as well. If you establish such useless principles and make a mistake later? Will you wash your hands out of guilt? Or will you take your own life? Why make your already difficult life harder on yourself?

The King of Assassins’s persuasion pressed in ever stronger.

Myeongsin could feel it. The moment when he would have to speak to the King of Assassins was approaching soon.

That he would break his principles.

No, that he would not break them.

At the end of that worry, a single doubt always waited for him.

‘What if I were not the second strongest in Myeongbu?’

Would he have survived until now? Could he have clung to that icy cliff within the King of Assassins’s heart? Did he survive because he was a friend? Or did he survive because he was too useful to discard?

These days, as the moment to decide regarding his principles drew near, his heart was complicated in many ways.

What he had said to Geom Mugeuk earlier was not an empty remark. The words about being a thorn in the eye. Perhaps they were using the hand of a powerful enemy like Geom Mugeuk to remove that thorn.

Geom Mugeuk silently watched Myeongsin looking down at his cup, then looked out the window.

For a moment, the two sank into their own thoughts.

It was Geom Mugeuk who broke the long silence.

“There is something I’m curious about. May I ask?”

“Ask all you like.”

“Why did you not flee? You know full well that many of your comrades have died by my hand until now, do you not?”

Those comrades—were they ordinary experts? They were absolute experts who would later become the Twelve Zodiac Kings.

As the right hand of the King of Assassins, he would know roughly as much.

Indeed, judging by his expression, he knew. The fact that they belonged to something.

Myeongsin answered calmly.

“We are people needed for times like this. Killing people is our specialty.”

Watching Geom Mugeuk nod silently, Myeongsin added slyly.

“Killing people—you people are specialists no less than we are, are you not? No, you are worse than us. We never move unless we are paid, but you people are those who kill indiscriminately regardless of money, are you not?”

This was how the martial world had viewed the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult until now.

“Indiscriminately? That is all old news. Our cult has changed greatly these days as well. It must have been detailed in that report of yours.”

Of course, Myeongsin knew. He also knew that the one leading that change was this young cult leader before him.

“Fine, since the topic has come up, let me ask one thing.”

Geom Mugeuk threw an unexpected question.

“Is the assassin’s life enjoyable?”

It looked like a casually thrown remark, but it was a question with intent.

“Who does their work for enjoyment? It is simply work, so I do it. But why do you ask?”

Myeongsin had thought he had investigated everything about Geom Mugeuk, but in fact, Geom Mugeuk also knew about Myeongsin. Though Myeongsin had more detailed information, Geom Mugeuk knew the decisive facts.

His final moments. Why he had been loyal to the King of Assassins, and why he had eventually met his death at the King of Assassins’s hands.

“You somehow do not seem suited to being an assassin.”

“I cannot trust that feeling of yours.”

Myeongsin’s gaze turned to the innkeeper sitting at the counter as he added.

“Have you not already proven how poor your eye for people is?”

Geom Mugeuk laughed brightly. At that unfeigned laughter, Myeongsin laughed as well. At this moment, he thought, At this moment, who is more off their guard?

Geom Mugeuk asked Myeongsin.

“The drink is mediocre as well; shall we move elsewhere? Do you know a tavern?”

Myeongsin slowly shook his head.

“I do not.”

In his heart, he wanted to say that there was a regular spot a little ways from here.

But he did not. He judged that Geom Mugeuk was testing him.

That place had to be reached naturally. At the moment he let his guard down. The sort of moment when the words ‘Surely not this person?’ would slip out on their own.

Instead, Myeongsin was the first to rise from his seat.

“Let us end it here for today. Let us drink again next time.”

“Already? Let us drink more!”

“I am busy. There are people lined up waiting to be killed.”

Before leaving, Myeongsin asked.

“Why did you brave the danger and come out to drink with me?”

An unexpected answer flowed from Geom Mugeuk.

“As your report would have stated, I enjoy making friends. Yet I do not have a single assassin friend.”

Myeongsin’s face showed surprise and bewilderment.

Geom Mugeuk said to him.

“Everyone begins with that expression.”

* * *

When Myeongsin returned to the King of Assassins’s tavern, the street had already been completely arranged.

The assassins had become the owners and staff of each building. Those supreme assassins had become cloth shop owners, innkeepers, and customers. Even another assassin looking on would feel no sense of disharmony.

Moreover, ordinary travelers who were not assassins were mixed in as well. Their presence was actually the most important. They would serve as the finest shield to hide the assassins.

‘If he comes here, the young cult leader will surely die.’

Myeongsin arrived at the King of Assassins’s tavern. There too, travelers passing through were eating and drinking.

The King of Assassins, who had been conversing with them and even sharing a table, welcomed Myeongsin.

“Welcome!”

Myeongsin sat in a corner seat and ordered a simple snack and some liquor.

After bringing the order, the King of Assassins chattered with the guests again. He looked like a talkative innkeeper one might meet while traveling through the Central Plains. Who would think that man was the master of Myeongbu?

Once the travelers left, the King of Assassins finally came to Myeongsin.

“Did you meet the young cult leader?”

“Yes, I met him.”

“How was he?”

“You said he resembled me?”

Of course, it had not been meant as a compliment. A demonic cultist, and an assassin at that—trying to walk a different path. That was what he had meant by saying they resembled each other.

“Then you overestimated me greatly.”

He meant by this that Geom Mugeuk was no ordinary person.

“In what way?”

“It is strange. It is difficult to put into words.”

The King of Assassins poured himself a drink from the liquor at Myeongsin’s table.

It was as expected. He already knew that Geom Mugeuk was no ordinary man.

“You said you would not kill unless they were evil, did you not? Is he evil? Is he good?”

Myeongsin could not answer. Generally speaking, as the young cult leader of the Demonic Cult, he would certainly belong to the evil. But was the man he had spoken with today evil?

The King of Assassins had tried to persuade Myeongsin through Geom Mugeuk.

“That is why. Because it is something you cannot answer. Let your principles flow away as the arrogance of youth.”

Myeongsin gave no answer. Seeing that those words were uncomfortable for him, it seemed he still could not accept them.

“Can you pull this off?”

“I will pull it off.”

It was a situation where he had even been told that were they not friends, he would have died many times over. The assassination had already begun; it was something that could no longer be stopped.

“We will go down in the history of the Murim with this assassination.”

At the King of Assassins’s words, Myeongsin gave no answer.

“If you cannot bring him, use the Geumnyong Clan. In any case, once this matter is over, they too must be cleaned up before we leave. Even if we do not kill them, they face annihilation. The moment the young cult leader dies, the Demonic Cult will sweep away every blade of grass related to them without leaving one behind.”

Myeongsin rose from his seat and bowed politely.

“I shall take my leave.”

As he left, the King of Assassins spoke. His words were the patience of an assassin that Hwi had spoken of, and the resolve of a man standing at the pinnacle of that patience.

“If you fail to bring the young cult leader, I will live out my days as an innkeeper here.”

Absolute Regression, Chapter 485

Chapter 485: We Are What Others See
-----------------------------------
Only after I returned to my quarters did Hwi reveal himself.

He appeared mysteriously, as if he had been standing there from the start.

Geom Mugeuk cupped his fist with solemn courtesy.

“You’ve worked hard protecting me.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s greeting, Hwi answered with a smile.

“It was nothing. If anything, I’m more comfortable when I’m in hiding.”

As if that could be true. It would be physically taxing, and since he had to keep watch on the opponent and our surroundings, mentally exhausting as well. He was only saying that so I wouldn’t feel uncomfortable.

“How did he seem to you?”

Geom Mugeuk asked Hwi about Myeongsin.

“He was an assassin of the highest caliber.”

As expected, Hwi had grasped his level without even crossing hands with him. From nothing more than his gait and the movements of his hands, he had assessed the man’s skill precisely.

Hwi asked cautiously.

“What do you intend to do now?”

Normally, he would not have asked. But he had seen me part from Myeongsin after maintaining such an amicable relationship with him, and so he asked. Geom Mugeuk then revealed his plan.

“I’m thinking of trying to make him one of mine.”

Hwi knew Geom Mugeuk’s abilities better than anyone, but the problem was that the other party was an assassin.

“Assassins are not people to be trusted.”

It was the first time Hwi had ever been so firm.

Geom Mugeuk understood Hwi.

Hwi and Myeongsin were natural opposites, standing at opposing extremes.

Hwi would not trust him. Rather, he would hate and despise him.

He simply did not show it in front of me.

Even knowing that, I was attempting to bring Myeongsin to my side because I knew about his life. But I could not say such a thing.

“Yes, I won’t trust him. But this effort will be meaningful. Even if he doesn’t become my ally, a single moment of hesitation from him at a decisive instant might save our lives.”

Hwi felt it. As he always had, the Young Cult Leader was seeing something he himself could not.

But when it came to the matter of assassins, he had to say it clearly.

“Assassins are not people to be trusted. Please, do not forget that one point.”

It was the first time in his life that Hwi had repeated the same words twice.

“Yes. I will keep it in mind.”

Only after receiving that confirmation did Hwi’s expression ease.

“I have a request for you as well, Uncle.”

“Please speak.”

Geom Mugeuk unwound the supreme-grade Heavenly Silkworm Silk wrapped around the hilt of the Black Demon Sword and handed it to him.

“Wrap it around your heart and dantian.”

“I am fine. You should wear it, Young Cult Leader.”

At that, Geom Mugeuk loosened his clothes and showed him the supreme-grade Heavenly Silkworm Silk wrapped around his heart and abdomen.

“I already am.”

“Wear two layers.”

“I’m wearing three layers. So you should wear one, Uncle.”

Hwi looked at Geom Mugeuk.

“It may not look like much, but this is supreme-grade Heavenly Silkworm Silk with a long and venerable history. The Demon Venerables are already wearing it too. Ah, of course, I washed it thoroughly. And I’m not giving it to you outright. You’ll have to return it later.”

Because in the final battle, I would have to wrap it around my whole body.

And I would stand at the forefront of that battle.

Hwi could tell. No matter how much he refused, Geom Mugeuk would not back down.

“Understood. Thank you, Young Cult Leader.”

Hwi wrapped the supreme-grade Heavenly Silkworm Silk around himself in front of Geom Mugeuk.

Only then did Geom Mugeuk smile in satisfaction.

“It suits you well. Here, look.”

Geom Mugeuk drew his sword and showed Hwi his reflection in the blade.

The sight of himself reflected in the sword felt unfamiliar.

He had spent his entire life protecting others, and this was the first time he had ever felt protected.

“Thank you, Young Cult Leader.”

Geom Mugeuk asked him casually.

“You want me to go see Father quickly now, don’t you?”

He had gone out with me instead of guarding Father, so he must have been curious whether Father was safe.

“No.”

“Then may I go out right away to take care of some other business?”

When I acted as though I might leave again at any moment, Hwi spoke with an expression that said, I lose.

“Still, since you have returned, should you not at least greet him before leaving?”

Geom Mugeuk laughed and turned around.

“Where would I be going?”

As Geom Mugeuk entered the building, he called out.

“Your son is back, Father.”

With a pleased expression, Hwi followed me inside.

* * *

Father was in his room.

He was reading a dispatch sent from Tongcheongak.

While keeping a firm grasp on the situation, he was entrusting the matter entirely to me.

Yes. Only when one has the patience to wait for a child who must crawl up a cliff alone does that cliff have any meaning.

“I have returned, Father.”

Father lifted his head and looked this way.

The moment our eyes met, I felt it.

He had grown stronger!

It seemed I was not the only one training.

In the past, I had only vaguely sensed that Father had grown stronger, but now I could clearly feel that he had.

Perhaps it was because I had grown stronger myself that I could feel it more distinctly. Either way, it was a good result.

Father, grow stronger still. Become so strong that Hwa Mugi, the unification of the martial world, all of it becomes laughable. I will fill that emptiness for you.

Father’s gaze turned to Hwi.

Hwi bowed his head respectfully and paid his respects to Father. Relief and gratitude bloomed across his face.

“It’s the first time, isn’t it? That you’ve left Father’s side like this.”

Hwi was human too, so how could he have remained by his side without resting even for a moment?

He had to eat, go to the latrine, wash, and change clothes.

But I could imagine it. How rushed those moments must have been. How hurried Hwi must have been in that brief span of time.

Please, let nothing happen while I am gone!

Hwi had said that dying before the person one had to protect was the only blessing bestowed upon a guard.

But there must also be a curse. To never leave one’s post, only for the person one must protect to die during the single moment one is away.

And more than anyone, Father understood Hwi’s heart.

“I will stay at the safe house for a while.”

It was Father’s consideration, telling him not to worry even a little.

Father had even already packed his belongings.

When Father shouldered a large leather pack, Hwi cried out in surprise.

“Please give that to me. I will carry it.”

Hwi was flustered. He had likely never once allowed Father to carry his own luggage.

Then Father said to Hwi,

“Are you not carrying out my command?”

“Cult Leader!”

“I am fine.”

Hwi looked at me. His eyes were telling me to hurry up, take the luggage, and lead the way.

“Father, I will carry it.”

“Forget it. Just don’t die.”

Father shouldered the leather pack and left the room.

“You look impressive carrying that leather pack, Father.”

I meant it sincerely. Above all, it was my first time seeing him like that, so it felt refreshing.

Watching Hwi fidget as he looked on, I spoke loudly enough for Father to hear.

“Stop staring already! Those shoulders could carry Mount Tai without budging, but your gaze is going to wear them down!”

* * *

Myeongsin returned three days later. Though he had wanted to come running immediately, he deliberately dragged out the time. He could not show that he was impatient.

“Shall we have a drink?”

“That sounds good.”

Before they set out, Myeongsin handed over the wooden case he had brought.

Inside were throwing daggers.

“To be honest, I’ll acknowledge swords and other weapons, but I cannot accept it when it comes to throwing daggers. Even after seeing these, will the Demonic Cult’s iron workshop still not try to kill me?”

It seemed his pride had been wounded when, the last time they met, he had been told that he was not yet at the level where the artisans of the main cult’s iron workshop would try to kill him out of jealousy.

Geom Mugeuk examined the throwing daggers.

“Ah, I mustn’t show these to our Artisan Gwak, if nothing else. It would be troublesome if he asked me to kill you.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s words, Myeongsin’s expression brightened.

“Then, since I’ve received throwing daggers as a gift too, I should buy the drinks today. Last time, I chose the wrong tavern, so you pick today.”

Myeongsin nodded as if it were nothing, but his heart began to pound.

‘Is it today?’

But if they had to leave the marketplace, that might arouse suspicion. He had to decide carefully.

And so the two of them once again headed toward the marketplace.

“May I ask you something?”

“Ask.”

“These people of the Golden Dragon Clan—aren’t they far too beneath your level?”

Myeongsin answered casually.

“You’ve lived a life of only giving orders, so you may not know, but we live a life where, if we’re ordered to do something, we must do it. What power do we have otherwise?”

“I don’t know about others, but hearing that from you makes it sound like an excuse.”

Myeongsin sensed that there was weight behind Geom Mugeuk’s words.

“You investigated me too, didn’t you?”

The intelligence network of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult was known to be the best in the martial world. Since they had become entangled like this, there was no way he would not know about him.

“I did.”

Geom Mugeuk answered honestly.

“What kind of person am I in the report you investigated?”

The answer came immediately.

“Myeongsin, second-in-command of Myeongbu. His assassinations follow three principles: he does not kill children, he does not kill those who do not know martial arts, and he does not kill those who are not evil.”

He had thought they might know that much. The other party was the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult, after all.

But the next piece of information startled Myeongsin.

“And because of that, you have recently been in conflict with the leader of Myeongbu.”

For an instant, Myeongsin flinched.

‘That matter is known only to the two of us. How did he find out?’

Pretending not to know, Myeongsin asked Geom Mugeuk,

“Conflict? Why do you think that?”

Geom Mugeuk, who had been walking until then, stopped. Myeongsin stopped with him and waited for his answer.

Geom Mugeuk spoke calmly.

“Because I’ve never seen an assassin like you.”

Geom Mugeuk’s gaze toward him was utterly serious.

“In this world today, there are no assassins who live by such principles. You’re the sort of assassin who would only appear in stories. If your leader were such a person as well, there would be no problem, but he was not.”

The two stood by the roadside and continued their conversation.

“To be honest, I dislike assassins too. I’d rather play alone than go out of my way to make one a friend. But when I heard that you were an assassin who kept to such principles, I grew curious.”

After a brief pause, Geom Mugeuk added,

“That must be why they sent you. They must have investigated that I would take an interest in someone like you. If someone other than you had come a few days ago, I would have killed them on the spot. If I have the chance, I eliminate even one more enemy in advance. That is the sort of person I am. In that sense, the leader who sent you is quite clever.”

Myeongsin had not expected Geom Mugeuk to know this much about him in such detail.

“Why are you telling me all this? Is it because you’re confident you can handle me even after telling me?”

At that, Geom Mugeuk shook his head and said,

“What have you been listening to until now? I said I became interested in an assassin like you. I thought, if it’s an assassin like this, perhaps it wouldn’t be bad to become friends.”

For a fleeting instant, Myeongsin felt the urge to draw a dagger and stab Geom Mugeuk in the throat.

Instinctively, he sensed that the other man had let his guard down.

And the fact that he had felt such an urge also meant that the other man might truly be speaking from the heart.

“You’re not trying to use me to sweep away Myeongbu?”

Geom Mugeuk feigned surprise and laughed brightly.

“You’ve already noticed?”

Myeongsin could not discern Geom Mugeuk’s true intentions.

Just then, a flock of birds flew overhead.

Both men’s gazes naturally turned toward the sky.

“The Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult and an assassin becoming friends? Is that even remotely possible?”

“The Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult can even become friends with a tavern owner.”

Myeongsin knew that those words were true.

“How is the position of a tavern owner the same as that of an assassin? A tavern owner could close his doors tomorrow, but we are people who cannot do that.”

Then Geom Mugeuk said something unexpected.

“I think it would be harder for a tavern owner to close his doors.”

Myeongsin, who had been looking up at the sky, turned his gaze to Geom Mugeuk.

“You don’t know how hard those people work. They are people who would only close their doors if their families were dragged away. You’re more diligent than someone like that? Are you truly?”

Because he sensed that Geom Mugeuk was asking sincerely, Myeongsin held back his answer.

“If he rests for a day, his customers might stop coming. If he rests, someone who came all that way for him would have to turn back. At the very least, you don’t have to worry about such things, do you? Someone I’m supposed to kill is waiting for me. You don’t worry about that, do you?”

One corner of Myeongsin’s eye twitched slightly. He had been trying to get along with Geom Mugeuk as much as he could. And yet Geom Mugeuk kept prodding at some place deep inside him.

“Is it truly that you cannot leave? Or is it simply that you find it bothersome? That you cannot bring yourself to change the life you’ve grown used to?”

Myeongsin’s expression stiffened slightly.
