“Do not speak so lightly just because it is someone else’s life. What do you know about me? All you have done is read the information gathered by spies and judge me by it, have you not?”

“Isn’t that you?”

Myeongsin flinched again.

“The me in your report, the you in my report. Is that not who we are? Is there some true version of me separate from how others see me? If so, then we are living inside misunderstandings. Is that not wrong?”

Myeongsin could not refute him.

“What others see is who we are. If anything, that is more accurate. We may wish to be people grander and more valuable in truth, but the you and I in those reports—is that not who we are?”

Myeongsin wanted to say it was not so. That he was a better person than what others saw.

And as he saw himself thinking that, he realized.

‘Ah! I wanted to become a better person.’

Because he had never once had a conversation like this with anyone, this was the first time he had discovered that such a thought lay deep within his heart. Perhaps the principles he had set for his assassinations had also begun from that very desire.

“Why are you doing this to me? If you truly wish to become my friend, then tell me honestly what you are thinking.”

Because he had to take Geom Mugeuk to the Assassin King, he forced himself to swallow the words that surged up inside him.

‘You are trying to use me right now in some dirty scheme to blow Myeongbu away. Just how lightly do you take me, to think you can dig into me like this?’

The gazes of the two men standing in the road tangled in midair. A conversation important enough to decide their fates was taking place in the middle of the road.

“To be honest, it is not for your sake, but to protect myself.”

Geom Mugeuk’s eyes were clearer and deeper than ever before.

“Because I do not want even someone like you to be swept away on the path I walk. Because I think it would be nice if, somewhere in this world, there were at least one admirable assassin who would accept a single coin offered by a child who unjustly lost his parents as the commission, and kill the wicked.”

“!”

“Because I do not want to walk my path while killing even such a person. That is right. This is not for your sake, but to protect myself.”

Then Geom Mugeuk took a single coin from his robe and held it out to him.

“So this money is the commission to protect me.”

Absolute Regression Chapter 486

Chapter 486: Sorry, But There Is No Next Time.
----------------------------------------------
Myeongsin silently stared at the coin Geommugeuk held out.

He had said this to Salwang:

—Did you say the Young Cult Leader resembles me? Then you greatly overestimated me.

That was not something he should have said to Salwang. Because the one overestimating him was right here.

Myeongsin did not take the coin Geommugeuk held out.

"I am not an assassin who takes commissions for a single coin."

Myeongsin began walking again.

Geommugeuk followed.

"Ah, of course, I am well aware that you are the assassin with the second-highest rate in Myeongbu."

But a thought different from his words rose in Geommugeuk's mind.

'No, you are that kind of assassin.'

His accepting a single-coin commission was a matter of the future.

He had lived as an assassin who left the organization and wandered the Central Plains, accepting a single coin offered by a child as a commission fee. When someone suffered an injustice, he avenged their grudge for them. In return, he sometimes received a lump of rice balls, and sometimes he set out to carry out a killing after listening to a song.

And in the end, he was killed by Salwang.

The reason he left the organization was because he had carried out a killing in which he could not keep his principles, due to Salwang.

Salwang was merciless. The person he deceived Myeongsin into killing by making him out to be a villain was an excellent person. An exceedingly good person who received the people's respect and praise.

As always, Myeongsin had investigated the commission target in his own way, but he could not escape Salwang's scheme to deliberately deceive him.

After that incident, Myeongsin was greatly shaken and went into hiding.

The reason he lived as an assassin who helped people was likely because the guilt of having killed an innocent person was added to the innately good heart he was born with.

And Salwang eventually found him and killed him.

What words had Salwang spoken when he killed him?

Had he inflicted a great wound upon Myeongsin's heart before killing him? Or had he killed him while saying something else? That was impossible to know.

In any case, before the regression, Salwang lived and Myeongsin died, but in this world now, the one who should live is Myeongsin, and the one who should die is Salwang.

The two walked in silence for quite some time.

Myeongsin was troubled.

It was rather an opportunity. Things were rather awkward now, and it felt like Geommugeuk would follow him wherever he went.

'Shall I take him with me like this?'

Even if he did not follow, he could simply wait for the next time.

Even though an unexpected opportunity had arrived, Myeongsin hesitated. It was because of what Geommugeuk had said.

—It would be nice if somewhere in this world, there was a cool assassin who would accept a single coin offered by a little kid as commission and kill a villain for them.

The moment he heard those words, his heart trembled.

Was it because he felt the desire to truly live as that kind of assassin?

Or was it due to anger toward Geommugeuk, who was trying to bewitch him with such words?

For a brief moment, his emotions had wavered at Geommugeuk's words. If some other assassin had been trying to kill him, they would have aimed for that very moment.

Whether he knew of this complicated heart or not, Geommugeuk was at ease.

"Now, to resolve this awkward relationship, we need to have a drink, don't we? Let's hurry."

Rather, Geommugeuk walked ahead.

Myeongsin followed behind him.

'If I take him there... the Young Cult Leader dies today.'

If he could not carry out this mission.

'I die.'

Because he would be ruining the greatest assassination in history, one that had even brought in external supreme assassins. Moreover, if his relationship with Salwang was not good these days as it was, it would be even harder to avoid that responsibility.

"What are you thinking so deeply about?"

Whether to kill you or not.

"Can't we just leave?"

"Why would you say that all of a sudden?"

The Young Cult Leader suddenly ran away.

Because he had wanted to report this to Salwang. Because he did not want to kill the only person who had made him look deep into his own heart.

Was that all? From the moment he killed the Demonic Cult's Young Cult Leader, he would have to hide for the rest of his life. Salwang might have a clear reason for accepting this matter, but he did not. It was something he was not emotionally inclined to do.

"Could it be you've received an order to kill me?"

At Geommugeuk's sudden question, Myeongsin inwardly flinched but moved his steps without showing it.

"I suppose it really is so."

Myeongsin stopped.

"I did not say anything."

"Don't ordinary people deny what isn't true, if only to avoid needless misunderstanding?"

"What does it matter to endure such petty misunderstanding?"

Geommugeuk did not let a single word pass by.

"Those who say petty misunderstanding is fine are actually people who truly hate being misunderstood. Just as those who love speaking ill of others terribly hate being spoken ill of themselves."

He had seen precisely. Though Myeongsin spoke like that, he hated being misunderstood. Because he despised people who spoke carelessly without knowing him well.

"The world is not comprised entirely of your experiences."

As if acknowledging this, Geommugeuk nodded vigorously.

The more Myeongsin conversed with Geommugeuk, the angrier he became.

Because he kept making him look into himself, when looking into others was his profession as an assassin.

Whether Geommugeuk knew that heart or not, he incessantly shook his emotions.

"In your principles, am I someone who must be killed, or not?"

Myeongsin remembered that Salwang had also asked this question.

—You said you wouldn't kill if they weren't a villain, didn't you? Is the Young Cult Leader a villain? A good person?

He couldn't answer, not to Salwang, nor to the man himself.

Based only on gathered information, Geommugeuk was not a villain. The only justification for killing him was his identity as the Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult.

If the Young Cult Leader was not a villain, then it would mean breaking his principles. Could it be that he was now trying to gloss things over using the Demonic Cult as an excuse? Hoping all of this would just pass by.

"You tell me. Are you a villain, or not?"

Geommugeuk answered without hesitation.

"I am someone who should not be killed."

"Why is that?"

"If your principle is not to kill if they aren't a villain, my principle is that a villain must certainly be killed. I am one step ahead of your principle. In the end, we can be seen as the same kind of person."

Geommugeuk's principle was something hard to believe even hearing it directly, but it was true. Because it had come up as the most important information regarding him.

A Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult who does not forgive evil?

Of course, Myeongsin had thought it was deception and disguise.

If that were the case? It would truly be an incredible disguise and deception. Because he kept thinking that those pieces of information might actually be true.

Rather, the prior investigation was becoming poison. If not for it, he would never have believed the Demonic Cult's Young Cult Leader in the first place.

In the meantime, the two arrived at the main street.

"Now, as you said, today let's go to the place you chose."

Myeongsin's heart pounded.

'Shall I take him there? Or shall I endure today?'

If he had to take him there someday, was there any need to delay? Even though he knew today was the opportunity, Myeongsin hesitated.

"If it's not this village, if you know another good place, let's go there."

Though a typhoon was rising in Myeongsin's heart, his expression was like a clear, still lake.

"There's an inn I know a short distance away. Would that be alright?"

If Geommugeuk suggested eating right here, he had no intention of forcing him to go.

But Geommugeuk approached and tugged at his sleeve.

"You should have said so sooner. Come, let's go. Even if we're having a drink, we should have it somewhere delicious."

Just as he had strode up in the iron chamber, taken his hand, and pulled the handle together, today too Geommugeuk was determining his own fate with his own hands.

He should have felt at ease, thinking, 'Yes, this is all the fate you decided for yourself.'

But as he moved his steps away from the main street, Myeongsin's heart was not at ease.

The coin that had lain on Geommugeuk's palm kept coming to mind.

'I must not get entangled.'

That coin was nothing but a demonic needle that had flown into his heart.

While walking immersed in such thoughts, words flowed from Geommugeuk that made Myeongsin stop.

"Before long, your leader will deceive you by making out someone you absolutely must not kill to be a villain, and make you kill them. What will you do then? Will you kill them? Or will you leave Myeongbu?"

"Why would he do such a thing to me, the second-in-command of Myeongbu?"

But contrary to the certainty that it absolutely couldn't happen, Myeongsin was recalling what Salwang had said.

—If you established such useless principles and later make a mistake? Would you wash your hands of it because of that guilt? Or would you take your own life?

Thinking about it, that mistake was a mistake Salwang would end up making. A mistake of mistaking a good person for a villain and giving the order. The Salwang he knew wasn't someone who would make such a mistake. Why had he said that?

But soon Myeongsin thought this too was the opponent's trick.

'He found out there is conflict between me and him, and is persistently digging into that point.'

Having stopped his steps, Myeongsin glared at Geommugeuk.

"Are you trying to sow discord between me and him?"

He had expected him to say of course not.

"I am."

Geommugeuk admitted readily.

"To bring you to my side, I must resort to sowing discord."

And Geommugeuk's open sowing of discord was powerful.

"Is the one who saved you really that person?"

Myeongsin's face hardened completely.

"In your childhood, you were the sole survivor of a massacred clan. He saved you, who were dying under a fallen wardrobe."

Myeongsin was surprised. The Young Cult Leader knew the fact that Salwang had saved him. And in great detail.

'This is something no one knows.'

To the surprise of how he knew, Geommugeuk answered calmly.

"I am the Young Cult Leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult. If I wish to know, I can find out everything down to who the Murim Alliance Leader had a crush on at what age."

What surprised Myeongsin wasn't that he had found out that fact.

"What do you mean, he didn't save me?"

"Suppose someone pushes another into a river, then saves them again. Does that mean they saved that person?"

At the unthinkable thought, Myeongsin's eyes went wide.

"The place that accepted the commission to annihilate your clan was Myeongbu."

The reason this discord-sowing was powerful was because its material was truth.

Myeongsin was someone who had trained countless times to maintain the tranquility of his heart while living as an assassin.

But at least in this moment, he couldn't stop the surging passion and confusion.

That moment from childhood came to mind.

When the heavily pressing wardrobe was lifted, darkness receded and light was seen.

The young boy who had been looking down at him in that light.

With ice-like eyes devoid of any emotion, the boy silently looked down at him.

"You didn't know either, did you?"

No, he hadn't known.

He had been merely seven years old then. He had lived with the vague resolve to repay that grace because he had been saved.

He hadn't dreamed of avenging his clan. No, he hadn't even had the luxury to dream. Because he had begun assassin training from then on. Thus he had lived as an assassin until now.

But what dominated him now wasn't a sense of betrayal, but doubt directed at himself.

The suspicion that perhaps, as Geommugeuk said, he had known.

Could it be that he hadn't asked all this time because of that?

Why had he saved him then? No, why had he been passing by that very place?

He had never once asked Salwang until now.

Could it be he had sensed it? That Salwang would calmly say, 'We annihilated your clan. As you know, it was just business.'

Fearing he would hear those words?

Had he been deliberately turning a blind eye to all that truth?

'What are you thinking! Don't fall for such tricks!'

It was literally nothing but cunning sowing of discord. The Demonic Cult bastards' sowing of discord based on the information that the relationship between him and Salwang had grown distant.

'How vicious!'

He turned the blade from himself toward Geommugeuk, but the hand gripping that blade's hilt was asking.

'Is this not also avoiding what you must face?'

Geommugeuk asked.

"Is the discord-sowing sufficient?"

Myeongsin had wanted to speak with a leisurely smile, but now emotions were loaded in the words he spat out fiercely.

"The moment of sowing discord is thrilling, isn't it? But do you know this? With that momentary thrill, you have lost my trust forever."

Myeongsin strode forward again.

Geommugeuk walked alongside him.

"There will likely remain old commission records regarding your clan. If your leader hasn't destroyed them, perhaps he had been hoping you would see them someday."

Myeongsin gritted his molars. Though he considered it the opponent's artifice, the reason he couldn't completely deny it was because the Salwang he knew was that kind of person.

Why had that hollow person saved him?

Why had he alone been kept close to that inner ice cliff of his?

He hadn't found that answer yet.

Because of the emotional battle with Geommugeuk, one thing was good. He had been able to bring him to the safe house easily.

That small street was bustling with many travelers.

"I didn't know there was a place like this."

Geommugeuk spoke as if nothing had happened, and Myeongsin answered in a softened tone.

"It's a place I come to occasionally."

But in Myeongsin's heart, two minds were clashing.

The desire to finish the mission quickly.

The hope that Geommugeuk would leave, saying his taste for drink had suddenly soured.

Though he was someone who troubled and angered people so, he didn't know why the wish for him to keep living kept arising.

'Leave even now!'

But Geommugeuk strode into the street first. His steps toward the trap of death were ever so light.

"We received new silk yesterday. Please take a look before you go!"

As a fabric store clerk.

As the owner of a bean curd shop dozing off.

As a woman beckoning a running child.

As a merchant hawking with a tray hung around his neck.

As still other appearances.

The assassins had become one with this street perfectly. They were supreme assassins with the greatest skill even among assassins.

Can you truly endure this perfect feast of true ones who have reached the pinnacle of killing?

Myeongsin looked at Geommugeuk. He rather looked excited.

"I received a dagger as a gift, so today I will buy. Next time, you buy. Let's make peace too, and drink excitedly today."

Sorry, but there is no next time.

"Is that the tavern over there?"

"It is."

"The atmosphere is already just right. You should have suggested coming here sooner."

Thus the two entered the tavern. A good-natured-looking owner who had been chatting with guests welcomed them.

"You have come?"

The innkeeper feigned recognition toward Myeongsin.

"Please sit where you are comfortable."

Geommugeuk spoke to the owner.

"I can tell just by looking at a tavern owner's face. Whether the alcohol tastes good or not."

Geommugeuk stared at the owner's face intently and determined the taste of the alcohol.

"This, this seems like a taste one could never forget in their lifetime?"

Then the owner smiled broadly and clapped his hands with loud cracking sounds.

"Today we will serve you in an utterly unforgettable fashion!"

Absolute Regression

Chapter 487

Chapter 487: Famous Among Assassins
-----------------------------------
The seat Geom Mugeuk chose was in the center of the inn.

As they sat down together, Myeongsin inwardly thought that Geom Mugeuk trusted him.

‘He’s let his guard down.’

Normally, in a suspicious situation, one would instinctively sit with one’s back to the wall.

But Geom Mugeuk had sat in a place wide open on all sides.

Myeongsin looked around the inside of the inn, glancing at several corner seats.

‘In the end, it was a good choice.’

Because they had prepared various devices in the walls, anticipating that he would sit with his back to one.

But by sitting in the middle, Geom Mugeuk had rendered several of those devices useless.

‘It must be a coincidence, right?’

Surely he had not known there were such things in the walls and chosen this seat because of it.

Geom Mugeuk looked at the proprietor and said loudly,

“Bring us the best liquor you have and the dish you make best.”

“Please wait just a moment!”

The proprietor went into the kitchen with excited steps.

“The atmosphere here is truly pleasant.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s words, Myeongsin nodded. The feeling that he had not wanted to bring the young cult leader here still lingered in him.

He even wished Geom Mugeuk would spring to his feet right now and leave this place. If only he would say something had suddenly happened in the Demonic Cult and depart. Then Myeongsin could carry out his mission, and Geom Mugeuk would live.

But that did not happen.

“Are you still not over it?”

“Never again try to sow such absurd discord.”

Though even if he wanted to in the future, he would not be able to.

Geom Mugeuk made it clear to the very end that he was telling the truth.

“What I said is true. It was written in our cult’s report.”

With a stiff face, Myeongsin looked out of the inn.

Did Myeongbu really exterminate my family?

If that is true, shouldn’t I be digging deeper and asking about this instead of turning my head toward the window?

If that is true, shouldn’t I make sure this young cult leader does not die here? Shouldn’t I instead help him fight and avenge my family?

But as he had always done until now, Myeongsin was looking away again. He was making an effort not to believe it, reminding himself of the possibility that this might be some wicked scheme of the Demonic Cult.

‘Am I perhaps afraid of him?’

His gaze turned toward the proprietor in the kitchen—the Assassin King.

Was he afraid of death? Or was he afraid that his relationship with him would be shattered?

Just then, as though he had peered into his heart, Geom Mugeuk abruptly asked,

“Are you afraid of change?”

“……”

“I understand you. It truly isn’t easy to break free from something that has taken hold of you once.”

Myeongsin still gave no answer.

Perhaps those words from Geom Mugeuk were the correct answer.

Just as he had never asked the Assassin King why he had saved him, had he not lived merely hoping that this life would flow on as it was, without becoming tangled in anything complicated?

At that moment, the proprietor bringing the liquor met Myeongsin’s eyes.

“It has been a while since you last came. How long has it been?”

“About three or four months, I think.”

The Assassin King’s kindly smile felt unfamiliar. He had completely become the proprietor of this tavern.

Myeongsin had experienced the Assassin King’s killings many times until now. And this was the first time he had seen him so immersed in a role.

‘Today, the young cult leader will surely die.’

And that was not all. Everywhere Myeongsin’s gaze fell, there were hidden traps of death, as well as elite assassins waiting outside.

“Now, please have some liquor first. The food will be out shortly.”

Geom Mugeuk offered liquor to the Assassin King.

“Before you go, accept a cup from me.”

“Gladly.”

The Assassin King accepted the drink.

“How long have you been in business?”

“For a long time.”

“I knew it! The moment I first saw you, I had that feeling!”

Listening to their conversation, Myeongsin was tense. He could not know when the Assassin King would launch a surprise attack. The Assassin King’s ambushes always exceeded his expectations.

Now? The moment he sets that cup down? Or the moment he sets the cup down and pours liquor for Geom Mugeuk?

He knew how fast the Assassin King’s move was. And on top of that, Myeongsin himself was here. The moment the ambush began, he too would help him and deliver a strike.

Would that young cult leader truly be able to withstand a joint attack by Myeongbu’s number one and number two assassins?

Not even knowing how perilous the situation he was in right now, that young cult leader was engaging in a carefree conversation.

“Since you’ve been in business for a long time, you must have experienced all sorts of things.”

“I suppose so.”

Right now, Geom Mugeuk was full of openings.

But the Assassin King was not attacking him. He was not someone who would miss an opportunity like this.

Was it to enjoy this moment? Or to savor an even more dramatic instant? No, the Assassin King had no such emotions. That was why he was the greatest assassin.

‘But why?’

Then Geom Mugeuk said something out of the blue to the Assassin King.

“Since you’re an experienced proprietor, please hear out a concern of mine.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s words, Myeongsin grew slightly tense.

‘A concern? What on earth is he trying to say?’

Those words went beyond the unexpected and struck Myeongsin like a shock.

“Someone belatedly discovered who his enemy was. But it turned out to be someone he had maintained a close relationship with for a long time. What should he do in such a case?”

Myeongsin had never imagined he would say such a thing to a tavern proprietor.

‘Could it be? Has he noticed the proprietor’s true identity?’

No. If he had noticed, shouldn’t he avoid saying such things instead? It was truly beyond common sense, so Myeongsin was confused.

The Assassin King said to Geom Mugeuk,

“What kind of relationship it is would be important.”

“One side is using the other, and the other side misunderstands him as the savior of his life.”

At that, the Assassin King immediately frowned.

“His enemy, and he even used him? What a truly vicious man. If it were me, I would definitely take revenge. If my own strength were insufficient, I would hire an assassin to take revenge.”

Myeongsin thought both Geom Mugeuk, who had asked this question, and the Assassin King, who had so casually mentioned assassins in his answer, were remarkable.

And he was surprised by how natural the Assassin King’s answer was.

If there was something weighing on his conscience, could he answer so boldly that he would take revenge? The claim that his family had been exterminated by Myeongbu’s hands was surely the young cult leader’s attempt to sow discord.

But what if the young cult leader’s words were true? Could it be that the Assassin King had even forgotten the fact that he was using me?

Of course, it had simply been an answer faithful to the question, but Myeongsin’s heart was now so complicated that if one spot were prodded, ten parts of his body would writhe.

He still did not know which side was the truth.

“Now then, please enjoy.”

When the Assassin King returned to the kitchen, Myeongsin looked at Geom Mugeuk and said,

“Why did you tell that story to him?”

“Wouldn’t a third party judge it objectively if asked?”

“Your words themselves are already not objective, are they?”

Geom Mugeuk looked at Myeongsin.

“I understand. You have only known me for a few days, but the years you spent with him must number in the decades.”

Geom Mugeuk drank the liquor. Watching him, Myeongsin grew tense.

Is there poison in that liquor?

Without the slightest suspicion, Geom Mugeuk emptied his cup.

“But a few days can sometimes overcome decades. Because this side is truth, and that side is falsehood. Even if falsehood is piled up for a hundred years, it cannot compare to a single night of truth.”

There was at least one thing he had to acknowledge.

“You really are tenacious.”

“I admit it. Perhaps it is because I am such a tenacious person that I was able to accomplish the things recorded in your report.”

Myeongsin’s eyes trembled. Because all the while he watched Geom Mugeuk speak of these things, his instincts had continued to cry out. That man is telling the truth. You have trusted this instinct all your life as an assassin, so why will you not trust it at this moment?

“And as I said earlier, this is also a way of protecting myself. Even if you never end up believing me, I will console myself that I tried this hard. I am a tenacious person, and at the same time, I am this selfish.”

Meanwhile, the Assassin King finished preparing the side dish and brought it over.

Geom Mugeuk picked up his chopsticks and tasted the food.

“It’s delicious! As expected, my eye was not wrong!”

“I’m glad it suits your taste.”

“Now, now, accept another cup.”

Seeing Geom Mugeuk growing increasingly open toward the proprietor, Myeongsin’s heart grew urgent.

He had to make a decision quickly.

Would he believe the young cult leader? Or not?

And so, would he help the young cult leader? Or not?

The life, death, and fate of both the young cult leader and himself depended on this decision.

If he believed the young cult leader?

He might very well die. There was no way the Assassin King would let him live.

But perhaps he could save that young cult leader.

And if luck was even better, perhaps he could succeed in taking revenge and live as a rather decent assassin who accepted one-coin commissions.

At that moment, the Assassin King’s voice transmission reached Myeongsin.

—There is someone protecting him.

It was the moment the reason why the Assassin King had not ambushed the young cult leader, who was full of openings, was revealed.

—His concealment technique is no ordinary thing.

It was skill that even Myeongsin, the second-in-command of Myeongbu, had failed to notice.

—We must deal with him first.

And at that moment, Hwi’s voice transmission also reached Geom Mugeuk.

—He has noticed my presence.

He had realized that the other party had perceived his existence.

From the beginning, Geom Mugeuk had known that the proprietor of this place was the Assassin King. The only person who would be waiting for him at a place Myeongsin led him to was him.

Geom Mugeuk took something out from his robe and placed it before Myeongsin.

It was the single coin he had presented earlier.

Myeongsin saw that coin, and so did the Assassin King.

Myeongsin felt that the moment had come for him to face the problem before him. Whether it was truth or falsehood. More important than the result was the version of himself that asked it.

After gazing quietly down at the coin, Myeongsin finally opened his mouth.

“Proprietor, there is something I would like to ask as well.”

Though the Assassin King was perfectly playing the role of a tavern proprietor, he was inwardly surprised by this unplanned action from Myeongsin.

“This lowly man is quite popular today. Please ask.”

“Proprietor, do you have a friend?”

It was an abrupt question, but the Assassin King was not surprised at all.

“I am too busy making a living to have time to make friends, but even so, I do have one.”

“What kind of friend is he?”

“He is weak-hearted and easily swayed, so others deceive him easily. But he is still a good friend.”

He was probably misunderstanding. That Myeongsin had said something unnecessary to the young cult leader of the Demonic Cult. Or that he had been bewitched by him.

But Myeongsin had something more important to do than clear up that misunderstanding.

“Proprietor, have you ever deceived that friend?”

“What do you mean by that?”

“Didn’t you just say it? That he is weak-hearted and easily swayed. Then wouldn’t you also have had opportunities to deceive him, even if unintentionally?”

“Well.”

The moment the Assassin King briefly fell into thought.

Myeongsin sent a voice transmission to the Assassin King.

—Was it Myeongbu that exterminated my family?

He knew it was not the right moment to ask this question, but once this moment passed, the question would become meaningless.

—A demon has taken hold of your heart.

At the Assassin King’s answer, Myeongsin asked again.

—I asked you. Was it Myeongbu that exterminated my family?

Having watched him for so long, Myeongsin seemed to know a great deal about him indeed. The answer he had expected, and feared, came.

—Yes. Myeongbu handled it.

Myeongsin was surprised at himself for not being shocked by this terrifying truth.

—As you know, that was work. If it had not been us, someone else would have taken the job. And do not forget. I was the one who spared you.

Strangely enough, what angered him at this moment was not the deed Myeongbu had committed. It was the way he admitted the fact so frankly and confidently.

Was this something that could simply be passed over like this? Just how lightly had he regarded him?

But soon, Myeongsin understood the Assassin King. Because he had always been this kind of person. Because he was a person with nothing at all inside.

“Why did you spare me?”

He asked not through voice transmission, but aloud.

It was the moment Myeongsin clearly revealed his choice.

The Assassin King gave no answer.

Then Geom Mugeuk answered in his stead.

“Do not attach meaning to that. Because even that man does not know.”

The gazes of both the Assassin King and Myeongsin turned toward Geom Mugeuk.

“It must have been the fickle heart of a little child who went out on an assassination with his father. Sometimes killing insects, sometimes sparing them and taking them away in a jar. Even if there was meaning to it, what great meaning could there have been?”

Geom Mugeuk’s gaze turned toward the Assassin King.

“You don’t know either, do you? You don’t even remember, do you?”

The very instant that question ended!

Shwaaaak!

A wind-splitting sound tore at the ears.

Kaaang!

A sharp metallic ring burst out.

A steel needle that had been deflected before Geom Mugeuk’s eyes flew up into the air before falling to the floor.

A single sword blocking Geom Mugeuk’s face was trembling faintly.

The person who had blocked the Assassin King’s lightning-like surprise attack was Hwi, who had appeared after releasing his concealment.

The Assassin King and Hwi looked at each other. Because the Assassin King had already perceived the concealed presence, he was not surprised at all. His expression even suggested that perhaps this attack had been meant to draw him out.

An assassin and a guard.

The meeting of the person best at killing in the martial world and the person best at protecting in the martial world.

Even among such antithetical meetings, these were two people who existed at opposite extremes.

“The Demonic Cult Leader’s guard.”

The Assassin King immediately recognized Hwi’s identity. There would be only one guard with such skill.

“You are quite famous among assassins.”

A strange emotion flickered through the Assassin King’s eyes.

Even though the other party had recognized him, Hwi merely stared at him in silence.

Speaking in Hwi’s place, Geom Mugeuk said to the Assassin King,

“Do you still think you can kill me?”

Even though his first surprise attack had failed, even though Myeongsin had learned the truth, and even though the chief guard of the Heavenly Demon stood before his eyes, the Assassin King was not flustered in the slightest.

The Assassin King’s two eyes became transparent. His killing intent was different from that of ordinary assassins. Born without any emotions from the start, his killing intent was as pure as snow and as clear as ice.

Just from this aura alone, one could feel it.

No one would be able to easily stop the death this man delivered. This would truly not be an easy fight.

As taut tension flowed through the air, Myeongsin picked up the single coin lying on the table and repeated exactly what the Assassin King had said earlier.

“If it were me, I would definitely take revenge. If my own strength were insufficient, I would hire an assassin to take revenge.”

Still, the Assassin King only stared at him with transparent eyes.

Myeongsin slowly clenched the coin in his palm. It was the moment he faced the life he had only ever avoided.

“You said this one-coin commission was to protect you? No. From now on, this commission is for me. With this one coin, you have entrusted a commission to me.”

Absolute Regression, Chapter 488

Chapter 488: Lowering the Sword Over a Hostage Crisis.
------------------------------------------------------
Myeongsin commissioned themselves.

To avenge their deceased parents.

It was the first commission they had ever given to themself in this life, and it would be the last.

Having accepted their own commission with this single coin, surely it was now time to accept the single-coin commission given by another as well. If they survived this fight.

And in this moment, Myeongsin savored a refreshing relief they had felt for the first time since birth.

Because they were now looking straight at a life they had been unable to face all along.

Even in this situation, Salwang merely gazed at them without revealing any emotion.

'Even at this moment when I am casting you aside, is that gaze all you have to give me?'

Geom Mugeuk's words came to mind.

"—Do not place meaning in that."

Until now, they had believed a deep heart unknown to them was hidden within that transparent gaze.

Once more, Geom Mugeuk's words surfaced.

"—Even if there is meaning, what great meaning could there be?"

Yes, there was nothing in that gaze.

They had merely tried desperately to find meaning out of the fear that if there were truly nothing there, their own life would amount to nothing as well.

It wasn't that he regarded them as special; it was merely that someone as special as them had survived.

Once these thoughts were organized, Myeongsin did not look back.

He was the head of the organization that had annihilated their clan, and he had raised them as an assassin while hiding that fact.

That alone was reason enough to settle things with him.

Before that, Myeongsin had informed Geom Mugeuk.

"There are five assassins mixed among the guests."

At those words, the guests, already frozen stiff by the preceding situation, turned deathly pale. As if the fight breaking out wasn't enough, assassins were hiding among them pretending to be guests?

They desperately wanted to flee outside, but they could not. The owner, whose identity had been revealed, was standing at the entrance, and if they moved in this situation, they would undoubtedly be the first to meet death.

Some shed tears in fear, some sank to the floor, and some trembled all over as they watched the situation.

Geom Mugeuk did not go out of his way to mention their safety. He did not warn the assassins not to touch them, nor did he bluster that he wouldn't bat an eye even if hostages were killed.

If they had investigated him, they would know well that he was someone who tried to save them.

They would actively use the guests as hostages. But there was an advantage to that too. Because they wouldn't kill them recklessly.

Myeongsin pointed out exactly those five.

"The assassins are the third, fourth, and fifth from the left. The eighth, and ninth."

On that side were Salwang and the five identified assassins.

On this side were Geom Mugeuk, Hwi, and Myeongsin.

In that taut confrontation, Geom Mugeuk and Salwang's gazes met in the air.

Salwang was not impatient. It was as if this situation were merely one of many things he had predicted.

"Why did you join hands with them?"

What desire of Salwang's had been stimulated for him to join hands with them?

Salwang answered impassively.

"I cannot speak about the client. It is the principle of assassins."

Because the clients for this assassination mission would be them.

Geom Mugeuk spoke as he slowly drew the Black Demon Sword.

"I too have principles. My demonic path does not break tavern tables. Yet today, I must break that principle."

Just before a massive fight broke out, Hwi spoke.

"There are not five assassins, but six. That person there as well."

The person Hwi pointed at was a guest sitting alone in a corner.

He was someone even Myeongsin did not know.

Hwi had found an assassin hiding his identity with the unique senses of a bodyguard.

"As expected! Uncle's eyes cannot be deceived."

As Geom Mugeuk rejoiced, Hwi's telepathic message flew in.

"—There are still those who remain uncertain. Do not let your guard down."

If so, it meant they had truly made extensive preparations. Because it meant they had hidden assassins capable of deceiving Hwi's eyes among the guests.

Meanwhile, Myeongsin realized one fact due to the existence of an assassin even they had not known about.

'You never trusted me.'

Originally, they too would have helped him in this fight and killed Geom Mugeuk. In such a situation, an assassin they didn't know about should absolutely not exist.

Perhaps that person was someone prepared to kill them.

They suddenly recalled Salwang's words.

"—If we weren't friends, you would already be dead."

Their relationship with him became even clearer. Now even the slightest possibility that they might have misjudged vanished.

'I didn't survive because we were friends.'

Now they could be certain. He had merely been surprised and amused that a child picked up on a whim had become his number two. He had only needed the killings that this number two carried out for the Myeongbu. That was the reason he had kept them alive.

Myeongsin had only denied it desperately because they didn't want to be miserable. They had desperately given it meaning, going so far as to construct an icy cliff.

In Myeongsin's heart, a vision was drawn of climbing an icy cliff to stand upon its summit.

"Since we're not friends now, try to kill me!"

The will to kill Salwang blazed in Myeongsin's eyes.

Geom Mugeuk looked at Hwi. Though he didn't speak, Hwi could understand Geom Mugeuk's intention.

It meant watching Myeongsin's back.

This fight was Geom Mugeuk's own fight, but it was also Myeongsin's fight, and at the same time, Hwi's fight.

While the three fought, Geom Mugeuk intended to finish off all the assassins here.

'Finish them as quickly as possible and help those two.'

Geom Mugeuk shouted.

"Everyone not identified as an assassin, lie face down on the floor!"

At that, everyone except the assassins lay face down.

At the same time, Myeongsin rushed at Salwang.

Chaeng! Chaeng! Chaeng!

Their daggers clashed in the air. They knew each other better than anyone. Their martial arts, their skills.

Pushed by the fiercely pressing momentum, Salwang sprang outside the tavern.

Geom Mugeuk could tell. That he had deliberately taken Myeongsin outside. Because of his confidence that the remaining assassins could kill him here. And to let them freely display what had been prepared in this space of death.

Hwi looked at Geom Mugeuk, and Geom Mugeuk nodded silently. He was someone who would never leave this spot under normal circumstances, but Geom Mugeuk said with his eyes:

Trust me, and protect Myeongsin. I will go out right away.

Hwi's deliberation was not long. This time, Hwi had volunteered to be Geom Mugeuk's escort not merely to protect his life. It was because Hwi wanted to protect the person Geom Mugeuk wished to protect, and moreover, the conviction he held.

Swoosh.

Hwi vanished without a sound.

At the same time, the assassins in the hall all reached their hands toward Geom Mugeuk.

Special hidden projectiles were concealed within their sleeve cuffs.

Soul-Slaying Thunderbolts (殺魂雷).

Assassination weapons crafted by the Myeongbu.

Soul-Slaying Thunderbolts were powerful hidden weapons; if the user's internal strength was even slightly insufficient, their arm would be torn off.

Boom! Boom! Boom!

Hidden weapons burst forth like explosions.

Geom Mugeuk flipped a table and infused it with internal strength. The moment he tried to block with the table.

Swish! Swish! Swish!

Steel needles were fired from small holes in the bottom of the table toward Geom Mugeuk.

It was a device that anticipated the opponent flipping a table to block the attack.

So much so that the Soul-Slaying Thunderbolts of the six assassins could be called a diversion for this ambush; this hidden move was devastating.

Fortunately, Geom Mugeuk's instinctive evasion was faster than the attack. If he had been even a moment slower, he would have been caught by this deceptive strike.

Crack crack crack crack crack crack crack!

Even amid that, hidden weapons flew and embedded themselves in the table. Soul-Slaying Thunderbolts were no ordinary hidden weapons. Each and every one possessed devastating power, capable of shattering iron swords and piercing stone walls.

But they could not destroy the table infused with Geom Mugeuk's internal strength.

Geom Mugeuk threw the table and soared toward the assassin.

The moment the assassin swung his sword to knock away the table!

Behind it, Geom Mugeuk was not there.

Shhk.

Geom Mugeuk's sword slid across the floor as he charged, slicing through the assassin's belly.

The assassin, cut in half at the torso, collapsed on the spot.

At the thick scent of blood wafting forth, screams flowed from the mouths of the guests lying face-down on the floor.

Attacks of Soul-Slaying Thunderbolts poured toward Geom Mugeuk.

Crack crack crack crack crack crack crack!

The hidden weapons pierced through the floor.

But Geom Mugeuk was already cutting through the air, charging toward the second assassin.

Then. Another assassin struck down the abacus that was on the counter.

In that instant!

Pop! Pop! Pop!

Abacus beads poured out toward the front all at once. They looked ordinary, but when they separated and flew, they were anything but.

That hidden weapon, known to invariably kill any enemy within its range, was none other than!

Ghostly Abacus Pellets (鬼算彈).

Dozens of abacus beads flew in at terrifying speed. The beads of the Ghostly Abacus Pellets were no ordinary hidden weapons. Rumor had it they could even pierce the protective auras of experts.

Geom Mugeuk hurled his body. It seemed an unavoidable attack, but Geom Mugeuk found the space for survival among those lines of fire. Twisting his waist, arms, and body greatly, he dodged the flying attacks.

His posture was bizarre, but not a single one struck his body. The beads of the Ghostly Abacus Pellets grazed past his whole body.

Thud thud thud thud thud thud thud thud!

They punched through the surrounding walls like Go stones.

Sunlight entered the room through the walls, creating dozens of lines.

A feast of light created in an instant.

At a glance, the sight looked fantastical.

Between the shafts of sunlight, hidden weapons flew, their blades gleaming.

Swish swish swish swish!

The assassins of the highest skill had no hesitation. Their judgment was quick, and their actions fearless. Had their opponent been anyone other than Geom Mugeuk, they would have already exchanged greetings, remarking on the ordeal.

Dodging the flying hidden weapons, Geom Mugeuk leaped over the counter.

Thud!

The assassin who had fired the Ghostly Abacus Pellets had his jaw shattered and flew backward, crashing into the wall.

Then another assassin was pulling the kitchen curtain hanging in the kitchen. The moment the curtain was pulled.

Shhhhhhriek!

Bone-Melting Powder poured down over Geom Mugeuk's head from above the counter.

Sssssssss!

A terrible odor rose together with smoke.

The earlier abacus attack and this poison attack were a linked ambush. If one tried to eliminate the attacker who launched the abacus attack, the Bone-Melting Powder would immediately be poured down.

But Geom Mugeuk's form crossed through the space in an instant and escaped that place.

The Wind God's Stride was displaying truly remarkable divine footwork in this narrow place.

Another assailant rushed in, kicking up a table as he charged.

The moment he kicked the table, it broke, and the objects on it sprang into the air.

Bowls, liquor bottles, and dishes on the table flew into the air.

Among them was a chopstick container holding chopsticks.

Boom!

What burst in front of Geom Mugeuk was the chopstick container.

It was a device where, when the chopstick container received an impact and chopsticks sprang out from it, the container would burst and toxic smoke would explode out.

When the opponent smashed or overturned a table, who would pay attention to a chopstick container? This too was something Myeongsin had made. This was the reason Salwang had taken Myeongsin outside from this place. Because most of the things prepared today were developed by Myeongsin.

Huuuuh.

Geom Mugeuk, hit in the face by the toxic smoke, collapsed straight away with a thud.

The man who had attacked while overturning the table also had his chest slashed by Geom Mugeuk's sword and collapsed together. Even as he died, he made a satisfied expression, as though he had accomplished his mission.

Two assassins carefully approached to confirm the death of Geom Mugeuk, who had collapsed on the floor.

The moment they, examining Geom Mugeuk, tried to finish him off by thrusting daggers into his neck from both sides!

Shwick.

Their daggers stopped in midair.

Geom Mugeuk's two hands, having opened his eyes at some point, were gripping their wrists.

Geom Mugeuk's hands were holding their wrists.

As they quickly tried to strike down with their other hands.

Huff!

Geom Mugeuk exhaled the toxic smoke he had been holding in his mouth with a huff.

So fearsome was the poison that the two assassins met their deaths screaming in agony.

Then, this time, the last remaining assassin grabbed the hostage who had been lying face-down on the floor and pulled her up.

"Aaaack!"

The assassin who had taken the woman hostage placed a dagger at her neck.

"Drop the sword. Or this wench dies."

"Seems you didn't investigate me properly. I'm not the kind of person who drops his sword over hostage theatrics."

The moment the words ended, Geom Mugeuk charged at him.

Shhhriek!

Using a flickering stride, he charged at the assassin holding the hostage.

The moment the assassin pushed the hostage woman he used as a shield forward and swung his sword from behind.

Slash!

Thud!

The sounds of tearing flesh were heard in succession.

Unfortunately, both the hostage and the assassin collapsed.

Blood flowed from the cut throat of the hostage woman, and blood flowed from the assassin's heart.

One might think the hostage woman was killed by the assassin, but surprisingly, Geom Mugeuk had killed both of them.

The dying assassin asked with a surprised face.

"...How did you know?"

A dagger was held in the dead hostage woman's hand.

Surprisingly, she too was an assassin.

Pushed forward, she had launched a surprise attack at the decisive moment.

Rather, her martial arts skill was stronger than the assassin who had taken her hostage.

She was precisely the core of the assassins prepared here today.

It was an unexpected and fatal attack that even Geom Mugeuk could not have avoided had he not known beforehand.

But Geom Mugeuk had cut her down and then the assassin behind her as if he had known in advance.

Geom Mugeuk left the dying assassin with words he could never know even in death.

"Our uncle said there was still someone suspicious remaining."

Absolute Regression, Episode 489.

Chapter 489: When One Who Knows How to Protect Makes Up Their Mind.
-------------------------------------------------------------------
Myeongsin was swinging his dagger in a state of selfless trance.

Sparks flew from the dagger clashing against the Killing King’s.

A battle between the foremost and second foremost of Myeongbu.

It was a fight where a single moment’s mistake would lead to death.

The colliding daggers were so fast they could not be seen.

He had lightly crossed blades with the Killing King before, but this was his first true battle of life and death.

Whoosh, whoosh, whoosh, whoosh, whoosh!

Their daggers stabbed at each other in rapid succession.

Footwork as swift as the attacks moved in a dazzling display, and with a lightness technique executed through extreme concentration, they shifted their upper bodies to evade the incoming blades.

The daggers struck one another with clear ringing sounds, and at times with the scraping screech of metal against metal.

‘He’s strong!’

Before his mind or instincts, Myeongsin’s hands could feel it. The weighty force transmitted through the Killing King’s attacks still had room to spare. He, on the other hand, was pouring out everything he had.

The reason he could fight on equal footing despite that difference in skill was momentum.

Myeongsin had resolved to stake life and death on this. His willingness to face death was barely making up for the gap in their abilities.

And just like that, over a hundred exchanges passed in an instant.

Clang!

With one great clash, the two men retreated backward.

They were standing on the roof of the building across from the tavern.

Myeongsin’s breathing was ragged, but the Killing King remained calm. This was exactly the gap in skill that existed between them.

‘Still, it’s fine.’

Because he had resolved himself to die, Myeongsin was not afraid.

Whenever he went out on an assassination mission, he would always imagine the moment it failed and he died.

Then, naturally, this scene would come to mind.

Martial artists rummaging through his corpse, trying to determine where this assassin had come from.

No one buried an assassin’s corpse, so they would toss it out into a field. Then wolves would come prowling and tear into the body.

Myeongsin had always thought that was an assassin’s end.

But dying while fighting the Killing King?

‘That’s a fairly decent death.’

At that moment, the Killing King was looking at the tavern across the way. Through the window, glimpses of the battle inside could be seen.

“The Young Cult Leader won’t make it out of there alive.”

Myeongsin spoke calmly.

“The Young Cult Leader will come out alive.”

Myeongsin, who had always spoken respectfully, now spoke down to him, but the Killing King did not care.

“If so, it means every device you made has become useless.”

Myeongsin knew that was why he had drawn him outside.

“I’m grateful that you trust me, but.”

There were three reasons he had come outside despite knowing that. First of all.

“They won’t work. Not on that Young Cult Leader.”

He believed in Geom Mugeuk.

The Geom Mugeuk he had seen in the records, and the Geom Mugeuk he had seen with his own eyes. This was the conclusion reached by combining the two.

Only a few days had passed since he met him, but Geom Mugeuk had influenced him more than anyone he had ever seen.

To the point that Myeongsin felt he ought to rewrite the report on him himself. Of course, there would be no chance for that now.

Another reason was that if he remained in the tavern, the Killing King would remain as well.

Then the fight would become that much more difficult. Better for him to stall the Killing King while hoping Geom Mugeuk would finish them off and come out. It was a choice that could not be made without faith in Geom Mugeuk.

And the final reason was that he wanted to kill the Killing King with his own hands.

Even knowing it would not be easy, he had a burning desire to bring this grudge to an end himself.

It was right then.

Swish!

Myeongsin dodged a hidden weapon that flew at him from behind. It was not meant to kill him, but to make him look that way.

Before he knew it, a man was standing on the roof of the building to the side. It was Hyeolla, the one who had mocked Myeongsin earlier and had his tongue cut out by the Killing King.

Hyeolla pleaded with the Killing King through his expression and gaze. Leave that Myeongsin to me.

The hatred in his eyes showed that he believed his tongue had been cut out because of Myeongsin.

The Killing King looked back and forth between Myeongsin and Hyeolla once, then nodded his permission.

By nature, he would normally have provoked Myeongsin with every manner of ridicule, but now he could not say a single word.

Hyeolla swung his sword and charged at Myeongsin.

Myeongsin also did not evade; he swung his dagger and met him head-on.

Assassins were those who killed their targets after preparation and planning. Thus, when the opponent’s guard reached its lowest point, they were supposed to simply stab them effortlessly and leave. That was why there were things they must never do.

An assassin must not use footwork.

An assassin must not use martial arts.

Because having to use them meant the preparations for the assassination had been insufficient.

One used footwork and martial arts only when an assassination failed.

That was why, now that they were openly fighting, it was less a battle as assassins and more a battle as martial artists.

It was truly a fight of fundamentals. A contest of martial arts, hidden weapon techniques, and lightness skills.

Shiiing!

The incoming sword brushed past Myeongsin’s face.

Even as the blade grazed his face and he felt its cold chill, Myeongsin charged forward, thrusting his dagger.

Hyeolla twisted his body to avoid the dagger, and the sword he thrust out twisted along with him.

The sword that had passed beside Myeongsin’s face changed direction and tried to cut into it, but Myeongsin had already lowered his body and was stabbing toward Hyeolla’s stomach.

Clang, clang, clang!

Hyeolla’s sword changed direction again and blocked the attack.

As befitted assassins representing Myeongbu, their battle was extraordinary.

Their movements were like the wind, and when they attacked, they were as fierce as wild beasts.

Short, clipped sounds of metal rang out continuously.

But gradually, the sword began to be pushed back by the dagger.

He had been more successful as an assassin lately, so he had been confident he could defeat Myeongsin.

Unfortunately for him, this fight was not a battle as assassins, but as martial artists, and he was being outmatched in fundamentals.

‘Damn it! I’ll never lose to you!’

But Hyeolla was being pushed back more and more, and his hands and feet began to grow disordered.

Thud!

Blood spurted from Hyeolla’s shoulder.

Hyeolla had considered himself the true second-in-command.

But between the two of them, there was a gap in skill greater than his severed tongue.

Hyeolla tried to unleash his trump card, but Myeongsin did not miss the fleeting opening that appeared when one used a major technique.

Thud! Thud! Thud!

Myeongsin’s dagger swiftly stabbed Hyeolla’s chest again and again.

“Kueeek.”

Hyeolla vomited a gush of blood.

His gaze turned toward the Killing King. His eyes held resentment, asking why he was not helping him, but even if the Killing King wanted to help, he could not.

From the time he had been fighting Myeongsin, he had sensed Hwi’s presence. The moment he showed an opening, Hwi would not miss it and would launch a fatal attack.

The Demonic Cult Leader’s guard.

The reason he had wanted to kill Myeongsin earlier but could not was also because of Hwi.

If I rashly try to kill him, I’ll die too.

Because his opponent was undoubtedly an expert capable of turning this instinctive sense of danger into reality.

It was then.

Papapapapapak!

Holes were riddled through the wall of the tavern across the way. He could tell that the Gwisantan had exploded.

A thick smell of blood wafted from the holes. He could tell that a fierce battle was continuing inside as well.

Myeongsin caught his breath before Hyeolla’s corpse. He knew too. He knew why the Killing King had not intervened in his fight with Hyeolla. He could feel Hwi protecting him.

That was why, in this moment, he could not help but think of Geom Mugeuk.

‘You really are something!’

It had not merely been words when he said they should be friends, nor merely words when he said he would spare him.

The Killing King looked silently at the tavern, then abruptly spoke.

“The target of your revenge should be the one who commissioned the job, not me.”

“That’s true.”

Myeongsin readily admitted it.

“Then why?”

Myeongsin knew what words had been omitted after the Killing King’s question.

Why did you betray me?

Myeongsin knew that the Killing King was genuinely wondering that.

That was why he did not get angry. Because that was the kind of person the Killing King was.

I spared him and even raised him as an assassin, so why is he not grateful and instead betrays me?

He was the sort of person who would have such a question. He could not understand the many complicated yet broad emotions of human beings.

“Then why did you kill your father?”

Killing intent shot from the Killing King’s transparent eyes.

But Myeongsin did not care at all. Rather, this was good. In a battle between experts, the angrier the opponent became, the more advantageous it was.

“That makes you feel bad, doesn’t it? Just as you don’t think you betrayed your father, I’m the same.”

The Killing King stared at him without a word.

Myeongsin wondered if the reason he had been captivated by the Killing King was because of those transparent eyes.

Rather than a feeling of evil, they gave off a feeling of purity.

Without realizing it, he had perceived him that way. Sometimes the impression given by one’s appearance preceded everything else.

The Killing King slowly walked toward him.

From his gaze, Myeongsin read the will to kill him no matter what.

Myeongsin shook Hyeolla’s blood from his dagger and walked forward to meet him.

If he wanted to stall for time, he could have dragged it out by leading him into various conversations, but he did not. This revenge did not belong to Geom Mugeuk; it was his own.

‘I’ll fight with everything I have.’

The two clashed again.

Clang, clang, clang, clang, clang, clang!

Predicted trajectories and unexpected trajectories intertwined.

Attacks continued, some to be blocked by skill, others by instinct.

He was being pushed back in internal energy, and his arm hurt as if it would be torn off, but Myeongsin gritted his teeth and fought.

If there was a terror to being the foremost, then there was also a terror to being the second foremost, but this clear gap in skill was not something that could be overcome by will.

Slash!

Blood spurted from Myeongsin’s arm.

Shiiing!

And as the following attack was about to pierce Myeongsin’s heart.

Clang!

A sword knocked away the Killing King’s dagger.

The one who struck his dagger aside was Hwi, who had undone his concealment and appeared.

This moment, when the Killing King was about to stab Myeongsin, had been a chance to attack him directly.

But if he did that, it was certain Myeongsin would die, so he had chosen to protect him instead.

Myeongsin quickly pressed the acupoints around his wound to stop the bleeding and said,

“I didn’t want to owe you anything. Thank you.”

But Hwi gave no answer. He was only protecting Myeongsin because it was the Young Cult Leader’s order; he had no personal feelings toward this assassin.

Myeongsin tried to rise again and fight.

Then a voice came from behind.

“Why did you prepare so much? I nearly died, you know!”

Geom Mugeuk was walking out of the tavern.

The Killing King could not hide his astonishment.

He had not expected him to clear out the tavern and emerge this quickly. On top of that, Geom Mugeuk was perfectly fine, without so much as a scratch.

In truth, Myeongsin was just as surprised. Struggling to hide his astonishment, Myeongsin asked,

“How were the things prepared in there? Most of them were made by me.”

“If I’d known you were the one who made them, I wouldn’t have asked Uncle Hwi to spare you.”

Myeongsin could feel it. Geom Mugeuk was not rebuking him, but praising the excellence of the hidden weapons he had made.

Just how many people in this martial world could joke with such ease in a situation like this? After escaping that tavern of death, one might have been expected to reveal even a word of pride, even as a joke. Yet instead, he was lifting up his opponent. He was showing what true self-respect was.

‘I’ve lost.’

Myeongsin felt respect for Geom Mugeuk. Respect for a young man barely out of youth? But he was not embarrassed at all. After all, Geom Mugeuk was also the benefactor who had saved his life.

“Are you badly hurt?”

At Geom Mugeuk’s question, Myeongsin knew the moment had come to hand this fight over to them.

He did not stubbornly insist on fighting to the end.

It was deeply regrettable, but he knew it was impossible to kill the Killing King with his own skills. Pointless stubbornness would only burden Hwi and Geom Mugeuk. The Killing King was far stronger than he had thought.

Myeongsin leapt down below.

“It’s hard fighting with a body that’s only been swinging a hammer. I’ll rest for a while, so you deal with him.”

At that, Geom Mugeuk lightly launched himself into the air and landed in the spot where Myeongsin had been standing.

“Thank you, Uncle.”

It was gratitude toward Hwi, who had safely carried out his request to protect Myeongsin.

When Geom Mugeuk stood behind him, Hwi’s expression changed. Once the one he had to protect became Geom Mugeuk, he became a completely different person.

Because the ones who changed completely depending on who stood behind them were precisely bodyguard martial artists.

Looking at Hwi’s back, Geom Mugeuk felt it.

Hwi desired the fight against the Killing King.

This battle was, in the truest sense, an inevitability created by two opposing destinies.

‘What if Uncle Hwi has to fight?’

There was something he could do to help him. Something he was as good at as swordsmanship.

Geom Mugeuk asked Myeongsin, who stood beneath the roof.

“Do you know why your leader joined hands with them?”

“I do not. Do you?”

“They must have offered him something. That they would make him king of all assassins under heaven. That they would make him ruler of the night.”

Myeongsin thought that perhaps it might have been so. He truly was a man mad for killing. Perhaps only such a condition could have moved him.

“It sounds plausible enough, but in the end, doesn’t that just mean they intend to use him as a blade?”

Seeing that clear gaze stir, Geom Mugeuk began provoking the Assassin King in earnest.

“What ruler exists under someone else’s command? A ruler of the night bowing his head to the ruler of the day? How utterly pitiful. Ruler, you say! He is nothing more than a mere tool.”

Geom Mugeuk continued spitting out words meant to provoke the Assassin King. Before the fight began, he intended to shake his heart.

“He only knew how to imitate another person’s life. As for what kind of life he himself ought to live…”

It was at that very moment.

Kaaang!

Two lines of sword light appeared and vanished at the same time.

One sword line sought to bisect Geom Mugeuk’s body, while the other was the sword line that blocked the incoming attack.

Hwi had stopped the Assassin King’s strike. It had not been an attack meant to kill Geom Mugeuk, but a warning strike that said, Shut your mouth!

Of course, if that were enough to make him shut his mouth, he would not be Geom Mugeuk.

As though certain Hwi would block any attack, Geom Mugeuk continued provoking the Assassin King with a relaxed expression. This time, he used Myeongsin to do it.

“Do you know why that man is trying to break your principles?”

This was something Myeongsin was purely curious about as well.

“Why?”

“Because he is jealous. He, the number one, commits murder without any principles at all, while you, the number two, have principles and…”

Kwa-kwa-kwa-kwa-kwang!

Soul-Slaying Thunder burst from the Assassin King’s sleeve. It was far more powerful than that of the assassins they had faced earlier at the tavern.

But an even more astonishing sight unfolded.

Hwi’s sword spun like a pinwheel, knocking away every hidden weapon that flew in.

Myeongsin was startled as he watched. Deflecting the hidden weapons of Soul-Slaying Thunder from that distance was something he had never even imagined possible.

‘The Cult Leader’s guard is far stronger than I thought.’

Hwi and the Assassin King stared at each other.

No other reason was needed for this fight. They were the kind of beings each hated most.

A battle of natural opposites representing the murim.

Hwi, who had always been taciturn, spoke his mind today.

“Assassins look down on guards. They think we only know how to stubbornly protect. So today, I intend to show him.”

That guards and assassins were, in the end, two sides of the same coin.

“How well someone who knows how to protect can kill, once he sets his mind to it.”

Absolute Regression, Chapter 490

Chapter 490: Please Don't Be Lonely.
------------------------------------
“Show me, Mister.”

If Hwi had been agitated, he would have been stopped, but he was calm as ever. This fight was neither impromptu nor emotional.

It was a solemn warning from a loyal man who had lived his entire life as a bodyguard to an assassin, and an inevitable clash born of two opposing destinies.

“Please show them that assassins have only hell to go to, Mister.”

The moment Geom Mugeuk’s words ended, Hwi leaped forward without hesitation.

Hwi rushed toward his enemy.

It was a new side of him. Until now, he had only ever been seen blocking his opponent’s attacks.

Salwang likewise met him head-on, swinging his dagger without dodging. His eyes grew even more transparent, and within that transparency, an unknowable emotion flickered.

Though it was impossible to tell what that emotion was, at least one thing could be felt with certainty.

He was acknowledging his opponent.

Clang!

The clear metallic sound of sword and dagger clashing signaled the start of the fight.

Both were martial artists unknown to the world.

They were people who moved in secret to accomplish their goals. This fight was between two such individuals.

Hwi’s swordsmanship was a supreme martial art passed down to the bodyguard commanders of the Cheonma.

Maryeong Suho Geombeop.

Maryeong Suho Geombeop was based on swift sword techniques. By nature, a bodyguard had to possess quick reactions, for the first move a bodyguard ever faced was always a surprise attack.

On the other hand, the martial art Salwang had mastered was the secret unique art of Saryeongje, the Emperor of Death Spirits, who had been the king of assassins three hundred years ago.

Amsal Bigyeoksul.

He had mastered a martial art known as a legend in the assassin world.

Hwi fiercely pressed Salwang.

Geom Mugeuk had never known until today that Hwi, who always showed only a peaceful demeanor, possessed such a rough side. Today, Hwi was like a reckless fighter who knew no retreat.

Sword and dagger clashed in midair, sending sparks flying.

Amsal Bigyeoksul was also a martial art based on speed.

Because of this, their clash was so fast they could barely be seen. In these exchanges, a moment’s carelessness led straight to death.

Swoosh.

The sword line created by Hwi drew a semicircle, attempting to cut Salwang down.

Clang!

Having blocked the attack, Salwang thrust his dagger in rapid succession. The three moves unleashed in an instant targeted his opponent’s vital points.

Hwi, having blocked with his sword, leaped into the air and stabbed down at Salwang continuously from above.

Salwang slid backward, avoiding the strikes. He too displayed incredible footwork befitting the martial art he wielded.

Salwang relentlessly targeted Hwi’s life.

Hwi sought to slice through Salwang’s torso.

Sword and dagger, filled with their wielders’ killing intent, met and produced a sound like a musical instrument playing a rapid melody.

Chaeng! Chaeng-chaeng! Chae-chae-chae-chae-chaeng!

Each time, sparks flew. Their exchange was so fast that sparks burst from all directions like fireworks.

A grand spectacle of swiftly unfolding defensive sword techniques and assassination arts.

They grazed past each other, leaped over each other, advanced and retreated, clashed and separated. Their bodies moved as fast as their sword and dagger. Blades scraped each other’s skin, spilling blood.

To the point that one wondered how he had lived quietly with such martial arts, Hwi was displaying a magnificent and splendid technique.

Pressed by Hwi’s fierce momentum, Salwang was being pushed back.

Hwi’s martial art, which had never attacked first in its entire existence, now seemed to be saying:

*“We simply did not kill.”*

A flustered expression flashed across Salwang’s face. He had known his opponent was strong, but he could not have known he was this strong.

Watching that scene, Myeongsin felt anew that he had survived against Salwang earlier entirely because of Hwi.

Because Hwi was such a strong person.

Sensing that, Salwang had not acted rashly.

But Salwang was no ordinary opponent either.

He was blocking all of Hwi’s incredible attacks.

It was truly a battle of evenly matched skill.

Though he wished and believed Hwi would win, Geom Mugeuk was inwardly tense.

“Mister!”

If the enemy had not been Salwang, he would not have been this nervous.

Shouldn’t he leave it to Hwi until the end, since it was Hwi’s fight?

Absolutely not. If Hwi was in even the slightest danger, he would intervene immediately.

Even if Hwi resented him later, he would jump in without a moment’s hesitation.

Hwi and Salwang.

From the beginning, Hwi’s life and Salwang’s life were not comparable. Even a thousand Salwangs—no, all the assassins in the Central Plains—could not be traded for Hwi.

Suddenly, his father’s words came to mind.

“If you put Hwi forward, you take responsibility.”

At this moment, he truly was putting Hwi forward.

“I will take responsibility, Father.”

It was right then.

Shhhk!

Someone launched a surprise attack from behind Geom Mugeuk.

Chaeng!

Turning around and swinging his sword to block the ambush, Geom Mugeuk gestured for his opponent to be quiet.

“Wait! It’s an important moment right now.”

Geom Mugeuk turned his head back toward Hwi and Salwang.

Indeed, it was a critical moment in the fight between Hwi and Salwang.

Something burst from Salwang’s hand.

Papapak!

A finger-thick small cylinder concealed in his left hand fired steel needles.

Chaeng-chaeng-chaeng!

Blocking the flying hidden weapons with his sword, Hwi soared upward. He had predicted this ambush would connect to the next attack, so while leaping up, he attacked first. He had replaced defense with offense.

Papapapak!

Even against the unexpected counterattack, Salwang avoided the strikes without panicking.

Hwi had been prepared for Salwang’s surprise attacks.

He was fighting while thinking it was rather strange for an assassin to engage in such a frontal confrontation.

Only after Hwi had safely overcome the surprise attack did Geom Mugeuk look at the assassin behind him.

Geom Mugeuk’s sword was already lodged in the assassin’s heart. There was no way the assassin would have stayed still just because Geom Mugeuk had shushed him.

Right when he was about to attack again, Geom Mugeuk had killed him by piercing his heart without even looking back.

Geom Mugeuk turned his head and looked at the street. People were poking their heads out everywhere, watching the fight. Many of them were likely assassins waiting for an opportunity.

Geom Mugeuk spoke to Myeongsin.

“Come, let’s go too!”

It was a call to eliminate all the assassins. Since there was no telling how they might interfere in the two’s fight, it was better to eliminate them as soon as possible.

Of course, even in the midst of this, Geom Mugeuk’s attention was on Hwi’s fight.

Outwardly, he seemed focused solely on this side, yet he was attentive to both—a display of movement possible only for a true master who had reached the pinnacle.

“Very well.”

Myeongsin couldn’t quite believe it. The fact that he was on the same side as the Young Cult Leader of the Magyo and facing Salwang. It was a life he couldn’t have imagined just a few days ago.

*Yes, I must carry out the commission I accepted.*

The two strode down the street.

There were those who were clearly assassins pretending to be onlookers.

But that was a foolish choice that underestimated Myeongsin’s memory.

Shhhk-shhhk-shhhk-shhhk!

He dodged the throwing knives Myeongsin sent flying in succession, but—

Puuk!

—he couldn’t dodge Geom Mugeuk’s sword that rushed in right after.

“Root them all out!”

Myeongsin nodded. It was a gesture signaling his severance from Myeongbu, and further, from all assassin organizations.

Then, something poured down from a building on this block.

Swoooosh.

What spilled onto the street were star-shaped hidden weapons.

Sharp blades protruded from where they fell to the ground.

They were not hidden weapons meant to kill by hitting the opponent, but rather meant to be scattered on the ground to impede movement. Stepping on those sharp points would result in immediate poisoning.

But these hidden weapons were not that kind.

“Be careful when you walk!”

At Geom Mugeuk’s warning, Myeongsin shouted.

“Get down now!”

The moment Myeongsin’s shout ended, the hidden weapons on the ground exploded and launched upward.

Pak-pak-pak-pak-pak-pak-pak-pak!

Blades shot out in all directions as they exploded.

Usually, upon seeing such hidden weapons, one would be careful not to step on them; this attack targeted the fact that one would never expect them to launch like this.

Geom Mugeuk was not someone who couldn’t dodge, especially with a warning.

“Don’t tell me you made this too?”

“I’m afraid so.”

“Use this technique for me later too!”

“If we survive here.”

At that, Geom Mugeuk said as he leaped.

“We will definitely survive!”

Geom Mugeuk flew up to the building. It was the very place from which the hidden weapons had just been scattered.

Crash.

Smashing through the window, Geom Mugeuk flew straight into the room.

Shhhk!

The dagger of an assassin waiting beside the window grazed past Geom Mugeuk’s face.

It must have been a precious moment for the assassin, but in that fleeting instant of crashing through the window, Geom Mugeuk had already sensed his presence.

The moment he killed him, a hidden weapon flew from beneath the bed, targeting Geom Mugeuk’s ankle. This too was an ambush that would not have been easy to avoid.

Thud! Thud!

But Geom Mugeuk was already standing on the bed, driving his sword into it.

The attacks designed for this room were not limited to that.

A wardrobe burst open, and the assassin’s attack continued.

Shhhk-shhhk-shhhk-shhhk-shhhk-shhhk!

In his hand was a very small crossbow. It was a crossbow capable of continuous fire, and its power was truly formidable.

Puk! Puk! Puk! Puk!

Holes were riddled in the wall where Geom Mugeuk had been standing.

But at the same time, the assassin collapsed backward. A throwing knife was lodged in his forehead. It was the knife Myeongsin had given him as a gift.

When he looked out the window again, two assassins were attacking Myeongsin.

They had targeted the gap while Geom Mugeuk briefly flew up to the building.

They were no ordinary assassins.

They were special-grade assassins hidden among the first-class assassins.

They were the Heukyeong Ssangsal, who had been disguised as the owner and clerk of the fabric store.

They attacked Myeongsin from left and right with swords. Whether owner or clerk, the disguise was meaningless since their opponent was Myeongsin. Their disguise would only prove effective if they eliminated Myeongsin as quickly as possible.

A bloody battle against two special-grade assassins.

Though Myeongsin was injured in the arm, he was showing that he had not won this position in Myeongbu by a coin toss.

The sight of Hwi and Salwang fighting on the roof was no longer visible. The sounds of their battle came from beyond the building.

Geom Mugeuk’s figure cut through the air. He was not headed toward where Hwi was fighting, nor where Myeongsin was fighting. He had spotted someone aiming at Myeongsin from a window of another building.

He was Eopwa, a special-grade assassin of Salcheon.

As Geom Mugeuk rushed toward him, the hidden weapon aimed at Myeongsin was fired at Geom Mugeuk instead.

Chararararang!

The hidden weapon that flew in with a sharp slicing sound was a secret hidden weapon used by the assassin organization Salcheon.

Mabi Yeonhwan.

An absolute hidden weapon with the highest kill rate.

The weapon possessed terrifying power that could rip apart a martial artist’s flesh and bone with just a graze. But the true nature of this hidden weapon lay elsewhere.

Swoosh!

The moment Geom Mugeuk cut the flying weapon with his sword.

Pung!

Paralysis powder burst from the severed weapon. It was not poison, but a drug that rendered the muscles unusable for a moment.

Geom Mugeuk, who had been flying through the air, fell downward.

Instead, Eopwa leaped from the window. This brief moment of paralysis was exactly what he had aimed for.

Sswaekaek!

In that instant when Eopwa tried to split the paralyzed Geom Mugeuk’s head in two with his falling momentum.

Shhhk! Swoosh!

Geom Mugeuk’s Heukmageom was drawn in an instant and severed his neck.
