Eopwa died with his eyes wide and an expression of incomprehension.

It had never failed until now?

But the paralysis powder hadn’t worked on Geom Mugeuk. Since Eopwa had never experienced such a case, the special-grade assassin fell in a single move.

Geom Mugeuk turned and threw a knife.

Shhhk-shhhk-shhhk!

The three throwing knives flew toward one of the Heukyeong Ssangsal.

The opponent turned and deflected the knives, but it was enough to break the deadlock of the fight.

Puuk!

Not missing that opportunity, Myeongsin continuously stabbed the opponent’s arm and thigh. With one injured, the tightly balanced fight tilted.

“So make me plenty of gifts from now on! They come back to you like this when you need them, don’t they?”

A joke even in the midst of this?

Myeongsin wanted to retort, but he didn’t have the leisure.

Leaving him to fight the remaining Heukyeong Ssangsal after taking one down, Geom Mugeuk’s figure landed on the roof of the building across the street.

Hwi and Salwang were fighting in the vacant lot behind.

Sword and dagger clashed to the point they could not even be seen.

Geom Mugeuk did not look at their weapons, but at their eyes.

‘I can win.’

Hwi’s skill was solid. Like a massive fortress wall that would not collapse before any wind. The Assassin King had probably felt that even more keenly.

The Assassin King launched his hidden weapons all at once to create distance.

At that very moment!

The Assassin King’s dagger shone white.

‘Dangerous!’

He could tell the man was about to use a major technique.

Just as Geom Mugeuk was about to step in, he saw Hwi’s face. His calm expression showed not the slightest tension.

From the tip of the Assassin King’s dagger, a silver radiance burst forth.

The next instant!

Swaaaaaaaaaang!

Dozens of silver streams of qi poured toward Hwi.

Flying Sword Light Rain (飛劍光雨).

One of the secret techniques of the Assassination Flying Strike Art.

Against the silver qi raining down like rain—no, like light—Hwi’s sword began to embroider the air.

Chachachachachachachak!

Geom Mugeuk saw them. The sword lines swiftly being drawn before Hwi. If one were to watch a spider weaving its web at tremendous speed, would it look like that? Sword lines formed in an instant.

A consummate technique of the Demon Spirit Guardian Sword Art, imbued with the will to block any attack without fail.

Guardian Sword Wall (守護劍壁).

It was the first time Geom Mugeuk had seen it. The sword wall Hwi had created.

Bang! Kwaang! Bangbangbang! Bangbang!

The qi and the sword wall collided. The silver radiance shattered into fragments and scattered as clusters of light. The Assassin King’s attack had been blocked.

Because he had never imagined that Flying Sword Light Rain would be blocked, the Assassin King was genuinely shocked.

“The Cult Leader’s guard.”

And he was sincerely impressed.

“You surpass the rumors.”

Hwi stood without a word, his sword lowered.

It was then.

Whishwhishwhishwhishwhishwhishwhishwhish!

A dozen assassins dropped down on either side of the Assassin King. One glance at their movements was enough to know their skill was far from ordinary.

As if his only purpose was to kill his opponent, the Assassin King charged toward Hwi together with the assassins.

Even facing the Assassin King alone had been a fight on a razor’s edge.

But Hwi neither cowered nor dodged. The more the situation was like this, the calmer a guard warrior had to be.

Hwi thought of the Cult Leader standing behind him. He thought of the Young Cult Leader standing behind him. Because that was the kind of man he was—one who could draw out twice, three times his strength that way.

“Assassins belong in hell!”

He repeated the words Geom Mugeuk had spoken at the start of the battle. Coming from Hwi’s mouth, they carried a different weight.

Hwi tightened his grip on his sword and charged forward with force. He did not retreat. Behind him stood the Cult Leader, the Young Cult Leader, and the pride of being the one responsible for guarding the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.

It was then that Geom Mugeuk’s voice came from behind.

“Don’t feel lonely, ajeossi! We have allies here!”

The next instant.

Ssssssseut.

The assassins charging toward Hwi were horrified.

Something had unfurled wide on either side of Hwi, who had been charging straight at them.

What made their eyes bulge wide was—

They were terrifying-looking evil spirits.

Geom Mugeuk had unleashed the Great Annihilation Form of the Nine-Flame Demonic Art.

Excluding only the evil spirit at Hwi’s position, the rest split apart and spread out like wings on either side of him.

Kwakwakwakwakwakwakwakwa!

The evil spirits charged together with Hwi.

Absolute Regression, Chapter 491

Chapter 491: Hwi's Stealth Is Not Martial Arts.
-----------------------------------------------
The instant the Assassin King saw the evil spirits, he knew.

‘It is the Heavenly Demon’s martial art!’

For there could be only one martial art that summoned such terrifying evil spirits.

Seeing with his own eyes the Heavenly Demon’s martial art, of which he had only ever heard rumors, the pressure it exuded was anything but ordinary. Merely looking at it was enough to make one’s fighting spirit wither.

If even the Assassin King felt so, what of the assassins charging alongside him?

Their bodies were still moving, but they had already lost the will to fight. Some of them slowed, while others came to a halt where they stood.

They were seasoned assassins who had been through every sort of hardship, yet the moment they saw the evil spirits of the Guhwa Demonic Art for the first time, their hearts froze solid.

Kwa-kwa-kwa-kwa-kwa-kwa-kwa-kwa-kwak!

The assassins resisted in their own ways.

Some thrust out daggers, while others unleashed hidden weapons. Some raised protective astral qi, and others sent sword qi flying.

But none of it was any use.

Neither attacks nor protective astral qi worked. The swords they thrust out could not even scratch the evil spirits.

The assassins who leapt up to evade them were dragged back down by invisible demonic qi. With their level of skill, it was a move they could not avoid by any means.

Thud-thud-thud-thud-thud-thud-thud-thud-thud!

Shattered, split apart, crushed. The evil spirits swept through, destroying everything in their path.

The only one left was the Assassin King, who had been charging straight toward Hwi.

The Assassin King’s heart pounded as if it would burst. It was the first time since the battle had begun that he had shown tension.

What if an evil spirit had also been in Hwi’s position and had come crashing toward him? Would he truly have been able to block it?

Chaaang!

After one clash, Hwi and the Assassin King retreated.

The two looked around.

The assassins had been reduced to unrecognizable blood and gore.

Hwi raised his sword toward that blood and paid his respects with solemn courtesy. It was not courtesy toward the dead.

It was courtesy toward the Guhwa Demonic Art.

Hwi was deeply moved. Whenever the Heavenly Demon trained in martial arts, he had always left or turned his back, never once watching. On the rare occasions he happened to see the Guhwa Demonic Art being unleashed, he always paid his respects.

Yet now, he had charged alongside that very Guhwa Demonic Art. To his great honor, that Guhwa Demonic Art had saved his life.

Could there be any moment more moving than this when it came to martial arts? He could say with certainty that there was not.

The Assassin King, meanwhile, looked around with eyes that had already sunk into calm. He felt no regret over the deaths of his subordinates. Only a cold wind blew through his empty heart.

Hwi turned to look at Geom Mugeuk.

In this situation, what need was there for words? At Hwi’s gaze, which conveyed his gratitude, Geom Mugeuk gave a single nod.

‘I am the one who is grateful, sir.’

For Geom Mugeuk had more gratitude and respect than anyone for Hwi, who always protected his father.

After exchanging that greeting with Hwi, Geom Mugeuk provoked the Assassin King again.

“I understand. Assassins are cowards by nature, aren’t they?”

It was a taunt mocking him for joining forces with the other assassins. Geom Mugeuk tried to touch his emotions, but it did not work on the Assassin King.

Then Myeongsin spoke.

“He is a man without emotion.”

At those words, the Assassin King looked at Myeongsin. At least toward Myeongsin, there was some lingering sediment of emotion in his eyes.

Geom Mugeuk stepped between those gazes.

“A person ought to speak. If you dislike something, say you dislike it. If you hate someone, say you hate them. How am I supposed to know what is in your heart if you only stare like that?”

At that, the Assassin King opened his mouth.

“What is there for us to say to each other?”

“I have many questions for you.”

The Assassin King waited for Geom Mugeuk to speak.

“What did you receive as my commission fee? Were you truly promised the position of Ruler of the Night?”

Geom Mugeuk had teased the Assassin King with that before, but he thought there might perhaps be another reason.

The Assassin King, however, did not answer.

“It is principle not to speak of commissions.”

Geom Mugeuk could sense it. In the Assassin King’s mind, there was only the thought that he had to complete this killing mission successfully, no matter what.

When the Assassin King sent a signal, the assassins revealed themselves all at once.

They walked out from alleys and appeared atop rooftops. The assassins hidden here and there numbered in the dozens.

“So many gathered just to kill me.”

All of them were skilled and successful assassins. In particular, mixed in among them were truly terrifying assassins classified as top-grade. They were concealing their identities as much as possible, but on this side, there was Myeongsin.

—Of the two on the left rooftop, the one on the right. The entrance to the alley on the right. The bearded one in the center…

He informed Geom Mugeuk by sound transmission of the top-grade assassins he knew.

—Are you worried I might die?

—Only if you live will my betrayal have any meaning.

Now that he knew Myeongbu had annihilated his family, there was no need to call it betrayal, but because it was a place where he had stayed for so long, the words slipped out without him realizing it.

—Do not think of it as betrayal. It is work for them, and it is work for you too, is it not?

—Work?

—You commissioned it yourself, didn’t you? You and they are both simply doing your jobs.

A faint smile formed on Myeongsin’s lips.

Even in this situation, Geom Mugeuk was looking after his heart. Those words Geom Mugeuk had thrown out were certainly a comfort.

Geom Mugeuk looked at the Assassin King and the assassins, then drew his sword.

“You are right. What need is there for words between us?”

Then Hwi took one step forward toward the Assassin King.

Hwi’s back seemed to say:

Leave the Assassin King to me until the very end. This fight is mine.

‘I understand, sir. But be careful!’

The Assassin King looked at Hwi with transparent eyes.

Just as with Hwi, at this moment, only Hwi was reflected in the Assassin King’s eyes.

The Assassin King knew.

The greatest obstacle in this commission was not Geom Mugeuk, who had learned the Guhwa Demonic Art, but this man, Hwi.

For assassins, it was not a problem if an opponent’s martial arts were strong.

No matter how powerful someone’s martial arts were, assassins were those who somehow found a weakness and killed them.

But that did not work on this guard of the Demonic Cult Leader.

Because he was the one who compensated for those weaknesses.

As if by unspoken agreement, Hwi and the Assassin King charged at each other and clashed.

With sharp metallic sounds, countless sword-lines came into being.

They were no ordinary lines. Lines containing a lifetime of training were drawn and drawn again toward the opponent’s life.

When the two began their fight, Geom Mugeuk was also charging toward the assassins.

If the ordinary assassins and top-grade assassins were dogs and wolves, then Geom Mugeuk was a massive tiger.

Among the assassins were those who had considered themselves reapers who governed life and death.

And now was the moment they met the true reaper.

Geom Mugeuk charged at his opponent and used Dark Shadow Step, vanishing from sight in an instant.

When the startled assassin turned his head to the left to find Geom Mugeuk’s position, what he saw was not the left, but the sky. His head lolled. Geom Mugeuk’s sword had already cut deep into his neck.

At that moment, a sword flew toward Geom Mugeuk.

It belonged to the fastest swordsman among Myeongbu’s assassins.

‘Got him!’

His blade had already reached Geom Mugeuk’s neck.

Puuuk!

But before that, Geom Mugeuk’s sword split his heart.

‘Why?’

Even as he died, he could not understand. His opponent had reacted far later, so why?

But for the assassins, was that the only incomprehensible thing?

Geom Mugeuk stabbed another assassin through the heart and then the neck in succession, using him as a shield as he pressed forward.

The assassin behind him had his throat pierced by a sword that flew out from behind the corpse.

Shwik! Shwik!

Swords flew in from left and right. The incoming blades passed by Geom Mugeuk’s body by a hair’s breadth.

Inward sighs rose.

‘So close.’

It was so close that one might think at first glance that there was hardly any difference in skill, but in fact, this was a situation that could only be staged because the difference in skill was overwhelming.

To dodge by a hair’s breadth meant that he was fighting with absolute efficiency.

Shiiiiii.

Slice! Slice!

Geom Mugeuk, gliding forward with Flash Step, swept his sword through the assassins standing on both sides. In the very instant of a flash, the assassins collapsed.

Even more astonishing was the fact that Geom Mugeuk was paying attention to Hwi’s fight while fighting like this.

From the fleeting glimpses of Hwi’s and the Assassin King’s movements, from the sounds of sword and dagger colliding, he was grasping how their battle was unfolding.

On top of that, Geom Mugeuk was even keeping an eye on Myeongsin.

Myeongsin was fighting well. He used every secret technique he possessed to face the assassins.

He also made use of the devices he had created. The hidden weapon he threw exploded, killing the assassins around it. He understood the minds of assassins better than anyone.

Of course, the assassins did not simply let themselves be taken down. They used every kind of hidden weapon. Blades sprang from their shoes, and hidden weapons coated in deadly poison flew in all directions.

In the midst of that melee, Myeongsin sensed an attack flying at him from behind and fell into despair.

‘Too late!’

It was an attack from a top-grade assassin brought in from outside. Since he knew nothing about him, he had not expected him to strike from behind so quickly.

‘I’ll give up my shoulder.’

Just as Myeongsin twisted his body to at least avoid instant death—

Shiiiiing!

Something passed by his face with a tremendous gust of wind.

Puuuk!

The thing that pierced through the neck of the assassin rushing in from behind was Geom Mugeuk’s Black Demon Sword.

Myeongsin had no leisure to express his gratitude. Assassins swarmed toward both of them like bees.

In particular, when the sword disappeared from Geom Mugeuk’s hand, they thought this was their chance.

But there was also an opportunity for Geom Mugeuk. An opportunity to try out a martial art in real combat.

Avoiding an incoming sword, Geom Mugeuk thrust out his fist.

Thud!

The assassin whose ribs were shattered flew far away and rolled across the ground.

Kwa-rururung!

Thunder roared from his fist. It was the Fist Demon’s Thunderclap Asura Fist.

Thud! Thud! Thud!

He lightly avoided the incoming swords and drove out his fists.

The assassins’ jaws shattered, and their internal organs burst apart. What awaited them was a death even more painful than being killed by a sword.

Overwhelmed by that momentum, the assassins stopped attacking for a moment.

Geom Mugeuk extended his hand toward one side.

Whiiiik.

Then the Black Demon Sword embedded in a corpse was pulled free and rose into the air.

Fear flooded the hearts of several assassins watching the sight.

They had lived believing death always belonged to their opponents.

But now, it felt as though death had risen into the air and was watching them. That death crossed the sky and entered Geom Mugeuk’s hand.

Of course, that did not mean the assassins simply retreated. They fought desperately to kill Geom Mugeuk and Myeongsin.

Sword qi poured down on Geom Mugeuk. A net of steel spread out, and hidden weapons coated in poison flew. They poured out every technique and every assassination skill they had learned.

But it did not work.

Where on earth was there a place to dodge? There was space to dodge.

How could one possibly counterattack in that situation? Geom Mugeuk did.

He found a direction that should never have existed, matched the angle, and controlled the speed.

Puaaak!

And so even the last assassin collapsed, spraying blood.

Geom Mugeuk was already drenched in blood, so he did not avoid the blood that rained down.

As always, Geom Mugeuk did not spare the dead even a single glance.

Myeongsin watched him in silence.

This was not the Geom Mugeuk he had seen until now. The man before him did not feel like the talkative, joke-loving Young Cult Leader, but like a lonely war god who had survived alone on a battlefield.

‘This is the Young Cult Leader’s true form.’

Then Myeongsin’s eyes met Geom Mugeuk’s.

The two eyes of Geom Mugeuk, which had so perfectly suited the word loneliness, filled with a bright smile.

It was the moment he returned once more to the bright Geom Mugeuk.

“You survived.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s words, Myeongsin nodded and said,

“I still have to finish the commission.”

Their gazes intertwined in midair.

But now was not the time to share their feelings.

The two looked toward where Hwi and the Assassin King were fighting.

Their battle, too, was nearing its end.

And that end was utterly still.

Neither the Assassin King nor Hwi could be seen. They were fighting while concealed.

An assassin’s concealment and a guard warrior’s concealment.

It was the final choice of two men who took pride in their stealth.

In empty space, only the blades of a sword and a dagger flashed.

Clang! Claang!

Sparks flew in midair. The dagger and sword that appeared in an instant vanished again.

Shwik! Paat!

Each time a blade flashed, someone’s blood scattered.

They sensed beings who did not even breathe, and cut them down.

It was truly a duel between those who possessed the utmost mastery of concealment arts.

Everything they had was at work in this fight.

Innate talent, instinct, all the effort they had put in until now, their state of mind, even luck.

Surely, one of them possessed the greater sum of it all.

Slice! Paaak!

Once again, the sound of flesh being cut rang out, and blood burst into the air. Judging by the amount of blood, the wound this time was deeper than before.

Paaak!

At the same time, blood burst from the other side as well.

Neither of them allowed an attack without answer. If one was cut, the other cut back.

Blood sprayed, and sprayed again.

Because neither man let out so much as a single scream, Geom Mugeuk and Myeongsin could not tell who was suffering the deeper wounds.

‘Uncle!’

Now that the fight had become a battle of concealment arts, it was not easy for him to intervene.

If he interfered carelessly, Hwi might be the one to suffer for it.

In a situation where Hwi was concentrating to the utmost, he himself would be the greatest variable that could disturb Hwi’s focus.

Cut, and cut again. Hide, and reveal. Sense, and sense again. Give up flesh to aim for bone. And hide once more.

Just as that suffocating fight continued on and on—

Shiiiiing.

Shwaaaang.

Two sounds of blade wind, different from anything before, rang out.

And the sound that followed was not merely the sound of flesh being cut as it had been until now. It was the sound of flesh and bone being pierced through.

Puuuuuk!

Silence fell over the place.

Drip, drip.

Blood flowed down from empty air.

From that place, the concealment began to unravel, and a figure started to appear.

A man slowly revealed himself.

Blood was flowing from his pierced heart.

Now, the figure of the one who had stabbed him also slowly appeared.

Blood running down the blade, the hand gripping its hilt, the outstretched arm, and then the man who had fought with everything he had—Hwi—revealed himself.

Geom Mugeuk let out a deep sigh of relief and looked up at the sky.

‘Thank you. Truly, thank you.’

He did not know how many times he had regretted it while watching the fight. I should have come alone. I should have found some excuse, any excuse, and come alone.

Because his heart had been wound so tight, Geom Mugeuk now felt so overjoyed that he wanted to shout.

Geom Mugeuk thought that perhaps this was what had decided the match.

It likely did not matter whose concealment art was the higher art, or whose was more refined.

What determined victory and defeat was that Hwi had lived concealed for far longer than the Assassin King.

Because Hwi had silently lived his entire life in concealment, protecting his father.

Because Hwi’s concealment was not martial arts, but his life itself.

In the end, had it not been Hwi’s devotion that killed the Assassin King?

Blood gushed from the Assassin King’s mouth.

When he had first begun this affair, the Assassin King had never imagined a death like this. If he died, he had thought it would be by the Demonic Cult Leader or that Young Cult Leader.

For the first time, emotion appeared on the Assassin King’s face. Anger and irritation. Though he said nothing, his expression seemed to say this:

—To think I would die to a mere bodyguard, of all people.

Hwi had shown him. He had shown just how well a man who knew how to protect could kill, once he set his mind to it.

Looking into the Assassin King’s clear eyes as the life faded from them, Hwi spoke calmly. Perhaps his words represented the hearts of all bodyguards.

“It’s just that we chose a life of protecting.”

Absolute Regression, Chapter 492

Chapter 492: Anxiety Must Be Put to Rest with Skill.
----------------------------------------------------
Having finished speaking, Hwi drew his sword in one swift motion.

The Assassin King sprayed blood and toppled backward. Before he hit the floor, he had already died where he stood.

Though now he was no more than the head of the Underworld, this was the moment when the man who would one day become the king of assassins and claim a seat among the Twelve Earthly Kings died.

Hwi left no lingering attachment to his death, nor to this battle in which he had staked his life.

Swaaash.

After shaking the blood from his blade, Hwi returned it to its sheath.

As though he had cast off the last of the fight along with the blood, Hwi did not even spare the Assassin King’s corpse a glance.

He had now ended the fight of killing and returned once more to being one who protected.

Geom Mugeuk walked over to Hwi and spoke with formal courtesy. He said nothing else. Because in this moment, these were surely the words a guard warrior needed to hear.

“Thank you for protecting me.”

Hwi looked at Geom Mugeuk in silence for a moment. The one who ought to be giving thanks was himself.

If the Young Cult Leader had not unleashed the Ninefire Demonic Art when the Assassin King and the assassins attacked together, he would have died then and there.

That was not the only way the Young Cult Leader had helped. Throughout today’s battle, Hwi had fought with the thought that he had to protect him. Because he could bring out his greatest skill when he had someone to protect.

Myeongshin was looking at the two of them.

The talkative Geom Mugeuk and the taciturn Hwi.

They were two utterly different people, yet Myeongshin suddenly felt that they were alike.

Why had that thought come to him?

At that moment, Myeongshin abruptly recalled the sight of Geom Mugeuk standing before him after killing the assassins.

He realized that the lonely air he had given off then resembled the atmosphere of this man named Hwi.

Myeongshin voiced what he felt.

“Somehow, the two of you seem alike.”

At Myeongshin’s words, Geom Mugeuk joked glibly.

“There are plenty of things Uncle and I have in common. We’re handsome, stylish, and taciturn.”

Hwi gave a faint smile, while Myeongshin looked dumbfounded at the word taciturn.

In that pleasant atmosphere, the three exchanged glances.

Hwi’s gaze toward Myeongshin had softened compared to before the fight. He still disliked assassins, but at least for today, they had been comrades who had staked their lives together.

Geom Mugeuk checked Hwi’s condition.

“By the way, are your injuries all right?”

He knew Hwi had avoided serious wounds, but his body was covered in blood.

“I am fine. Fortunately, they are only cuts to the skin.”

Hwi opened the front of his garment at his chest.

“Because of this, which the Young Cult Leader gave me, I was able to fight more aggressively.”

It was not as though he had defeated the Assassin King because of the finest Heavenly Silkworm Silk, but Hwi attributed the merit to Geom Mugeuk all the same.

That was the kind of man Hwi was.

He gave the credit to others and quietly lived a life of protecting someone.

That life had killed the Assassin King today.

“You were splendid today, Uncle.”

Hwi smiled faintly. Geom Mugeuk wanted to praise him to his heart’s content, if only for today, but Hwi felt awkward in such an atmosphere.

So Hwi hurriedly changed the subject.

“I will leave the cleanup here to this branch.”

“Please do.”

Now Myeongshin asked Geom Mugeuk.

“What should I do?”

“Why are you asking me that?”

Because it depends on you. If you tell me to become your subordinate, I must. If you tell me to die, I must.

Of course, that was only because he still did not know what kind of person Geom Mugeuk was.

“From now on, you should live your own life.”

After a brief pause, Myeongshin asked cautiously.

“Is it truly all right for me to leave like this?”

Geom Mugeuk nodded. Myeongshin could feel it. Geom Mugeuk was sincerely trying to give him freedom.

Then, without realizing it, a sentence slipped from him as if he were talking to himself.

“What should I do with my life now?”

It was not a question for Geom Mugeuk, but for himself.

Geom Mugeuk regarded that uncertainty as a joy.

“Isn’t this the best time? Right now, when nothing has been decided. Choosing something is enjoyable, and making plans is enjoyable too. So savor this moment.”

You really are…

Myeongshin did not know how many times he had said or thought those words about Geom Mugeuk.

Myeongshin honestly revealed his feelings to him.

“You said it before. That it would be nice if somewhere in this world, there was at least one admirable assassin who would accept a single coin offered by a child who had unjustly lost his parents as payment for a commission, and kill the wicked. But you misjudged me. I am not such a broad-minded man that I would accept a commission for a single coin. I set principles for myself when I went out to kill, but even amid that, I was greedy for money.”

Geom Mugeuk looked at him with clear, deep eyes.

But the future you lived that kind of life. Then again, there is no guarantee that you will live that life again in this life.

That had been his life before the regression, and in this life, he was a different person. In this life, they were clearly living different lives.

“Take your time deciding how you will live.”

“I have already decided.”

Geom Mugeuk asked in surprise.

“Is it all right to decide something this important so easily?”

“That is why. Because it is too important, no amount of worrying will give me a proper answer. After long deliberation, I would only make a bad move. At times like this, the thought that first came to mind has often been the correct answer.”

Myeongshin had that thought.

He had lived his entire life as an assassin. What other life could he possibly live? Open a teahouse or an inn and live leisurely? His insides would only burst from dealing with troublesome customers.

“I think I will live by running a small forge.”

Because making various weapons and devices was one thing he truly loved.

“That suits you well.”

Geom Mugeuk pictured him working in a small forge. Fate would not simply leave alone a man who had weathered such storms. Perhaps the act of accepting a single coin to resolve someone’s injustice might happen in that forge.

“If, one day, while hammering iron, you suddenly think, This isn’t it, then seek out Master Gwak of our cult’s forge. If you say I introduced you, he will meet you. If it is him, he will surely give you an answer. Whether it is weariness of your work, or weariness of your life.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s consideration, Myeongshin’s eyes shone intensely.

“Why are you being this good to me?”

“It is not only for your sake. If Master Gwak sees your skills, he will surely receive some sort of stimulation. Then it will be a good thing not only for the two of you, but for our cult as well, would it not?”

He said it that way, but Myeongshin could feel it. That this was for his sake. He did not say that he would never forget this grace. Such things always had to be answered through action.

“If I make something good from time to time, I will send it to you.”

“We are the sort who never refuse gifts, you see. I will gratefully accept them.”

The two looked at each other and smiled.

Myeongshin clasped his fist and palm together with solemn courtesy. His final farewell was exceedingly respectful.

“I believe the Young Cult Leader will become a Heavenly Demon the martial world will never see twice. I sincerely hope you accomplish your great work.”

At his heartfelt farewell, Geom Mugeuk also returned the courtesy with solemn respect. After all, Myeongshin had spent his whole life bound to an organization.

“May you live freely.”

Myeongshin nodded, then also gave Hwi a respectful farewell.

“Through meeting you, I learned that a life of protecting is far more difficult and far more valuable than a life of killing.”

Hwi also clasped his fist and palm respectfully and sent him off.

“Live well, wherever you go.”

And so Myeongshin left that place.

Geom Mugeuk watched until his figure had completely disappeared.

“Watching someone begin a new life feels good, even if all you do is look on like this.”

Hwi could sense a certain longing in Geom Mugeuk’s eyes. Perhaps this Young Cult Leader, more than anyone, desperately yearned for such a free life as well.

Geom Mugeuk turned his head toward Hwi. The longing that had filled both his eyes had already vanished.

“Uncle, we should return as well.”

Geom Mugeuk and Hwi also left that place, shoulder to shoulder.

* * *

Father was making something at the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult’s safehouse.

He was so engrossed in it with his back turned that I could not even greet him.

“Don’t tell me? Are you making a weapon capable of conquering the martial world?”

That was almost the atmosphere.

Then Father turned to look this way. After confirming what was in his hands, I burst into laughter.

“I suppose not. You were trying to conquer fish.”

What he was making was a fishing rod.

On the way here to Shaanxi, I had defeated Father twice in fishing contests.

We had said we would compete again on the way back, and Father was already preparing for the return. Naturally, Father had believed that we would come back safely.

“I have returned, Father.”

“We have returned after completing the mission, Cult Leader.”

When we formally greeted him, Father looked after Hwi before his own son.

“You are hurt.”

Before entering this place, Hwi had changed out of his bloodstained martial robe, but Father noticed at a glance.

“They are all merely scratches.”

I could not simply let that worried look toward Hwi pass.

I raised my hand high toward Father.

“There is a son here too! I may have suffered internal injuries you cannot see!”

Father pretended not to hear me and rose from his seat, walking toward one wall.

Then he searched for something in the leather pack standing there. Father usually took things out with Void Grasp, so what kind of item was it that he was looking for it himself?

What Father brought from the leather pack and held out to Hwi was a small pure-white bottle.

“Apply this.”

It was the wound salve used by the Heavenly Demon himself. Naturally, it was a medicine with efficacy incomparable to ordinary wound salve.

“I cannot. How could I dare apply the Cult Leader’s medicine?”

“Apply it.”

At Father’s firm command, Hwi could no longer refuse.

“Thank you.”

Father’s eyes were deep as he looked at Hwi, whose head was bowed. The fact that Hwi had been injured this much meant that Father understood just how fierce that battle had been.

“Father! I also have cuts!”

At that, Father extended his hand with a contemptuous laugh.

The leather pack opened on its own, and this time another bottle came out and flew toward me.

“That is the most insincere Void Grasp I have ever seen.”

That was not all. After checking the medicine bottle, I cried out.

“This is too much! This is wound salve I already have!”

The one he had given Hwi was the highest-grade product reserved for the Heavenly Demon, while this was a grade below.

When Hwi’s eyes met mine, he lowered his head with a flustered expression.

“If you do not want it, forget it.”

When Father extended his hand as if telling me to give it back, I swiftly tucked it into my chest.

“Still, it is the first wound salve Father has given me, so I should apply it. I will use it for a long, long time.”

Father rose from his seat.

“Since you allowed that man to be injured, you should be punished. Follow me.”

If you are going to put Hwi in front, then you are responsible for protecting him. I had broken that promise.

“You misunderstand. I did everything in my power not to get hurt because Uncle’s heart would ache if I were injured! Of course, I do feel I was the only one trying too hard! Father!”

Father took me to an empty lot with no one around near the safehouse.

There, he drew his sword and said,

“We must make sure there will be no more need to apply medicine.”

I could tell. Through a spar, he was going to bestow a lesson upon me. It was not a punishment, but a reward. It was a reward for both of us returning safely, and Father’s way of expressing his joy.

‘Thank you, Father.’

I stood about ten paces away from Father.

And today’s spar was different.

Father revealed no aura whatsoever. Even so, there was an energy dominating the space.

It was the energy of the Heavenly Demon Sword.

It seemed Father was telling me to feel only the energy of the sword.

‘If that is so?’

I too withdrew all of my aura. With not even a single handful of energy, striving not to reveal even the faintest presence, I slowly drew the Black Demon Sword.

The energy of the Heavenly Demon Sword could be felt.

The energy of the Black Demon Sword could be felt.

It had truly been a long time since I had focused solely on feeling the energy of the Black Demon Sword like this.

‘Now that I think of it, I have been too indifferent.’

Because I was learning the Ninefire Demonic Art, because I was using the Time-Heaven Secret Art, because I was increasing my internal energy.

I had been paying attention to other things and neglecting the Black Demon Sword.

Suddenly, I recalled the first time I met Ian after my regression.

Back then, I had reflected on this as well.

I had taken her existence for granted. Just like living without realizing how grateful one should be for the air one breathes every day.

It was the same. I had faced my enemies with this Black Demon Sword all this time, yet instead of being grateful, I had taken it for granted.

“I am this foolish. Thank you truly for teaching me, Father.”

A faint smile formed at Father’s lips. It was clearly a smile of satisfaction.

In this way, Father constantly gives me instruction and stimulation.

And today’s lesson was not only that.

“Unleash the Nine Flames Demonic Art.”

I performed the Nine Flames Demonic Art before Father. I did not hide my accomplishment, and displayed it with all my might.

Father needed to know my skill accurately. And I hoped that, in turn, it would become a driving force that stimulated him once more.

‘Father, how much has my Nine Flames Demonic Art changed from yours?’

After seeing my Nine Flames Demonic Art, Father once again lectured on the formula of the Nine Flames Demonic Art.

It was the same martial art, and I had heard the lecture many times, but today there were things that felt different yet again.

It must have been because my realm had changed, and Father’s realm had changed. Even the exact same words felt different. Truly, training with Father felt as though my skill grew with every word we exchanged.

We asked and answered, thought, then performed it again. By the time that round of training ended, it was already deep into the night.

For a while, I looked up together with Father at the moon hanging bright and full in the sky.

Then Father suddenly asked,

“What is it that makes you uneasy?”

It was the first time I had received such a question. After my regression, he must have seen the changes in his son and read the deep unease contained within them.

“You brought me into the world as a perfect son, but I have exactly one anxiety.”

“What sort of anxiety?”

“The anxiety that some colossal evil, jealous of you and me, will try to devour us.”

Father wore an expression of incomprehension.

“Did you not say that we are the only ones capable of devouring that evil?”

Father remembered precisely the demonic path I had spoken of.

“It seems I’m afraid of meeting something too large to swallow.”

What would Father think of this unease?

After staring at me for a moment, Father rose from his seat and drew his sword.

“Then you must make your mouth and belly larger.”

That simple, lucid answer was also a single phrase that defined the path I had to take from now on. Yes, unease was something to be filled with skill.

I sprang to my feet, opening my mouth wide.

“Yes! No matter how large it is, I will swallow it whole.”

And so, my martial training with Father continued throughout the night.

The moon that had watched over us set, and the morning stars inherited its place. Then, when even the stars withdrew, the dawn light took their place.

As a bright yet reddish energy awakened the earth, the shadow of one man stretched long.

Throughout our training, only one person stood alone with his back turned, watching over us.

Absolute Regression Chapter 493

Chapter 493: Next, the Yeon of Fate.
------------------------------------
After finishing the martial arts training that had kept us up all night until dawn, we walked back together.

Father led the way, and one step behind him, Hwi and I walked on either side.

I had ordered Hwi not to conceal himself if possible on this journey, so he was walking with us. Though he had defeated the King of Assassins through concealment, a life without hiding was also Hwi’s life.

“By the way, Father. Before we return, I think I’ll need to settle matters with the Golden Dragon Clan.”

Father knew that I intended to give them a chance. He also knew I had the third child in mind as the successor.

“To be honest, this feels far more difficult than dealing with assassins, but if you give me just a few days, I’ll take care of it.”

Father looked at me with puzzled eyes.

“Can it be done in just a few days?”

“It would be impossible through ordinary persuasion.”

The family head and his three children.

This was a matter of sorting out each of their different hearts and desires.

“So I plan to use another method instead of persuasion.”

A flicker of interest passed through Father’s eyes, then vanished. He did not ask what method. He would find out soon enough anyway.

When Father returned to our quarters, he began working on his fishing rod again. He examined it this way and that, changed the line, shaved the rod with a dagger to adjust its balance.

“If a spy from the Murim Alliance or the Unorthodox Alliance saw this, the report would say: The Heavenly Demon is pouring his heart and soul into making an unidentified object. Judging from his concentration, it appears to be an extremely dangerous item, though it has been disguised as a fishing rod.”

Even at my joke, Father gave no reply and remained focused on the fishing rod.

He was more serious than when he had taught me martial arts earlier. In those careful hands was the will never to lose.

‘Still, I won’t deliberately lose for you!’

Rather, I had to win. Only then would there be another chance to go fishing.

* * *

A few days later, Geum Arin and Geom Mugeuk entered the Golden Dragon Clan’s Family Head Hall.

They had hurried over at her father’s urgent summons, and Geum Ahyeok and Geum Ahjong were already there.

Not only them, but all the clan’s experts had gathered. Everyone’s faces were tense, as if they were moments away from a decisive battle.

Considering that Geum Arin had been told to bring Geom Mugeuk and even her guard Im Hyeok, she could tell that something had happened to the clan.

When she arrived, the voice of a Golden Dragon Clan warrior rang out from outside.

“The guests have arrived.”

At those words, Geum Cheonbang’s face stiffened with tension. It was the first time she had ever seen her father look so nervous.

A moment later, four warriors entered.

The instant she saw them, Geum Arin understood why her father was so tense.

‘Demonic practitioners!’

The aura emanating from them was demonic energy. Moreover, they did not hide their demonic energy. In particular, the presence of the demonic practitioner walking in the center was anything but ordinary.

‘Experts!’

Just as she had expected, he was no ordinary man. The middle-aged man introduced himself in a cold tone.

“I am Tai Yang, Branch Master of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult’s Shaanxi Branch.”

Geum Arin was even more shocked. Astonishingly, it was not merely an expert from the Demon Cult, but the branch master responsible for the Shaanxi region himself who had come.

Only three experts followed behind Tai Yang as escort warriors, yet it felt even more oppressive than if he had brought dozens to intimidate them.

Geum Cheonbang stepped forward and greeted him courteously. A branch master of the Demon Cult was not someone who could be treated lightly.

“I am Geum Cheonbang, head of the Golden Dragon Clan.”

Geum Cheonbang was an expert who had mastered the Golden Dragon Divine Art, but since childhood, he had carried a fundamental fear of martial artists. And now, facing these demonic practitioners, that fear had awakened again.

Even so, he struggled to appear calm.

“Our family has had no dealings with your cult. May I ask what brings you here?”

That morning, an urgent message had arrived from the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult’s Shaanxi Branch.

It had instructed him to summon not only the family head but also the successors and wait. The way they handled matters was just like the Demon Cult. They had given no reason, only the time of their visit.

As soon as he received the message, Geum Cheonbang had asked the Murim Alliance’s Shaanxi Branch for help. But before the Murim Alliance experts could arrive, the Demon Cult had come first.

Tai Yang looked slowly around at the people in the hall. Those whose gazes met his grew inwardly tense. Regardless of their level of martial arts, they all knew that if one became entangled with the Demon Cult in the wrong way, one could not avoid the calamity of extermination.

Tai Yang’s gaze stopped on Geum Ahyeok. One of the warriors who had come with him had informed him through sound transmission that this was Geum Ahyeok.

“Are you Geum Ahyeok?”

“I am.”

Though he had been acting sharp and rebellious toward his father lately, he did not dare behave recklessly before these demonic practitioners.

“Do you know a man named Myeongsin?”

“Who is that?”

“Do not think of playing dumb. We have evidence that you frequently visited his ironworks and met with him.”

Only then did they understand. The real name of the man Geum Ahyeok had followed while calling him elder was Myeongsin.

“We also have evidence that an enormous amount of Golden Dragon Clan funds flowed to him.”

Geum Ahyeok explained calmly.

“That is correct. That man helped our family with various matters.”

At that, Tai Yang spoke coldly.

“He was an assassin of the Netherworld, a man upon whom our cult had issued a kill order.”

Not only Geum Ahyeok, but Geum Cheonbang as well was startled. It was shocking that he was an assassin of the Netherworld, but what was even more shocking was the fact that they were enemies of the Demon Cult.

Geum Cheonbang hurriedly stepped forward on behalf of his son.

“We did not know that.”

Tai Yang fixed Geum Cheonbang with a cold gaze.

“That is something we will discover through investigation. We will take Geum Ahyeok with us.”

Geum Cheonbang’s face turned ashen. If his son was dragged away by the Demon Cult, there was no telling what would happen to him.

On the other hand, Geum Ahyeok first asked after Myeongsin.

“Where is that gentleman now?”

“That gentleman?”

Tai Yang’s expression hardened. He had just said the man had been under a kill order, and yet Geum Ahyeok still referred to him respectfully.

Just as the startled Geum Cheonbang was about to step forward in his son’s place, Tai Yang delivered astonishing news.

“Not only Myeongsin, but all the assassins of the Netherworld have been eliminated by our cult.”

At the news that Myeongsin was dead, Geum Ahyeok’s expression stiffened.

Geum Cheonbang, however, was inwardly pleased. His son had been so taken with that man that he had even mobilized assassins. And now the man who had been exerting a bad influence over his son was dead. In that sense, the Demon Cult was his benefactor.

“You killed him?”

Geum Ahyeok raised his voice. The news of Myeongsin’s death had agitated him. He was the person who had taught him more than anyone else. Geum Ahyeok had genuinely liked and respected him.

Geum Cheonbang quickly stepped forward and stopped his son.

“You be quiet.”

But Geum Ahyeok spat out words he should not have said.

“The Demon Cult framed that man and killed him!”

“I told you to shut up!”

“He was not that kind of man. This is a plot by those Demon Cult bastards……”

Geum Cheonbang quickly sealed his son’s mute and paralysis acupoints. Even in that state, Geum Ahyeok glared fiercely, his face twisted. The clan experts present frowned at Geum Ahyeok’s rashness.

“Please forgive my son’s discourtesy.”

Tai Yang merely sent him a cold look.

Now that matters had come to this, he could all the less send his son with them. If he went in this agitated state, he would be dead before a day had passed.

Just then, the people Geum Cheonbang had been waiting for arrived. The warriors of the Murim Alliance’s Shaanxi Branch had arrived.

“Welcome!”

Geum Cheonbang greeted them gladly. However, the branch master, with whom he was ordinarily acquainted, had not come personally; only a few of his subordinates had arrived.

“We have come to convey the branch master’s words.”

Surprisingly, the Murim Alliance already knew about the situation here. And the words that came from them struck like a bolt from the blue.

“The assassins of the Netherworld are also listed as public enemies of murim by the Murim Alliance.”

The fact that the Murim Alliance’s Shaanxi Branch Master had sent only his subordinates to convey this meant that he would not involve himself in this matter.

Tai Yang emitted cold demonic energy and pressured Geum Cheonbang.

“May we take him now?”

Geum Cheonbang could not protest. His son had formed ties with someone who was both an enemy of the Demon Cult and listed as a public enemy of murim by the Murim Alliance. One wrong move, and the entire Golden Dragon Clan could be plunged into danger.

“Since the situation is like this, you should send Elder Brother for now.”

The second son, Geum Ahjong, stepped forward. Geum Cheonbang frowned at him. Geum Ahjong stopped wagging his tongue and withdrew. Inwardly, he was delighted. This was the moment his elder brother was finished.

At that moment, Geum Arin felt Geom Mugeuk’s gaze beside her. He was looking at her calmly, as if he had expected this to happen. His eyes asked, Are you just going to stand there?

—What should I do?

She sent a sound transmission.

—What do you want to do? Do you want to step forward and save your elder brother?

—Honestly, no.

—Even so, you’ll have to stop them from taking him away. If you want to become the successor.

It was advice from Geom Mugeuk that seemed to see through the situation.

There was no time to deliberate at length. The demonic practitioners were already approaching to take Geum Ahyeok.

She was afraid to step forward in this situation and did not want to do it, but she mustered her courage. She trusted Geom Mugeuk’s advice.

“Branch Master, I have a request.”

Everyone’s gazes focused on her. Geum Arin spoke calmly to Tai Yang.

“Even if you investigate this matter, I ask that you conduct it within our family.”

“Do you know who we are and still say such a thing?”

“I say it because I do. We are not a murim sect, but a faction belonging to the merchant world. If the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult were to barge in without restraint and take away a blood relative of our family, it would provoke outrage not only from the entire merchant world, but from murim people as well. Furthermore, I am not saying we will refuse an investigation. I am asking that you allow him to be investigated here.”

Though her voice trembled, she said everything she had to say to the end.

“And if I refuse?”

If she was going to act weak, she should not have stepped forward in the first place.

“I will do everything I can to make this matter public throughout murim. I cannot say for certain, but it may also bring dishonor to your reputation, Branch Master.”

A taut tension flowed between the two.

Geum Arin was certain the Demon Cult would not attack rashly. Warriors of the Murim Alliance were here as well, so they would not start a fight. If they did, it truly could escalate into a major incident.

“This is a matter involving the blood relative of a family head. I ask that you handle it with just a little caution.”

She asked courteously and lowered her head. The clan members watched her with surprise. If she were fighting for succession, she should have tried to send Geum Ahyeok off with the demonic practitioners like the second son had.

Tai Yang also knew their situation well.

“I hear you are competing with your elder brothers for the succession. Why did you step forward?”

Geum Arin answered calmly.

“The succession struggle is important, but our clan’s honor is even more important.”

Geum Cheonbang and the clan experts looked at her with astonished expressions. Geum Cheonbang, in particular, was deeply moved. His daughter was trying to save her elder brother while also protecting the clan’s honor. At least in this moment, she was better than him, and better than anyone else present.

After gazing at her for a moment, Tai Yang took a step back.

“You are the best of his children.”

Along with this unexpected praise.

“Very well. We will conduct the investigation here. For now, we will withdraw, and tomorrow I will send investigators.”

Geum Arin expressed her gratitude with a courteous cupped-fist salute.

“Thank you for your consideration.”

Tai Yang looked at Geum Ahyeok and warned him coldly.

“Do not leave this place. If you leave, a kill order will be issued against you.”

Leaving those words behind, all the demonic practitioners withdrew.

The Murim Alliance warriors also left soon after. At least regarding this matter, they showed no intention of involving themselves deeply.

Geum Cheonbang looked at Geum Arin and nodded with an expression that said she had done well. The expressions of the clan experts as they looked at her were also gentle.

By contrast, their gazes toward Geum Ahyeok were cold. He had nearly plunged the Golden Dragon Clan into great danger.

As the chaotic situation in the hall was being settled, Geom Mugeuk and Geum Arin spoke separately in front of the Family Head Hall.

“I will be leaving this place immediately.”

“What?”

“I’ve done everything I came to do, so I should leave.”

Done everything he came to do? In that instant, one thought surfaced in Geum Arin’s mind.

“Don’t tell me. What just happened—was it something you set up?”

“If not, do you think the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult would have withdrawn so obediently? Even praising you?”

“!”

She was confused.

“How on earth?”

He had moved the Demon Cult? And the Shaanxi Branch Master at that? How on earth?

Could this man be a demonic practitioner? But I can’t sense even a trace of demonic energy from him. Or did he move the Murim Alliance and use it to move the Demonic Cult? Does the granddaughter of the Murim Alliance leader truly like this man? All sorts of thoughts flashed through her mind.

“Because of this incident, your elder brother, who had been the leading candidate, has been pushed away from the position of successor. Conversely, you have become the likely choice. You collected the debts owed by the various merchant groups of Shaanxi and helped those in need. And this time, you stepped forward, saved your elder brother, and even preserved the honor of your family.”

“But my second elder brother is still there.”

Geom Mugeuk stared at her and said,

“Do you wish for even that much?”

For an instant, Geum Arin flinched. Geom Mugeuk’s eyes were saying it: Should that not be something you accomplish yourself? Show everyone, with your own hands, the capabilities of a successor.

“No. I should do it myself.”

What weighed on her mind now was not Geum Ajong.

“Even so, drawing in the Demonic Cult was a mistake.”

Geom Mugeuk looked up at the sky. His gaze deepened, and only then did he say what he had meant to tell her before leaving.

“You, your father, and your brothers are not good people. You joined hands with evil to increase your wealth. You even tried to drag places like the Ju Clan Sword Family into unfair contracts. You have probably committed other wrongs as well.”

Geum Arin could not offer any excuse. It was true.

“Do you know why I am still trying to give you a chance?”

Geum Arin asked in a trembling voice,

“Why?”

“Because the assassins of the Netherworld deliberately targeted you. Those bastards are so vicious that they would have dragged you in one way or another. Of course, that does not mean the sins of those who fell for it disappear.”

Geom Mugeuk’s gaze turned to her. Unlike the eyes she had seen until now, they were stern.

“Correct all the wrongs you committed while running your secret organization. And that is not the end. After you become the successor, and later the head of the family, you must live with the heart of repaying your past mistakes. I chose you because I believed you were at least capable of that.”

Only now did Geum Arin understand. The successor’s seat he had promised her was not a reward, but a punishment. He had chosen her because she seemed the person most likely to bear that punishment well.

“I asked you before whether you could kill your elder brothers. At the time, you answered that you did not know. Now, answer me again. Can you kill them?”

After pondering for a moment, she shook her head.

“I don’t think I can.”

Geom Mugeuk eased her worry.

“That is why the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult will, rather, be of help to you. As long as they are watching your family, your elder brothers will not dare covet your position. In the meantime, build your strength. So that even without outside surveillance and management, you can protect yourself.”

Geum Arin felt as though she could faintly see how she ought to live from now on.

This incomprehensible man was far greater than she had thought. He was someone who had eliminated the Netherworld, drawn in the Demonic Cult, and given her this opportunity.

“Then I shall be going.”

Geom Mugeuk strode away at once. Realizing he was truly leaving just like that, she was flustered.

“Thank you so much!”

Geom Mugeuk, who had already walked quite a distance away, looked back and shouted,

“Keep living as someone blessed with good food fortune!”

Geum Arin laughed. She had once jokingly introduced herself to the head of the Eunha Trading Company as a woman blessed with good food fortune, but to think he would use those words as his final farewell.

“Just who are you?”

“I am Geom Yeon.”

He had said it was not Yeon as in fated bond, but Yeon as in smoke. And just as he had said, he was truly disappearing like smoke.

Geum Arin could not leave that spot until Geom Mugeuk vanished from sight.

‘Next time, let’s meet not with Yeon as in smoke, but Yeon as in fated bond.’

Absolute Regression, Chapter 494

Chapter 494: The Original Incarnation of Material Greed.
--------------------------------------------------------
When Geom Mugeuk returned to the An estate, preparations for departure were well underway.

The luggage had already been loaded, and Hwi was inspecting the horses and carriage.

His father stood in the courtyard, hands clasped behind his back, gazing up at the sky.

Geom Mugeuk approached his father's side.

"I have finished the matter with the Golden Dragon Family."

As always, Geom Mugeuk reported everything that had happened in detail to his father. Though his father occasionally made an expression as if asking why he had to go into such exhaustive detail, he reported as thoroughly as possible.

Geom Mugeuk knew that these reports had greatly helped his relationship with his father thus far. At least he was now accustomed to the act of conversation itself.

"I left the cleanup to the parties involved."

Fortunately, the Murim Alliance had also actively lent its aid in this matter. Since they had agreed to handle the forces behind the scenes together through a trilateral meeting, they had gladly acceded to the Heaven-Piercing Pavilion's request.

"In the end, it was resolved by pressing through with our cult's authority and power."

Geom Ujin, who had been staring up at the sky, turned his gaze toward his son.

"Our cult's power is your power."

Geom Mugeuk smiled happily. It was a statement acknowledging the authority of the Young Cult Leader. But the important thing was not such a thought.

Thank you, Father. For expressing it in this way.

Geom Ujin boarded the carriage first, and Geom Mugeuk followed after him.

"Farewell, Seomseo!"

At the signal of Geom Mugeuk's farewell, the carriage driven by Hwi departed from the An estate and began racing toward the Heavenly Demon Cult.

"Father and I had agreed to compete once to see who was more popular with the young ladies in Seomseo, yet we're leaving without having done so. Father, if the opportunity arises next time, let us formally compete."

Geom Ujin did not say he would not do it. He did not say it was a contest advantageous to the young one, either.

'This is my father.'

A lofty arrogance revealed itself on Geom Ujin's face.

It was a minute change in expression that others would have difficulty noticing, but Geom Mugeuk could now sense it.

His father was a man, too.

Geom Mugeuk stuck his head out the window and shouted to Hwi in the driver's seat.

"Mister. Don't forget we have to stop by Jungyeong on the way."

They had to visit the treasure vault that Eunha Sangdanju had given as a gift.

"I said I'd pick thirty items from there, didn't I?"

Immediately, Geom Ujin's voice rang out.

"I never made such a promise."

"Right? Even I think thirty is too many. I'll pick just ten items."

"..."

"Actually, ten is too many as well. As if I'm the very incarnation of greed. Very well. I'll happily pick eight. After all, by fate our cult also has eight Demon Lords."

"..."

"Right? What do Demon Lords have to do with gifts? I'll just take five. No, Father! Then just three! Please!"

A smile formed at the corners of Hwi's mouth as he listened to the conversation coming from behind. Had he ever smiled this much in his life?

This journey had been an incredibly precious time for him. It had been a journey of the Cult Leader and the Young Cult Leader, but at the same time, a journey for himself as well.

The Cult Leader and the Young Cult Leader had made it so.

Today on the road, the Cult Leader had let him stop by his hometown, and the Young Cult Leader had let him earn a large sum of money by dividing it into equal thirds. He had also given him the opportunity to kill the Slaughter King himself. And the Cult Leader had personally given him the Geumchang medicine.

And instead of concealing themselves in the shadows, the three of them had always walked together.

Had any guard in the history of the Heavenly Demon Cult ever had such an experience? Hwi could swear. Absolutely none.

'Thank you, both of you.'

A short while later, Geom Mugeuk—who had failed to obtain even three treasures—pouted and leaned his body out the carriage window to feel the wind.

"This is too much! How can you be so cold-hearted!"

Hwi glanced back. Contrary to his complaining words, Geom Mugeuk's face as he gazed at the distant scenery was smiling pleasantly.

In truth, at this moment, Geom Mugeuk was feeling regretful.

When would he be able to come out with his father like this again? Now that they were returning, his father wouldn't be able to come out for some time.

Why did time flow so quickly? They had only just departed, yet they would surely arrive before he knew it.

"Mister, let us go back slowly. Slower than when we came. If you see bandits robbing people on the way, stop the carriage. If you see thieves, stop as well, and a hostage crisis is fine too! Don't pass by a thirsty traveler on the road or a lost cat. Please, let us go back slowly!"

He wished his father felt the same regret as him, but his father said nothing.

Only a fishing rod poking out of the carriage window swayed gently in the wind.

* * *

The carriage arrived at the foot of Mount Okhwa in Jungyeong.

Eunha Sangdanju had told them to seek out Jungsong, who gathered medicinal herbs in this area.

After asking after his whereabouts in the village and arriving at his house below the mountain, Jungsong had been waiting at home for Geom Mugeuk's party to arrive.

"I heard the news that the owner of the key has appeared."

Eunha Sangdanju had sent word that someone would come before long. So he had not gone out to gather herbs recently and had been waiting at home for his guests.

"I shall guide you. Let us go."

Perhaps he had been waiting for this moment even more than the one who would obtain the treasure. For it was the moment he would gain freedom from bonds he had not even known were tying him down.

Jungsong led the three up Mount Okhwa.

Having reached the peak, he looked down below the cliff.

"You must jump down this way. As you descend, you will see a protrusion about midway down the cliff face. You must land precisely there."

"Go down first."

Jungsong looked at Geom Mugeuk with somewhat worried eyes. The young Geom Mugeuk seemed to worry him more than the other two.

Geom Mugeuk reassured him.

"Do not worry. I have a father who raised me by throwing me off cliffs."

At Geom Mugeuk's words, Jungsong could tell his assumption had been correct.

'As expected! Those two were father and son.'

They resembled each other, yet their auras were different.

Jungsong leaped off the cliff. Falling rapidly, he waved his arms and legs to reduce his speed.

Down below, there was a jutting space about midway down the cliff.

He was confident in his lightness skill, but he tensed up every time. Landing on a protrusion after leaping off a cliff was by no means an easy task, though it looked simple enough to watch.

One needed a high level of lightness skill, and above all, guts. One also needed experience. Moreover, the wind was blowing strongly today, making it an even harder day.

Jungsong landed on the protrusion.

'Will they be able to descend safely?'

Worried, he looked up.

Geom Mugeuk plummeted down with a whoosh and landed lightly beside him.

"Huh?"

Jungsong looked at him with a surprised face.

"Why are you so surprised?"

"It is nothing."

He had literally dropped straight down. Normally, one would spread one's lightness skill and move one's arms and legs to reduce speed.

But Geom Mugeuk had fallen as fast as a falling rock, yet when he landed, he had come down as light as a downy feather. He had not even moved his arms or legs.

'This is the first time I've seen such lightness skill. If the young one is like this, what of the others?'

Then, Jungsong happened to glance at the other side and was startled.

'Gasp!'

Hwi was already standing beside him. There had been no sound, no presence at all. It was enough to make him wonder if Hwi had leaped down together with him from the start.

The one who delivered the final surprise was Geom Ujin.

Geom Ujin, who had descended at some point, was floating in midair, his clothes billowing.

With a face as comfortable as if he were standing on the ground, he floated in the air before the protrusion.

At the martial arts prowess of the three, Jungsong conveyed words he had not originally intended to say.

"The Sangdanju would come every year and give me new treasures and keys, and I would enter this place and fill it. So this place was known only to the Sangdanju and myself. At first, I thought it was the Sangdanju's personal vault, but one day he told me. He said this was a place to gather gifts for my benefactor. I could not understand it. Just what kind of benefactor would warrant going this far?"

Jungsong lightly bowed his head to the three and spoke.

"Now I understand."

They were far from ordinary martial artists. It was a moment in which he was meeting masters he could not hope to encounter once in his entire life as a martial artist, and three of them at that.

"There were difficult times while waiting, but looking back, it seems it was a worthwhile wait."

He was a master who could gauge an opponent's level to some degree through lightness skill alone.

Then Geom Mugeuk expressed his gratitude to him.

"Thank you for waiting."

"There is no need to thank me. I only undertook this because of my friendship with the Sangdanju. I was also paid handsomely."

"Still, I know how hard it is for a martial artist to be tied down to one place. You have truly suffered."

Through this journey, Hwi could now be certain. That such expressions of gratitude were Geom Mugeuk's true heart. That the one charging forward with that sincerity was the Young Cult Leader of the current era's Heavenly Demon Cult.

Hwi's gaze slid briefly toward Geom Ujin.

'I think I finally understand. Just how difficult a battle the Cult Leader has been fighting.'

Against the heart of Geom Mugeuk that he had shown on this journey.

'I think I have already lost.'

It was not because of dividing the money into thirds. He had already lost when Geom Mugeuk brought it, saying it was for him. When he laid out the leather mat for him while camping, he had already lost. When he dragged him to eat together, he had already lost.

'Will the Cult Leader be able to win? There are still five years left.'

Meanwhile, Jungsong pulled out a small stone embedded in the cliff wall.

There was a hole there, the kind commonly found in cliffs.

"Insert the key here and turn it."

No one in the world could know that this place was the entrance to a secret vault. Even if someone were to fall from the cliff and miraculously land on this protrusion, they would never imagine that this small hole in the wall was a keyhole.

"Then I shall take my leave."

Jungsong bowed respectfully and leaped down below the cliff. His body, having found freedom, flew away as light as the wind.

Geom Ujin walked through the air to the spot where Jungsong had stood and alighted.

He took out the Myriad-Year Coldiron Key he had received from Yong Jamyeong and handed it to Hwi.

When Hwi inserted the key into the hole and turned it.

Rumble.

A door hidden in the cliff wall opened.

"I shall wait here."

Then Geom Mugeuk would not have it.

"Isn't this journey supposed to be together unconditionally?"

Geom Ujin nodded.

"Enter together."

"Yes."

And so the three entered the cave.

There were no separate mechanical traps to defend against intruders. Because it was in a location absolutely impossible to find, the core of this treasure vault was trust in the man called Jungsong.

If he set his mind to it with ulterior motives, he could break through the wall and enter.

Geom Mugeuk could understand. That there was a reason Eunha Sangdanju had become the wealthiest person in the Murim.

He had trustworthy people to whom he could entrust treasures, and once he trusted someone, he had the faith and daring to truly leave matters in their hands.

The interior was bright thanks to night-illuminating pearls embedded all around, and air circulated from somewhere, making the inside comfortable.

The first thing to catch the eye was the bronze mirror in the center. It was a large mirror that could reflect one's entire body.

Treasures were displayed on either side of it.

Clothes hung on the left wall. Of course, they were not ordinary martial uniforms.

"Father, isn't this the Black Dragon Flying Armor?"

The treasure armor embroidered with a black dragon was a very famous treasure armor in the Murim.

It was not the only one. The treasure armor hanging beside it was no less impressive.

"This is the Flying Tiger Iron Garment. Ah! That one is the Adamantine Divine Armor."

From treasure armors recognizable at a glance like these.

"What is this?"

"It is the Smiling Proud True Armor. A very old treasure armor."

To treasure armors only his father could recognize.

In an ordinary vault, such treasure armors would be displayed splendidly on the walls. The most precious item in the center, the rest around it.

But here, there were so many treasure armors that they were hung stacked atop one another like ordinary martial uniforms.

If treasure armors were gathered like this, how could there be no weapons?

The treasured swords displayed on one side were gleaming, as if their moment to shine had finally arrived.

"The Taiji Divine Sword, the Lotus Sword, and that one is the Bloodwind Radiance Sword, right?"

Not only treasured swords but also treasured blades, spears, and daggers. All kinds of treasures filled the place.

That was not all.

Martial arts secret manuals were also shelved there, and judging by the level of the treasured swords and armors, the martial arts techniques would likewise be difficult to obtain.

In addition, there were poison-warding pearls, night-shining pearls, and all manner of other priceless treasures. They were treasures whose value could scarcely be converted into money.

It was clear that Yong Jamyeong had gathered them over many long years. They were not things one could obtain overnight simply because one had money.

“My father really did save the right man.”

Here were gathered the things the richest man in the Central Plains had prepared his entire life for his benefactor. It was also a moment in which one could feel the dignity of the richest man in the martial world.

“This robe here seems to be the White Dragon Martial Armor (白龍武甲). I heard it was one of the treasured garments orthodox martial artists liked best, and it was here all along. Father, look at this. Huh?”

Geom Mugeuk turned around and flinched in surprise.

His father had already put on a treasured garment and was standing before the bronze mirror.

It was a black robe that suited his father truly well.

“What is that outfit?”

His father answered.

“It is the Soul-Startling Armor (驚魂甲). I had wanted to try wearing it for a long time, but I could never obtain it. So it was here.”

He had seemed like someone who would refuse to wear such things, saying, What need does a martial artist have for treasured garments?

But his father possessed deeper knowledge of martial treasures than anyone.

His father extended his hand again. Then something that had been hanging there flew toward him.

Clack, clack.

His father fastened them around both wrists. They were guards that protected the wrists.

“They are Traceless (無痕).”

Even Geom Mugeuk had heard of them. Legendary wrist guards said to be impossible to cut with any sword energy.

When his father extended his hand again, this time a dagger flew over.

“It is the Heart at World’s End (天涯丹心).”

It was a dagger ranked among the top five in the martial world. He inserted it into Traceless. Traceless had been made from the start so that a dagger could be fitted into it.

Watching this, Geom Mugeuk did not forget to joke.

“Now I know why I always wanted treasures whenever I saw them. The blood of the original incarnation of material desire was flowing in me!”

At Geom Mugeuk’s joke, a sneer formed at the corner of his father’s mouth.

Seeing that sneer after so long put Geom Mugeuk in a good mood.

Yes, this was the kind of man his father was. He was peerlessly impressive when he stood with his hands behind his back, looking up at the sky, but that was not where his father’s essence lay.

He was someone who wanted to try on treasured garments when he saw them and wanted to wield treasured swords when he saw them. When he saw a strong opponent, he wanted to fight, and if he fought, he absolutely wanted to win.

What made this moment precious to Geom Mugeuk was that his father was now showing him that side without restraint.

As his father looked into the bronze mirror and equipped himself with the treasures, he suddenly said,

“What are you two doing, not choosing anything?”

Geom Mugeuk opened his eyes wide with joy and asked,

“We can choose too?”

From within the mirror, his father spoke as though it were only natural.

“Both of you are to equip yourselves properly. Don’t think about giving anything to others. Think only of yourselves.”

Absolute Regression, Chapter 495

Chapter 495: Your Demonic Path Is Being Stained with Blood
----------------------------------------------------------
“Which color do you think suits me better?”

Geom Mugeuk held one armor in each hand.

In his left was the Cheolbi Armor (Iron Mystery Armor), and in his right was the Hwayang Armor (Glorious Sun Armor). Both were the kind of armors that martial artists of the jianghu would brave blades and bloodshed to claim.

“Shouldn’t function be more important than color?”

At Hwi’s cautious reply, Geom Mugeuk firmly shook his head.

“You don’t understand! Above all, there must be flair. Just imagine. In the middle of a life-or-death battle, your clothes flare open and the armor beneath is revealed in all its glory!”

Geom Mugeuk picked up an armor he had tossed aside.

“And what if I were wearing something this drab underneath! Ah, I’d rather just be stabbed and get it over with.”

Geom Ujin looked at his son with an exasperated expression.

“It’s all your fault that I can’t choose.”

Geom Ujin asked with a bewildered look.

“Why is it my fault?”

“The Gyeonghon Armor you chose first was the coolest. I simply cannot find anything cooler than that. In that sense, don’t you think about yielding it to your son?”

Of course, Geom Ujin’s expression said not a chance.

“I’ll find one. An armor even cooler and more incredible.”

Geom Mugeuk began selecting armors again. Having picked one up, he turned and asked,

“Which of these is better?”

But Geom Ujin was picking out an armor for Hwi. Because he knew well that if left alone, that man would never choose one for himself.

“Try this on.”

“I’m fine, my Lord.”

“Try it on.”

“Yes.”

While Hwi put on the armor, Geom Mugeuk shook the clothes he was holding.

“There’s a person here! Did I perhaps put on an armor that turns invisible automatically?”

Geom Ujin pretended not to hear, focusing only on Hwi.

The armor Geom Ujin had chosen for Hwi was the Muyeong Armor (Shadowless Armor). It was the lightest among the armors present. It was the most suitable armor for Hwi, who valued stealth and speed.

“It suits you well.”

Hwi’s eyes trembled as he looked in the mirror. It was not because of his own appearance in the Muyeong Armor. It was because the reflection of the Lord, looking at his own reflection with satisfaction, could be seen in the mirror.

“I do not know if it is right for me to receive something so precious.”

“Accept it.”

“Yes.”

Of course, it was not only protective armor that had been chosen for Hwi.

If told to choose for himself, Hwi would obviously pick the worst among them, so Geom Ujin personally selected a weapon for him as well.

“Try holding this.”

What Geom Ujin handed over was a single dagger. A cold, eerie energy emanated from the blade, as if it could cut through flesh and bone, and even a man’s heart, along with mysterious inscriptions carved into the hilt.

“This is no ordinary dagger.”

“It is Jinhon (Spirit Suppressing).”

At the name Jinhon, Hwi was startled. He had heard of this dagger. It was the supreme dagger known to all who wielded such blades.

It was known to be superior even to the Cheonae Dansim that Geom Ujin had first chosen.

“Why do you not choose Jinhon for yourself? Please use it, my Lord.”

“As you know, have you ever seen me use a dagger? Instead, do you not wield them?”

Hwi understood. That though Geom Ujin had seen Jinhon first, he had chosen the second-best Cheonae Dansim in order to give this one to him.

‘My Lord!’

Geom Ujin held out Jinhon, and Hwi respectfully received it with trembling hands.

“Now, infuse it with your internal energy. As you know, the first time is important.”

Hwi carefully channeled his internal energy into Jinhon.

Humm.

Jinhon reacted to Hwi’s internal energy and vibrated. It was the moment it met its new master.

“Ah!”

Hwi was not one to show his emotions, but in this moment, he could not conceal his awe.

With just a single infusion of internal energy, he could feel how fine a dagger this Jinhon was. He had never held such an excellent dagger before. It filled him with confidence that he would not lose to anyone if he fought with this blade.

‘Let’s do well from now on.’

Hwi wrapped the leather belt he had originally been wearing over the Muyeong Armor. In the belt were sheathed a few daggers and several items of unknown purpose.

Hwi drew one dagger and replaced its spot with Jinhon.

Then he turned toward Geom Ujin, clasped his hands in a respectful pugilistic salute, and bowed his head.

“Thank you, my Lord.”

He said no other words. Because no words could express his emotions at this moment.

“It suits you well. Look around, and if there is anything you need, take it.”

“This alone is sufficient.”

Hwi spoke firmly. He looked ready to return everything that had been given if more was pressed upon him, so Geom Ujin did not insist further.

Only after taking care of Hwi first did Geom Ujin look at his son.

He had expected him to be sulking over not being outfitted as well.

“Well, what do you think?”

Geom Mugeuk was already fully dressed.

“I decided on the Heukryong Secret Armor to match the color of your armor, Father.”
